| D50 | brtrue/brfalse object-condition normalization | port | `brtrue` on an object-typed condition (StackType O) now normalizes to `comp(cond != null)` in the IL reader (was a bare `ldloc`, so the seed rendered `if (value)` and ConditionDetection's inversion wrapped it as `value == 0`). `brfalse` already used `ldnull` for O. `NegateCondition` (ConditionDetection) now wraps a bare non-Comp/non-If condition as `comp(x == null)` for O types instead of always `comp(x == 0)`. Fixes `value == 0` -> `value == null`, `values[0]` -> `values[0] != null`, `text` -> `text != null` across the output. 133/133 gtest cases pass (no new tests; behavior change) |
| D51 | LoopDetection external-branch repoint + Branch TargetOffset | port | Two label bugs. (1) `ConstructLoop` only repointed back-edge branches *inside* the loop container to the new entry point; branches *outside* the loop that targeted the old entry (loop header) still pointed at it, so both the pre-header and the header carried the same `IL_XXXX` label (duplicate `IL_006C:`). Fixed by walking the whole function and repointing every branch targeting the old entry to the new entry. (2) The `Branch(Block*)` constructor left `TargetOffset = 0`, so a transform-synthesised block-target branch (e.g. the loop pre-header's exit) made the seed label the target `IL_0000` instead of its real offset. Fixed by setting `TargetOffset = target->StartILOffset` in the constructor. 134/134 gtest cases pass (1 new) |
| D52 | StObjToStLoc (EarlyExpressionTransforms subset) | port | Ports the StObjToStLoc piece of `EarlyExpressionTransforms.cs`: `stobj(ldloca V, value)` (rendered `*(&V) = value`) becomes `stloc V, value` (`V = value`) -- the shape ILInlining and the rest of the pipeline expect. Skipped: the volatile/unaligned guard (our StObj carries no such flags) and the Conv wrap for unknown-typed locals. The CLI runs it before ILInlining so the converted store can be inlined. 137/137 gtest cases pass (3 new) |
| D53 | ConditionDetection shared-tail merge + interleaved fixpoint | port | Adds the common-exit merge to ConditionDetection: `if (cond) { ...; goto X } else { ...; goto X }` (both arms end with a Branch to the same next block) drops both gotos, the block falling through to X. Turns the shared-tail if/else (e.g. `System.Decimal..ctor(int)`: `if (num >= 0) { flags = 0 } else { flags = min; num = -num }` then `lo = num; ...`) into clean structured C# with no gotos. The three transforms (inline-fall-through, invert-if-exit, drop-common-exit) now run in a combined fixpoint so a step that enables another (a post-invert inline opening up a common exit) is picked up. The post-invert inline is gated on exit compatibility (both arms must end with a Branch to the same block) so the early-exit pattern (`if (!cond) { throw }` fall-through to the rest) is NOT wrapped in an else -- the true arm being a bare Branch (the `if-goto` shape) is the ungated first inline that enables the invert. `TryInlineIfFallThrough`'s true-arm check relaxed from `Op == Branch` to `EndPointUnreachable` to handle Block true-arms. 145/145 gtest cases pass (1 new) |
| D54 | CFS value-return folding + fall-through deletion guard | port | CFS now folds `br returnBlock` (returnBlock = `leave` with a value) into a direct `leave` duplicating the value when it is a cheap pure load (ldloc/ldc.i4/ldc.i8/ldc.f4/ldc.f8/ldnull/ldstr) via a `ClonePureLoad` helper. This turns `goto returnBlock` into `return value`, eliminating the goto and turning the branch's arm into an exit (the early-return pattern: `if (cond) { return value }` fall-through to the rest). The general case (non-cloneable values) is DetectExitPoints, deferred. Also fixes a deletion bug: CFS's `if (IncomingEdgeCount == 0) MarkForDeletion` could delete a block that was still a fall-through target (the previous block's final is not EndPointUnreachable) -- the count only tracks Branch edges, so a block reachable only by fall-through could hit count 0 and be wrongly deleted, losing the fall-through path. Adds `IsFallThroughTarget` to guard the deletion. The goto count across mscorlib types drops (Math 18->12, Version 40->19, Decimal 64->53, String 513->479). 146/146 gtest cases pass (tests updated for the new folding) |
| D55 | ILAstToCSharp seed polish: byref/this deref, loop rendering, C# type keywords, bool negation | port | A batch of ILAstToCSharp rendering improvements: (1) `ldobj/stobj` on a byref variable or the implicit `this` renders as the bare name (`*(this)` -> `this`, `*(array)` -> `array`); (2) a Loop-kind BlockContainer renders as `while (true) { ... }` with the last block's back-edge dropped (implicit iteration), mid-loop back-edges as `continue`, loop-exit leaves as `break`, and the loop header unlabeled; (3) `CSharpTypeName` (now public) maps KnownTypeCode to C# keywords and recurses into array/byref, used for local declarations, the method signature, newarr, casts, and `as` (`int num`, `byte[] array`, `new byte[1]`, `(string)x`, `as string`); (4) `comp(eq, ldloc boolVar, 0)` renders as `!boolVar` for Boolean-typed variables. Together these make the `--csharp` output read like idiomatic C# (e.g. `int Max(int val1, int val2) { if (val1 >= val2) { return val1; } return val2; }`). 148/148 gtest cases pass |
| D56 | RemoveInfeasiblePathTransform (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/RemoveInfeasiblePathTransform.cs: a block that stores a known constant (0 or 1) to a StackSlot variable and branches to a multi-predecessor block that tests that variable skips the test -- the branch is redirected straight to the feasible exit (the arm the constant drives the test to) and the dead constant store is dropped. The C# block model carries the if as a non-terminal with an explicit fall-through branch as the block's last instruction; this port's block model makes the IfInstruction the block's final with an implicit fall-through to the next block, so the two exits are the if's TrueInst target (X) and the next block in the test block's container (Y): brfalse `comp(eq,s,0)` gives s==0->X/s!=0->Y, brtrue bare `ldloc s` gives s!=0->X/s==0->Y. Test blocks whose every predecessor is redirected are dropped (mirrors `SortBlocks(deleteUnreachableBlocks)`), keeping the container entry point and positional fall-through targets. The CLI runs it after InlineReturnTransform (matching the C# `GetILTransforms()` order). Fires 5 times on a 3000-method mscorlib sweep after the full pre-pipeline; the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. 155/155 gtest cases pass (7 new) |

| D57 | DetectPinnedRegions + PinnedLocal marking (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the core of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/DetectPinnedRegions.cs. IL pins locals so the GC will not move them; the only C# surface is a scoped `fixed` block, so this must run after inlining and before LoopDetection (per `GetILTransforms()`). First the local-signature decoder now reports each local's 0x45 ELEMENT_TYPE_PINNED flag (a new winmd-free `LocalTypeInfo` header, so the public `MetadataFile.hpp` does not pull the vendored winmd `<windows.h>` into every consumer), and the IL reader marks such locals `VariableKind::PinnedLocal` (the C# wraps the type in a PinnedType and unwraps it in the reader; this port carries the flag alongside the type). A new `PinnedRegion` ILAst node (Variable + Init + Body; DirectFlags=MayWriteLocals, result Void) wraps the region. The transform: `SplitBlocksAtWritesToPinnedLocals` ensures a pinned write is the block's last non-final instruction followed by a Branch; `DetectPinnedRegion`/`CreatePinnedRegion` BFS-collects the blocks reachable from the pin's branch target until the unpin store, validates every reached block's edges are all inside the region (the clone case -- a block reachable both inside and outside -- is deferred, so such methods keep their pinned local without the `fixed` sugar), moves them into a `PinnedRegion` body, strips the leading unpin store on a single-predecessor exit, and makes the pin block fall through to the next outer block (this port's block model needs an explicit Branch final where the C# uses fall-through). A leftover-writes post-pass drops dead pure stores to a `PinnedLocal` the region replaced. Skipped: the null-safe-array-to-pointer and custom-ref pin patterns (multi-block shape detection), `ProcessPinnedRegion`'s native-pointer replacement, string-to-pointer handling, and nested-region recursion. The C# `IsReferenceType == false` value-type skip is approximated as `Kind == Struct || Enum || Void` (by-ref/pointer are null, not false). The CLI now runs DetectPinnedRegions right after RemoveInfeasiblePath (before the second CFS), so `fixed (...) { ... }` appears in `--csharp` output. mscorlib has 204 methods with pinned locals (303 pinned locals); the sweep confirms the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus and the transform fires. 160/160 gtest cases pass (5 new). |

| D58 | Fix second-CFS crash: RemoveInfeasiblePath deletion + CFS SimplifyBranchChains use-after-free | port | The second CFS (the one the CLI runs after DetectPinnedRegions) segfaulted (SEH 0xc0000005) on a System.IO.FileStream method (~0x0600184E). Root cause: `RemoveInfeasiblePathTransform::Run` deleted unreachable test blocks by erasing their `unique_ptr` from the container (mirroring the C# `SortBlocks(deleteUnreachableBlocks: true)`). But a block with `IncomingEdgeCount == 0` may still contain nested containers (EH/loop bodies) whose inner blocks are referenced by branches elsewhere in the tree. Erasing the block frees those inner blocks immediately (C++ `unique_ptr` is not GC), dangling the external branches' `TargetBlock` pointers; the second CFS's `SimplifyBranchChains` then dereferenced the freed block (its memory showed the 0xDD debug fill) and crashed. The C# is safe because its GC keeps the blocks alive and because branches only target same-or-enclosing containers (an invariant this port does not yet fully guarantee). Fix: the deletion is dropped -- the dead blocks stay in the tree (they are simply unreachable, harmless; the later CFS/transforms handle them without dereferencing freed memory). Also hardened `SimplifyBranchChains` against a second use-after-free: it pre-collects all `Branch*` into a vector, but a branch replaced by a Leave (`ReplaceWith` destroys it) would dangle for later iterations. A `std::set<Branch*> destroyed` now tracks replaced branches and skips them (mirrors the C# lazy `Descendants.OfType<Branch>()` enumerable that walks the tree live). 161/161 gtest cases pass (1 new: a full 8000-method second-CFS sweep that previously crashed). |

| D41 | LoopDetection: back-edge loop containerization | port | Ports the core of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/LoopDetection.cs (subset): detects back edges (branches to a block that dominates the source), collects the natural loop, and wraps it in a BlockContainer(Kind=Loop) with the header's instructions moved to a new entry point, body blocks moved in, and the exit branch rewritten to Leave(loopContainer). Skipped: ExtendLoop (exit-point minimization), SwitchDetection integration, the full BlockILTransform post-order driver. Runs as a standalone IILTransform: processes each Normal-kind container in one pass (reverse-post-order over the original CFG), skips Loop-kind containers to avoid re-detecting the loop just constructed (the C# avoids this because the newly created entry point has no CFG node). Fix for the use-after-free crash the rewrite caused: collect Branch pointers first, mutate second (ReplaceWith destroys the Branch mid-traversal). The CLI runs CFS+ILInlining+LoopDetection before the C# seed. 112/112 gtest cases pass (3 new) |

| D59 | DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the core of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks.cs (subset): a `catch (T e) when (cond)` filter starts with a redundant isinst type test (the catch is already typed T), so the entry block's `stloc temp(isinst T(ldloc ex)); if (comp(ldloc temp != ldnull)) br whenCond` (3-instruction variant) or `if (comp(isinst T(ldloc ex) != ldnull)) br whenCond` (2-instruction variant) -- plus the `falseBlock: stloc ret(ldc.i4 0); br exitBlock` / `exitBlock (2 preds): leave container(ldloc ret)` it falls through to -- is rewritten to drop the test: the entry branches straight to the when-condition block, and (3-instruction variant) the temp store is rewritten to copy the caught exception. The catch variable's type is refined to the isinst type. The CLI runs it after DetectPinnedRegions, before the second CFS (matching `GetILTransforms()`). Two model divergences adapted: (1) the IfInstruction is this port's block final with a positional fall-through, so the C# `br falseBlock` (the block's last instruction) is the next block in the filter container, and (2) the C# counts a container-entry edge (+1 when a BlockContainer connects) so it requires `entryPoint.IncomingEdgeCount == 1`, but this port's `RecomputeIncomingEdgeCounts` does not count that edge -- a filter entry (reached by the EH mechanism, no Branch in the tree) has `IncomingEdgeCount == 0` here, so the check is `== 0`. Skipped: `PropagateExceptionVariable` (copy-propagates the exception variable into the temp/intermediate copies the filter introduces; needs per-variable load-instruction lists this port does not maintain, and is a rendering nicety) and `SortBlocks(deleteUnreachableBlocks: true)` (erasing the now-unreachable falseBlock is unsafe in this port -- see D58 -- so the dead blocks stay, and exitBlock keeps both Branch predecessors in the count). mscorlib carries no `catch ... when` (filter) handlers in the scanned methods, so the transform does not fire on the corpus; the hand-built tests verify the rewrite and the sweep verifies the invariant holds. 167/167 gtest cases pass (6 new). |
| D60 | LdLocaDupInitObjTransform + DefaultValue node (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/LdLocaDupInitObjTransform.cs: the Roslyn (>= 2) codegen for `var v = default(T);` plus a use of `&v` emits `ldloca v; dup; initobj T`, which the reader lowers to `stloc s(ldloca v); stobj T(ldloc s, default(T), T)` (the `dup` makes the address stack slot `s`; `initobj` is the `stobj` of `default(T)` to `ldloc s`). The transform rewrites that pair to `stloc v(default(T)); stloc s(ldloca v)` so the address slot `s` can be inlined into its subsequent uses (the C# runs it before the inlining pass in EarlyExpressionTransforms). Required adding a `DefaultValue` ILAst node (a SimpleInstruction carrying the type operand; result = StackTypeOf(Type)) and changing the reader's `initobj` to model it as `stobj(addr, DefaultValue(type), type)` instead of the prior type-erased `stobj(addr, ldnull|ldc.i4 0, type)` -- so the C# seed now renders `default(T)` (e.g. `VersionResult versionResult = default(VersionResult);`) rather than `= null` for a struct init, and the transform's `inst2.Value is DefaultValue` guard is faithful. The ILAstToCSharp seed renders `DefaultValue` as `default(CSharpTypeName(type))`. Skipped vs the C#: `inst2.UnalignedPrefix == 0` / `!inst2.IsVolatile` (our StObj carries no prefix flags -- see D52) and the `s.LoadCount > 1` guard from the comment (the C# code itself does not check it; the rewrite is correct regardless). `TypeUtils.IsCompatibleTypeForMemoryAccess(v.Type, inst2.Type)` is approximated inline (equal ReflectionName, or both reference types, or both integer types of equal StackType, or either unknown) -- skpped the C#'s NormalizeTypeVisitor.TypeErasure (this port carries no nullable/modifier wrappers). The CLI runs the transform after DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks, before the second CFS (the deferred DetectExitPoints would sit between them in the C# order). mscorlib (.NET Framework 4, compiled with the legacy csc) emits the legacy `ldloca; ldloca; initobj` pattern (no `dup`/stack-slot `s`), which StObjToStLoc handles directly, so this transform fires 0 times on the corpus; the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (positive + three negatives: non-`ldloc s` target, non-`DefaultValue` value, incompatible type) and the 8000-method sweep verifies the invariant holds. 173/173 gtest cases pass (6 new). |
| D61 | EarlyExpressionTransforms (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the core of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/EarlyExpressionTransforms.cs (the next transform in `GetILTransforms()` order after LdLocaDupInitObjTransform): a tree-walking transform that folds the early expression-level rewrites the rest of the pipeline depends on. Three pieces ported: (1) `StObjToStLoc` -- `stobj(ldloca V, value)` becomes `stloc V, value` (already ported as a standalone early transform in D52; included here for faithfulness to the C# structure, where it lives inside this visitor); (2) `LdObjToLdLoc` -- `ldobj(ldloca V, type)` becomes `ldloc V`, the load-side analog (the C# comment: "this transform is required because ILInlining only works with stloc/ldloc"); (3) `FixComparisonKindLdNull` -- a comparison against `ldnull` whose kind is vacuous for a reference (`gt`/`le` on the right, `lt`/`ge` on the left) normalizes to the equivalent `ne`/`eq`, and `box T(arg) ==/!= ldnull` (T a type parameter) unwraps to `arg ==/!= ldnull` (a boxed generic-default null check is really a check on the underlying value). Skipped vs the C#: `AddressOfLdLocToLdLoca` (needs an `AddressOf` node this port does not model -- the reader emits `ldloca` directly, not `addressof(ldloc)`), the `Conv` wrap for unknown-typed locals in StObj/LdObjToStLoc (rare; the local's type usually matches the memory type), the `IsLifted` guard on `FixComparisonKindLdNull` (this port does not model nullable lifting, so no `Comp` is ever lifted), `TransformAsyncHelpersAwaitToAwait` (needs an `Await` node + an `IsAsync` function flag), and `TransformDecimalCtorToConstant` (needs an `LdcDecimal` node + the decimal type). The `box T` unwrap is implemented by `box->TakeChild(0)` (detach the Argument) then `comp->SetChild(0, ...)` (destroy the now-childless Box, wire the Argument as `comp->Left`). The CLI runs `EarlyExpressionTransforms` after `LdLocaDupInitObjTransform` and before the second CFS (matching the C# `GetILTransforms()` order); the standalone `StObjToStLoc` still runs early (before the first `ILInlining`) so the converted stores fold in the first inlining pass. The 8000-method mscorlib sweep confirms the invariant holds and that both `ldobj` and `stobj` counts drop (some `ldobj(ldloca)`/`stobj(ldloca)` become `ldloc`/`stloc`). 187/187 gtest cases pass (14 new: 2 StObjToStLoc, 3 LdObjToLdLoc, 8 FixComparisonKindLdNull, 1 sweep). |

| D62 | RemoveDeadVariableInit (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the core of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/RemoveDeadVariableInit.cs (the next transform in `GetILTransforms()` order after EarlyExpressionTransforms, skipping the deferred SplitVariables that sits between them): drops dead stores to variables that are never read from. A variable flagged `RemoveIfRedundant` (a new ILVariable field, set by RemoveInfeasiblePathTransform on the redirected stack slot -- matching the C# `s.RemoveIfRedundant = true`) or under the `RemoveDeadStores` setting, with `LoadCount == 0` and `AddressCount == 0`, has every `StLoc` to it dropped: a pure value goes with the store (`RemoveInstructionAt`); an impure value is unwrapped so its side effect survives (the value moves into the block instruction slot, reparented, mirroring the DetectPinnedRegions leftover-writes pass and ILInlining's dead-store path). Dead-copy chains (a removed store whose value was a `LdLoc` of a now-dead variable) collapse via a recompute fixpoint -- `ComputeVariableUsage` after each pass picks up the decremented load count, so the next pass drops the now-dead variable's stores too (the C# uses a variable queue with stale counts; the recompute fixpoint is equivalent and avoids reasoning about staleness). The CLI runs `RemoveDeadVariableInit` after `EarlyExpressionTransforms` and before the second CFS (matching `GetILTransforms()`, where it sits after the deferred SplitVariables so `stobj(ldloca V, ..)` has already collapsed to `stloc V, ..`). Skipped vs the C#: `ResetUsesInitialValueFlag` (needs the `DefiniteAssignmentVisitor` dataflow over the `DataFlowVisitor<State>` framework -- a larger slice; it only affects `var x;` vs `var x = default;` rendering, which this port's seed does not model) and the `StackType.Ref` stack-slot `IType` inference (needs `InferType`, a rendering nicety). `IsAsync`/`IsIterator` and `StateMachineField` are not modeled, so `removeDeadStores` is just the setting (the C# fallback; default false). CFS's existing `RemoveDeadStackStores` only handles single-definition (`IsSingleDefinition`) stack slots; `RemoveDeadVariableInit` fills the multi-store dead-variable gap (a variable with `StoreCount > 1` but `LoadCount == 0`). The 8000-method mscorlib sweep (running the full pre-pipeline through `RemoveDeadVariableInit` with `RemoveInfeasiblePath` firing and flagging stack slots) confirms the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. 195/195 gtest cases pass (8 new). |
| D63 | LongSet/LongInterval + SwitchSection adoption (Phase 4 switch-family foundation) | port | Ports the foundational data structure the whole switch-detection family (SwitchAnalysis, SwitchDetection, SwitchOnStringTransform, SwitchOnNullableTransform) depends on, ahead of those transforms. `LongSet` (ICSharpCode.Decompiler/Util/LongSet.cs) and `LongInterval` (the 64-bit half of Util/Interval.cs) are an immutable set of longs stored as a sorted list of non-empty, non-overlapping, non-touching intervals. The interval form is mandatory: the switch transforms compute the *complement* of a finite set (SwitchAnalysis.AnalyzeCondition returns `new LongSet(val).Invert()` for an inequality, and `MakeSetWhereComparisonIsTrue` builds the LessThan/GreaterThan sets as complements of [val..Max] / [Min..val]); that complement is infinite and cannot be held in `std::set<int64_t>`, which is why `SwitchSection::Labels` could not stay a plain value set. Ported faithfully: `Inclusive/InclusiveEnd/IsEmpty/Contains/Intersect/Range` (LongInterval); `Empty/Universe/Count/Overlaps/IntersectWith/UnionWith/ExceptWith(=IntersectWith(Invert()))/Invert/AddOffset/IsSubsetOf/IsSupersetOf/IsProperSubsetOf/IsProperSupersetOf/Contains(upper_bound binary search)/ContainingInterval/SetEquals/Values/ToString` (LongSet). The C# `unchecked` integer arithmetic is replicated through `uint64_t` casts (signed overflow is UB in C++); the `End == long.MinValue` sentinel for `long.MaxValue + 1` and the `MergeOverlapping` special case for an interval reaching Max are preserved. `SwitchSection::Labels` changed from `std::set<int64_t>` to `Util::LongSet`; the IL reader builds one single-value `LongSet` per case index, the ILAstToCSharp seed renders a single-value interval as `case N:` and a multi-value interval as `case A..B:` (the reader only ever emits single-value labels today, so existing output is unchanged), and the `SwitchSection::WriteTo` dump renders intervals readably (`section(0, 1, default)` style). The int-sized `Interval` from the same C# file is deferred (no in-scope transform needs it yet). 32 new gtest cases cover LongInterval, LongSet, and the exact `AnalyzeCondition` value-set shapes the switch transforms consume (Equality=singleton, Inequality=complement, LessThan/GreaterThan/LessThanOrEqual/GreaterThanOrEqual signed, AddOffset label-shift with wraparound); the existing `SwitchEmitsCaseLabelsWithGotos` test and the 8000-method mscorlib sweeps (which decode and render real `switch` instructions in System.Convert etc.) confirm no regression. 227/227 gtest cases pass (32 new). |
| D64 | SwitchAnalysis (Phase 4 switch-family core, next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/SwitchAnalysis.cs: the analysis helper SwitchDetection depends on. It reconstructs a C# switch (compiled to a sequence of if-statements testing one integer variable, e.g. for non-contiguous case labels) as a list of (LongSet labels, body instruction) sections plus the inner blocks the switch absorbs. `AnalyzeBlock` seeds from a root block's tail (tailOnly) and recursively walks the if-chain: each block's final `if (cond) br X` with implicit fall-through to the next block tests the switch variable (`AnalyzeCondition` handles `comp(V OP val)`, `comp((V - sub) OP val)` via AddOffset, bare `ldloc V` = all-except-0, and `logic.not(comp(...))` = comp(eq, X, 0) unwrap via recursion); the true arm (a Branch to a block) is recursed into or becomes a section, the false arm is the next block in the container (recursed or a synthesized-Branch section). `AnalyzeSwitch` handles an existing IL SwitchInstruction (offset via `switch(V +/- val)`), and `AddSection` de-duplicates sections that branch to the same block or leave to the same container. `MakeSetWhereComparisonIsTrue` / `MakeGreaterThanOrEqualSet` / `MakeLessThanOrEqualSet` port the value-set constructors (the C# `Sign` enum collapsed to a bool `unsigned_` matching this port's `Comp::Unsigned`; signed overflow via `uint64_t` casts). Adapted to this port's block model: the IfInstruction is the block's `FinalInstruction` with an implicit fall-through to the next block (the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at `Instructions[Count-2]` with an explicit fall-through Branch as the last instruction), so the false arm is the next block in the container and the fall-through section body is a synthesized Branch kept alive in `ownedBodies_` (not in the tree). `MatchLdcI` handles LdcI4/LdcI8 (the C# also unwraps Conv sign/zero-extend, which this port's reader does not emit for ldc). 14 new gtest cases cover the two-case if-chain (3 sections), same-target de-dup, brfalse/brtrue value sets, signed/unsigned LessThan/GreaterThanOrEqual (wrap), logic.not unwrap, Sub AddOffset, IL switch (ContainsILSwitch + the AllowUnreachableCases guard for the empty default), plain-branch/non-comparison negatives, and the MakeSetWhereComparisonIsTrue contract. 241/241 gtest cases pass (14 new). |
| D65 | SwitchDetection.SimplifySwitchInstruction subset (Phase 4 switch-family, next-in-order) | port | Ports the self-contained subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/SwitchDetection.cs that is reachable without the deferred LoopContext / HighLevelLoopTransform / NullableLiftingTransform / SwitchOnStringTransform dependencies the full `SwitchDetection.Run` needs. `SimplifySwitchInstruction(block, context)` is the static method the C# pipeline calls twice -- once from `ControlFlowSimplification` (1st pass, before branch-chain collapse) and once from `SwitchDetection.ProcessBlock` (2nd pass, when a block does not form a switch) -- and it operates on the SwitchInstructions the IL reader already emits from `switch` opcodes (per D29), so wiring the 1st pass into CFS makes real progress on the corpus now. It does three things: (1) de-duplicates sections whose body is a Branch to the same block, merging their label sets via `LongSet::UnionWith` (the first section branching to a block is the primary; later ones merge into it and are removed) -- only Branch bodies are de-duplicated, matching the C# `MatchBranch` (Leave bodies are left to the later exit-point transforms); (2) `AdjustLabels` moves an Add/Sub offset from `switch(V +/- val)` into the section labels -- the switch value becomes the bare operand (`bop->Left`, detached then re-parented as `sw->Value`, destroying the bop) and each label is `AddOffset`-shifted (Add: `unchecked(-val)` via a `uint64_t` cast; Sub: `+val`); skipped for a non-Add/Sub operator or an overflow-checked bop (`!bop.CheckForOverflow`); (3) `SortSwitchSections` orders the sections by label value when the `SortSwitchSections` setting is on, or by branch-target IL offset (the C# default; preserves the original case order across rebuilds) otherwise. Added `SortSwitchSections` to `ILTransformSettings` (default false, matching the C#). Skipped vs the C#: `primarySection.HasNullLabel |= section.HasNullLabel` (this port's `SwitchSection` does not model `HasNullLabel` -- a SwitchOnNullable concern) and the `Leave l => l.StartILOffset` sort key (this port does not carry per-instruction ILRange, so a Leave body uses offset 0 as a fallback -- but the CFS first pass only sees Branch bodies, so this does not affect output today). The full `SwitchDetection.Run` (ProcessBlock / UseCSharpSwitch / AnalyzeControlFlow / LoopContext / AddNullCase / InlineSwitchExpressionDefaultCaseThrowHelper) and its deferred dependencies (LoopContext's `HighLevelLoopTransform.MatchIncrementBlock`/`MatchDoWhileConditionBlock`, `NullableLiftingTransform.MatchHasValueCall`, `SwitchOnStringTransform.MatchComputeStringOrReadOnlySpanHashCall`, the `SparseIntegerSwitch` setting gate) remain for later iterations. 8 new gtest cases (de-dup, Add/Sub AdjustLabels positive, three AdjustLabels no-op negatives, offset sort, label-value sort with the setting on, no-op for a non-switch block, and an 8000-method mscorlib CFS sweep exercising the reader-emitted switches and confirming the invariant holds). 249/249 gtest cases pass (8 new). |
| D66 | HighLevelLoopTransform helpers + SwitchDetection.LoopContext (Phase 4 switch-family foundation, next-in-order) | port | Ports the continue/break analysis half of the deferred `SwitchDetection.Run` dependencies, ahead of Run itself. (1) The static helper subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/HighLevelLoopTransform.cs that LoopContext (and the later while/for loop transform) call: `MatchIncrement` (`stloc v(add(ldloc v, const))` -> the loop's increment variable; the `CompoundAssignmentInstruction` arm is deferred -- this port models no compound-assign node), `MatchIncrementBlock` (a block whose final is a Branch to the loop head and whose other instructions are all simple -- the for-loop increment block, the `continue;` target), `MatchDoWhileConditionBlock` (a block whose final is an if with no else, true arm a Branch to the loop head and fall-through to the exit -- the do-while condition block, also a `continue;` target), and `IsSimpleStatement` (calls and assignments; in this port call/callvirt/newobj all emit a `Call` node and the compound-assign opcodes have no node class, so only Call/StLoc/StObj are recognized). Adapted to this port's block model: the IfInstruction is the block's `FinalInstruction` with an implicit fall-through to the next block in the container (the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at `Instructions[Count-2]` with an explicit fall-through Branch as the last instruction), so `MatchDoWhileConditionBlock` reads the if from `block->FinalInstruction` (not `Instructions[Count-2]`) and the fall-through from the next block in the container (not `Instructions.Last()`); a fall-through with no next block is treated as a `return` only when the container is the function body (the C# `last.MatchReturn` / `Leave.IsLeavingFunction`). The full HighLevelLoopTransform (the while/for restructuring, `MatchForLoop`, `GetIncrementBlock`) is a larger slice and stays deferred. (2) `SwitchDetection.LoopContext` (ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/SwitchDetection.cs's nested `LoopContext` class): constructed for one `contextNode` (the switch head) over a per-container `ControlFlowGraph`; it walks the contextNode's successors collecting every node that dominates the contextNode (a loop head), maps each loop head's continue target (its increment/do-while-condition block, via the HighLevelLoopTransform helpers) to a 1-based depth ordered by post-order number, and exposes `MatchContinue`/`GetContinueDepth` and `GetBreakTargets` (a pre-order dominator-tree walk collecting each node's successors that leave the subtree and are not depth-1 continues). The recursive C# `Analyze` local is an iterative DFS with an explicit stack (avoids deep recursion on large methods); `TreeTraversal.PreOrder` is an inline iterative pre-order over `DominatorTreeChildren`. The C# `MatchContinue(node, out int depth)` out-overload is exposed as `GetContinueDepth` instead, since a C++ `int&` out-overload alongside the by-value `int` overload is ambiguous for an lvalue argument. The `OnlyOrDefault` predicate helper is inlined in `FindContinue`. This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation: `SwitchDetection.Run` (which consumes LoopContext via `UseCSharpSwitch`/`AnalyzeControlFlow`/`SwitchUsesGoto`/`IsSingleCondition`) is the next iteration; LoopContext is exercised directly by an 8000-method mscorlib sweep that runs the pre-pipeline through the second CFS (the point where Run will sit, before LoopDetection, so loops are flat back-edges), builds a CFG per container, and constructs LoopContext for each in-loop block -- it finds 504 loop-bearing containers and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. 16 new gtest cases (4 MatchIncrement, 3 MatchIncrementBlock, 3 MatchDoWhileConditionBlock, 1 IsSimpleStatement, 4 LoopContext continue/break/no-loop/diamond, 1 mscorlib sweep). 265/265 gtest cases pass (16 new). |
| D67 | SwitchDetection.Run / ProcessBlock / UseCSharpSwitch (Phase 4 switch-family, next-in-order) | port | Ports the full `SwitchDetection.Run` transform that the LoopContext / HighLevelLoopTransform foundation (D66) unblocked, completing the switch-detection family's core. `Run` iterates every BlockContainer and, per block, runs `SwitchAnalysis.AnalyzeBlock`; when the block forms a switch (`UseCSharpSwitch`), it builds a `SwitchInstruction` from the detected (LongSet labels, body) sections, replaces the block's tail, clears the absorbed inner blocks, and sorts the sections; otherwise it runs `SimplifySwitchInstruction` as the 2nd pass (the 1st pass is in CFS, per D65). `UseCSharpSwitch` ports the full heuristic: the default-section lookup (the section with > MaxValuesPerSection=100 values is the complement-of-the-cases default), the `ContainsILSwitch` early return (an existing IL switch is a strong signal), the `ifCount < intervalCount` guard (prefer an if-chain when it has fewer branches than the switch would have label-intervals), `AnalyzeControlFlow` (build the CFG + LoopContext, collect the flow nodes = switch head + inner blocks, and the case nodes = section-body target blocks in this container that are not continues), `IsSingleCondition` (a 2-section switch that is one short-circuited condition, e.g. a loop test `while (c == '\n' || c == '\r')`, reads better as an if), and `SwitchUsesGoto` (more than one external case, or multiple/zero break targets -> the switch would need gotos, so stay as ifs). Adapted to this port's block model: (1) the `IfInstruction` is the block's `FinalInstruction` with an implicit fall-through, so replacing the final with the `SwitchInstruction` is the whole operation (the C# removes a separate fall-through Branch as the block's last instruction); (2) `IsFlowNode` checks an empty `Instructions` list with an `IfInstruction` final (the C# checks `Instructions.FirstOrDefault() is IfInstruction` -- the if is a non-terminal in the C# block model); (3) the cleared inner blocks are removed by building a new `Blocks` vector without them (the C# `Blocks.RemoveAll(empty)` / `SortBlocks(deleteUnreachable)`; the latter is unsafe in this port per D58 -- a block may hold nested containers referenced by external branches, and erasing it dangles those `TargetBlock` pointers -- so the dead blocks are dropped only when fully empty and unreachable, never via `SortBlocks`). The C# builds the SwitchInstruction holding the *same* Branch objects the analysis found (the GC keeps them alive after the inner blocks are cleared); this port has no GC, so each section body is **cloned** (`CloneBody`: a Branch is trivial, a Leave may carry a simple-load value -- LdLoc/LdcI4/LdcI8/LdNull/LdStr -- and an uncloneable body aborts the switch for that block, leaving the if-chain as ifs). SwitchAnalysis.SwitchVariable changed from `ILVariable*` to `ILVariablePtr` so Run can build a `LdLoc` from it; the switch value is widened to I8 via a `Conv` when not I4/I8 (matching the C#). Added `SparseIntegerSwitch` (default true, the C# C# 1.0 setting) and `RemoveDeadCode` (default false, an F# decompilation aid) to `ILTransformSettings`; Run is a no-op when `SparseIntegerSwitch` is off. Wired Run into the CLI pipeline after the second CFS, before LoopDetection (the `GetILTransforms()` position). Skipped vs the C#: `MatchRoslynSwitchOnString` (needs `SwitchOnStringTransform.MatchComputeStringOrReadOnlySpanHashCall`), `AddNullCase` (needs `NullableLiftingTransform.MatchHasValueCall`), and `InlineSwitchExpressionDefaultCaseThrowHelper` (needs `IMethod`/`IType` resolution via the type system, not yet carried) -- the first two return false/no-op, the third is a rendering nicety for switch-expression default throws. The 8000-method mscorlib sweep (full pre-pipeline through Run) confirms the invariant holds and the reader-emitted SwitchInstructions survive (the 2nd-pass SimplifySwitchInstruction keeps them; the if-chain reconstruction only adds switches). 5 new gtest cases (if-chain -> switch reconstruction, SparseIntegerSwitch-off no-op, non-switch block left alone, existing IL switch handled, mscorlib Run sweep). 270/270 gtest cases pass (5 new). |
| D68 | NullableLiftingTransform helpers + SwitchInstruction nullable fields (Phase 4 switch-family foundation, next-in-order) | port | Ports the static pattern-matching helper subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/NullableLiftingTransform.cs that the next in-order transform (`SwitchOnNullableTransform`) and SwitchDetection's deferred `AddNullCase` consume, ahead of those. `MatchHasValueCall(inst, out arg)` recognises `call get_HasValue(arg)` on `System.Nullable<T>` (1 argument) and `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, out arg)` the 1-argument `call GetValueOrDefault(arg)`; both check the method name (the short part of the Call's resolved display name, after `::`) and that the call's declaring type resolves to `KnownTypeCode::NullableOfT`. The C# checks `call.Method.DeclaringTypeDefinition?.KnownTypeCode == KnownTypeCode.NullableOfT`; this port's `Call` carried only the display-name string, so `Call` gained an optional `DeclaringType` (an `ITypePtr`) that the IL reader now populates from the method token via a new `MetadataFile::ResolveMethodDeclaringType(token)` (MethodDef parent TypeDef, MemberRef parent TypeRef/TypeDef/TypeSpec, MethodSpec unwrapped to its MethodDefOrRef) -- mirroring `GetMethodSignature`'s token handling. A file-local `KnownTypeCodeOf(IType*)` unwraps a `ParameterizedType` to its `GenericType()` (e.g. `Nullable<int>` -> `Nullable`1`) before reading the `KnownType`'s code, since the resolved declaring type may be the parameterized instantiation rather than the generic definition the C# already holds. A null `DeclaringType` (token did not resolve) is treated like the C# null `DeclaringTypeDefinition` -- the helpers return false, so an unresolvable method never matches. `SwitchInstruction` gained `IsLifted` (bool) and `Type` (the `Nullable<T>` lift context) and `SwitchSection` gained `HasNullLabel` (bool, the `case null:` arm) faithful to the generated `Instructions.cs`; the ILAst dump renders a lifted switch and a `null`-label section, and the ILAstToCSharp seed renders `case null:` for a `HasNullLabel` section (harmless today -- no section has `HasNullLabel` until SwitchOnNullable runs). Added `LiftNullables` (default true, matching `DecompilerSettings`, which only turns it off for C# < 2) to `ILTransformSettings`. Skipped vs the C#: the 2-argument `GetValueOrDefault(value, fallback)` overload (the switch-on-nullable patterns use the 1-arg form), the `MatchHasValueCall`/`MatchGetValueOrDefault` `(out ILVariable v)` ldloca-variants (used by the full `NullableLiftingStatementTransform`, deferred), `MatchNegatedHasValueCall`/`MatchNullableCtor`/`MatchNull` (full lift transform), and the nullable-lift rewriting itself. This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (like the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext foundations): no pipeline consumer yet, so it does not change output. An 8000-method mscorlib sweep decodes real methods, walks every `Call`, and confirms the reader populates `DeclaringType` for real call sites and the helpers never misfire (they assert a non-null arg on every match); the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. 13 new gtest cases (5 MatchHasValueCall positive/negative -- instantiation + definition declaring type, non-Nullable type, null declaring type, wrong method name, wrong arg count, non-Call; 3 MatchGetValueOrDefault -- positive, non-Nullable, two-arg-form rejected; SwitchInstruction nullable fields + dump; LiftNullables default; mscorlib sweep). 283/283 gtest cases pass (13 new). |
| D69 | SwitchOnNullableTransform (Phase 4 switch-family, next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/SwitchOnNullableTransform.cs: the next in-order transform after SwitchDetection (the D68 NullableLifting helpers foundation unblocked it). It detects the two `switch`-on-`Nullable<T>` code shapes the C# compiler emits and folds each into a single lifted `SwitchInstruction` carrying an explicit `case null:` arm (`SwitchSection.HasNullLabel`), so the seed renders a switch over a nullable with a null case. (1) Legacy csc `MatchSwitchOnNullable`: `stloc tmp(ldloca V); stloc sw(call GetValueOrDefault(ldloc tmp)); if (logic.not(call get_HasValue(ldloc tmp))) br nullCase; [fall-through] switchBlock { switch (ldloc sw) { ... } }` -- the block's two stlocs store the nullable's address (`tmp`) and its underlying value (`sw`), and the if guards on HasValue; the fall-through switchBlock dispatches on `sw`. Folded into `switch (ldloc V) lifted { <switchBlock's sections>; case null: nullCase }`. Requires `tmp.IsSingleDefinition && tmp.LoadCount == 2` and `sw.IsSingleDefinition && sw.LoadCount == 1`, both calls receiving `ldloc tmp`, the switchBlock single-predecessor with a `SwitchInstruction` final. (2) Roslyn `MatchRoslynSwitchOnNullable`: `if (logic.not(call get_HasValue(target))) br nullCase; [fall-through] switchBlock` where switchBlock holds either `stloc sw(call GetValueOrDefault(target)); switch (ldloc sw) { ... }` or the inlined `switch (call GetValueOrDefault(target)) { ... }`. Folded into `switch (ldloc v) lifted { ...; case null: }` when `target` is `ldloca v`, else `switch (ldobj target, Nullable<T>)` (a typed load of the nullable). Requires `IsPure(target.Flags())` (folding an impure target into the switch value would drop the side effect) and structural equality between the get_HasValue and GetValueOrDefault arguments (`target2.Match(target).Success` -- a focused `StructurallyEquals` covering LdLoca/LdLoc/LdcI4/LdNull/Call, conservative-unequal on unhandled kinds so the transform leaves the block as-is rather than mis-folding). `BuildLiftedSwitch` moves the source switch's sections into the new switch (the source switchBlock is left with an empty switch -- unreachable, harmless) and appends a `case null:` section. Gated on the `LiftNullables` setting (default true, from D68). Wired into the CLI pipeline after SwitchDetection and before LoopDetection (the `GetILTransforms()` position), so ifs are still block finals with positional fall-through (ConditionDetection has not run) and loops are flat back-edges. Adapted to this port's block model: the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at `Instructions[Count-2]` with an explicit fall-through `br switchBlock` as the block's last instruction, and the switchBlock's switch at `Instructions[0]` (Count==1); this port makes the IfInstruction the block's `FinalInstruction` with an implicit fall-through to the next block in the container, and the switchBlock's switch its `FinalInstruction` (Instructions empty), so the `br switchBlock` fall-through is `NextBlockInContainer(block)` (or the if's `FalseInst` Branch target when an earlier transform set one) and replacing the `FinalInstruction` with the new switch is the whole rewrite (the C# replaces the `br switchBlock` instruction and removes the if + stlocs by index). The C# `SortBlocks(deleteUnreachableBlocks: true)` cleanup is unsafe in this port (D58 -- a block may hold nested containers referenced by external branches, and erasing it dangles those `TargetBlock` pointers), so the dead switchBlock stays in the tree (unreachable, harmless) and only the edge counts are refreshed via `RecomputeIncomingEdgeCounts`; `ComputeVariableUsage` is run first so the IsSingleDefinition/LoadCount guards read fresh counts. After the fold, `SwitchDetection::SimplifySwitchInstruction` is run on the modified block (de-dup / adjust-labels / sort), mirroring the C#. The section bodies are `Branch`es to case blocks that stay in the tree (reachable from the new switch), so no cloning is needed (unlike SwitchDetection.Run's CloneBody, where the inner blocks are cleared); the new switch's sections move the source switch's `unique_ptr<SwitchSection>` ownership directly. 9 new gtest cases (legacy fold; legacy rejects non-Nullable declaring type; legacy rejects non-switch fall-through; Roslyn fold with the stloc sw + switch; Roslyn inlined fold; Roslyn rejects impure target; Roslyn rejects mismatched HasValue/GetValueOrDefault targets; LiftNullables-off no-op; and an 8000-method mscorlib sweep running the full pre-pipeline through SwitchOnNullable that confirms the ILAst invariant holds -- whether the legacy-csc .NET Framework 4 corpus has any switch-on-nullable is corpus-dependent, so the sweep asserts the invariant, not a fold count). 292/292 gtest cases pass (9 new). |
| D70 | MatchInstruction ILAst node + IsPatternMatch helper (Phase 4 pattern-matching foundation, next-in-order) | port | Ports the MatchInstruction ILAst node (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/MatchInstruction.hpp, from the generated Instructions.cs + the hand-written MatchInstruction.cs) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order transform (PatternMatchingTransform), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting precedent. The node is the C# `is` pattern: it evaluates TestedOperand, matches the value against the flags (CheckType = `is T`, CheckNotNull = `is {}`, neither = `is var`), stores the matched value into Variable (it is an IStoreInstruction in the C#), and evaluates to I4 (1 if the pattern matches, 0 otherwise); SubPatterns carries the recursive/positional sub-patterns. DirectFlags = MayWriteLocals | SideEffect | MayThrow | ControlFlow (faithful); the base `ILInstruction::Flags()` (DirectFlags | union of children) already equals the C# `ComputeFlags`, so no Flags override is needed. `IsVar` is `!CheckType && !CheckNotNull && SubPatterns.empty()`; `HasDesignator` is `Variable.LoadCount + Variable.AddressCount > SubPatterns.Count`. The static `IsPatternMatch(inst, out testedOperand, settings)` helper (the core PatternMatchingTransform's recursive sub-pattern path consults) recognises a MatchInstruction (testedOperand = TestedOperand), a Comp as a constant/relational pattern (testedOperand = Left, gated on RelationalPatterns for non-==/!= kinds and PatternCombinators for !=), a logic.not-wrapped pattern (this port's `comp(eq, X, ldc.i4 0)` shape, gated on PatternCombinators), and a `string.op_Equality(x, "lit")` Call (a string constant pattern; recognised by the method-name suffix `::op_Equality` and a KnownTypeCode::String DeclaringType -- this port has no `Method.IsOperator`, so the suffix is the approximation). `IsConstant` covers LdcI4/LdcI8/LdcF4/LdcF8/LdNull (LdcDecimal deferred -- no node). Deferred vs the C# generated node: the deconstruct patterns (`IsDeconstructCall`/`IsDeconstructTuple` + the `IMethod` operand and `GetDeconstructResultType`/`NumPositionalPatterns`/`IsDeconstructMethod`) need an `IMethod` type-system object and `TupleType`, neither of which this port carries yet -- the type/non-null/var-pattern fields and helpers (the ones PatternMatchValueTypes/PatternMatchRefTypes produce) are ported; IsVar therefore reduces to `!CheckType && !CheckNotNull && SubPatterns.empty()`. ComputeVariableUsage (ControlFlow/VariableUsage.cpp) counts the node as a store to its Variable (the C# `Connected()` hook calls `variable.AddStoreInstruction(this)`; this port walks the tree, so a `case OpCode::MatchInstruction` increments `Variable->StoreCount`). The ILAstToCSharp seed renders a MatchInstruction condition as the C# `is` pattern (matching the real back end's `VisitMatchInstruction`): `expr is var x` (IsVar), `expr is T x` (CheckType + designator), `expr is {} x` (CheckNotNull, no type), `expr is T` (CheckType, no designator). Added the four pattern-matching settings to `ILTransformSettings` (PatternMatching, RecursivePatternMatching, PatternCombinators, RelationalPatterns, all default true, matching DecompilerSettings) that PatternMatchingTransform and `IsPatternMatch` consult. This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform constructs MatchInstructions yet, so `--csharp` output is unchanged (no real `is` patterns appear until PatternMatchingTransform lands; the seed's MatchInstruction case is exercised by the unit tests, not the corpus). The remaining PatternMatchingTransform dependencies stay deferred: `MatchIfAtEndOfBlock` (the C# block-model helper; this port's if-as-final model needs the same adaptation as SwitchAnalysis), `CheckAllUsesDominatedBy` (needs per-variable `LoadInstructions`/`AddressInstructions`/`StoreInstructions` lists this port does not maintain -- a repeatedly-deferred infrastructure piece, see D11/D62/D68), `DetectExitPoints.CompatibleExitInstruction` (ExitPoints.cs, deferred), and `PropertyOrFieldAccess` (needs `IMember`/`IField` accessor metadata). 9 new gtest cases (node invariant/flags/dump for the three flavours; IsVar/HasDesignator; SubPatterns-as-children invariant; store-counting in ComputeVariableUsage; IsPatternMatch for MatchInstruction/Comp-constant/relational-gating/logic.not-combinator-gating/null; string op_Equality; the four settings default; seed rendering of `is var x`/`is T x`/`is {} x`/`is T`; and an 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructing MatchInstructions from real decoded LdLoc operands confirming the invariant/IsPatternMatch/dump hold across the corpus). 301/301 gtest cases pass (9 new). |
| D71 | PatternMatchingTransform (Phase 4, next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/PatternMatchingTransform.cs (the next in-order transform after the D70 MatchInstruction foundation): detects the C# 7.0 `is` patterns the Roslyn compiler emits for `expr is T x` (a type test plus a variable capture) and rewrites the isinst + null-test block tail into a single MatchInstruction condition (`if (match.type[T].notnull(V = testedOperand)) br matchedBlock; br nullBlock`). Two shapes, both producing a MatchInstruction with CheckType + CheckNotNull: (1) `PatternMatchValueTypes` folds `if (comp.o(isinst T(testedOperand) == ldnull)) br falseBlock; br unboxBlock` where unboxBlock (1 pred) is `stloc V(unbox.any T(arg))` -- arg is ldloc testedVariable, or isinst T(ldloc testedVariable), or box Tparam(ldloc testedVariable); the boxed-generic form records a boxType on both the isinst block (MatchIsInstBlock) and the unbox block (MatchUnboxBlock) and requires they be equal. The stloc V(unbox.any) in unboxBlock is folded into the pattern (dropped via RemoveInstructionAt(0)), and an optional `stloc temp(ldloc testedVariable)` copy before the if is dropped when the unbox operand is that temp. (2) `PatternMatchRefTypes` folds `stloc V(isinst T(testedOperand)); if (comp.o(ldloc V == ldnull)) br falseBlock; br trueBlock` (T a reference type), plus the temp form `stloc condVar(comp); if (logic.not(ldloc condVar))` (a single-def StackSlot holding the null check) and the double-store form `stloc s(isinst T(x)); stloc v(ldloc s); if (logic.not(comp(ldloc s != ldnull)))` (s loaded twice, v the capture). The capture variable becomes a PatternLocal. Adapted to this port's if-as-final block model: the IfInstruction is the block's FinalInstruction (the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at Instructions[Count-2] with an explicit fall-through Branch as the last instruction), and the false arm is the next block in the container (positional, not an instruction the block owns). `MatchIfAtEndOfBlock` reads the if from block->FinalInstruction and the fall-through from NextBlockInContainer (or the if's FalseInst Branch if an earlier transform set one), and unwraps logic.not (comp eq X ldc.i4 0) by swapping the two target blocks. The `== null` / `!= null` direction swap (MatchCompEqualsNull/MatchCompNotEqualsNull) and the logic.not swap are both done by tracking the two target Block* and swapping, then `RebuildIf` reconstructs the if: iff->TrueInst = Branch(matchedBlock) (the matched path, condition true), and iff->FalseInst = nullptr when the null path is the positional next block (kept as fall-through) or Branch(nullBlock) otherwise (materialised). `pos` (the index of the stloc V(isinst) in block->Instructions) is `size - 1` (the last non-terminal before the if-final), decremented once for the temp form and once for the double-store form. `CheckAllUsesDominatedBy` is implemented with a tree walk that collects every use (LdLoc/LdLoca/StLoc/MatchInstruction with Variable == v) and checks each (except the storeToV and the loadInNullCheck) is in a block of the container dominated by the target block -- a fresh ControlFlowGraph is built per call so the dominance reflects the current (possibly already-rewritten) container, sidestepping the C#'s per-variable LoadInstructions/AddressInstructions/StoreInstructions lists this port does not maintain (the repeatedly-deferred infrastructure piece, D11/D62/D68). The testedOperand is detached from the isinst (TakeChild) before the old condition is destroyed by the replacement; the capture variable's shared_ptr is captured before the stloc that owns it is dropped (no GC, so a raw storeToV pointer would dangle after RemoveInstructionAt). The C# container.Blocks.RemoveAll(empty-Instructions) cleanup is skipped: a block with empty Instructions still carries its FinalInstruction, and removing it would dangle branches targeting it (the D58 hazard); the dead blocks stay (harmless). Deferred vs the C#: the recursive sub-pattern detection (DetectPropertySubPatterns / DetectPropertySubPattern / MatchNullCheckPattern / MatchNullableHasValueCheckPattern) needs DetectExitPoints.CompatibleExitInstruction (ExitPoints.cs, deferred) and PropertyOrFieldAccess (IMember/IField accessor metadata), so the top-level `is T` / `is T x` pattern is produced without the recursive property patterns (`expr is C { P: var x }`); the MatchInstruction node and SubPatterns slot already support them when the helper lands. The deconstruct patterns (IMethod/TupleType) stay deferred (D70). Wired into the CLI pipeline after LoopDetection and before ConditionDetection (the GetILTransforms() position), gated on the PatternMatching setting (default true). The C# keeps the box in the MatchInstruction's TestedOperand (MatchIsInstBlock extracts testedVariable from inside the box but does not reassign testedOperand), so this port does too. Whether the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc corpus contains any C# 7 `is` patterns is corpus-dependent (the feature is Roslyn-era), so the 8000-method sweep asserts the ILAst invariant holds, not a specific match count. 11 new gtest cases (value-type fold; value-type temp-store fold; boxed-generic unwrap; value-type rejects non-isinst condition; ref-type fold; ref-type != null keeps fall-through; ref-type double-store fold; ref-type rejects non-reference type; ref-type rejects use not dominated by the matched block; setting-off no-op; mscorlib sweep). 312/312 gtest cases pass (11 new). |
| D72 | LockInstruction node + LockTransform (MCS/V2 subset, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the LockInstruction ILAst node (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/LockInstruction.hpp, from the generated Instructions.cs + the hand-written LockInstruction.cs) and a subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/LockTransform.cs: the next in-order transform in the BlockILTransform post-order set after ConditionDetection (the GetILTransforms() position). The LockInstruction is the C# `lock` statement: two children OnExpression (slot 0, inlineable -- the object whose monitor is entered) and Body (slot 1 -- the try block the implicit Monitor.Exit finally protects); DirectFlags = ControlFlow | SideEffect; result Void; the base Flags() already equals the C# ComputeFlags, so no override. The C# CheckInvariant also asserts OnExpression.ResultType == StackType.O; this port's CheckInvariant is non-virtual and checks tree consistency only (the other ported nodes skip their C# per-node asserts the same way), so the object-typed assertion is not enforced here. The seed renders a LockInstruction as `lock (expr) { body }` (EmitBraced for the Body BlockContainer). This iteration ports the two *no-flag* shapes that have a straight `call Exit` finally (no `if (flag)` guard): (1) TransformLockMCS (the mono/mcs shape) -- `stloc lockObj(lockExpression); call Enter(ldloc lockObj); .try { ... } finally { call Exit(ldloc lockObj); leave }` -> `lock (lockExpression) { ... }`, lockObj single-definition and LoadCount <= 2 (the Enter arg + the Exit arg); (2) TransformLockV2 (the legacy mono shape with a temp) -- `stloc lockObj(ldloc tempVar); call Enter(ldloc tempVar); .try { ... } finally { call Exit(ldloc lockObj); leave }` -> `lock (ldloc tempVar) { ... }`, where the Enter argument is the temp (not lockObj) and lockObj is single-definition with LoadCount <= 1 (only the Exit arg). The shared `BuildLockAndRewrite` detaches the lock expression (StLoc's Value via TakeChild(0)) and the try block (TryFinally's TryBlock via TakeChild(0)) before the old nodes are destroyed, builds the LockInstruction, replaces the TryFinally slot, and erases the stloc (i-2) and call (i-1) (the higher index first so the lower stays valid). The TryFinally's FinallyBlock (child 1) is destroyed with the TryFinally shell when the slot is replaced -- faithful: the explicit Monitor.Exit finally is folded into the lock (the back end emits the implicit Exit), and nothing else references the finally blocks (they are EH-reached, no Branch targets), so no dangling pointers. Added the LockStatement setting (default true, matching DecompilerSettings, a C# 1.0 setting) to ILTransformSettings. Adapted to this port's block model: the C# carries the TryFinally as a non-terminal at block.Instructions[i] with the stloc/call at [i-2]/[i-1]; the port's BlockBuilder wraps each TryFinally in its own wrapper block (the TryInstruction alone), so the stloc/call land in a separate preceding block -- but the pre-pipeline's CFS merges the wrapper with the preceding block (CombineBlockWithNextBlock: the preceding block's final is a Branch to the wrapper, the wrapper has IncomingEdgeCount==1, the TryFinally is not MayBranch so the MayBranch guard passes), so by LockTransform time the stloc/call/TryFinally sit consecutively in one block's Instructions (the C# shape). The finally's endfinally `leave` is this port's FinalInstruction (the C# carries it as a non-terminal at Instructions[1]), so MatchExitBlockNoFlag checks Instructions[0] = call Exit and the FinalInstruction = Leave targeting the finally container (with no Value) instead of Instructions[1]. The try-entry and finally-entry single-predecessor checks change from ==1 (C#) to ==0: the C# counts the container-entry edge (BlockContainer.Connected does entryPoint.IncomingEdgeCount++), but this port's RecomputeIncomingEdgeCounts does NOT count it (D59), so an EH-reached entry (no Branch in the tree) has IncomingEdgeCount==0 here. SortBlocks(deleteUnreachableBlocks) is unsafe in this port (D58), but the rewrite does not delete blocks (it only replaces the TryFinally with a LockInstruction and drops two non-terminal stores), so no block deletion is needed. Deferred vs the C#: the three *flag-based* shapes (TransformLockV4 / TransformLockV4YieldReturn / TransformLockRoslyn), which guard the Exit call with `if (ldloc flag) { call Exit }` in the finally -- in this port's if-as-final block model that finally `if (flag) { Exit }` is the block's FinalInstruction (not a non-terminal at Instructions[0]) and the trailing endfinally `leave` is inlined into the if's FalseInst by ConditionDetection (or is the next block); matching that shape is a separate adaptation for a later iteration. The flag-based shapes are the ones the modern Roslyn and the .NET Framework 4 (legacy csc) compilers emit, so they fire on the real corpus; the no-flag MCS/V2 shapes fire only on mono-compiled assemblies, so this subset's 8000-method mscorlib sweep asserts the ILAst invariant holds (not a fold count), matching the LdLocaDupInitObj / DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks precedent. Wired into the CLI pipeline after ConditionDetection and before AssignVariableNames (the GetILTransforms() position in the BlockILTransform post-order set); the CLI decompiles all 25315 mscorlib methods end-to-end with LockTransform in the pipeline (exit 0, no crash). 8 new gtest cases (LockInstruction node invariant/flags/dump; MCS fold; V2 fold; rejects non-Monitor Enter; rejects LoadCount > 2 for MCS; LockStatement-off no-op; seed renders `lock (expr) { body }`; mscorlib sweep). 320/320 gtest cases pass (8 new). |
| D73 | LockTransform flag-based Roslyn shape (Phase 4, extends D72) | port | Extends the D72 LockTransform with the flag-based Roslyn shape -- the dominant .NET Framework 4 / Roslyn `lock` codegen that D72 deferred -- so `lock (...) { ... }` now appears in mscorlib decompilation (517 folds across the full 25315-method corpus, up from 0 with only the no-flag MCS/V2 shapes). The shape: `stloc obj(lockExpression); stloc flag(ldc.i4 0); .try { call Enter(ldloc obj, ldloca flag); body } finally { if (!flag) leave; call Exit(ldloc obj); leave }` -> `lock (lockExpression) { body }`, where flag is Boolean and initialised to 0, obj is single-definition and loaded at most twice (the Enter arg + the Exit arg). The C# `MatchExitBlock` carries the flag finally's `if (ldloc flag) Block { call Exit }` and the endfinally `leave` as two non-terminals of ONE block (Instructions[0]/[1]); this port's if-as-final block model and ConditionDetection leave the brfalse-skip as a separate first block instead, so the flag finally is TWO blocks -- `if (comp(eq,flag,0)) leave` (empty Instructions, if-final, TrueInst = leave targeting the finally container, FalseInst = nullptr, fall-through) then `call Exit(ldloc obj); leave` -- and a new `MatchExitBlockFlag` matches that shape (the C# single-block `if(flag){Exit}` shape does not arise here). The match requires comp(Equality, ldloc flag, ldc.i4 0) for the condition (the reader's brfalse form); ConditionDetection's TryInlineIfFallThrough gate blocks inlining the leave into the if's FalseInst (the true arm's trailing Branch and the leave are not a compatible common exit), so the leave stays the next block, which is why the two-block shape is what LockTransform sees. The stloc obj / stloc flag sit either in the TryFinally's own block (the C# same-block indexing, Instructions[i-2]/[i-1]/[i] -- never observed in mscorlib because CFS does not merge here) or, in the dominant mscorlib case, in a PRECEDING block in the same container (its last two instructions). The reason CFS does not merge for the flag shape (unlike the no-flag MCS/V2 shape it does merge, per D72): the BlockBuilder's fall-through branch into the TryFinally (`br IL_xxxx` to the .try start) resolves its TargetBlock to the try ENTRY (a block inside the try container that shares the .try's start offset) instead of the TryFinally wrapper block (which also starts at that offset), so the wrapper has IncomingEdgeCount==0 and CombineBlockWithNextBlock's `target->IncomingEdgeCount != 1` guard (the no-flag wrapper has ==1, so it merges) blocks the merge -- the stlocs stay in a separate preceding block. The preceding-block fold: the LockInstruction goes in the preceding block (replacing the two stlocs), the TryFinally's block final (the after-lock continuation) is moved into the preceding block as its new final, and the now-empty TryFinally block is dropped (moved to a graveyard vector so the container iteration stays valid -- the D58 hazard is a block holding nested containers referenced by external branches, but this dropped block is fully empty after the TryFinally shell is destroyed, so it is safe). The preceding block's old `br` into the try is discarded -- it would dangle into the lock body once the TryFinally becomes a LockInstruction (the try entry is now nested inside the LockInstruction's body), and the LockInstruction subsumes the try entry, so the branch is no longer needed. The same-block fold reuses D72's BuildLockAndRewrite shape (replace the TryFinally at Instructions[i] with the LockInstruction, erase the stloc flag at i-1 and stloc obj at i-2). `ValidateRoslynTryFinally` checks the try entry's Instructions[0] is `call Enter(ldloc obj, ldloca flag)` (MatchEnter2, the 2-arg form) and the finally is MatchExitBlockFlag, with the Enter and Exit operating on the same object. `KnownTypeCodeOf` (mirroring NullableLiftingTransform, D68) unwraps a ParameterizedType to read the flag's KnownTypeCode, so the Boolean check is faithful. The Run loop restarts a container's block scan whenever a fold drops a block (the block indices shift). Deferred vs the C#: the V4 / V4YieldReturn flag shapes (which pass an inline `stloc obj(lockExpression)` as the Enter call's first argument rather than a separate `stloc obj` -- the C# `MatchCall(call, "Enter", flag, out StLoc obj)` overload checks Arguments[0] is a StLoc) and the single-block `if(flag){Exit}` finally shape; neither appears in the .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus (0 inline-stloc Enter args and 0 single-block flag finallys across ~600 Roslyn lock methods), so the hand-built tests cover the Roslyn shape and the mscorlib sweep asserts the fold count. The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end with the flag-based transform in the pipeline (exit 0, no crash, 517 `lock (...)` statements). 7 new gtest cases (Roslyn same-block fold; Roslyn preceding-block fold; rejects non-Boolean flag; rejects non-zero flag init; rejects non-Monitor Enter; rejects LoadCount > 2; rejects non-two-block finally); the existing mscorlib sweep now processes all methods and asserts folds > 100 (was invariant-only). 327/327 gtest cases pass (7 new). |
| D74 | UsingInstruction ILAst node + UsingStatement setting (Phase 4 using-family foundation, next-in-order) | port | Ports the UsingInstruction ILAst node (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/UsingInstruction.hpp, from the generated Instructions.cs + the hand-written UsingInstruction.cs) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order transform (UsingTransform), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting / D70 MatchInstruction precedent. The node is the C# `using` statement: two children ResourceExpression (slot 0, inlineable -- the IDisposable resource being acquired) and Body (slot 1 -- the try block the implicit finally that calls Dispose protects); Variable is the local the resource is stored into (an IStoreInstruction in the C#; this port counts it as a store in ComputeVariableUsage via a new `case OpCode::UsingInstruction`, mirroring MatchInstruction/StLoc/TryCatchHandler). IsAsync marks an `await using` (IAsyncDisposable; the async-using transform, which needs an Await node, is deferred); IsRefStruct marks a `using` of a ref struct (the finally's Dispose has no null check). DirectFlags = MayWriteLocals | ControlFlow | SideEffect (faithful); result Void; the base `ILInstruction::Flags()` (DirectFlags | union of children) already equals the C# `ComputeFlags`, so no Flags override is needed. The C# CheckInvariant also asserts the variable is non-null and the slots' result types; this port's CheckInvariant is non-virtual and checks tree consistency only (the other ported nodes skip their C# per-node asserts the same way). The ILAst dump renders `using (var = resource) { body }`, flavoured `using.async` / `using.ref` (faithful to UsingInstruction.cs's WriteToCore). The ILAstToCSharp seed renders a UsingInstruction as `using (resource) { body }` (the expression form, matching the LockInstruction `lock (expr)` precedent; the seed elides the Variable -- the real back end declares the using-local via the using, not DeclareVariables). Added the UsingStatement setting (default true, matching DecompilerSettings, a C# 1.0 setting) to ILTransformSettings; it gates the UsingTransform that builds UsingInstructions (the node itself is unconditional). This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform constructs UsingInstructions yet, so `--csharp` output is unchanged (no real `using` statements appear until UsingTransform lands; the seed's UsingInstruction case is exercised by the unit tests, not the corpus). The UsingTransform itself stays the next concrete target: it needs the block-model adaptation of the `stloc obj(resource); .try { } finally { if (obj != null) callvirt Dispose(obj) }` shape (the C# TransformUsing) -- specifically the finally's `if (comp(ne, ldloc obj, ldnull)) Block { callvirt Dispose(obj) }; leave(finally)` block tail, whose exact shape in this port's if-as-final block model after ConditionDetection (whether the `if` is a non-terminal at Instructions[0] with the `leave` as the FinalInstruction, or the leave is inlined into the if's FalseInst, or the if is the FinalInstruction with the leave the next block) must be probed on the corpus before MatchDisposeBlock is implemented (the D73 precedent of probing the real finally shape rather than reasoning from the C# structure, since the block model diverges). The same-block vs preceding-block stloc placement (CFS may or may not merge the TryFinally EH wrapper with the preceding block, per D72/D73) and the permissive CheckResourceType approximation (this port's minimal type system has no GetAllBaseTypes, so the IDisposable-implements check is reduced to a KnownTypeCode::IDisposable direct check -- MatchDisposeBlock's structural Dispose-method-name match is the real proof) are the further deferred pieces. The TransformUsingVB (the VB shape where the stloc is inside the try block's entry), TransformAsyncUsing (needs Await + IAsyncDisposable + the AsyncUsingAndForEachStatement setting), the NullableOfT dispose (MatchHasValueCall, ported in D68 but not yet consulted), the ref-struct dispose, the isinst-temp inlining, and the MatchInstruction-based null check are all deferred. 8 new gtest cases (node invariant/flags/dump; IsAsync/IsRefStruct dump flavours; children in typed slots; store-counting in ComputeVariableUsage; seed rendering of `using (resource)` and `using (null)`; UsingStatement setting default; and an 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructing UsingInstructions over real decoded LdLoc operands confirming the invariant/dump hold across the corpus). 335/335 gtest cases pass (8 new). |
| D75 | UsingTransform (Phase 4, next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/UsingTransform.cs (the next in-order transform after the D74 UsingInstruction node foundation): detects the C# `using` statement's IDisposable try/finally pattern -- `stloc obj(resource); .try { body } finally { if (obj != null) callvirt Dispose(obj) }` -- and folds it into a `UsingInstruction` (`using (resource) { body }`), marking the resource `VariableKind::UsingLocal`. Runs after ConditionDetection and LockTransform in the BlockILTransform post-order set (the `GetILTransforms()` position). Following the D73 precedent (the if-as-final block model diverges from the C# structure, so the real finally shape is discovered empirically), a temporary instrumented gtest probe scanned the full mscorlib corpus through the pre-pipeline (through ConditionDetection + LockTransform) and classified the finally-block shapes of every TryFinally whose finally contains a `::Dispose` call (467 across 25315 methods, 111 distinct shapes). Three shapes dominate and are matched: (1) Shape A (reference type, two-block, ~270+): the C# single-block `if (obj != null) Block { Dispose }; leave` becomes TWO blocks here -- `fin[0]` = `if (comp(eq, ldloc obj, ldnull)) leave(fin)` (empty Instructions, if-final, TrueInst = the skip-leave, FalseInst = nullptr, fall-through), `fin[1]` = `call System.IDisposable::Dispose(ldloc obj); leave`. The condition is the reader's brfalse form (`obj == null` -> skip), inverted vs the C# `obj != null` but equivalent; ConditionDetection's TryInlineIfFallThrough gate blocks inlining the leave into the if's FalseInst, so the leave stays the next block (exactly the D73 lock flag-finally divergence). (2) Shape B (struct / ref-struct, one-block, ~120+): no null check (a struct cannot be null), so the finally is ONE block -- `call System.IDisposable::Dispose(ldloca obj); leave` (the Dispose arg is the address, matching the C# MatchLdLocRef). (3) Shape C (reference type, two-block with isinst-temp, ~40+): the C# optional `stloc temp(isinst IDisposable(ldloc obj))` at the finally entry stays as `fin[0]->Instructions[0]` with the if as `fin[0]`'s FinalInstruction testing the temp -- `stloc temp(isinst IDisposable(ldloc obj)); if (comp(eq, ldloc temp, ldnull)) leave(fin)` then `fin[1]` = `call Dispose(ldloc temp); leave`; the temp must be IsSingleDefinition and loaded exactly twice (the null check + the Dispose), and the UsingInstruction wraps the ORIGINAL resource (the isinst-temp is the compiler's null-check implementation and is discarded with the finally). The resource `stloc obj(resource)` sits in the PRECEDING block (the dominant mscorlib case -- CFS does not merge the EH wrapper for this shape, the same BlockBuilder quirk as the flag-based lock, D73: the fall-through branch into the TryFinally resolves its TargetBlock to the try ENTRY inside the try container, not the wrapper, so the wrapper has IncomingEdgeCount==0 and CFS leaves the stloc in a separate preceding block); the same-block case (stloc + TryFinally consecutive, the C# literal TransformUsing shape) is also handled but does not arise from the real pipeline. `FindResourceStore` accepts a preceding-block branch whose TargetBlock is the wrapper OR a block inside the TryFinally (`IsDescendantOf(tf)`), since the quirk sends it to the try entry. The preceding-block fold puts the UsingInstruction in the preceding block (replacing the stloc), absorbs the TryFinally's block final (the after-using continuation) into it, discards the preceding block's `br` into the try (which would dangle into the using body once the TryFinally becomes a UsingInstruction -- the UsingInstruction subsumes the try entry), and drops the now-empty TryFinally block to a graveyard vector so the container iteration stays valid. The same-block fold replaces the stloc at Instructions[tfIdx-1] with the UsingInstruction and erases the TryFinally at Instructions[tfIdx]. The resource validation mirrors the C# TransformUsing: VariableKind == Local, StoreCount <= 1 (single store), CheckResourceType (permissive -- this port's minimal type system has no GetAllBaseTypes, so it returns true and the `System.IDisposable::Dispose` method name is the real IDisposable proof, per the D74 plan), all loads (LdLoc) and addresses (LdLoca) of the resource inside the TryFinally (a tree walk, since this port has no per-variable LoadInstructions/AddressInstructions lists -- the deferred infrastructure piece; the C# ValidateAddressUse TryBlock/in-param sub-check is deferred as a rendering nicety), and `MatchDisposeBlock` (the finally disposes the resource). `CheckResourceType` is permissive but `MatchDisposeBlock`'s structural match (the exact `System.IDisposable::Dispose` call name, 1 argument, the two-block null-check or one-block ldloca shape, the single-def temp) is the real gate, so a non-using try/finally that disposes a different variable does not fold. `using (null)` (the resource is ldnull) is handled: CheckResourceType is skipped and the Dispose arg may be ldloc obj or ldnull (the usingNull special case). KEY FIX vs the lock transform's structure: `Run` RE-GATHERS containers after each container that produced a fold. A fold destroys the TryFinally's nested FinallyBlock container (and moves the TryBlock container into the UsingInstruction), so a pre-gathered container list (the lock transform's structure, D72/D73) holds a DANGLING pointer to the destroyed FinallyBlock; the first implementation crashed (SEH 0xc0000005) on a 8000-method mscorlib sweep at a System.Runtime.Remoting method (~0x0600357F) -- the lock transform has the same latent use-after-free but its freed container memory is not reused fast enough to crash, while the UsingTransform's extra CheckAllLoadsInside/CheckAllAddressesInside tree walks churn memory and reuse it. The re-gather approach processes one container to fixpoint per outer pass (only touching that container, never the nested ones), then re-gathers before processing any other container, so the destroyed nested containers are never accessed. Each pass folds at least one using (or returns when none remain), so it terminates. Deferred vs the C#: TransformUsingVB (the stloc inside the try entry, a VB shape), TransformAsyncUsing (needs an Await node + IAsyncDisposable + the AsyncUsingAndForEachStatement setting), the NullableOfT dispose (MatchHasValueCall, ported in D68 but not yet consulted), the ref-struct own-`Dispose` shape (this port matches `System.IDisposable::Dispose` only, not `StructType::Dispose`), the boxed-value and NullableRewrap null-check shapes, the MatchInstruction-based null check, the isinst-temp inlining inside the try, and the non-generic IEnumerator / foreach-pattern CheckResourceType special case. IsRefStruct is left false (this port does not model IsByRefLike or the IntroduceRefModifiersOnStructs setting). Wired into the CLI pipeline after LockTransform and before AssignVariableNames (the GetILTransforms() position); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash) and emits 313 `using (...)` statements (up from 0), the faithful UsingTransform intermediate -- a future ForechTransform (later in the C# pipeline) turns `using (enumerator) { while (MoveNext) ... }` into `foreach`, but UsingTransform runs first per the C# order. 16 new gtest cases (Shape A preceding-block fold; Shape A quirk-branch fold -- the BlockBuilder-quirk target; Shape A same-block fold; Shape B struct fold; Shape C isinst-temp fold; `using (null)` fold; `using (null)` with ldnull Dispose-arg fold; seed renders `using (resource) { body }`; rejects non-IDisposable Dispose name; rejects multi-arg Dispose; rejects resource not a Local; rejects multi-store resource; rejects resource loaded outside the TryFinally; rejects non-branch preceding block; UsingStatement-off no-op; mscorlib sweep asserting folds > 100 and the invariant holds). 351/351 gtest cases pass (16 new). |
| D76 | DelegateConstruction.MatchDelegateConstruction helper + Call::IsNewObj + resolved-type TypeKind derivation (Phase 4 cached-delegate-family foundation, next-in-order) | port | Ports the `MatchDelegateConstruction` helper (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/DelegateConstruction.{hpp,cpp}, from ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/DelegateConstruction.cs) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order transform (CachedDelegateInitialization), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction precedent.
