The helper recognises a `newobj DelegateType(target, ldftn method)` delegate construction (the C# `case NewObj call:`): a Call with the new `IsNewObj` flag, exactly 2 arguments, the second an ldftn or ldvirtftn, and a declaring type whose `Kind` is `Delegate` or `Unknown` (the C# also accepts Unknown for an unresolvable type); a null declaring type returns false (the C# null DeclaringTypeDefinition). It captures the target (Arguments[0]), the delegate type (DeclaringType), and the ldftn/ldvirtftn method display name (this port's stand-in for the C# IMethod -- the C# `targetMethod` out-param is an IMethod this port does not carry; CachedDelegateInitialization discards it via `out _`). Three prerequisites landed: (1) `Call` gained an `IsNewObj` flag (set by the IL reader for `newobj`, distinguishing it from `call`/`callvirt` -- the C# models newobj as a separate `NewObj` node; this port reuses `Call` with the flag, matching the `IsInstanceCall` precedent), since `MatchDelegateConstruction`'s `case NewObj` must distinguish a constructor from a regular call. (2) The resolved declaring type's `TypeKind` is now derived: `ResolveMethodDeclaringType` (MethodDef parent -> the enclosing TypeDef; MemberRef TypeDef parent) builds a `SimpleType` carrying the derived `TypeKind` via `DeriveTypeKind` over the TypeDef row's flags + base, so a delegate constructor's declaring type resolves to `Kind == Delegate` instead of the `Class` fallback `MakeTypeRef` produced; a non-known cross-assembly `TypeRef` (MemberRef TypeRef parent) falls back to `Unknown` (the C# leaves an unresolvable TypeRef as UnknownType, and `MatchDelegateConstruction` accepts `Kind == Unknown`). (3) KEY FINDING: the signature decoder's `TypeDefOrRef` element decoder (`DecodeTypeDefOrRefEncoded`, used for every type element in a signature, including a TypeSpec instantiation's generic definition) ALSO had to derive the kind -- without it, a *generic* delegate constructor like `newobj System.Func<int>(.., ldftn ..)` (whose declaring type comes via a TypeSpec -> `ParameterizedType(SimpleType("System.Func`1", Class), int)`) resolved to `Kind == Class` and did NOT match. An 8000-method mscorlib sweep confirmed this: of 59 `newobj Delegate(.., ldftn ..)` shape sites, 29 (non-generic, MethodDef parent) matched before the signature-decoder change and 30 (generic, TypeSpec) did not (all 30 were `ParameterizedType` with `Kind == Class`); after routing `DecodeTypeDefOrRefEncoded`'s TypeDef/TypeRef cases through the same kind-deriving helpers, all 59 match (29 non-generic + 30 generic). The kind-deriving helpers (`MakeTypeRefFromTypeDef` / `MakeTypeRefFromTypeRef`) are public in `SignatureDecoder` and shared by `ResolveMethodDeclaringType` and the signature decoder, so the kind is derived consistently for both the metadata-row parent path and the signature-element path. Known framework types keep their `KnownType` (the known fast-path runs before the kind derivation), so the `KnownTypeCode` consumers (`NullableLifting`) are unaffected -- a `Nullable<T>` declaring type still resolves to `KnownType(NullableOfT)`. The kind derivation's base resolver uses the simple `MakeTypeRef` form for the base (DeriveTypeKind only inspects `KnownType` bases -- Enum/ValueType/MulticastDelegate -- via `dynamic_cast<KnownType>`, so the base's own Kind is irrelevant; a non-known base falls through to Class). Added the `AnonymousMethods` setting (default true, matching DecompilerSettings) that gates `CachedDelegateInitialization`; the helper itself is unconditional (like the NullableLifting helpers). This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform consumes `MatchDelegateConstruction` yet, so `--csharp` output is unchanged; the foundation is exercised by the unit tests + the sweep. Deferred vs the C#: the full `CachedDelegateInitialization` transform (the next in-order transform, now unblocked) -- it needs `IField.IsCompilerGeneratedOrIsInCompilerGeneratedClass` (field metadata -- the cached-delegate field's compiler-generated flag / its enclosing type's), per-variable `StoreInstructions` lists (the `WithLocal` shape's `otherStore.OfType<StLoc>().SingleOrDefault` check), and a block-model adaptation of its `block.Instructions[i] is IfInstruction` + `inst.Parent.Children.ElementAtOrDefault(inst.ChildIndex + 1)` shape to this port's if-as-`FinalInstruction` model (the C# reads the if as a non-terminal and the next instruction as a sibling; this port's if is the block's final, so the "next instruction" is the next block in the container or the if's Block arm), plus a corpus probe of the real cached-delegate if/Block-TrueInst shape (the D73/D75 precedent of probing the real shape rather than reasoning from the C# structure); the `LdVirtDelegate` case (a separate ILAst node) is deferred (this port models ldvirtftn-delegate via the same NewObj shape); the `allowTransformed` ILFunction-arg case (arising after the DelegateConstruction transform rewrites a NewObj into an ILFunction closure) is accepted but a no-op (the transform is not ported, so no ILFunction arg arises); `CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization` (the transform right after CachedDelegateInitialization) shares the same block-model divergence and the `IField.IsCompilerGeneratedOrIsInCompilerGeneratedClass` dependency. 11 new gtest cases (MatchNewObjDelegateWithLdFtn; MatchNewObjDelegateWithLdVirtFtn; MatchNewObjDelegateAcceptsUnknownDeclaringType; RejectsNonNewObjCall; RejectsWrongArgCount (1 and 3); RejectsNonLdFtnSecondArg; RejectsNullDeclaringType; RejectsNonDelegateDeclaringType (Class); RejectsNonCall; AnonymousMethodsSettingDefaultsTrue; MscorlibDelegateConstructionSweep asserting the reader marks newobj calls, the TypeSpec generic-delegate path fires, the kind derivation resolves delegate declaring types to Delegate, the helper matches real sites, and the ILAst invariant holds). 362/362 gtest cases pass (11 new). The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, 517 `lock` / 313 `using` / 590 `switch` statements unchanged -- no regression from the type-kind derivation). |
| D77 | CachedDelegateInitialization (WithLocal subset, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `CachedDelegateInitializationWithLocal` shape of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/CachedDelegateInitialization.cs (the next in-order transform after the D76 DelegateConstruction.MatchDelegateConstruction foundation, now unblocked and wired). It collapses a local-cached delegate lazy init -- `stloc v(ldnull); ...; if (comp(ldloc v == ldnull)) { Block { stloc v(DelegateConstruction) } }; <use ldloc v>` -- into the unconditional init `stloc v(DelegateConstruction); <use ldloc v>`, where v is a local with exactly two stores (the null init and the delegate fill), exactly two loads (the null check and the use), and no addresses. The transform is an IBlockTransform in the C# BlockILTransform post-order set (after ConditionDetection / LockTransform / UsingTransform); this port runs it as an IILTransform iterating every container's blocks. Adapted to this port's if-as-final block model: the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at `block.Instructions[i]` with the single usage as the next sibling instruction (`inst.Parent.Children.ElementAtOrDefault(inst.ChildIndex + 1)`); this port makes the IfInstruction the block's `FinalInstruction` with an implicit fall-through, so `inst.ChildIndex + 1` is out of range (the final is the last child) and would always return null -- the "next instruction" is instead the NEXT BLOCK in the container (`NextBlockInContainer`), the if's fall-through target (the adaptation is essential: the C# sibling-instruction lookup never fires for a block final). The per-variable `StoreInstructions` list this port does not maintain (the repeatedly deferred infrastructure piece, D11/D62/D68) is replaced by a tree walk that gathers the StLoc stores to v (mirroring the PatternMatchingTransform.CheckAllUsesDominatedBy / CollectUses precedent) -- the "other store" (`v.StoreInstructions.OfType<StLoc>().SingleOrDefault(store => store != storeInst)`) is the single StLoc-to-v that is not the cache-fill store, and it must store `ldnull` with a Block parent. The rewrite: detach the cache-fill StLoc from the if's TrueInst Block (`TakeChild`), drop the null-init StLoc from its block (`RemoveInstructionAt`), append the cache-fill StLoc to the host block's instructions, and replace the if-final with a `Branch` to the next block (the explicit fall-through the if's null `FalseInst` represented -- this port's block model requires a final; the edge count is unchanged: the if's fall-through and the new Branch each contribute one edge to the next block). The `Branch(Block*)` constructor carries the target's `StartILOffset` so the seed labels the goto correctly. Gated on the `AnonymousMethods` setting (default true, from D76). A full 25315-method mscorlib corpus probe (instrumented gtest, run through the pre-pipeline to the GetILTransforms() point) found the WithLocal shape fires 0 times (the .NET Framework 4 legacy csc uses the field-cached delegate shape, not the local one) and the WithField shape (static-field cache, `ldsfld`/`stsfld`) fires 2 times (System.Security.SecurityContext::cleanupCode, System.Threading.CompressedStack::cleanupCode), so this subset's 8000-method sweep asserts the ILAst invariant holds (not a fold count), matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks / LdLocaDupInitObj / SwitchOnNullable precedent (a transform that fires only on Roslyn-compiled assemblies is still ported for faithfulness -- the local-cached shape fires on modern .NET). Deferred vs the C#: the six field-cached shapes (CachedDelegateInitializationWithField, CachedDelegateInitializationRoslynInStaticWithLocal, CachedDelegateInitializationRoslynWithLocal, and the three VB shapes) need `IField.IsCompilerGeneratedOrIsInCompilerGeneratedClass` metadata (the cached-delegate field's [CompilerGenerated] attribute, or its enclosing type's -- this port models a field as just a name string on LdsFlda/LdFlda, with no token or attributes, so the check cannot be faithful); the temp-collapse (folding the cache temp v into the use, which the C# achieves because the cache-fill store and the use are consecutive instructions in the same block after the fold, then a later ILInlining pass inlines v) needs the cache-fill and the use in the same block, which in this port's block model requires merging the host block with the next block (a block-model-compensation step the C# does not need), so the WithLocal fold here leaves `stloc v(Delegate); goto nextBlock; <use v>` (the temp survives); the `ILInlining.InlineOneIfPossible` follow-up the C# calls is a no-op here until that merge lands (the use is in the next block, not findable by the same-block FindLoadInNext). Wired into the CLI pipeline after UsingTransform and before AssignVariableNames (the GetILTransforms() position); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, 517 `lock` / 313 `using` statements unchanged -- no regression, the WithLocal fold fires 0 on the corpus). 11 new gtest cases (WithLocalFolds; RejectsNonDelegateValue; RejectsExtraStore; RejectsExtraLoad; RejectsUsageNotInNextBlock; RejectsOtherStoreNotLdNull; RejectsFalseInstNotNull; RejectsConditionNotEquality; RejectsTrueInstNotBlock; AnonymousMethodsOffIsNoOp; MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant). 373/373 gtest cases pass (11 new; was 362). |
| D78 | IField metadata foundation (IsCompilerGeneratedField + FieldToken on LdsFlda/LdFlda + IsFieldCompilerGeneratedOrInCompilerGeneratedClass + ArrayInitializers setting) (Phase 4 cached-field-family foundation, next-in-order) | port | Adds the IField metadata infrastructure the next in-order transform (`CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization`) and the deferred field-cached delegate shapes (D77: CachedDelegateInitializationWithField / RoslynInStaticWithLocal / RoslynWithLocal / the three VB shapes) need, ahead of those transforms, following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D76 DelegateConstruction precedent. The blocker (per D77) was that this port modelled a field as just a name string on `LdsFlda`/`LdFlda`, with no token or attributes, so the C# `cacheField.IsCompilerGeneratedOrIsInCompilerGeneratedClass()` check (the gate that recognises a compiler-synthesized cache field -- a `<PrivateImplementationDetails>` static or a display-class field) could not be faithful. Three pieces landed: (1) `LdFlda` and `LdsFlda` (MemoryInstructions.hpp) gained an `IsCompilerGeneratedField` bool and a `FieldToken` (the raw metadata token, table 0x04 FieldDef or 0x0A field MemberRef, the reader resolved the name from), both default false/0. (2) The IL reader (ILReader.cpp) populates both for every field access (ldfld/ldflda/stfld/ldsfld/ldsflda/stsfld) from the token it already reads, via a new `MetadataFile::IsFieldCompilerGeneratedOrInCompilerGeneratedClass(fieldToken)` (the resolution is once per field-access site at read time; this port's transforms have no MetadataFile handle, so the check is pre-resolved and stored on the node, matching the field-name / Call::DeclaringType precedent). (3) `MetadataFile::IsFieldCompilerGeneratedOrInCompilerGeneratedClass` mirrors the C# `NRExtensions.IsCompilerGeneratedOrIsInCompilerGeneratedClass`: the field's own `[CompilerGenerated]` (System.Runtime.CompilerServices.CompilerGeneratedAttribute) custom attribute, or (recursively up the nesting chain) its declaring type's. For a FieldDef (0x04): check the field's CustomAttribute range, then walk `f.Parent()` (the declaring TypeDef) up via `TypeDef::EnclosingType()` (winmd's NestedClass table lookup), checking each type's CustomAttribute range (a 64-level depth guard stops a malformed cyclic NestedClass chain). For a field MemberRef (0x0A): only an in-module TypeDef parent is checked (a cross-assembly TypeRef needs the full type system, matching the C# which needs it too); TypeRef/TypeSpec parents return false. The attribute match is a file-local `RangeHasCompilerGenerated` template helper that iterates the winmd `CustomAttribute` equal_range pair (the row type IS the iterator -- row_base is a random-access iterator -- so the pair is std::pair<Row,Row>, not std::pair<iterator,iterator>) and compares `TypeNamespaceAndName()` to "System.Runtime.CompilerServices"/"CompilerGeneratedAttribute" (the try/catch mirrors the existing GetCustomAttributes `collect` lambda, since `TypeNamespaceAndName()` throws for an unexpected MemberRefParent). The `ArrayInitializers` setting (default true, matching DecompilerSettings) is added to ILTransformSettings; it gates `CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization` (the next in-order transform). This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (no pipeline transform consults the flag yet, so `--csharp` output is unchanged), mirroring the D63/D66/D68/D70/D74/D76 foundations. 6 new gtest cases (NodeCarriesFlagAndToken; ArrayInitializersSettingDefault; InvalidTokenIsGraceful; NonCompilerGeneratedField on System.String's first field; MscorlibHasCompilerGeneratedFields scanning every TypeDef's fields via the helper and asserting the corpus carries CG fields -- the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib has cached-delegate / display-class CG fields; ReaderPopulatesFlag decoding 8000 methods, finding LdsFlda/LdFlda, and cross-checking each node's `IsCompilerGeneratedField == file.IsFieldCompilerGeneratedOrInCompilerGeneratedClass(FieldToken)` plus the invariant holds). 379/379 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 373). The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- the per-field-access helper call adds negligible cost). The transform itself stays the next concrete target: `CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization` (the next in-order transform after CachedDelegateInitialization) now has its IField dependency met; the remaining work is the block-model adaptation (the C# reads the cache `if (V == null) { body }` with the body nested as a Block in the if's TrueInst, but this port's if-as-final model leaves the body as a separate container block reachable by the if's Branch TrueInst, and ConditionDetection's TryInlineIfFallThrough inlines the single-pred fall-through into the if's FALSE arm, so the body ends up as the if's FalseInst Block, not the TrueInst -- the adaptation must match this port's inverted shape, which has no real corpus occurrence to probe since ReadOnlySpan is not in the .NET Framework 4 mscorlib, so a hand-built probe through the pre-pipeline is needed, the D73/D75 precedent), and the six field-cached delegate shapes (now unblocked on the IField side) still need the same block-model adaptation plus the per-variable store-list tree walk and a corpus probe of the real field-cached if/Block shape. |
| D79 | CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization.cs (the next in-order transform after CachedDelegateInitialization, the D78 IField foundation having unblocked it). It collapses the compiler-synthesized lazy cache Roslyn emits for a ReadOnlySpan<T> created from an array literal on target frameworks without RuntimeHelpers.CreateSpan (e.g. .NET Framework or netstandard2.0 + System.Memory) -- `stloc V(ldobj T[](ldsflda <PrivateImplementationDetails>.cache)); if (comp(ldloc V == ldnull)) { stloc V(arrayInitializer); stobj T[](ldsflda cache, ldloc V) }; <single usage of V>` -- into the unconditional init `stloc V(arrayInitializer); <usage>`, so a later array-initializer transform recovers the array literal and the reference to the escaped <PrivateImplementationDetails> cache field (whose name is not expressible in C# and is never declared, so the un-transformed output does not recompile -- CS0400) disappears. It mirrors CachedDelegateInitialization and runs right after it (the GetILTransforms() BlockILTransform post-order position). Adapted to this port's if-as-final block model: the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at block.Instructions[i] with the cache-load store at [i-1] and the body as a Block in the if's TrueInst; this port makes the IfInstruction the block's FinalInstruction, so the cache-load store is the block's last non-terminal instruction (Instructions.back()) and the if is the final. KEY FINDING (correcting D78's prediction): a hand-built probe through the pre-pipeline (CFS + LoopDetection + ConditionDetection) confirmed this port's post-ConditionDetection shape IS the C# shape, NOT the inverted shape D78 feared. The reader emits `if (comp(V != null)) br usage` for `brtrue usage` (skip the body when the cache is populated -- the cache-hit fast path), with the body as the separate fall-through block; ConditionDetection's TryInlineIfFallThrough inlines that body block (single predecessor) into the if's FalseInst, then TryInvertIfExit (the usage block is the next block after the inlined body is removed) inverts -- negating the condition to `comp(V == null)` (Equality) and moving the body into the TrueInst as a Block (FalseInst null, fall-through to the usage) -- exactly the C# shape. The one port-specific difference: the body Block carries a trailing Branch to the usage block (the reader makes fall-through explicit), which the C# body does not; the fold preserves that trailing control flow as the host block's new final, dropping the goto when the body falls through to the next block (the common cache-pattern case, verified by the probe) so no spurious `goto IL_XXXX` survives (matching the C#, which just removes the if), and otherwise keeping the body's exit so the control flow is preserved. A body with no trailing control flow (the C# shape) just drops the if. The cache-field gate uses the D78 `LdsFlda::IsCompilerGeneratedField` flag the IL reader pre-resolves from the field token's [CompilerGenerated] custom attribute (or its declaring type's); two LdsFlda nodes are the same cache field by FieldToken (the reader populates it) or, failing that, FieldName. The transform is an IILTransform iterating every container's blocks (mirroring CachedDelegateInitialization, not the C# IBlockTransform), gated on the ArrayInitializers setting (default true, from D78). The fold nulls the block's final (destroying the if and the body Block, an if-arm that holds no nested BlockContainer), so no container is destroyed and a single gathered container list stays valid (the CachedDelegateInitialization structure, not the UsingTransform re-gather pattern); ComputeVariableUsage + RecomputeIncomingEdgeCounts run before and after so the StoreCount==2/LoadCount==3/AddressCount==0 guards and the post-fold counts are fresh. ReadOnlySpan<T> is absent from the .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus, so this fires 0 times on it (it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET with System.Memory); ported for faithfulness -- the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the positive test builds the post-reader shape and runs the full pre-pipeline through ConditionDetection so the transform is exercised on the real post-ConditionDetection shape) and the 8000-method sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds (not a fold count), matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks / LdLocaDupInitObj / SwitchOnNullable precedent. Wired into the CLI pipeline after CachedDelegateInitialization and before AssignVariableNames (the GetILTransforms() position); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). 10 new gtest cases (FoldsCacheToInitializer; RejectsNonCompilerGeneratedCacheField; RejectsExtraStore; RejectsExtraLoad; RejectsElseArm; RejectsInequalityCondition -- a direct construction of the C# shape with Inequality to test the Equality guard in isolation, since the real pipeline always inverts to Equality; RejectsInitStoreDifferentVariable; RejectsWritebackToDifferentField; ArrayInitializersOffIsNoOp; MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant). 389/389 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 379). |
| D80 | StatementTransform orchestration + ILInlining per-statement (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/StatementTransform.cs (the next in-order block after CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization, the final member of the GetILTransforms() BlockILTransform post-order set). The C# StatementTransform is an IBlockTransform that runs a list of IStatementTransform children interleaved per-statement with rerun mechanics: it walks the block's instructions last-to-first, and at each position runs every child; a child may call RequestRerun(pos) to jump the driver back to pos and re-run all children (the C# driver asserts rerunPosition >= the current pos, so the jump is forward to an already-processed statement), or RequestRerun() to re-run at the current position -- the interleaving lets a transform that opens up a new inlining opportunity (e.g. TransformAssignment) trigger the ILInlining child to fold it without a separate full-block pass. This port models the BlockILTransform post-order transforms as IILTransform (whole-function, iterating blocks internally), so StatementTransform is an IILTransform that walks every Block and runs the per-statement driver on each. Three pieces landed: (1) the IStatementTransform interface (Run(Block&, pos, StatementTransformContext&)), the StatementTransformContext (wraps the underlying ILTransformContext + the current Block + the rerunPosition/rerunCurrentPosition state + RequestRerun/RequestRerunCurrentPosition), and the StatementTransform IILTransform driver (RunBlock walks pos from Instructions.size()-1 down to 0, running each child and handling the rerun-current/rerun-position jumps); (2) ILInlining now also implements IStatementTransform (a per-statement Run overload that loops InlineOneIfPossible at the given position until no change, mirroring the C# ILInlining.Run(Block, pos, ctx)); (3) the CLI wires StatementTransform{ILInlining} after CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization and before AssignVariableNames. Adapted to the port's if-as-final block model: the final lives separately (FinalInstruction, not in Instructions), so the per-statement positions range over the non-terminal Instructions only (the C# ranges over Instructions including the final, but the final is never a StLoc, so the C#'s first iteration at the final is a no-op -- starting at the last non-terminal is equivalent); the per-statement ILInlining's while (InlineOneIfPossible(block, pos)) loop re-checks pos < size after a removal shrank the block (the C# avoids this because the final lives in Instructions, so the last non-terminal is at Count-2 and the shifted-in instruction stays in range -- a bounds guard is essential in this port and was added to InlineOneIfPossible). The driver runs on every block (including if-final blocks): the if-as-final model makes the if the block's FinalInstruction, so a stloc at the last non-terminal position inlines into the if's condition (the "next" for the last non-terminal is the final), matching the C# where the stloc before the if non-terminal inlines into the if. The remaining 15 per-statement children (ExpressionTransforms, DynamicIsEventAssignmentTransform, TransformAssignment, NullCoalescingTransform, NullableLiftingStatementTransform, NullPropagationStatementTransform, TransformArrayInitializers, TransformCollectionAndObjectInitializers, TransformExpressionTrees, IndexRangeTransform, DeconstructionTransform, NamedArgumentTransform, RemoveUnconstrainedGenericReferenceTypeCheck, UserDefinedLogicTransform, InterpolatedStringTransform) and the ILInlining AllowInliningOfLdloca option (the ldloca-into-addressof path the C# second pass enables, which needs an AddressOf node + IsGeneratedTemporaryForAddressOf + ClassifyExpression + the value-type/readonly/in-parameter copy semantics) are deferred to later iterations. The ILInlining IILTransform whole-function pass (the early inlining, D40) is unchanged; the per-statement Run is the C# pipeline's SECOND inlining pass, which folds the single-use variables the intervening transforms (ConditionDetection / Lock / Using / CachedDelegate / CachedReadOnlySpan) created. Wired into the CLI pipeline after CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization and before AssignVariableNames (the GetILTransforms() position); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using statements unchanged; the second inlining pass is a safe fold across the corpus). 6 new gtest cases (OrchestrationRunsChildrenLastToFirst; OrchestrationRequestRerunCurrentPosition; OrchestrationRequestRerunPosition -- the three orchestration tests use a mock IStatementTransform to pin the rerun mechanics without a real transform; PerStatementInliningFoldsSingleUseLocal; PerStatementInliningFoldsIntoIfFinalCondition -- pins the if-as-final block-model shape where the stloc before the if-final inlines into the if's condition; MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant running the full pre-pipeline through StatementTransform{ILInlining} over 8000 methods). 395/395 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 389). |
| D81 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitComp subset, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the self-contained VisitComp subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (the second per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform, after ILInlining -- the next in-order target after the D80 orchestration). The C# ExpressionTransforms is an ILVisitor IStatementTransform folding simple expression patterns; this iteration ports the three highest-value, self-contained VisitComp rewrites: (1) `logic.not(comp op) -> comp(op.Negate)` (push negation into the comparison -- `!(a == b)` -> `a != b`), where `logic.not` is this port's `comp(Equality, X, ldc.i4 0)` shape (the reader's brfalse, D55/D64/D68); the C# MatchLogicNot is `Kind == Equality && LiftingKind.None && Right.MatchLdcI4(0)` (the port drops LiftingKind -- no nullable-lifting model); if X is a Comp the negation is pushed (the float guard `!comp.InputType.IsFloatType()` is approximated by checking the inner comp's operands' StackType for F4/F8, since the port's Comp carries no InputType; an equality/inequality is always pushed, the `|| IsEqualityOrInequality`), else the logic.not is kept and X's subtree is visited; (2) `comp(x != 0) -> x` when the comp is in a condition slot (an if condition, or nested in an if's true/false arm, or as one side of a comp whose other side is ldc.i4 0) or its left is itself a comp (`comp(comp(...) != 0)` -> `comp(...)`) -- a port of IfInstruction.IsInConditionSlot via Parent + ChildIndex (Condition=0, TrueInst=1, FalseInst=2; the NullCoalescingInstruction.FallbackInstSlot case is dropped, no NullCoalescing node); (3) `comp.unsigned(left > 0) -> comp(left != 0)` and `comp.unsigned(left <= 0) -> comp(left == 0)` (an unsigned compare against 0 is a (non-)zero test), with the C# `UnwrapConv(SignExtend/ZeroExtend)` of the right operand approximated by `IsLdcI4ZeroMaybeConv` (a bare `ldc.i4 0` or a Conv wrapping it -- the port's Conv carries no Kind, so UnwrapConv-by-Kind cannot be faithful; the rare non-sign/zero-extend conv around 0 does not arise from the reader's comparisons). The transform is a recursive Visit dispatching on OpCode (the C# ILVisitor's AcceptVisitor / Default): non-Comp nodes recurse into their children; VisitComp runs the head rewrites (logic.not, comp(!=0)) before recursing into the operands, then the tail rewrite (unsigned normalization) after, re-visiting the node on a rewrite (matching the C# `VisitComp(inst); return;`). KEY block-model work: the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at Instructions[Count-2] so the per-statement driver visits it and recurses into its condition; this port makes the if the FinalInstruction (not in Instructions), so (a) ExpressionTransforms::Run visits the if-final's Condition at `pos == Instructions.size()-1` (the port's equivalent of the C# visiting the if at Count-2) so the `if (comp(x != 0))` -> `if (x)` rewrite fires on if-final blocks with non-terminal statements, and (b) the StatementTransform driver gained a block-model compensation: a block whose ONLY instruction is the if-final (no non-terminal Instructions, `pos` starts at -1 so the per-statement loop never enters) is visited by running each child once with the sentinel `pos = -1`; children that only handle non-terminal positions (ILInlining, which guards `pos >= 0 && pos < size`) no-op, and ExpressionTransforms visits the if-final's Condition at `pos == size-1` (which is `-1 == -1` for the size==0 case). This is essential: if-final-only blocks (every `if (cond) { ... } else { ... }` with no preceding statements) are the COMMON case after ConditionDetection, so without the compensation the comp(!=0)->x / logic.not rewrites would almost never fire on the corpus -- the CLI output now shows `if (array.Length)` (from `comp(ldlen != 0)` -> `ldlen` in a condition slot) and `if (!value)` (the kept logic.not of a bool param). Deferred vs the C#: the NullableLiftingTransform call (needs the full nullable-lift transform), FixComparisonKindLdNull (already in the standalone EarlyExpressionTransforms, D61), the ldlen / conv o->i null-comparison special cases (need Conv Kind), and every other visit method (VisitConv / VisitBox / VisitLdElema / VisitNewArr / VisitCall / VisitNewObj / VisitLdObj / VisitLdObjIfRef / VisitStObj / VisitStLoc / VisitIfInstruction [HandleConditionalOperator, logic.and/or canonicalization, NullableLifting, UserDefinedLogic, match(x)?true:false] / HandleSwitchExpression [needs SwitchExpressions setting + SwitchInstruction guards] / VisitDynamic* / VisitBinaryNumericInstruction [shift-size] / VisitTryCatchHandler) -- each needs further infrastructure (AddressOf, LdcDecimal, dynamic nodes, the resolver, MatchLogicAnd/Or, IndexRangeTransform, TransformAssignment, ...) and is a later iteration; the logic.and/logic.or negation push in VisitLogicNot is deferred (needs MatchLogicAnd/Or on IfInstruction true/false-arm patterns plus new Comp/If construction). Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (after ILInlining, the GetILTransforms() order); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, 456917 lines). 9 new gtest cases (LogicNotPushesNegationIntoComparison; CompNotEqualsZeroDropsToOperandInIfCondition; CompNotEqualsZeroDropsToOperandWhenLeftIsComp; CompUnsignedGreaterThanZeroBecomesOperand; CompUnsignedLessThanOrEqualZeroBecomesLogicNot; CompNotEqualsZeroStaysWhenNotInConditionSlot; LogicNotFloatComparisonNotPushed; IfFinalOnlyBlockConditionIsVisited -- pins the driver's block-model compensation for an if-final-only block, and that a Leave-final-only block is skipped; MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant running the full pre-pipeline through StatementTransform{ILInlining, ExpressionTransforms} over 8000 methods and asserting the ILAst invariant holds and the total Comp count is monotone non-increasing -- the rewrites are monotone: logic.not push and comp(!=0)->x each remove a Comp, unsigned normalization keeps the count). 404/404 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 395). |
| D82 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitIfInstruction subset: HandleConditionalOperator + logic.and/or canonicalization, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the VisitIfInstruction subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (extending the D81 VisitComp subset of the second per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform). Two rewrites: (1) HandleConditionalOperator -- `if (cond) stloc A(V1) else stloc A(V2)` -> `stloc A(if (!cond) V2 else V1))` (the conditional/ternary operator fold, `V = cond ? V1 : V2`); both arms must be expression Block arms (no FinalInstruction -- the shape after ConditionDetection's TryDropCommonExit drops the common trailing Branch) with exactly one StLoc to the SAME variable (the ternary's temp), and the true/false values are detached (TakeChild) and re-wired into a new IfInstruction whose condition is the C# Comp.LogicNot of the original (so the true arm stays V1 and the false arm V2, matching `cond ? V1 : V2`). KEY block-model adaptation: the C# does an in-place ReplaceWith (the if is a non-terminal at Instructions[Count-2]); this port makes the IfInstruction the block's FinalInstruction, and a StLoc is NOT a valid block final (every block must end in unconditional control flow), so the StLoc is appended to the block's non-terminal Instructions and a Branch to the next block (NextBlockInContainer, the positional fall-through -- the common exit the arms branched to, which TryDropCommonExit already made the next block) replaces the if-final. The fold requires a next block so the new Branch final is well-defined; a ternary whose exit is not the next block (rare; the arms would not have had their trailing Branches dropped) is left as if/else. The if (and its Block arms, now value-less shells) is destroyed by SetFinal; the new StLoc is Visited to cascade the Comp rewrites into the negated condition (matching the C# context.RequestRerun). (2) The logic.and/or canonicalization -- `if (cond) ldc.i4 0 else RHS` -> `if (!cond) RHS else ldc.i4 0` and `if (cond) RHS else ldc.i4 1` -> `if (!cond) ldc.i4 1 else RHS` (bring &&/|| into their canonical forms by swapping the arms + negating the condition); the if stays the block's FinalInstruction (no block-model issue -- only the arms are swapped and the condition negated), so this is a clean in-place mutation. The C# arms are bare LdcI4; this port may wrap a single-instruction arm in a Block (the inlined-fall-through shape), so ArmIsLdcI4 accepts both a bare LdcI4 and an expression Block (no FinalInstruction) wrapping a single LdcI4. The infinite-loop guard is preserved: when both arms are ldc.i4 1 (or both 0), the swap is suppressed (`!inst.FalseInst.MatchLdcI4(0)` / `!inst.TrueInst.MatchLdcI4(1)`). NegateCondition is a local helper mirroring ConditionDetection's (logic.not(comp(inner == 0)) -> inner; logic.not(comp(a op b)) -> comp(a op.Negate b); else wrap as comp(cond == 0) for primitives / comp(cond == null) for object-typed). VisitIfInstruction also visits the arms (VisitArm skips control-flow Block arms -- those with a FinalInstruction, the C# skips BlockKind.ControlFlow -- and visits expression Block arms + bare non-Block arms) and the condition AFTER the rewrites (the C# order: TrueInst, FalseInst, HandleConditionalOperator, canonicalization, then Condition). The Run compensation (D81 visited only the if-final's Condition) now calls VisitIfInstruction on the if-final at pos == size-1 so both the Comp rewrites on the condition AND HandleConditionalOperator / the canonicalization fire on if-final blocks (the common case after ConditionDetection). Deferred vs the C#: the NullableLiftingTransform call (needs the full nullable-lift transform), FixComparisonKindLdNull (already in EarlyExpressionTransforms D61), the Conv unwrap (no Conv Kind), the ldlen/conv o->i null-comparison special cases, VisitConv/VisitBox/VisitLdElema/VisitNewArr/VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitLdObjIfRef/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc (HandleCompoundAssign -- needs TransformAssignment), the remaining VisitIfInstruction pieces (NullableLifting, UserDefinedLogic, the match(x) ? true : false pattern -- needs IsPatternMatch D70 + the arms as LdcI4 1/0 with the same block-model StLoc+Branch adaptation, TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign -- needs DynamicIsEventInstruction), HandleSwitchExpression (needs SwitchExpressions setting + SwitchInstruction guards), VisitDynamic*, VisitBinaryNumericInstruction (shift-size), VisitTryCatchHandler (TransformCatchWhen/TransformCatchVariable). Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also handles VisitIfInstruction); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, 452010 lines). The ILAstToCSharp seed gained an Expr case for IfInstruction (rendering the conditional-operator form `stloc V(if (!cond) V2 else V1))` as `V = (cond ? V2 : V1)` -- a Block arm renders its single instruction, a bare expression arm renders directly; the statement form `if (cond) { ... } else { ... }` is emitted by the Statement path and does not reach Expr), so the ternary fold now renders readably (1003 `V = (... ? ... : ...)` ternaries in the mscorlib output, e.g. `var S_2 = (!writable ? 1 : 2);` and `string text = ((num == -1) ? keyName.ToUpper(...) : keyName.Substring(0, num).ToUpper(...));`) instead of the `(default)/*op=...*/` placeholder Expr's default produced for an unhandled IfInstruction value. 6 new gtest cases (HandleConditionalOperatorFoldsTernary -- the positive fold verifying the StLoc wraps an IfInstruction with the negated condition, swapped arms V2/V1, and a Branch-to-next-block final; HandleConditionalOperatorRejectsDifferentVariables; HandleConditionalOperatorRejectsArmWithFinal -- a control-flow Block arm; HandleConditionalOperatorRejectsMultiInstructionArm; LogicAndOrCanonicalizationSwapsZeroTrueArm; LogicAndOrBothOneNoSwap -- the infinite-loop guard; plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the StLoc-wrapping-IfInstruction count is monotone non-decreasing and totalFolds > 0). 410/410 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 404). |
| D83 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitIfInstruction: match(x)?true:false -> match(x), Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `match(x) ? true : false -> match(x)` fold of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (extending the D82 VisitIfInstruction subset of the second per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform). This is the last VisitIfInstruction rewrite before the deferred NullableLifting / TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign / UserDefinedLogic pieces: a conditional whose condition is a pattern match (`MatchInstruction.IsPatternMatch`, ported in D70) and whose arms are `ldc.i4 1` / `ldc.i4 0` is redundant -- the MatchInstruction (or the Comp constant/relational pattern IsPatternMatch also recognises) already evaluates to 1 (matched) / 0 (not matched), so the if just re-wraps it; the fold replaces the if with the condition (the pattern match). The C# does `inst.ReplaceWith(matchCondition)` (the if is always a non-terminal there, so the swap is in-place); this port's if-as-final block model needs an adaptation when the if is a block's FinalInstruction (a MatchInstruction/Comp is a value, not control flow, so it cannot be the final): the match becomes a non-terminal statement (its side effect -- storing the matched value into Variable -- is preserved, and the 1/0 results were discarded either way) and a Branch to the next block (the positional fall-through) replaces the if-final -- the same block-model adaptation HandleConditionalOperator uses (D82). When the if is a sub-expression value (e.g. `stloc V(if (match) 1 else 0)`) or a non-terminal in a Block, ReplaceWith is a clean in-place swap (the C# shape). KEY ordering safety: the block-final case's fall-through target is resolved BEFORE the condition is detached (`TakeChild(0)`) -- a detached condition with no fold would free the match and leave the if with a null condition, corrupting the tree (the precondition-before-mutation discipline the D58/D75 use-after-free lessons reinforce). The fold is placed in VisitIfInstruction AFTER visiting the arms, HandleConditionalOperator, CanonicalizeLogicAndOr, and the condition (the C# order: the condition is visited before the fold, so the match's children are already rewritten; the C# does NOT RequestRerun here, so the port does not re-visit the replacement -- unlike HandleConditionalOperator, which RequestReruns because it builds a NEW IfInstruction). The C# consults `context.Settings` via IsPatternMatch (PatternCombinators for the logic.not-wrapped pattern case, RelationalPatterns for non-==/!= Comp kinds); this port now snapshots the settings for the duration of a Run (a new `settings_` member set from `context.Base.Settings`) so IsPatternMatch receives the real settings rather than the nullptr default. Deferred vs the C#: NullableLifting, TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign, UserDefinedLogic (all between the condition visit and this fold in the C# -- they need the full nullable-lift transform, DynamicIsEventInstruction, and MatchLogicAnd/Or respectively), and every other visit method (see D81/D82). KEY FINDING (correcting the D82 deferral note): the fold DOES fire on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- not on MatchInstruction conditions (PatternMatchingTransform fires 0 on the corpus, so no MatchInstructions exist) but on Comp CONSTANT-PATTERN conditions (`comp(OP, x, const)`, which IsPatternMatch treats as a constant pattern -- `if (x == 5) 1 else 0` -> `x == 5`); a temporary instrumented probe counted 12 firings across the full 25315-method corpus (all Comp, 0 Match), out of ~48k VisitIfInstruction calls. This is faithful to the C# (its IsPatternMatch also treats a Comp-with-constant-right as a constant pattern), so the fold is correct, not a mis-fire. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D82 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also handles the match-true-false fold); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash). NOTE: the CLI `--csharp` line count is non-deterministic across runs (~+/-140 lines for the SAME binary, a pre-existing pipeline issue from unordered-container iteration order, unrelated to this change), so the line-count is not a reliable regression signal; the 415/415 gtest suite (including the deterministic 8000-method mscorlib sweep asserting the ILAst invariant holds and the ternary-fold count is monotone non-decreasing) is the correctness gate. 5 new gtest cases (MatchTrueFalseFoldsToMatchInValuePosition -- the if as a sub-expression value `stloc V(if (match) 1 else 0)` folds to `stloc V(match)` via in-place ReplaceWith; MatchTrueFalseFoldsToMatchAsBlockFinal -- the if as a block FinalInstruction folds to a non-terminal match + a Branch-to-next-block final; MatchTrueFalseRejectsNonPatternCondition -- a bare ldloc condition does not fold; MatchTrueFalseRejectsNonOneTrueArm; MatchTrueFalseRejectsNonZeroFalseArm). 415/415 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 410). |
| D84 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitBox subset) + shared IsReferenceType helper (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `VisitBox` subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (extending the D81/D82/D83 ExpressionTransforms subset of the second per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform) and promotes the previously file-local `IsReferenceType` query to a shared, faithful header. The rewrite is `box ref-type(arg) => arg`: for a reference type, `box` is a no-op (the value is already on the heap), so the C# checks `inst.Type.IsReferenceType == true && inst.Argument.ResultType == inst.ResultType` and replaces the box with its argument. The `ResultType` guard (the argument is stack-type O, the box is O) is the real protection against a value-type mis-fire: a value type's argument is I4/I8/F4/.. (never O), so even a non-known TypeRef whose `Kind` defaulted to `Class` (this port's plain `MakeTypeRef` leaves an unresolved type as Class) does not fold when the argument is not object-typed. `IsReferenceType` returns `nullopt` for the uncertain kinds (TypeParameter/ByRef/Pointer/Unknown/None/Other/UnboundTypeArgument/ArgList/Tuple/Intersection/ModOpt/ModReq), so a `box T(arg)` over a generic T does not fold (the C# folds it only when T has a `class` constraint this minimal type system does not track) -- conservative, matching the C# `IsReferenceType == true` requirement. The fold is `TakeChild(0)` (detach the argument) then `ReplaceWith(arg)` (the C# `inst.ReplaceWith(arg)`); the argument is visited first (the C# `inst.Argument.AcceptVisitor(this)`) so the Comp/StLoc rewrites cascade into the boxed expression before the box is considered for removal. Foundation piece: a new header `Decompiler/TypeSystem/TypeUtils.hpp` declares `std::optional<bool> IsReferenceType(const IType*)` faithful to the C# `bool? IType.IsReferenceType` (true for Class/Interface/Delegate/Array/Dynamic/Null; false for Struct/Enum/Void/NInt/NUInt/FunctionPointer; nullopt otherwise -- a null ITypePtr is nullopt; ParameterizedType/ModifiedType/Tuple defer to their underlying Kind() which this port's Kind() overrides already fold in). This dedups the previously file-local `IsReferenceType` in PatternMatchingTransform.cpp (which had minor faithfulness bugs: Dynamic/Null mapped to nullopt instead of true, NInt/NUInt mapped to true instead of false) to the shared faithful one, and supersedes the inline `IsRef(TypeKind)` bool checks in EarlyExpressionTransforms / LdLocaDupInitObjTransform (left as-is -- different signature/purpose). The PatternMatchingTransform dedup is behavior-preserving on the common kinds (Class/Interface/Delegate/Array -> true, Struct/Enum/Void -> false, TypeParameter/ByRef/Pointer/Unknown -> nullopt) and more faithful on the edge kinds (Dynamic/Null -> true, NInt/NUInt -> false) that never arise as an isinst target, verified by the unchanged PatternMatchingTransform sweep. KEY FINDING: the VisitBox fold DOES fire on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (not 0, unlike several earlier transforms) -- the sweep counted 42 firings across 8000 methods; the C# compiler/codegen does emit `box <ref-type>(arg)` (redundant boxes the JIT would elide) at that rate, so this is a real-corpus ILAst-cleaning transform, not a faithfulness-only one. The ILAstToCSharp seed already renders `box(arg)` as just `arg` for ALL boxes ("boxing is implicit in C#"), so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed drops the box at render time regardless); the value is ILAst-level cleanliness for the future real back end (the Phase 5 seed is a placeholder) and the 42 redundant boxes are removed from the ILAst. Deferred vs the C#: VisitConv (the next visit method in file order -- its first rewrite `conv.i4(ldlen) => ldlen.i4` is blocked by this port's LdLen model divergence: the C# LdLen returns native int `I` and the conv converts it, but this port's LdLen already returns `I4`, so the conv is a no-op I4->I4 and `conv.i8(ldlen)` cannot be faithfully rewritten since the port's LdLen cannot produce `I8`; the second `conv.rN(conv.r.un(...))` rewrite needs `Conv::Kind` (ConversionKind.IntToFloat) which the port's Conv does not carry), VisitLdElema/VisitNewArr's `CleanUpArrayIndices` (needs `Conv::Kind` -- Truncate/ZeroExtend/SignExtend -- the same recurring blocker), and the other visit methods (see D81/D82/D83). Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also dispatches `OpCode::Box`); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). 5 new gtest cases (VisitBoxDropsBoxOfReferenceType -- `box string(ldloc s)` folds to `ldloc s`; VisitBoxKeepsBoxOfValueType -- `box int(ldloc i)` stays, the I4 argument does not match the O box result; VisitBoxKeepsBoxOfTypeParameter -- `box T(ldloc t)` stays, IsReferenceType is nullopt; VisitBoxKeepsBoxWithNullType -- a null type does not dereference; VisitBoxDropsBoxOfObjectReferenceType -- `box object(ldloc o)` folds to `ldloc o`; plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the Box count is monotone non-increasing across the corpus and printing the fold count). 420/420 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 415). |
| D85 | Conv ConversionKind model + ExpressionTransforms (VisitLdElema/VisitNewArr CleanUpArrayIndices) (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the faithful `ConversionKind` model for the Conv node (the recurring blocker D84 identified for the next several ExpressionTransforms visit methods) and the `VisitLdElema`/`VisitNewArr` `CleanUpArrayIndices` rewrite that consumes it. The C# Conv carries `Kind` (ConversionKind), `InputType` (StackType), `InputSign` (Sign), `TargetType` (PrimitiveType), `CheckForOverflow`, `IsLifted`; the Kind is derived via `GetConversionKind(targetType, inputType, inputSign)` (Ecma-335 Table 8). This port previously stored only the target `StackType` + a `CheckForOverflow` bool on Conv, collapsing the signed/unsigned sizes (I4 covered both I4 and U4, I8 both I8/U8, I both I/U) so the sign- vs zero-extension distinction CleanUpArrayIndices needs (conv.i vs conv.u) was unrepresentable. Foundation: three new headers -- `Decompiler/TypeSystem/Sign.hpp` (the `Sign` enum None/Signed/Unsigned, mirroring TypeUtils.cs), `Decompiler/IL/PrimitiveType.hpp` (the `PrimitiveType` enum None/I1/I2/I4/I8/R4/R8/U1/U2/U4/U8/I/U/Ref/R/Unknown plus `GetStackType`/`IsIntegerType`/`IsFloatType` helpers faithful to ILTypeExtensions), and `Decompiler/IL/ConversionKind.hpp` (the `ConversionKind` enum Invalid/Nop/IntToFloat/FloatToInt/FloatPrecisionChange/SignExtend/ZeroExtend/Truncate/StopGCTracking/StartGCTracking/ObjectInterior plus a faithful `GetConversionKind(PrimitiveType, StackType, Sign)` porting Conv.cs's Ecma-335 Table 8 verbatim, including the I8/U8 and I/U signedness-decides-when-inputSign-is-None branch and the input-sign-decides branch when overflow-checking). The Conv node (`Decompiler/IL/Instructions/Conv.hpp`) is rewritten to store `Kind`/`InputType`/`InputSign`/`TargetType`/`CheckForOverflow`; the constructor mirrors the C# Conv constructor's `needsSign = checkForOverflow || (!IsFloatType(InputType) && IsFloatType(targetType))` rule (InputSign is the passed sign when needsSign, else None) and derives Kind via GetConversionKind; ResultType is `GetStackType(TargetType)`; DirectFlags adds MayThrow when CheckForOverflow; the dump renders the target/sign/Kind annotation (sign/zero-extend) faithful to the C# WriteToCore. The IL reader is restructured: the generic `IL_CONV(opc, target)`/`IL_CONVOVF(opc, target)` macros (which emitted `Conv(v, StackType::target, ...)`) are replaced by per-sign macros `IL_CONV` (Sign::None), `IL_CONV_S` (Sign::Signed -- conv.r4/r8), `IL_CONV_U` (Sign::Unsigned -- conv.r.un), `IL_CONVOVF` (Sign::Signed), `IL_CONVOVFU` (Sign::Unsigned -- the _un overflow variants), each passing the opcode's `PrimitiveType` (I1/I2/I4/U1/U2/U4/I8/U8/I/U/R4/R8/R) -- faithfully mirroring the C# ILReader's per-opcode `(Pop(), PrimitiveType.X, checkForOverflow, sign)` emission. The two existing Conv constructor call sites outside the reader are updated: SwitchDetection.cpp's switch-value conv to I8 (`Conv(switchValue, PrimitiveType::I8, false, Sign::None)`, the Kind is SignExtend/Nop/Truncate depending on the switch value's StackType -- harmless, the switch value is already I4/I8) and the ILAstToCSharp seed/test conv-over-ldlen (`Conv(ldlen, PrimitiveType::I4, false, Sign::None)`, a Nop). The seed's Conv rendering (`ILAstToCSharp.cpp`) is updated to consult `conv.ResultType()` (was the removed `TargetStackType` field) for both the ldlen-implicit special case and the cast-name lookup. Consumer: `CleanUpArrayIndices` (ported into ExpressionTransforms as `VisitLdElema`/`VisitNewArr` -- the C# VisitLdElema additionally calls `IndexRangeTransform.HandleLdElema`, deferred; the CleanUpArrayIndices part is self-contained). It drops a `conv.i` (or `conv.ovf.i`) widening of an array index -- a Conv whose ResultType is I (native int) and whose Kind is SignExtend, ZeroExtend, or a checked Truncate (Kind == Truncate && CheckForOverflow) -- replacing the conv with its argument (the index is implicitly native-int in C#). An unchecked Truncate (conv.i from I8, no overflow check) is a real truncation and is kept; a Nop conv (conv.i4, ResultType I4) is excluded by the `ResultType == I` guard. The replacement is `conv->TakeChild(0)` (detach the argument) then `index->ReplaceWith(arg)` (the C# `index.ReplaceWith(conv.Argument)` -- ReplaceWith destroys the conv and reparents the argument into the LdElema/NewArr Indices slot, mirroring the VisitBox pattern). The Visit dispatch gains `OpCode::LdElema`/`OpCode::NewArr` cases routing to VisitLdElema/VisitNewArr (which visit the children first, mirroring the C# `base.VisitLdElema`/`base.VisitNewArr`, then CleanUpArrayIndices). The D81 `IsLdcI4ZeroMaybeConv` approximation (which accepted ANY conv around ldc.i4 0 because the Conv carried no Kind) is retired in favor of a faithful `UnwrapConv` free function (a non-owning port of `ILInstruction.UnwrapConv(kind)`): `UnwrapConv(UnwrapConv(inst, SignExtend), ZeroExtend)` peels sign/zero-extending convs around the 0 before the ldc.i4 0 test -- the real array-length-comparison pattern (`comp.unsigned(ldlen > conv.i(ldc.i4 0))` -> the conv.i SignExtend unwraps to ldc.i4 0), and a Nop conv.i4 around 0 no longer mis-folds. KEY FINDING: the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus emits `conv.i` before ldelema only rarely -- the 8000-method sweep counted 1 array-index-conv drop (the legacy csc mostly emits ldelema with bare I4 indices); CleanUpArrayIndices is a real-corpus ILAst-cleaning transform that fires on Roslyn-compiled / 64-bit-indexed code more than on this corpus, but it does fire (1 drop), so it is not faithfulness-only. The Conv Kind model is exercised on the corpus via the existing ILReader tests (every conv.* opcode the reader emits now carries the faithful Kind). The conv.ovf.u from I4 with a Signed input sign-EXTENDS (not zero-extends) -- the C# GetConversionKind for the I/U target decides SignExtend vs ZeroExtend by the INPUT sign when overflow-checking, not the target -- a faithful-but-surprising behavior the Conv_Test pins. Deferred vs the C#: VisitConv (the next visit method in file order -- its `conv.i4(ldlen) => ldlen.i4` rewrite is still blocked by the LdLen model divergence: this port's LdLen already returns I4 so conv.i4(ldlen) is a no-op I4->I4 -- droppable for the I4 case but conv.i8(ldlen) cannot be faithfully rewritten since LdLen cannot produce I8; its `conv.rN(conv.r.un(...))` rewrite is now UNBLOCKED by the Conv Kind model but is a separate iteration), the remaining visit methods (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitLdObjIfRef/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc -- HandleCompoundAssign needs TransformAssignment; VisitIfInstruction's NullableLifting/UserDefinedLogic/TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign; HandleSwitchExpression; VisitDynamic*; VisitBinaryNumericInstruction; VisitTryCatchHandler), and the remaining StatementTransform per-statement children -- each needs further infrastructure. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also dispatches LdElema/NewArr); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, 451941 lines). 24 new gtest cases (18 in a new `tests/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/Conv_Test.cpp` covering the PrimitiveType/StackType helpers, the per-opcode Kind computation -- conv.i/conv.u/conv.ovf.i/conv.ovf.u/conv.ovf.i_un from I4, conv.i from I8/native, conv.i4/i1/i8/u8, conv.r4/r8/r.un, conv.r4 from F8/F4, the CheckForOverflow MayThrow flag, and UnwrapConv; plus 6 CleanUpArrayIndices cases -- DropsConvIFromLdElema, DropsConvUFromNewArr, DropsConvOvfIFromLdElema, KeepsConvIFromI8, KeepsConvI4, LeavesBareIndex -- and the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the array-index-conv count is monotone non-increasing). 444/444 gtest cases pass (24 new; was 420). |
| D86 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitConv conv.r.un combining subset) (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `VisitConv` conv.r.un combining subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (extending the D81/D82/D83/D84/D85 ExpressionTransforms subset of the second per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform). The C# VisitConv has two rewrites: (1) `conv.i4(ldlen array) => ldlen.i4(array)` -- the first rewrite -- is still BLOCKED by the LdLen model divergence (this port's LdLen already returns `I4`, so `conv.i4(ldlen)` is a no-op I4->I4 -- droppable for the I4 case, but `conv.i8(ldlen)` cannot be faithfully rewritten since the port's LdLen cannot produce `I8`; the C# LdLen returns native int `I` and the conv converts it); (2) `conv.rN(conv.r.un(...)) => conv.rN.un(...)` -- the second rewrite -- is now UNBLOCKED by the D85 Conv Kind model and is ported here. IL `conv.r.un` does not indicate whether to convert the target to R4 or R8, so the C# compiler usually follows it with an explicit `conv.r4` or `conv.r8`; to avoid emitting `(float)(double)val`, the two conversions are combined into a single `Conv` carrying the inner `conv.r.un`'s input type/sign but the outer's R4/R8 target. The C# checks `inst.TargetType.IsFloatType() && inst.Argument is Conv conv && conv.Kind == ConversionKind.IntToFloat && conv.TargetType == PrimitiveType.R`; the port checks the same (`IsFloatType(outer->TargetType) && outer->Argument->Op == Conv && inner->Kind == IntToFloat && inner->TargetType == R`). The new Conv is `Conv(inner->TakeChild(0), outer->TargetType, outer->CheckForOverflow, inner->InputSign)` -- the C# `new Conv(conv.Argument, conv.InputType, conv.InputSign, inst.TargetType, inst.CheckForOverflow, inst.IsLifted | conv.IsLifted)`: the port's Conv constructor derives `InputType` from the argument's `ResultType` (which equals the inner's `InputType`, since the inner conv's InputType is its argument's ResultType), and `IsLifted` is dropped (no nullable-lifting model). The inner conv's `InputSign` is captured into a local BEFORE `TakeChild(0)` detaches its argument (the inner conv is destroyed when the outer is replaced by `ReplaceWith`, and the inner conv's `InputSign` member is read in the new Conv's constructor call which runs before the destruction -- but capturing it first is the precondition-before-mutation discipline the D58/D75/D83 use-after-free lessons reinforce). The argument is visited first (the C# `inst.Argument.AcceptVisitor(this)`) so the Comp/StLoc/Box rewrites cascade into the converted expression before the conv.r.un combining is considered; the C# does not `RequestRerun` here (the argument was already visited), so the port does not re-visit the replacement. The `OpCode::Conv` case is wired into the recursive `Visit` dispatch (after `Box`, before `LdElema`). KEY FINDING: the conv.r.un combining fold DOES fire on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- a temporary instrumented probe counted 1 firing across 8000 methods (the legacy csc emits `conv.r.un` rarely; it fires more on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET where `conv.r.un` + explicit `conv.r4`/`conv.r8` is a common codegen pattern), so it is a real-corpus ILAst-cleaning transform, not faithfulness-only -- matching the D85 CleanUpArrayIndices precedent (1 firing on the legacy-csc corpus, more on Roslyn). Deferred vs the C#: the VisitConv `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite (still blocked by the LdLen model divergence -- reconciling the port's I4-returning LdLen with the C#'s native-int `I` LdLen is a separate slice), and the remaining visit methods (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitLdObjIfRef/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc -- HandleCompoundAssign needs TransformAssignment; the remaining VisitIfInstruction pieces -- NullableLifting, UserDefinedLogic, TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign; HandleSwitchExpression; VisitDynamic*; VisitBinaryNumericInstruction; VisitTryCatchHandler) -- each needs further infrastructure. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81-D85 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also dispatches `OpCode::Conv`); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, 451865 lines, no crash, no regression). 5 new gtest cases (VisitConvCombinesConvR4OverConvRUn -- the positive R4 fold verifying the result is a single Conv with TargetType R4, Kind IntToFloat, InputSign Unsigned, and the integer argument preserved; VisitConvCombinesConvR8OverConvRUn -- the R8 variant; VisitConvKeepsConvR4OverBareFloat -- a bare-float-argument conv.r4 (the arg is not a Conv) does not fold; VisitConvKeepsConvR4OverConvR8 -- conv.r4(conv.r8(...)) does not fold, the inner TargetType is R8 not R; VisitConvKeepsConvI4OverConvRUn -- conv.i4(conv.r.un(...)) does not fold, the outer TargetType is I4 not a float type; plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the conv.r.un-nested count is monotone non-increasing across the corpus). 449/449 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 444). |
| D87 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitBinaryNumericInstruction shift-size subset) (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `VisitBinaryNumericInstruction` shift-size subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (extending the D81-D86 ExpressionTransforms subset of the second per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform). The C# VisitBinaryNumericInstruction has two cases: (1) the ShiftLeft/ShiftRight case -- `a << (b & 31) => a << b` / `a >> (b & 63) => a >> b` -- a shift's right operand masked with the bit-width minus one is redundant in C# (the shift already masks the count), so the `& mask` is dropped (the BitAnd's Left becomes the shift's new Right); the mask must be the expected width for the shift's result type (`MatchExpectedShiftSize`: ldc.i4 31 for I4, ldc.i4 63 for I8, or the native-int `sizeof(IntPtr) * 8 - 1` for I). This case is ported here. (2) the BitAnd case -- `bitAnd(bool, bool)` when both operands InferType to Boolean runs NullableLiftingTransform (e.g. `(a.GetValueOrDefault() == b.GetValueOrDefault()) & (a.HasValue & b.HasValue)`) -- is deferred (needs the full NullableLiftingTransform + InferType, neither of which this minimal type system carries). The ported shift-size fold checks `inst->Operator == ShiftLeft/ShiftRight && inst->Right is BinaryNumericInstruction(BitAnd) && MatchExpectedShiftSize(bitAnd->Right, inst->ResultType())`; the rewrite is `auto amount = bitAnd->TakeChild(0); inst->SetChild(1, std::move(amount));` (detach the BitAnd's Left -- the real shift amount -- before the BitAnd is destroyed, then SetChild(1, ...) destroys the old Right (the BitAnd, now with a null Left) and reparents the amount into the Right slot -- the same TakeChild-then-SetChild pattern VisitBox/VisitConv use). The `MatchExpectedShiftSize` helper covers I4 (ldc.i4 31) and I8 (ldc.i4 63); the native-int (I) case -- `sizeof(IntPtr) * 8 - 1` -- is deferred (the port's SizeOf is a minimal SimpleInstruction carrying only a name string, no IType, so the C# `size.MatchSizeOf(out var sizeofType) && sizeofType.GetStackType() == StackType.I` check cannot be faithful; it returns false so the native-int mask is left in place). The children are visited first (the C# `base.VisitBinaryNumericInstruction`) so the Comp/StLoc/Box/Conv rewrites cascade into the operands before the shift-size mask is considered; the C# does not `RequestRerun` here, so the port does not re-visit the replacement. The `OpCode::BinaryNumericInstruction` case is wired into the recursive `Visit` dispatch (after `NewArr`, before the Default). The existing `IsLdcI4(inst, value)` file-local helper (the non-owning ldc.i4 value test) is reused for the 31/63 mask checks. KEY FINDING: the shift-size fold fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus only rarely -- the 8000-method sweep's per-method monotone-non-increasing `CountMaskedShifts` invariant holds across the corpus (the legacy csc emits the explicit `& 31`/`& 63` mask on a shift count rarely; it fires more on Roslyn-compiled / hand-written bit-twiddling code), so it is a real-corpus ILAst-cleaning transform on those assemblies and faithfulness-correct on this corpus -- matching the D85 CleanUpArrayIndices / D86 conv.r.un precedent (1 firing on the legacy-csc corpus, more on Roslyn). Deferred vs the C#: the BitAnd/Boolean nullable-lift case (needs NullableLiftingTransform + InferType), the native-int (I) mask `sizeof(IntPtr) * 8 - 1` (needs SizeOf to carry an IType with GetStackType), and the remaining visit methods (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitLdObjIfRef/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc -- HandleCompoundAssign needs TransformAssignment; the remaining VisitIfInstruction pieces -- NullableLifting, UserDefinedLogic, TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign; HandleSwitchExpression; VisitDynamic*; VisitTryCatchHandler) -- each needs further infrastructure. TEST GOTCHA (gtest): a variable declared directly after an `ASSERT_*` whose value is then used in the next `EXPECT_EQ` triggers MSVC C3536 "cannot be used before it is initialized" -- the gtest `ASSERT_*` fail-goto jumps over the following declaration. The existing tests sidestep this by separating the declaration from the preceding ASSERT with an intervening `EXPECT_EQ` (no goto) or by declaring the variable before the ASSERT; the new `BitAndStaysForDeferredNullableLift` test follows the latter (declares `bni` right after `st`, before any ASSERT). A second gotcha: `auto& blk = fn->Body->Blocks[0];` yields a `std::unique_ptr<Block>&` (Blocks is `vector<unique_ptr<Block>>`), so block members are reached via `blk->Instructions` (arrow), not `blk.Instructions` (dot) -- the dot form produces a cascade "cannot be used before initialized" on the dependent variable because `blk.Instructions` is ill-formed (unique_ptr has no Instructions member), which initially masqueraded as the goto-over-init issue. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81-D86 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also dispatches `OpCode::BinaryNumericInstruction`); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). 6 new gtest cases (ShiftLeftDropsBitAnd31Mask -- `shl(a, b & 31)` folds to `shl(a, b)`; ShiftRightDropsBitAnd31Mask -- the right-shift variant; ShiftLeftI8DropsBitAnd63Mask -- the long `shl(a, b & 63)` fold; ShiftKeepsWrongWidthMask -- an I8 shift with a 31 mask (not the expected 63) stays; ShiftKeepsBareShiftAmount -- `shl(a, b)` with a bare amount stays; BitAndStaysForDeferredNullableLift -- a top-level `bitAnd(bool, bool)` stays since the nullable-lift is deferred; plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the masked-shift count is monotone non-increasing across the corpus). 455/455 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 449). |
| D88 | NullCoalescingInstruction ILAst node (foundation for ExpressionTransforms.VisitCall Nullable<T>.GetValueOrDefault(a,b)->a??b fold + NullCoalescingTransform) (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the NullCoalescingInstruction ILAst node (`cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/NullCoalescingInstruction.hpp`) -- the C# `??` (null-coalescing) operator node -- as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction precedent: port the node the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). Faithful to the generated NullCoalescingInstruction in ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Instructions.cs and the hand-written NullCoalescingInstruction.cs. Two children: ValueInst (slot 0, inlineable -- the value tested for null) and FallbackInst (slot 1 -- the alternative). A new `NullCoalescingKind` enum (Ref / Nullable / NullableWithValueFallback) distinguishes the three semantics the C# back end lowers differently (Ref: `a != null ? a : b`; Nullable: `a.HasValue ? a : b`; NullableWithValueFallback: `a.HasValue ? a.Value : b` -- the kind the `Nullable<T>.GetValueOrDefault(a, b) -> a ?? b` fold in ExpressionTransforms.VisitCall produces). `UnderlyingResultType` (StackType, default O) is the stack type the back end uses to recover the result type when Kind == Nullable (the result is a Nullable<U> of that underlying type); it defaults to O and is otherwise the fallback's type. DirectFlags = ControlFlow; ResultType = FallbackInst.ResultType (the C# getter returns fallbackInst.ResultType, with a degenerate-null-fallback fallback to UnderlyingResultType the C# does not need -- the C# CheckInvariant asserts the non-null slots the port's non-virtual CheckInvariant does not). Flags() is overridden to faithfully port the C# ComputeFlags: `ControlFlow | valueInst.Flags | CombineBranches(None, fallbackInst.Flags)` -- valueInst is always executed, fallbackInst only sometimes (the branch endpoint is the join), so the fallback's flags are combined via CombineBranches(None, ..) (the None side is the unreachable "value was null but we took the value anyway" path), matching IfInstruction's Flags() override pattern. The ILAst dump renders `if.notnull(value, fallback)` (the C# WriteToCore writes `OpCode` which maps to the `if.notnull` mnemonic via the Instructions.cs OpCode name table); the C# does NOT render the Kind in the dump, but this port annotates the non-default kinds (Nullable -> `.nullable`, NullableWithValueFallback -> `.value`) as a suffix so the semantic flavour is visible (matching the MatchInstruction / UsingInstruction convention of surfacing flags in the dump); the default Ref kind renders bare, exactly as the C# does. The ILAstToCSharp seed (`ILAstToCSharp.cpp`) gained an Expr case rendering a NullCoalescingInstruction as `value ?? fallback` (the C# null-coalescing operator), faithful to the real back end's VisitNullCoalescingInstruction (a BinaryOperatorExpression with the NullCoalescing operator); the Kind is a semantic flavour the back end uses to pick the conversion, the surface syntax is the same `??` for all three. The node is NOT an IStoreInstruction (it has no Variable), so unlike MatchInstruction (D70) / UsingInstruction (D74) it does not need a ComputeVariableUsage store-counting case. The node is NOT wired into any pipeline transform yet (no transform constructs a NullCoalescingInstruction), so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (0 `??` lines -- the seed case only fires when a NullCoalescingInstruction is in the tree, which the current pipeline never produces); the value is ILAst-level infrastructure for the next in-order ExpressionTransforms.VisitCall piece (the `Nullable<T>.GetValueOrDefault(a, b) -> a ?? b` fold, which needs the 2-arg MatchGetValueOrDefault helper extending the D68 1-arg form + SemanticHelper.IsPure + the LdObj construction) and the later NullCoalescingTransform (a separate StatementTransform child that builds NullCoalescingInstructions from `if.notnull` block tails). The foundation is exercised on the real corpus: the 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructs NullCoalescingInstructions of all three Kinds over real decoded LdLoc operands and asserts the ILAst invariant holds and the dump renders -- the volume the foundation will see once VisitCall / NullCoalescingTransform are wired. Deferred vs the C#: the deconstruct/positional patterns do not apply (this is not a MatchInstruction); the whole VisitCall Nullable<T>.GetValueOrDefault(a, b) fold (the transform that CONSUMES this node) is the next iteration -- it needs the 2-arg MatchGetValueOrDefault (extending the D68 1-arg helper), SemanticHelper.IsPure (the InstructionFlags.hpp IsPure is already ported), the LdObj construction (LdObj node exists), and the block-model adaptation for the call being a value (the fold is a value-position ReplaceWith, the same in-place swap FoldMatchTrueFalse uses). Wired into the ilspy CMake build is NOT needed (the node is header-only, like LockInstruction / UsingInstruction / MatchInstruction); the test is wired into the tests CMake build. 10 new gtest cases (NodeInvariantFlagsAndDump; FlagsCombineBranchesFallback -- pins the Flags() override's CombineBranches(None, fallback) and the ControlFlow | MayReadLocals propagation; KindFlavoursDump -- the three Kind suffixes in the dump; UnderlyingResultTypeDefaultsToObject -- the field default + that mutating it does not change ResultType; ChildrenAreInTypedSlots -- the two-child tree + SetChild re-parenting; NullFallbackUsesUnderlyingResultType -- the degenerate null-fallback ResultType fallback; SeedRendersNullCoalescing -- `a ?? b`; SeedRendersValueFallbackKind -- the NullableWithValueFallback kind renders the same `??`; SeedRendersNullFallback -- `a ?? null`; MscorlibConstructFromRealLoadsSweep -- constructs NullCoalescingInstructions of all three Kinds over real decoded LdLoc operands across 8000 methods, asserting the invariant holds and the dump renders). 465/465 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 455); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 452170 lines). |
| D89 | ExpressionTransforms (VisitCall Nullable<T>.GetValueOrDefault(a,b)->a??b subset) + 2-arg MatchGetValueOrDefault helper (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `VisitCall` `Nullable<T>.GetValueOrDefault(a, b) -> a ?? b` fold of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs (the next in-order ExpressionTransforms visit method after the D88 NullCoalescingInstruction node foundation, now unblocked). The C# VisitCall checks `NullableLiftingTransform.MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, out nullableValue, out fallback) && SemanticHelper.IsPure(fallback.Flags)`, then builds `new LdObj(nullableValue, inst.Method.DeclaringType)` and `new NullCoalescingInstruction(NullCoalescingKind.NullableWithValueFallback, ldObj, fallback) { UnderlyingResultType = fallback.ResultType }` and does `inst.ReplaceWith(replacement); replacement.AcceptVisitor(this)`. Two pieces landed: (1) the 2-arg `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, nullableValue, fallback)` overload in NullableLiftingTransform (extending the D68 1-arg form): a Call with method name "GetValueOrDefault", exactly 2 arguments, and a DeclaringType that resolves (via the D68 KnownTypeCodeOf -- a ParameterizedType unwraps to its generic definition) to KnownTypeCode::NullableOfT; returns `Arguments[0]` (nullableValue) and `Arguments[1]` (fallback). A null DeclaringType does not match (the C# null DeclaringTypeDefinition). The 1-arg helper's `Arguments.size() != 1` guard and the 2-arg helper's `!= 2` guard keep the two overloads disjoint (a 2-arg call does not match the 1-arg, a 1-arg call does not match the 2-arg). (2) `VisitCall` in ExpressionTransforms: when the 2-arg MatchGetValueOrDefault matches and `IsPure(fallback->Flags())` (the InstructionFlags.hpp IsPure -- the fallback has no side-effects/throws/branches/writes, so folding it into the NullCoalescing's FallbackInst does not drop a side effect when the value is non-null), the fold captures `inst->DeclaringType` (an ITypePtr shared_ptr copy) and `fallback->ResultType()` into locals BEFORE detaching the children (the call is destroyed by ReplaceWith -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline the D58/D75/D83 use-after-free lessons reinforce), builds `LdObj(inst->TakeChild(0), declaringType)` (the ldobj loads the Nullable<T> from the receiver's address -- the C# `new LdObj(nullableValue, inst.Method.DeclaringType)`), detaches the fallback via `inst->TakeChild(1)`, builds a `NullCoalescingInstruction(NullableWithValueFallback, ldObj, fallback)` with `UnderlyingResultType = underlying`, and `inst->ReplaceWith(std::move(replacement))` (a clean value-position swap), then `Visit(repPtr)` (the C# `replacement.AcceptVisitor(this)` -- re-visits the NullCoalescing's children so the Comp/StLoc/Box/Conv rewrites cascade into the ldObj's target and the fallback; the NullCoalescing is not a Call so the fold does not re-trigger). When the fold does not fire, `base.VisitCall` visits the Arguments (the C# recurses into the children); the C# then calls `TransformArrayInitializers.TransformRuntimeHelpersCreateSpanInitialization` / `InlineArrayTransform.RunOnExpression` / `TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign` -- all deferred (need TransformArrayInitializers / InlineArrayTransform / TransformAssignment). The `OpCode::Call` case is wired into the recursive `Visit` dispatch (after BinaryNumericInstruction, before the Default). KEY block-model point (matching the D88 prediction): a Call is ALWAYS a value (never a block final -- it is not control flow, it returns a value), so the fold is a clean value-position `ReplaceWith` (the C# `inst.ReplaceWith(replacement)`); NO block-model adaptation is needed (unlike HandleConditionalOperator/FoldMatchTrueFalse, where the if-as-block-final requires the StLoc+Branch adaptation). The C# `WithILRange` is skipped (this port carries no per-instruction ILRange, per D65). KEY FINDING: the 2-arg `GetValueOrDefault(nullableValue, fallback)` is the Roslyn `a ?? b` (Nullable<T> ?? T) lowering; the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib uses the 1-arg `GetValueOrDefault()` + a separate if/ternary for `a ?? b`, NOT the 2-arg call, so the fold fires 0 times on that corpus (the 1-arg GetValueOrDefault appears ~47 times in the CLI output, rendered as calls; 0 `??` lines). This matches the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks (D59) / LdLocaDupInitObj (D60) / SwitchOnNullable (D69) precedent: a transform that fires only on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET is still ported for faithfulness -- the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the positive test builds the 2-arg call on a Nullable<int> declaring type and asserts the fold produces a NullCoalescingInstruction with the right Kind/UnderlyingResultType/ValueInst-is-ldobj/FallbackInst-is-ldc.i4, and the 5 negatives pin the guards), and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep asserts the per-method monotone invariants hold (the 2-arg GetValueOrDefault count is non-increasing, the NullCoalescingInstruction count is non-decreasing) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus.
