Deferred vs the C#: the remaining VisitCall pieces (TransformArrayInitializers.TransformRuntimeHelpersCreateSpanInitialization / InlineArrayTransform.RunOnExpression / TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign), the remaining visit methods (VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitLdObjIfRef/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc -- HandleCompoundAssign needs TransformAssignment; the remaining VisitIfInstruction pieces -- NullableLifting, UserDefinedLogic, TransformDynamicAddAssignOrRemoveAssign; HandleSwitchExpression; VisitDynamic*; VisitTryCatchHandler), and the later NullCoalescingTransform (a separate StatementTransform child that builds NullCoalescingInstructions from `if.notnull` block tails) -- each needs further infrastructure. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81-D88 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also dispatches `OpCode::Call`); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 452173 lines). 11 new gtest cases (5 in NullableLiftingTransform_Test -- MatchGetValueOrDefaultTwoArgOnNullable, MatchGetValueOrDefaultTwoArgRejectsNonNullable, MatchGetValueOrDefaultTwoArgRejectsOneArgForm, MatchGetValueOrDefaultTwoArgRejectsWrongMethodName, MatchGetValueOrDefaultTwoArgRejectsNullDeclaringType; 6 in ExpressionTransforms_Test -- VisitCallFoldsGetValueOrDefaultToNullCoalescing, VisitCallRejectsNonNullableDeclaringType, VisitCallRejectsOneArgGetValueOrDefault, VisitCallRejectsWrongMethodName, VisitCallRejectsImpureFallback, VisitCallRejectsNullDeclaringType; plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the 2-arg GetValueOrDefault count is monotone non-increasing and the NullCoalescingInstruction count is monotone non-decreasing across the corpus). 476/476 gtest cases pass (11 new; was 465). |
| D90 | NullCoalescingTransform (TransformRefTypes reference-type subset) (Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `TransformRefTypes` subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/NullCoalescingTransform.cs (the next in-order per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform after the D88 NullCoalescingInstruction node + the D89 ExpressionTransforms.VisitCall fold; the C# order has DynamicIsEventAssignmentTransform / TransformAssignment before it, both deferred -- Dynamic needs Dynamic nodes, TransformAssignment needs the compound-assign transform). The C# NullCoalescingTransform constructs NullCoalescingInstructions (`if.notnull(a, b)`, the C# `??`) from `if.notnull` block tails -- the reference-type `??` pattern: `stloc s(value); if (comp(ldloc s == ldnull)) { stloc s(fallback) }` -> `stloc s(if.notnull(value, fallback))` (then ILInlining folds the single-use `s` into its load). Two cases ported: (1) the simple case (the true arm unwraps via `Block.Unwrap` to `stloc s(fallback)`), and (2) the temp case (the true arm is a 2-instruction expression Block `{ stloc temp(fallback); stloc s(ldloc temp) }`, temp single-def + loaded once; the fold uses the temp's value as the FallbackInst). Adapted to this port's if-as-final block model: the C# carries the if as a non-terminal at `block.Instructions[pos+1]` and removes it via `block.Instructions.RemoveAt(pos+1)`; this port makes the IfInstruction the block's FinalInstruction, so the if is `block.FinalInstruction` (not `Instructions[pos+1]`) and "removing" it means replacing the if-final with a Branch to the next block (`NextBlockInContainer`, the positional fall-through the if's null FalseInst represented -- the `??` pattern has no else; the else is the fall-through to the use, which ConditionDetection dropped via TryDropCommonExit + TryInvertIfExit). The block model requires a final, so the Branch is the explicit fall-through; the fold requires a next block (the use block) and bails when there is none (a `??` at the very end of a function is degenerate). The `MatchCompEqualsNull` helper (`comp(Equality, X, ldnull) -> X`, either side the null) recognises the reader's brfalse form that ConditionDetection's TryInvertIfExit produces from the brtrue `comp(ne, ..)` form (D44/D53); `BlockUnwrap` ports `Block.Unwrap` (a single-instruction expression Block unwraps to its instruction). The fold detaches the value (`stloc->TakeChild(0)`) and the fallback (`fbSt->TakeChild(0)` for the simple case, `st0->TakeChild(0)` for the temp case) before the if and its TrueInst Block are destroyed by `SetFinal` (no GC; a raw pointer would dangle -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline). `InlineOneIfPossible` is called after the fold (the C# `ILInlining.InlineOneIfPossible(block, pos, InliningOptions.None, context)`); it was exposed as a public free function (moved out of ILInlining.cpp's anonymous namespace to namespace scope, declared in ILInlining.hpp) so other per-statement transforms can call it -- matching the C# static `ILInlining.InlineOneIfPossible`. The use is typically in the next block (not the same block), so the inline is usually a no-op here (the variable's StoreCount is stale -- ComputeVariableUsage ran before the fold counted both the value and the fallback stores; after the fold the fallback store is gone but the count is still 2, so VariableCanBeUsedForInlining's StoreCount==1 guard blocks it), and a later CFS / ILInlining pass merges the blocks and folds it; the call is faithful to the C# and handles the same-block use case. The `??` if's FalseInst is checked == nullptr (the `??` pattern has no else); an if with an else is not the `??` pattern and is left as-is (the C# does not check, but the `??` never has an else, so bailing is safer). Deferred vs the C#: the throw-expression cases (TransformRefTypes's `trueInst is Throw` arm for `a ?? throw ...`, TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard, TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes) need the ThrowExpressions setting + a mutable Throw ResultType (this port's Throw has a fixed Void ResultType, but the NullCoalescingInstruction's FallbackInst, the Throw, must be O-typed to match the value) + ILFunction.Method metadata (IsConstructor/IsStatic) + ILInlining.IsInConstructorInitializer + ILInlining.FindLoadInNext with a movable expression + MatchLogicNot / MatchHasValueCall wiring for the value-types case. KEY FINDING (corpus probe): the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus emits NO reference-type `??` pattern -- a temporary instrumented probe across 8000 methods (run through the full pre-pipeline + StatementTransform{ILInlining, ExpressionTransforms}) counted 1797 `if (comp(eq, ldloc X, ldnull))` null-check ifs and 513 `comp(ne, ..)` but ZERO whose true/false arm unwraps to a StLoc to the same variable. So this transform fires 0 times on that corpus -- the `??` operator's reference-type lowering is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern that fires on modern .NET. Ported for faithfulness (matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks D59 / LdLocaDupInitObj D60 / SwitchOnNullable D69 precedent): the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the simple fold + the temp fold + 7 negatives: non-StackSlot variable, non-if final, Inequality condition, condition on a different variable, true arm not a StLoc to s, if with an else arm, no next block) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (asserting the per-method monotone non-decreasing NullCoalescingInstruction count, not a fold count). The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (0 `??` lines -- the seed's NullCoalescingInstruction case only fires when a NullCoalescingInstruction is in the tree, which the current pipeline never produces on this corpus). Wired into the CLI pipeline as the third child of StatementTransform (after ILInlining, ExpressionTransforms -- the C# GetILTransforms() position, skipping the deferred DynamicIsEventAssignmentTransform / TransformAssignment); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 452164 lines). 10 new gtest cases (SimpleFoldProducesNullCoalescing, TempFoldProducesNullCoalescing, RejectsNonStackSlotVariable, RejectsNonIfFinal, RejectsInequalityCondition, RejectsConditionOnDifferentVariable, RejectsTrueArmNotStLocToS, RejectsIfWithElseArm, RejectsNoNextBlock, MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant). 486/486 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 476). |
| D91 | Comp nullable-lifting model + MatchCompOrDecimal helper (foundation for the next in-order NullableLiftingStatementTransform, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the faithful nullable-lifting model for the Comp node (the foundation the next in-order NullableLiftingStatementTransform -- and the ExpressionTransforms VisitComp nullable-lifting pieces -- need) and the MatchCompOrDecimal helper that consults it. This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation iteration (the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction precedent: port the foundation the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). The C# Comp (ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Instructions/Comp.cs) carries `LiftingKind` (ComparisonLiftingKind None/CSharp/ThreeValuedLogic), `IsLifted` (LiftingKind != None), `InputType` (the underlying input StackType -- for a non-lifted comparison this is the operands' ResultType, for a lifted comparison the inner type inside the Nullable<T> operands), `UnderlyingResultType` (I4), and `ResultType` (I4 for ordinary and C#-style lifted comparisons; O for the SQL-style ThreeValuedLogic lift, whose null result is itself a nullable value). This port's Comp previously carried only `Kind` + `Unsigned` (no LiftingKind, no InputType), so a lifted comparison was unrepresentable -- the nullable-lifting transform produces lifted Comps (`new Comp(ComparisonKind.Equality, ComparisonLiftingKind.CSharp, StackType.I4, Sign.None, left, right)` for a `Nullable<T> == ...` lift). Three changes to `Decompiler/IL/Instructions/Comp.hpp`: (1) a new `ComparisonLiftingKind` enum (None/CSharp/ThreeValuedLogic) faithful to Comp.cs (the C# doc-comment semantics are preserved in the porting comment -- None is an ordinary comparison, CSharp is the C#-style lift whose operands whose ResultType is O are Nullable<T> of this InputType and whose ResultType stays I4, ThreeValuedLogic is the SQL-style lift for operator! on bool? whose ResultType becomes O); (2) the Comp node gains `LiftingKind` (default None) and `InputType` (default I4) fields, an `IsLifted()` helper (`LiftingKind != None`), an `UnderlyingResultType()` helper (I4), and a `ResultType()` override that returns O for ThreeValuedLogic and I4 otherwise (faithful to Comp.cs); a second constructor `Comp(left, right, kind, lifting, inputType, unsigned_)` is added for the nullable-lifting machinery to build a lifted Comp, while the existing 4-arg constructor `Comp(left, right, kind, unsigned_)` derives `InputType = Left->ResultType()` in its body (after the base init moves Left) and defaults LiftingKind to None, so every existing call site is unchanged -- all existing Comps stay non-lifted, ResultType stays I4; (3) the WriteTo dump appends `.lifted[C#]` / `.lifted[3VL]` when LiftingKind != None (faithful to Comp.cs WriteToCore, which switches on LiftingKind) while the default (None) renders bare, so existing dumps (the `comp(eq, ...)` form the tests check via `find("comp(eq")`) are unchanged. The helper: `NullableLiftingTransform::MatchCompOrDecimal(inst, result)` (extending the D68 NullableLiftingTransform static-helper subset) ports the Comp branch of NullableLiftingTransform.cs's MatchCompOrDecimal -- a non-lifted IL `Comp` reports its Kind/Left/Right/IsLifted via the new `CompOrDecimal` struct (faithful to the C# struct; Instruction/Kind/Left/Right/IsLifted fields). The Decimal-operator branch (a Call to op_Equality/op_Inequality/op_LessThan/op_LessThanOrEqual/op_GreaterThan/op_GreaterThanOrEqual on System.Decimal) is deferred -- it needs `Call.Method.IsOperator` (this port's Call carries only a resolved name + declaring type, no operator flag) + KnownTypeCode::Decimal, so a Call never matches here (returns false, matching the C# fall-through). The helper is the bridge the nullable-lifting lift machinery (`Lift`, `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison`, `LiftCSharpComparison`) consults to recognise the C#-style lifted comparison shape -- MatchCompOrDecimal's `comp.IsLifted` is the gate that distinguishes a lift the transform already produced from a fresh comparison. Deferred vs the C# (the full NullableLiftingStatementTransform is a larger slice that is the next in-order target): the `RunStatements` entry (the if-as-final block model requires the C# `pos == Count-2` / `Instructions[pos] is IfInstruction` / `Instructions[pos+1] is Leave` shape to be adapted -- the if is the block's FinalInstruction, the two leaves are the if's arms + a separate block, a corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection shape is needed per the D73/D75 precedent), the full `Lift` machinery (LiftNormal, LiftCSharpEqualityComparison, LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison, LiftCSharpComparison, the bool? `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? v.HasValue : false` -> `v == true` folds, the ThreeValuedBoolAnd/Or nodes, the `& `| on bool?` patterns -- each needs MatchGetValueOrDefault (1-arg, in D68) + MatchHasValueCall (D68) + MatchNegatedHasValueCall + MatchNullableCtor + MatchLogicOr/And + NullableType.GetUnderlyingType + a mutable Comp LiftingKind, all of which the Comp model + MatchCompOrDecimal foundation now unblock), and NullPropagationTransform (the `context.Settings.NullPropagation` path Lift consults FIRST -- it is a separate 583-line transform). The foundation is exercised on the real corpus via the existing ILReader tests (every Comp the reader emits now carries the faithful InputType) and the existing NullableLiftingTransform mscorlib sweep. No pipeline transform consumes the lifted Comp or MatchCompOrDecimal yet, so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the Comp changes are a no-op for the corpus since no existing Comp is lifted -- LiftingKind defaults to None, ResultType stays I4); the value is ILAst-level infrastructure for the next in-order NullableLifting transform. 10 new gtest cases (6 in a new `tests/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/Comp_Test.cpp` -- OrdinaryComparisonDefaults: LiftingKind None, IsLifted false, InputType derives from the left operand's ResultType (I4 for an Int32-typed local), ResultType I4, dump renders `comp(eq` with no suffix; UnsignedComparison: the `.un` flag + no lifting suffix; CSharpLiftedComparison: the lifted constructor sets LiftingKind CSharp, IsLifted true, InputType I4, ResultType stays I4, dump renders `.lifted[C#]`; ThreeValuedLogicLiftedComparison: ResultType flips to O, dump renders `.lifted[3VL]`; LiftedComparisonCarriesExplicitInputType: the lifted constructor records the passed InputType (I8) not the operand's ResultType, with `.un`; NegateComparisonIsFaithful: pins the == <=> != / < => >= / <= => > / > => <= / >= => < table; plus 4 in NullableLiftingTransform_Test -- MatchCompOrDecimalOnNonLiftedComp, MatchCompOrDecimalOnLiftedComp, MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsCall [the deferred Decimal branch], MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsNonComp/null). 496/496 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 486); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D92 | ExpressionTransforms.VisitComp NullableLifting Run(Comp) entry (the first NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `Run(Comp comp)` entry of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/NullableLiftingTransform.cs -- the simplest of the NullableLifting entry points and the first piece of the nullable-lifting machinery the D91 Comp nullable-lifting model + MatchCompOrDecimal foundation unblocked -- into the C++ ExpressionTransforms.VisitComp. The C# `Run(Comp comp)` recognises the VS2022.10 / Roslyn 4.10.0 optimization that turns `a == 42` into `a.GetValueOrDefault() == 42` without any HasValue check and lifts it back: a non-lifted equality/inequality (`!comp.IsLifted && comp.Kind.IsEqualityOrInequality()`) whose one side is `call GetValueOrDefault(arg)` on System.Nullable<T> (the D68 1-arg MatchGetValueOrDefault) and whose other side is a non-zero integer constant (`MatchLdcI(out value) && value != 0`) has that side replaced by `new LdObj(arg, call.Method.DeclaringType)` and is marked C#-lifted (`comp.LiftingKind = ComparisonLiftingKind.CSharp`, the D91 setter). Both the Left-is-GetValueOrDefault and the Right-is-GetValueOrDefault (constant-on-left) shapes are handled. The port captures `call->DeclaringType` (an ITypePtr shared_ptr copy) and detaches `arg` via `call->TakeChild(0)` BEFORE `comp->SetChild(...)` destroys the old Call (the precondition-before-mutation discipline -- the D58/D75/D83 use-after-free lessons: the call is destroyed by the slot swap, so its DeclaringType and Arguments must be read/copied/detached first), builds `make_unique<LdObj>(std::move(argOwned), declaringType)`, sets `comp->LiftingKind = ComparisonLiftingKind::CSharp`, and `comp->SetChild(0/1, ...)` reparents the LdObj into the slot (SetChild wires Parent/ChildIndex and destroys the old occupant -- the same TakeChild-then-SetChild pattern VisitBox/VisitConv/VisitBinaryNumericInstruction use). A `MatchLdcI(long long&)` helper is added to ExpressionTransforms.cpp's anonymous namespace (matching the SwitchAnalysis.cpp file-local one: LdcI4 or LdcI8, no conv unwrap -- the reader does not wrap ldc constants in conv). KEY block-model point (matching the D88/D89 prediction): a Comp is ALWAYS a value (never a block final -- it is not control flow), so the fold is a clean child-slot swap with NO block-model adaptation (unlike HandleConditionalOperator/FoldMatchTrueFalse, where the if-as-block-final requires the StLoc+Branch adaptation); the comp itself is not replaced, only its Left/Right child is swapped and LiftingKind is set. The placement in the Visit(OpCode::Comp) dispatch matches the C# VisitComp order: (1) VisitCompHeadRewrites (logic.not push + comp(x!=0)=>x -- these handle the value==0 case, which is disjoint from Run(Comp)'s value!=0 gate), (2) RunCompNullableLift (NEW), (3) Visit(Left)+Visit(Right) (base.VisitComp -- recurse into the operands, now the freshly-built LdObj + the constant), (4) `if (comp->IsLifted()) return;` (NEW -- the C# `if (inst.IsLifted) return;` after base.VisitComp skips the tail rewrites for a lifted comp; a RunCompNullableLift-produced comp is an eq/ne lifted comp so the unsigned GT/LE tail rewrite would not fire on it anyway, but the guard is faithful and forward-compatible), (5) VisitCompTailRewrites (the unsigned > 0 / <= 0 normalization). Gated on `settings_->LiftNullables` (the C# `context.Settings.LiftNullables`, default true). KEY FINDING: the `a.GetValueOrDefault() == const` (no HasValue check) optimization is a VS2022.10 / Roslyn 4.10.0 codegen pattern, so the fold fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET) -- matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks (D59) / LdLocaDupInitObj (D60) / SwitchOnNullable (D69) / NullCoalescingTransform (D90) precedent of porting a 0-firing-on-corpus transform for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the lift (positive: Left-is-GVO + ldc.i4, constant-on-Left + GVO, ldc.i8; negatives: zero constant, relational kind, non-Nullable declaring type, already-lifted comp, LiftNullables-off) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants hold (the C#-lifted Comp count is non-decreasing, the 1-arg GetValueOrDefault-call count is non-increasing) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed renders a lifted comp the same as an ordinary comp -- the lift is ILAst-level infrastructure for the future real back end). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining NullableLifting entry points -- `Run(IfInstruction)` (the full lift machinery: Lift/LiftNormal/LiftCSharpEqualityComparison/LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison/LiftCSharpComparison, the bool? `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? v.HasValue : false` -> `v == true` folds, MatchNegatedHasValueCall, MatchNullableCtor, MatchLogicOr/And, NullableType.GetUnderlyingType) and `Run(BinaryNumericInstruction)` (the BitAnd-as-short-circuit analysis) -- and the `RunStatements(Block, int)` block transform (the if-as-final block model requires the C# `pos == Count-2` / `Instructions[pos] is IfInstruction` / `Instructions[pos+1] is Leave` shape to be adapted, a corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection shape is needed per the D73/D75 precedent), and the NullPropagationTransform path `Lift` consults FIRST (a separate 583-line transform), are the subsequent in-order targets the D91 foundation + this entry unblock. The head rewrites' LiftingKind.None guard (the C# MatchLogicNot / comp(x!=0)=>x both check `LiftingKind == None`) is not added: no lifted comp exists when the head rewrites run in the current pipeline (RunCompNullableLift is the only lifted-Comp producer and runs AFTER the head rewrites), so the guard would be a no-op; it is a faithfulness improvement left for a later iteration if a future NullableLifting entry point produces lifted comps before ExpressionTransforms. 8 new gtest cases (RunCompNullableLiftFoldsGetValueOrDefaultEqualToConst, RunCompNullableLiftFoldsConstEqualToGetValueOrDefault, RunCompNullableLiftFoldsLdcI8Constant, RunCompNullableLiftRejectsZeroConstant, RunCompNullableLiftRejectsRelationalKind, RunCompNullableLiftRejectsNonNullableDeclaringType, RunCompNullableLiftRejectsAlreadyLiftedComp, RunCompNullableLiftNoOpWhenLiftNullablesOff) plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the per-method monotone non-decreasing C#-lifted Comp count and monotone non-increasing 1-arg GetValueOrDefault-call count. 504/504 gtest cases pass (8 new; was 496); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 452171 lines). |
| D93 | NullableLifting ILVariable-based helpers + GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable + MatchNullableCtor + MatchNull (foundation for the next in-order NullableLifting Run(IfInstruction), Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the remaining static helper subset of ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/NullableLiftingTransform.cs that the next in-order NullableLifting entry point (`Run(IfInstruction)`, and the deferred LiftNormal/LiftCSharp* paths) consult, as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model precedent: port the helpers the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). The helpers added to `NullableLiftingTransform` (extending the D68/D89/D91 static-helper subset): (1) `MatchHasValueCall(inst, ILVariablePtr& v)` -- the ldloca-v overload of the D68 1-arg `MatchHasValueCall(inst, out ILInstruction arg)`: `call get_HasValue(ldloca v)` on System.Nullable<T> (1 argument, the argument a LdLoca) -> v (the LdLoca's variable); the 1-arg overload recognises the call and returns its argument, this overload additionally requires the argument to be a `ldloca v` and reports the variable. (2) `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, ILVariablePtr& v)` -- the ldloca-v overload of the D68 1-arg `MatchGetValueOrDefault` (the 1-argument form whose argument is a LdLoca). (3) `MatchNegatedHasValueCall(inst, const ILVariable* v)` -- `logic.not(call get_HasValue(ldloca v))` -> recognises v; the logic.not is this port's `comp(Equality, X, ldc.i4(0))` shape (the reader's brfalse, per the SwitchAnalysis/ConditionDetection/MatchInstruction convention), unwrapped by a file-local `MatchLogicNot(inst, out arg)` helper faithful to MatchInstruction's MatchLogicNot, then the inner call must operate on the given variable (compared by shared_ptr identity -- `ILVariablePtr::get() == v`). (4) `MatchNullableCtor(inst, const IType*& underlyingType, ILInstruction*& arg)` -- `newobj Nullable<T>(arg)` -> (T, arg): a newobj (a Call with the D76 `IsNewObj` flag -- newobj is always a constructor, so IsNewObj is the faithful equivalent of the C# `newobj.Method.IsConstructor`) whose declaring type resolves (via the D68 `KnownTypeCodeOf` unwrap) to KnownTypeCode::NullableOfT with exactly one argument; the underlying type is `GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable(DeclaringType)`. (5) `MatchNull(inst, const IType*& underlyingType)` -- `default(Nullable<T>)` -> T: a DefaultValue (the D60 node) whose Type is a Nullable<T>; the C# also checks `NullableType.IsNullable(type)` -- `GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable` returning non-null is the equivalent (only a Nullable<T> instantiation unwraps). (6) `GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable(const IType*)` -- the type-system helper the whole lift machinery needs (the C# `NullableType.GetUnderlyingType`): for a Nullable<T> -- a `ParameterizedType` whose generic definition is `KnownType(NullableOfT)` with one type argument -- returns the type argument T (non-owning); otherwise null. This port's IType for `Nullable<int>` is `ParameterizedType(KnownType(NullableOfT), {Int32})`; a bare `Nullable`1` (the generic definition) has no type argument and returns null. The full C# `NullableType.GetUnderlyingType` also unwraps type parameters and other nullable wrappers (deferred -- this port's minimal type system carries no NullableType/TypeParameter-with-constraint machinery); the ParameterizedType case is the shape the metadata reader produces for every real Nullable<T>. (7) `IsKnownType(const IType*, KnownTypeCode)` -- a faithful subset of the C# `type.IsKnownType(code)` extension: a KnownType compares its Code directly; a ParameterizedType (e.g. `Nullable<int>`) is not itself a known type; null is not a known type (matching the C# which reads the type's KnownTypeCode, None for a non-known type). The `MatchHasValueCall`/`MatchGetValueOrDefault` ldloca-v overloads take `ILVariablePtr&` (a shared_ptr out-param) rather than a raw `ILVariable*` out-param, so they are overload-disjoint from the existing `MatchHasValueCall(inst, ILInstruction*& arg)` / `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, ILInstruction*& arg)` / `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, ILInstruction*&, ILInstruction*&)` helpers (an `ILVariablePtr` lvalue does not bind to an `ILInstruction*&`/`const ILVariable*` and vice versa -- no shared_ptr/raw-pointer overload ambiguity, avoiding the D66 int&/int and the MatchLdLoc ILVariable*&/const ILVariable* precedent). `MatchNegatedHasValueCall` uses the extract `MatchHasValueCall(arg, ILVariablePtr& tmp)` + compares `tmp.get() == v` (it does NOT need a match-against-v overload of MatchHasValueCall, sidestepping the D66 ambiguity entirely). This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform consumes the new helpers yet (the D92 `Run(Comp)` entry is the only NullableLifting piece wired into ExpressionTransforms.VisitComp so far), so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged; the foundation is exercised by the unit tests + the mscorlib sweep (the sweep now also counts the ldloca-v HasValue/GetValueOrDefault matches, the `MatchNullableCtor` matches on real newobj sites, and the `MatchNull` matches on real DefaultValue nodes, asserting each match yields a non-null variable/underlying-type so the helpers never misfire on the corpus). The next in-order target the foundation unblocks is `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)` (the C# entry called from `ExpressionTransforms.VisitIfInstruction`): the `MatchGetValueOrDefault(condition, v)` + bool? path -- the four `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? v.HasValue : false` -> `v == true` / `v == false` / `v != true` / `v != false` folds producing a C#-lifted Comp (the D91 model) -- which needs these ldloca-v overloads + MatchNegatedHasValueCall + GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable + IsKnownType; the LiftNormal/LiftCSharp* paths additionally need MatchNullableCtor + MatchNull (now ported) + DoLift/DoLiftBinary (the recursive expression-lifting over GetValueOrDefault/Conv/BinaryNumericInstruction/Comp that needs a BitSet nullableVars-relevance analysis -- the larger deferred slice). The `Run(IfInstruction)` block-model adaptation (the if is this port's block FinalInstruction, so a value-producing Lift result -- a Comp -- replacing the if-as-final needs the StLoc+Branch-to-next-block adaptation the HandleConditionalOperator/FoldMatchTrueFalse transforms use, OR the if is a sub-expression value-if for a clean ReplaceWith) and a corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection bool? if shape (the D73/D75/D79 precedent of probing the real shape rather than reasoning from the C# structure, since the block model diverges) are the further deferred pieces the next iteration handles. 15 new gtest cases (GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullableUnwrapsInstantiation; GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullableBareDefinitionIsNull; GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullableNonNullableIsNull; IsKnownTypeChecksCode; MatchHasValueCallLdLocaReportsVariable; MatchHasValueCallLdLocaRejectsNonLdLocaArg; MatchHasValueCallLdLocaRejectsNonNullable; MatchGetValueOrDefaultLdLocaReportsVariable; MatchGetValueOrDefaultLdLocaRejectsTwoArgForm; MatchNegatedHasValueCallMatchesLogicNot; MatchNegatedHasValueCallRejectsNonNegatedAndDifferentVar; MatchNullableCtorMatchesNewObj; MatchNullableCtorRejects [non-newobj / non-Nullable / wrong arg count / null declaring type]; MatchNullMatchesDefaultNullable; MatchNullRejectsNonNullableDefaultAndNonDefaultValue) plus the updated MscorlibDeclaringTypeSweep asserting the ldloca-v / MatchNullableCtor / MatchNull helpers never misfire (every match yields a non-null variable/underlying-type) across 8000 methods and the ILAst invariant holds. 519/519 gtest cases pass (15 new; was 504); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451840 lines). |
| D94 | NullableLifting Run(IfInstruction) bool? equality folds (the second NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the bool? equality comparison subset of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)` (the C# entry called from `ExpressionTransforms.VisitIfInstruction`) -- the next in-order NullableLifting entry point the D91 Comp-lifting model + D92 Run(Comp) + D93 ILVariable-based helpers foundation unblocked -- into the C++ `ExpressionTransforms.VisitIfInstruction` (wired between the condition visit and the FoldMatchTrueFalse call, matching the C# VisitIfInstruction order: arms, HandleConditionalOperator, CanonicalizeLogicAndOr, visit condition, Run(IfInstruction), [deferred TransformDynamic/UserDefinedLogic], FoldMatchTrueFalse). The C# `Lift` method's logic.not unwrap loop (`while (condition.MatchLogicNot(out arg)) { condition = arg; Swap(ref trueInst, ref falseInst); }`) is ported (a file-local `MatchLogicNot` in ExpressionTransforms.cpp, faithful to the NullableLiftingTransform.cpp / MatchInstruction.hpp ones -- logic.not is this port's `comp(Equality, X, ldc.i4(0))` brfalse shape); each peeled logic.not swaps the arms. The NullPropagation path (`context.Settings.NullPropagation && !NullPropagationTransform.IsProtectedIfInst`) is deferred (needs the separate 583-line NullPropagationTransform); the AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal path (the multi-HasValue `&&` lift, needs DoLift + the BitSet nullableVars relevance analysis) and the MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* path (the comparison lift) are deferred; the `&`/`|` on bool? path (ThreeValuedBoolAnd/Or, needs MatchNullableCtor + the ThreeValuedBool nodes) is deferred. The ported subset is the four bool? equality folds: a conditional whose condition is `call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca v)` on a Nullable<bool> (`MatchGetValueOrDefault(condition, v)` the D93 ldloca-v overload + `IsKnownType(GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable(v.Type), Boolean)`) and whose arms are `v.HasValue` / a ldc.i4 constant folds into a C#-lifted Comp (the D91 model): `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? v.HasValue : false` -> `comp.lifted[C#](ldloc v, ldc.i4 1)` (`v == true`); `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? false : v.HasValue` -> `comp.lifted[C#](ldloc v, ldc.i4 0)` (`v == false`); `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? !v.HasValue : true` -> `comp.lifted[C#](ldloc v, ldc.i4 1)` Inequality (`v != true`); `v.GetValueOrDefault() ? true : !v.HasValue` -> `comp.lifted[C#](ldloc v, ldc.i4 0)` Inequality (`v != false`). The Comp is a value (not control flow), so the block-model adaptation follows FoldMatchTrueFalse: a clean `ReplaceWith` when the if is a sub-expression value (e.g. `stloc V(if (...) ..)`, the C# `ifInst.ReplaceWith(lifted)`), or the Comp becomes a non-terminal statement + a `Branch` to the next block (`NextBlockInContainer`) replaces the if-final when the if is a block's `FinalInstruction` (a Comp cannot be a block final). The `FinishIfNullableLift` helper (file-local in ExpressionTransforms.cpp's anonymous namespace, NOT a member -- the hpp only declares `RunIfNullableLift(IfInstruction*)`, avoiding the `ComparisonKind`/`ILVariablePtr` forward-declaration churn in the hpp) builds the fresh `LdLoc(v)` + `LdcI4(constant)` Comp and applies the adaptation. The `MatchHasValueCall(trueInst, v)` / `MatchHasValueCall(falseInst, v)` calls use the NEW match-against-v overload `MatchHasValueCall(inst, const ILVariable* v)` (`v.get()`) -- the C# distinguishes `MatchHasValueCall(inst, out ILVariable v)` (report) from `MatchHasValueCall(inst, ILVariable v)` (match) by the `out` modifier; this port distinguishes them by `ILVariablePtr&` (report, an `ILVariablePtr` lvalue binds via identity) vs `const ILVariable*` (match, `v.get()` binds via qualification), so an `ILVariablePtr` lvalue binds to the report overload and `v.get()` to the match overload (no shared_ptr/raw-pointer ambiguity, the D66 precedent). The match-against-v overload is the C# `MatchHasValueCall(inst, out v2) && v == v2` (it calls the D93 report-variable overload and compares the variable by `ILVariablePtr::get() == v`); `MatchNegatedHasValueCall` (the D93 match-against-v overload, already taking `const ILVariable*`) is reused for the `!v.HasValue` arms. KEY bug found + fixed: the initial port called `MatchHasValueCall(trueInst, v)` with `v` an `ILVariablePtr` lvalue, which bound to the D93 REPORT-variable overload and OVERWROTE `v` with the arm's HasValue-call variable -- so the `v != true`/`v != false` folds (Branches 3/4, which use MatchNegatedHasValueCall AFTER the report overloaded `v`) failed to fire (the overwritten `v` no longer matched the GVO's `v`), and the different-variable negative test incorrectly folded (the arm's variable was overwritten into `v` and the Comp built with it). The match-against-v overload (`v.get()`) fixes all three: the GVO's `v` is checked against the arm's HasValue-call variable (not overwritten), so a HasValue call on a different variable does not match and the fold bails. Gated on `settings_->LiftNullables` (the C# `context.Settings.LiftNullables`, default true). The CanonicalizeLogicAndOr interaction: the `v == false` and `v != true` shapes have a `ldc.i4 0` / `ldc.i4 1` true/false arm that CanonicalizeLogicAndOr swaps (swapping the arms + negating the condition to `comp(eq, call GVO, 0)`); the RunIfNullableLift logic.not unwrap then re-swaps the arms back and peels the negated condition, so the four branches see the canonical `v.HasValue`-is-an-arm + `ldc.i4`-is-the-other-arm shape and the correct fold fires (the `v == false` test verifies the `IsLdcI4(trueInst, 0) && MatchHasValueCall(falseInst, v)` branch fires after the double-swap, producing `comp.lifted[C#](ldloc v, ldc.i4 0)`). KEY block-model point (matching the D92 prediction and the D93 notes' StLoc+Branch-to-next-block / clean-ReplaceWith alternatives): the Comp is a value (not control flow), so the if-as-final block-final case (a Comp cannot be a block final) needs the Add+SetFinal(Branch) adaptation; but the bool? pattern requires value arms (`MatchHasValueCall`/`IsLdcI4`, not Branches), so the if-as-final after ConditionDetection (arms are Branches) never matches the bool? pattern -- the fold fires only for a sub-expression value-if (the common case), where ReplaceWith is a clean in-place swap (the C# `ifInst.ReplaceWith(lifted)`). The block-final case (tested by RunIfNullableLiftFoldsAsBlockFinal with a hand-built if-as-final + value arms) applies the Add+SetFinal adaptation: the Comp becomes a non-terminal statement (its value is discarded, matching the if-as-statement whose value was discarded) and a Branch to the next block replaces the if-final. The C# does not re-visit the lifted Comp (it is a fresh leaf-load + constant comparison; no VisitComp rewrite fires on a lifted eq/ne with a LdLoc Left and a LdcI4 Right -- the head rewrites require Right == 0 for logic.not / comp(!=0)=>x, and RunCompNullableLift requires a GetValueOrDefault call on one side), so no cascade is needed. KEY FINDING: the `bool?` (`Nullable<bool>`) comparison codegen (`v.GetValueOrDefault() ? v.HasValue : false` for `v == true`) is a Roslyn-era pattern; a corpus probe across the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib sweep confirmed the fold fires 0 times on that corpus (it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET) -- matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks (D59) / LdLocaDupInitObj (D60) / SwitchOnNullable (D69) / NullCoalescingTransform (D90) / RunCompNullableLift (D92) precedent of porting a 0-firing-on-corpus transform for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the four folds (the `v == true` / `v == false` / `v != true` / `v != false` positives + 6 negatives: non-Boolean underlying type, wrong constant arm, non-GetValueOrDefault condition, HasValue on a different variable, LiftNullables-off, and the block-final fold) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants hold (the C#-lifted Comp count is non-decreasing, the 1-arg get_HasValue-call count is non-increasing -- each bool? fold removes one HasValue call from an arm; nothing in this subset creates one) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed renders a lifted comp the same as an ordinary comp -- the lift is ILAst-level infrastructure for the future real back end). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining `Run(IfInstruction)` paths (AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal -- the multi-HasValue `&&` lift, needs DoLift + the BitSet nullableVars relevance analysis; MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* -- the comparison lift, needs MatchLogicOr/And + the lifted-Comp construction; NullPropagation -- the `context.Settings.NullPropagation` path, a separate 583-line transform; the `&`/`|` on bool? -- ThreeValuedBoolAnd/Or + MatchNullableCtor + MatchThreeValuedLogicConditionPattern), `Run(BinaryNumericInstruction)` (the BitAnd-as-short-circuit analysis), and the `RunStatements(Block, int)` block transform (the if-as-final block model requires the C# `pos == Count-2` / `Instructions[pos] is IfInstruction` / `Instructions[pos+1] is Leave` shape to be adapted, a corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection shape is needed per the D73/D75 precedent) are the subsequent in-order targets. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81-D93 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also runs the Run(IfInstruction) bool? folds in VisitIfInstruction); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451829 lines, within the D30 non-determinism range). 10 new gtest cases (RunIfNullableLiftFoldsGetValueOrDefaultHasValueFalse -- the `v == true` fold; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsFalseHasValue -- the `v == false` fold; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsNegatedHasValueTrue -- the `v != true` fold; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsTrueNegatedHasValue -- the `v != false` fold; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsNonBooleanUnderlyingType; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsWrongConstantArm; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsNonGetValueOrDefaultCondition; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsHasValueOnDifferentVariable; RunIfNullableLiftNoOpWhenLiftNullablesOff; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsAsBlockFinal -- the block-final fold) plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the per-method monotone non-decreasing C#-lifted Comp count and monotone non-increasing 1-arg get_HasValue-call count (a new CountHasValueCall helper). 529/529 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 519); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D95 | ThreeValuedBoolAnd / ThreeValuedBoolOr ILAst nodes (foundation for the next in-order NullableLifting `&`/`|` on bool? path, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `ThreeValuedBoolAnd` / `ThreeValuedBoolOr` ILAst nodes (`cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/ThreeValuedBoolInstructions.hpp`, a combined header mirroring the C# `LogicInstructions.cs` grouping and the port's `ArrayInstructions.hpp`/`MemoryInstructions.hpp`/`TryInstructions.hpp` multi-node convention) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order NullableLifting piece (the `&`/`|` on bool? path of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`, the section of `Lift` after the D94 bool? equality folds), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model precedent (port the node the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). The nodes are the C# three-valued logic `&` / `|` on `bool?` (Nullable<bool>): both are `BinaryInstruction` (Left + Right children, both inlineable), inputs are `bool?` or I4, the output is `bool?` (StackType::O, a boxed Nullable<bool> on the eval stack), and unlike logic.and()/logic.or() they do NOT short-circuit (the three-valued truth tables require evaluating both sides to detect a `null` result). Both implement `ILiftableInstruction` (IsLifted = true, UnderlyingResultType = I4) in the C#; this port has no ILiftableInstruction interface, so `IsLifted()` (returns true) and `UnderlyingResultType()` (returns I4) methods are added directly to each node (the Comp precedent), so a future lift consumer (the `&`/`|` on bool? path, or DoLift) can recognise them. DirectFlags is None (inherited from BinaryInstruction); the base `ILInstruction::Flags()` (DirectFlags | union of children = None | Left | Right) equals the C# `ComputeFlags` (left.Flags | right.Flags | None), so no Flags() override is needed. The ILAst dump renders the faithful mnemonics from the C# OpCode name table: `3vl.bool.and(left, right)` / `3vl.bool.or(left, right)`. The C# `CheckInvariant` also asserts `Left.ResultType == StackType.I4 || Left.ResultType == StackType.O`; this port's `CheckInvariant` is non-virtual and checks tree consistency only (the other ported nodes skip their C# per-node asserts the same way), so that is not enforced here. The ILAstToCSharp seed gained Expr cases rendering a `ThreeValuedBoolAnd` as `left & right` (the C# `&` on bool?, faithful to the real back end's `VisitThreeValuedBoolAnd` -- a `BinaryOperatorExpression` with the `BitwiseAnd` operator) and a `ThreeValuedBoolOr` as `left | right` (the C# `|` on bool?, faithful to `VisitThreeValuedBoolOr` -- a `BinaryOperatorExpression` with the `BitwiseOr` operator); the operands are converted to `bool?` by the real back end (a semantic flavour the seed does not model), so the surface syntax is the plain `&` / `|`. The nodes are NOT `IStoreInstruction` (they are `BinaryInstruction` with no Variable), so unlike MatchInstruction (D70) / UsingInstruction (D74) they do NOT need a `ComputeVariableUsage` store-counting case (the NullCoalescingInstruction D88 precedent -- the default case + children recursion handles them). The `OpCode::ThreeValuedBoolAnd` / `OpCode::ThreeValuedBoolOr` enum values were already present in the port's `OpCode.hpp` (declared ahead of the nodes); the node classes are header-only and are reached via includes from the seed and the tests (no CMake source listing needed, matching every other header-only node). This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform constructs these nodes yet, so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed's cases only fire when a ThreeValuedBool node is in the tree, which the current pipeline never produces); the value is ILAst-level infrastructure for the next in-order `&`/`|` on bool? path. The next in-order consumer is the `&`/`|` on bool? section of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`'s `Lift`: `condition ? v : (bool?)false` -> `3vl.bool.and(condition, v)`; `condition ? (bool?)true : v` -> `3vl.bool.or(condition, v)`; and the two-nullable `condition ? v : v2` case via `MatchThreeValuedLogicConditionPattern` (which needs `MatchLogicOr`/`MatchLogicAnd` matching the IfInstruction `if (a) ldc.i4 1 else b` / `if (a) b else ldc.i4 0` patterns -- new helpers not yet in this port -- plus the D93 `MatchGetValueOrDefault` ldloca-v overload, `MatchHasValueCall` match-against-v, `MatchNullableCtor`, and `IsKnownType`). The foundation is exercised on the real corpus: the 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructs a `ThreeValuedBoolAnd` and a `ThreeValuedBoolOr` over two real decoded LdLoc operands for every method with two distinct loaded variables, asserting the node invariant holds, `ResultType()` is O, `IsLifted()` is true, `UnderlyingResultType()` is I4, and the dump renders -- the volume the foundation will see once the `&`/`|` on bool? path is wired. The `&`/`|` on bool? codegen is a Roslyn-era pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D92/D94 precedent), so the wired transform (a later iteration) will fire on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET and be verified by hand-built tests + the sweep invariant. 9 new gtest cases (AndNodeInvariantFlagsAndDump; OrNodeInvariantFlagsAndDump; IsLiftedAndUnderlyingResultType; ChildrenAreInTypedSlots -- the two-child tree + SetChild re-parenting; FlagsPropagateFromChildren -- None | Left | Right with MayReadLocals from two LdLoc operands; SeedRendersThreeValuedBoolAnd -- `a & b`; SeedRendersThreeValuedBoolOr -- `a | b`; SeedRendersNullOperand -- the degenerate null-operand renders `(default)` not `(null)`; MscorlibConstructFromRealLoadsSweep). 538/538 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 529); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- the new seed cases are unreachable from the current pipeline). |

[NEW], return null). Three shapes, each producing a ThreeValuedBoolAnd/Or (the D95 nodes): (1) `condition ? v : (bool?)false` ==> `3vl.bool.and(condition, v)` (the true arm is `ldloc v`, the false arm is `newobj Nullable<bool>(ldc.i4 0)` -- the D93 MatchNullableCtor + IsKnownType(utype, Boolean) + IsLdcI4(arg, 0)); (2) `condition ? (bool?)true : v` ==> `3vl.bool.or(condition, v)` (the `else if (MatchLdLoc(falseInst, v))` branch, the true arm `newobj Nullable<bool>(ldc.i4 1)`, the false arm `ldloc v`); (3) the two-nullable `condition ? v : v2` case via the new `MatchThreeValuedLogicConditionPattern` -- the condition is `nullable1.GetValueOrDefault() || (!nullable2.GetValueOrDefault() && !nullable1.HasValue)` (a logic.or whose lhs is a 1-arg GetValueOrDefault on a Nullable<bool> and whose rhs is a logic.and of `!nullable2.GetValueOrDefault()` and `!nullable1.HasValue`); when `v==nullable1 && v2==nullable2` ==> `3vl.bool.or(v, v2)`, when `v==nullable2 && v2==nullable1` ==> `3vl.bool.and(v2, v)`. Four new file-local helpers in ExpressionTransforms.cpp's anonymous namespace: `MatchLdLoc(inst, ILVariable*& v)` (a bare LdLoc reports its variable -- the `&`/`|` arms are bare value arms of a sub-expression value-if, the only shape the fold fires on since a block-final if has Branch arms, so a bare match is faithful), `MatchLogicOr`/`MatchLogicAnd` (port ILInstruction.MatchLogicOr/And: an IfInstruction whose TrueInst is `ldc.i4 1` / FalseInst is `ldc.i4 0`; lhs = Condition, rhs = the other arm -- the arms of a sub-expression value-if are bare, so a bare `IsLdcI4` match is faithful, matching the C# MatchLdcI4), `MatchThreeValuedLogicConditionPattern` (the C# private method, using the D93 ldloca-v MatchGetValueOrDefault + match-against-v MatchHasValueCall + the file-local MatchLogicOr/And/MatchLogicNot + GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable + IsKnownType), and `DetachFromParent(inst)` (TakeChild at inst->ChildIndex -- works uniformly whether `inst` is a direct child of the if (an arm) or a sub-expression of the if's Condition (the logic.not-peeled inner expression), since Parent + ChildIndex identify the slot in both cases) plus `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue(iff, lifted)` (the shared block-model adaptation: a clean ReplaceWith for a sub-expression value-if, or the lifted value becomes a non-terminal statement + a Branch to the next block replaces the if-final for a block-final if -- a value node cannot be a block final; the next block is resolved before any mutation). `FinishIfNullableLift` (the D94 bool? equality helper) is refactored to call `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue`, sharing the adaptation. The condition/arms are detached (DetachFromParent) BEFORE the if is destroyed (no GC; the non-owning views would dangle -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline the D58/D75/D83 lessons reinforce); for the two-nullable case the condition (the logic.or pattern) is fully captured by the operands and is discarded with the if (not detached). Gated on `settings_->LiftNullables`. KEY analysis: the `Visit(Condition)` recursion (which visits the nested logic.or/logic.and of the two-nullable condition before the outer RunIfNullableLift runs) does NOT mutate the pattern -- RunIfNullableLift only swaps LOCAL non-owning views in its logic.not peel loop (not the if's actual arms), CanonicalizeLogicAndOr does not swap (the arms are ldc.i4 1 / a logic.and, neither the `ldc.i4 0` true arm nor the `ldc.i4 1` false arm it swaps on), and VisitCompHeadRewrites keeps the `!n2.GVO`/`!n1.HV` logic.not comps (their X is a Call, not a Comp, so the negation is not pushed) -- so the outer RunIfNullableLift sees the intact pattern. KEY FINDING: the `&`/`|` on bool? codegen is a Roslyn-era pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET) -- matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D92/D94 precedent of porting a 0-firing-on-corpus transform for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the three folds (the `(bool, bool?)` `&` and `|` folds, the two-nullable `|` and `&` folds, the block-final `&` fold) and 4 negatives (non-Boolean underlying type, non-zero ctor arg, non-matching condition, mismatched arm variables) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone non-decreasing ThreeValuedBool count and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed's ThreeValuedBool cases only fire when a node is in the tree, which the current pipeline never produces on this corpus -- the `&`/`|` on bool? is ILAst-level infrastructure for the future real back end, like the lifted Comp). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining `Run(IfInstruction)` paths (AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal -- the multi-HasValue `&&` lift, needs DoLift + the BitSet nullableVars relevance analysis; MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* -- the comparison lift; NullPropagation -- the `context.Settings.NullPropagation` path, a separate 583-line transform), `Run(BinaryNumericInstruction)` (the BitAnd-as-short-circuit analysis), and the `RunStatements(Block, int)` block transform are the subsequent in-order targets. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81-D94 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also runs the `&`/`|` on bool? folds in VisitIfInstruction); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451861 lines, within the D30 non-determinism range). 9 new gtest cases (RunIfNullableLiftFoldsAndBoolBoolNullable; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsOrBoolBoolNullable; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsOrTwoNullables; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsAndTwoNullables; RunIfNullableLiftAndBoolBoolNullableRejectsNonBooleanUnderlying; RunIfNullableLiftAndBoolBoolNullableRejectsNonZeroCtorArg; RunIfNullableLiftTwoNullablesRejectsNonMatchingCondition; RunIfNullableLiftTwoNullablesRejectsMismatchedVars; RunIfNullableLiftAndBoolBoolNullableFoldsAsBlockFinal) plus the updated MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant asserting the per-method monotone non-decreasing ThreeValuedBool count (a new CountThreeValuedBool helper). 547/547 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 538); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D97 | NullableLifting Run(IfInstruction) AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal `v.HasValue ? v : fallback => v ?? fallback` early-out (the fourth NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `AnalyzeCondition`/`LiftNormal` path of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`'s `Lift` (the C# section BEFORE the D94 bool? equality folds -- the C# `Lift` order: logic.not unwrap [D94], NullPropagation [deferred], AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal [NEW], MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* [deferred], bool? equality [D94], `&`/`|` on bool? [D96]) -- the next in-order NullableLifting piece after the D96 `&`/`|` on bool? folds. Two pieces: (1) a file-local `AnalyzeCondition(condition, nullableVarS)` helper in ExpressionTransforms.cpp's anonymous namespace, porting the C# `AnalyzeCondition`: walks a condition that is a HasValue call (the D93 `MatchHasValueCall(inst, ILVariablePtr& v)` ldloca-v overload) on a Nullable<T>, or a BitAnd(I4) of such calls (`BinaryNumericOperator::BitAnd && ResultType() == I4`, recursing into Left/Right), collecting the nullable variables into `nullableVarS`; returns true if the whole tree is HasValue calls (and BitAnds of them), false otherwise. This is the gate the LiftNormal path consults: `(v1 != null && ... && vn != null) ? trueInst : falseInst` (the condition is `v1.HasValue && ... && vn.HasValue`, the BitAnd lowering of `&&`). (2) the LiftNormal `v.HasValue ? v : fallback => v ?? fallback` early-out in `RunIfNullableLift`, wired AFTER the logic.not unwrap loop and BEFORE the bool? equality folds (the C# `Lift` order): when `AnalyzeCondition(condition, nullableVarS)` succeeds and `nullableVarS.size() == 1` and `!MatchNullableCtor(trueInst, ...)` (the true arm is not a NullableCtor) and `MatchLdLoc(trueInst, vLd) && vLd == nullableVarS[0].get()` (the true arm is `ldloc` of the single nullable var), build a `NullCoalescingInstruction(Nullable, trueInst, falseInst)` (the D88 node) with `UnderlyingResultType = StackTypeOf(GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable(nullableVarS[0]->Type))` (the underlying type's StackType -- I4 for Nullable<bool>) and `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue(iff, ...)` (the D96 shared block-model adaptation: ReplaceWith for a sub-expression value-if, or the node becomes a non-terminal + a Branch final for a block-final if). The true/false arms are detached (DetachFromParent) before the if is destroyed (no GC). When AnalyzeCondition succeeds but the early-out does not fire (the DoLift / LiftCSharpUserComparison / conv.nop.lifted paths that handle the rest of LiftNormal are deferred), return false (NOT fall through to the bool? equality folds) -- matching the C# which returns `LiftNormal(...)?.WithILRange(ifInst)` = null when LiftNormal fails, so the whole `Lift` returns null and the if stays as-is. Foundation: a new raw-pointer `StackType StackTypeOf(const IType*)` overload (StackTypeOf.hpp/cpp) -- the NullableLifting lift machinery holds the underlying type of a Nullable<T> as a non-owning `const IType*` (GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable returns one); the shared_ptr overload delegates to it, and it maps the raw IType to a StackType without constructing a shared_ptr, faithful to the C# `NullableType.GetUnderlyingType(...).GetStackType()`. KEY ordering safety: the LiftNormal path runs BEFORE the bool? equality folds and the `&`/`|` on bool? folds, so a `v.HasValue ? v : (bool?)false` folds to `v ?? (bool?)false` (the LiftNormal early-out) NOT to `v.HasValue & v` (the `&`/`|` fold) -- the C# `Lift` order runs AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal first, and the `v.HasValue` condition makes AnalyzeCondition succeed (it is a HasValue call), so the LiftNormal early-out fires before the `&`/`|` section is reached. The existing D96 `&`/`|` tests use a PLAIN bool condition (ldloc b), for which AnalyzeCondition returns false (a LdLoc is not a HasValue call or a BitAnd), so they still reach the `&`/`|` section unchanged. KEY block-model point (matching the D92/D94/D96 precedent): the NullCoalescingInstruction is a value (not control flow), so the if-as-final block-final case needs the Add+SetFinal(Branch) adaptation (a value node cannot be a block final); the `v.HasValue ? v : fallback` pattern's arms are values (ldloc v / ldloc fallback), so the if-as-final after ConditionDetection (arms are Branches) never matches the pattern -- the fold fires only for a sub-expression value-if (the common case), where ReplaceWith is a clean in-place swap. The block-final case (tested by RunIfNullableLiftFoldsHasValueLdLocFallbackAsBlockFinal with a hand-built if-as-final + value arms) applies the Add+SetFinal adaptation. KEY FINDING: the `v.HasValue ? v : fallback => v ?? fallback` pattern (the `Nullable<T> ?? Nullable<T>` lowering where the true arm is `ldloc v` and the condition is `v.HasValue`) fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the CLI `--csharp` `??` count is 0, matching the D90 NullCoalescingTransform / D89 VisitCall fold which also produce `??` and fire 0 on this corpus) -- it is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern that fires on modern .NET, matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D92/D94/D96 precedent of porting a 0-firing-on-corpus transform for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the positive sub-expression-value fold + the block-final fold + 3 negatives: a 2-nullable BitAnd condition [the early-out requires nullableVars.Count == 1, and the DoLift path is deferred], a true arm that is not ldloc of the nullable var, and a NullableCtor true arm [the `!MatchNullableCtor` branch is not taken; the DoLift path that handles the NullableCtor true arm is deferred]) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants hold (the NullCoalescingInstruction count is non-decreasing -- already asserted by the D89 VisitCall fold's sweep invariant; the 1-arg get_HasValue-call count is non-increasing -- already asserted by the D94 bool? fold's sweep invariant, since the LiftNormal early-out removes the condition's HasValue call) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed renders a NullCoalescingInstruction as `??` only when one is in the tree, which the current pipeline never produces on this corpus -- the lift is ILAst-level infrastructure for the future real back end, like the lifted Comp / ThreeValuedBool). Deferred vs the C#: the `MatchIfInstructionPositiveCondition` pre-processing in LiftNormal (unwraps a `trueInst` that is itself `if (v.HasValue) X else Y` when v is in nullableVars -- a Roslyn quirk for `nullable.HasValue && (!nullable.HasValue || ...)`), the DoLift path (the recursive expression-lifting over GetValueOrDefault/Conv/BinaryNumericInstruction/Comp/BitNot that needs a BitSet nullableVars-relevance analysis), the LiftCSharpUserComparison path, and the conv.nop.lifted case (the `v.HasValue ? v.GetValueOrDefault() : fallback => conv.nop.lifted(ldloc v) ?? fallback` special case) are the remaining LiftNormal pieces; the MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* path (the comparison lift), the NullPropagation path (a separate 583-line transform), `Run(BinaryNumericInstruction)` (the BitAnd-as-short-circuit analysis), and the `RunStatements(Block, int)` block transform are the subsequent in-order targets. Wired into the CLI pipeline as the second child of StatementTransform (unchanged from D81-D96 -- the same ExpressionTransforms IStatementTransform now also runs the AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal early-out in VisitIfInstruction before the bool? equality folds); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 452123 lines, within the D30 non-determinism range). 5 new gtest cases (RunIfNullableLiftFoldsHasValueLdLocFallback -- the sub-expression-value `v ?? fallback` fold; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsHasValueLdLocFallbackAsBlockFinal -- the block-final fold; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsTwoNullableBitAndCondition -- a 2-nullable BitAnd must not fold [DoLift deferred]; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsTrueArmNotLdLocOfNullableVar; RunIfNullableLiftRejectsTrueArmNullableCtor -- a NullableCtor true arm must not fold [the DoLift path that handles it is deferred]). 552/552 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 547); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D98 | NullableLifting Run(IfInstruction) LiftNormal conv.nop.lifted case + Conv IsLifted model + MatchGetValueOrDefault match-against-v + GetSign/ToPrimitiveType/ToKnownTypeCode type-system helpers (the fifth NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `conv.nop.lifted` case of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`'s `LiftNormal` (the C# section after the D97 `v.HasValue ? v : fallback` early-out -- the C# `Lift` order: logic.not unwrap [D94], NullPropagation [deferred], AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal [D97 early-out + NEW conv.nop.lifted], MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* [deferred], bool? equality [D94], `&`/`|` on bool? [D96]) -- the next in-order NullableLifting piece after the D97 early-out. The C# `LiftNormal`'s conv.nop.lifted case: `v.HasValue ? v.GetValueOrDefault() : fallback => v ?? fallback` -- when AnalyzeCondition succeeds with a single nullable var and the true arm (exprToLift) is a `call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca v)` on that var (the NEW `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, const ILVariable* v)` match-against-v overload), build a fresh `ldloc v` (or `conv.nop.lifted(ldloc v)` when the underlying type differs from the GVO's return type -- the C# `!inputUType.Equals(utype) && utype.ToPrimitiveType() != PrimitiveType.None`; a no-op I4->I4 lifted conv for Nullable<bool> where the underlying Boolean != the I4-stacked Int32 the GVO returns; no conv for Nullable<int> where Int32 == Int32), wrap it in a `NullCoalescingInstruction(NullableWithValueFallback, lifted, falseInst)` with `UnderlyingResultType = exprToLift.ResultType` (the GVO call's ReturnType, I4). Four infrastructure pieces landed: (1) the Conv IsLifted model (`Decompiler/IL/Instructions/Conv.hpp`): a `bool IsLifted` field (default false -- every IL-reader conv.* opcode is non-lifted), a 6-arg lifted constructor `Conv(argument, StackType inputType, Sign inputSign, PrimitiveType targetType, bool checkForOverflow, bool isLifted=false)` that takes InputType explicitly (the argument's ResultType is O when lifted, not InputType -- the 4-arg non-lifted constructor derives InputType from the argument's ResultType; the two overloads are disjoint by the 2nd arg's type -- PrimitiveType vs StackType), a `ResultType()` override returning `IsLifted ? StackType::O : GetStackType(TargetType)` (a lifted conv produces a boxed Nullable<T>), an `UnderlyingResultType()` method (`GetStackType(TargetType)`, the C# ILiftableInstruction impl), and a `.lifted` dump suffix (faithful to Conv.cs WriteToCore) -- matching the D91 Comp nullable-lifting model precedent. (2) the type-system helpers in `Decompiler/TypeSystem/TypeUtils.hpp` (extending the D84 IsReferenceType): `GetSign(const IType*)` (the C# TypeUtils.GetSign -- Signed for the signed primitives + float/decimal, Unsigned for the unsigned primitives + char + bool + pointer/native-uint/function-pointer, Signed for native-int, None otherwise), `ToPrimitiveType(const IType*)` (the C# TypeUtils.ToPrimitiveType -- Unknown->Unknown, ByRef->Ref, NInt/FunctionPointer->I, NUInt->U, otherwise the KnownTypeCode's ToPrimitiveType), `ToPrimitiveType(KnownTypeCode)` (the KnownTypeCode->PrimitiveType table), and `ToKnownTypeCode(StackType, Sign)` (the C# TypeUtils.ToKnownTypeCode -- I4->Int32/UInt32, I8->Int64/UInt64, I->IntPtr/UIntPtr, F4->Single, F8->Double, O->Object, Void->Void, else None). These are the helpers the conv.nop.lifted case consults (`utype = FindType(trueInst.ResultType)` via a new file-local `FindTypeFromStackType(StackType, Sign)` helper that builds a KnownType(ToKnownTypeCode(..)); `inputUType.GetStackType()` via the D97 `StackTypeOf(const IType*)` overload; `inputUType.GetSign()` via GetSign; `utype.ToPrimitiveType()` via ToPrimitiveType). The C# SkipModifiers/GetEnumUnderlyingType are not modelled (no ModifiedType, no per-enum underlying type) -- a KnownType is used directly and an Enum KnownType falls through to None (the conv.nop.lifted case only deals with primitive underlying types). (3) `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, const ILVariable* v)` match-against-v overload in NullableLiftingTransform (the D94 MatchHasValueCall match-against-v precedent: the report-variable `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, ILVariablePtr&)` + `v2.get() == v`; disjoint by the shared_ptr/raw-pointer split, called with `v.get()`). (4) the conv.nop.lifted fold in `RunIfNullableLift` (the C# LiftNormal structure: MatchNullableCtor sets utype+exprToLift for the NullableCtor true-arm case, FindType(trueInst.ResultType)+trueInst for the non-NullableCtor case -- `isNullCoalescingWithNonNullableFallback` distinguishes the wrap kind; the D97 early-out fires first for the `ldloc v` true arm; the conv.nop.lifted case fires for the `call GVO(ldloca v)` true arm; the wrap is NullableWithValueFallback for the non-NullableCtor case, Nullable / no-wrap for the NullableCtor case gated on MatchNull(falseInst, utype); when neither fires, return false -- the DoLift/LiftCSharpUserComparison paths are deferred). The block-final case (tested by RunIfNullableLiftFoldsHasValueGetValueOrDefaultAsBlockFinal with a hand-built if-as-final + value arms) applies the Add+SetFinal adaptation. The fresh LdLoc + the optional Conv are new nodes (not detached from the if); only falseInst is detached before the if is destroyed (the precondition-before-mutation discipline). KEY FINDING: the `v.HasValue ? v.GetValueOrDefault() : fallback` pattern is a Roslyn-era `Nullable<T> ?? T` lowering that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the CLI `--csharp` `??` count is 0, matching the D89/D90/D97 precedent), matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D92/D94/D96/D97 precedent of porting a 0-firing-on-corpus transform for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the Nullable<bool> conv fold, the Nullable<int> no-conv fold, the block-final fold, and the different-variable reject) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants hold (the NullCoalescingInstruction count is non-decreasing -- already asserted by the D89 sweep invariant; the conv.nop.lifted produces one; the 1-arg get_HasValue/GetValueOrDefault counts are non-increasing -- the fold removes the condition's HasValue call and the true arm's GVO call) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus.
