| D99 | BitSet util (foundation for the next in-order NullableLifting DoLift/DoLiftBinary relevance analysis and the deferred DefiniteAssignment/Dominance/ReachingDefinitions, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports `BitSet` (`cpp/Decompiler/Util/BitSet.hpp`, header-only in `namespace ILSpy::Decompiler::Util`, faithful to `ICSharpCode.Decompiler/Util/BitSet.cs`) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order NullableLifting piece (the `MatchCompOrDecimal`/`LiftCSharp*` comparison-lift path, the first deferred item in D98's list), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model / D93 ILVariable helpers / D95 ThreeValuedBool / D98 Conv IsLifted precedent (port the self-contained util/node the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). `BitSet` is the relevance-analysis data structure `DoLift`/`DoLiftBinary` return: a `BitSet(nullableVars.Count)` whose bit i is set iff nullable var i was "relevant" to the lifted expression, and the `bits.All(0, nullableVars.Count)` gate is the "every nullable var contributed to the result" check that decides whether a lifted expression is allowed. The full faithful API is ported: a capacity constructor rounding up to whole 64-bit words (`max(1, ceil(capacity/64))` words, matching the C# `new ulong[Math.Max(1, WordIndex(capacity + 63))]` -- a 0-or-negative capacity yields a single all-clear word), the `operator[]` getter, `Any()`, `All(start, end)` (the relevance gate; true for the empty/vacuous range, matching the C# `if (startIndex >= endIndex) return true`), the set-relation predicates `SetEquals`/`IsSubsetOf`/`IsSupersetOf`/`IsProperSubsetOf`/`IsProperSupersetOf`/`Overlaps`, the mutators `UnionWith`/`IntersectWith`/`Set(i)`/`Set(start,end)`/`Clear(i)`/`Clear(start,end)`/`ClearAll`, `NextSetBit(start,end)`/`SetBits(start,end)` (the C# enumerable returned as a `std::vector<int>` -- the C++-idiomatic eager form the consumers iterate), `ReplaceWith`/`Clone`/`Capacity`/`ToString`. KEY C#-vs-C++ divergence handled: C# `ulong` shift counts are masked to 6 bits by the runtime, so `1UL << index` and `Mask << index` are well-defined for any index; in C++ a shift of >= 64 bits is undefined behaviour, so every shift is masked to the low 6 bits explicitly (`(index & 63)` / `(startIndex & kMask63)`). The C# `Mask >> -endIndex` end mask is replicated as `Mask >> ((64 - (endIndex & 63)) & 63)`, which reproduces the C# 6-bit masking (when `endIndex` is a multiple of 64 the shift collapses to 0 and the whole word is selected -- verified by the AllAtWordAlignedEnd test). `NextSetBit` uses a portable `TrailingZeroCount64(uint64_t)` helper (`_BitScanForward64` on MSVC, `__builtin_ctzll` on GCC/Clang; returns 64 for 0, faithful to the vendored `BitOperations.TrailingZeroCount(ulong)`) since `std::countr_zero` is C++20 and the project is C++17. The set-relation/mutation methods are defensive about mismatched capacities (a private `WordAt(i)` returns 0 for an out-of-range word) so they are safe and behaviour-preserving for the equal-capacity case the callers use, where the C# Debug.Asserts equal word counts. The `BitSet` is NOT wired into any pipeline transform yet (it is a foundation), so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged; it is exercised by the unit tests (no mscorlib sweep is needed -- it is a pure data structure with no ILAst/decoder dependency). 32 new gtest cases in `cpp/tests/Util/BitSet_Test.cpp` (CapacityRoundsUpToWholeWord; DefaultConstructorIsOneAllClearWord; SetAndReadBitsWithinFirstWord; SetAndReadBitsAcrossWordBoundary; ClearAll; SetRangeWithinOneWord; SetRangeAcrossWordBoundary; SetRangeWholeWords; SetEmptyRangeIsNoOp; ClearRangeWithinOneWord; ClearRangeAcrossWordBoundary; AnyIsFalseForEmptyAndTrueAfterSet; AllSingleWordRange; AllEmptyRangeIsVacuouslyTrue; AllAcrossWordBoundary; AllAtWordAlignedEnd; SetEqualsSubsetSuperset; SetRelationsAcrossWordBoundary; Overlaps; UnionWith; IntersectWith; NextSetBitSingleWord; NextSetBitAcrossWordBoundary; NextSetBitEmptyRange; SetBitsEnumeratesAscending; ReplaceWith; CloneIsIndependent; ToString; plus the four RelevanceGate tests -- RelevanceGateAllIsTrueWhenEveryVarRelevant, RelevanceGateAllIsFalseWhenAnyVarIrrelevant, RelevanceGateSingleVar, RelevanceGateFoundIndicesAny -- pinning the exact `DoLift`/`DoLiftBinary` `bits.All(0, nullableVars.Count)` and `foundIndices.Any()` contract). The test is wired into the ilspy_tests CMake build (`cpp/tests/CMakeLists.txt`); `BitSet.hpp` is header-only so no ilspy CMake source change is needed (matching the LongSet/Interval header-only precedent). 592/592 gtest cases pass (32 new; was 560); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451769 lines, within the D30 non-determinism range). The next in-order target the foundation unblocks is the `MatchCompOrDecimal`/`LiftCSharp*` comparison-lift path of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`'s `Lift` (the C# section after the D97/D98 LiftNormal pieces): the Comp-branch subset (the Decimal branch and the user-defined-operator paths need `Call.Method.IsOperator` + `IMethod` parameters + `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator`, deferred to Phase 5+), which needs `DoLift`/`DoLiftBinary` (now unblocked by BitSet) + the `CompOrDecimal` struct's `MakeLifted` Comp branch (building a C#-lifted Comp, the D91 model) + `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison`/`LiftCSharpComparison` + the `nullableVars` per-transform list (the C# field populated by `AnalyzeCondition` and the equality-comparison paths). |
| D100 | NullableLifting DoLift/DoLiftBinary/NewNullable + BitNot node + BinaryNumericInstruction IsLifted (the sixth NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `DoLift`/`DoLiftBinary`/`NewNullable` core of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`'s `LiftNormal` (the C# else-branch after the D98 conv.nop.lifted case) -- the next in-order NullableLifting piece the D99 BitSet foundation unblocked -- into the C++ `NullableLiftingTransform` (static helpers) wired into `ExpressionTransforms.VisitIfInstruction.RunIfNullableLift`'s LiftNormal else-branch. `DoLift(inst, nullableVars)` is the recursive lift: builds a NEW lifted Nullable<T> instruction from the GetValueOrDefault/Conv/BinaryNumericInstruction/Comp/BitNot shape inside `inst` WITHOUT modifying `inst` (the C# "Creates a new lifted instruction without modifying the input instruction"); returns a `(Lifted, Bits)` pair where Bits is a `BitSet(nullableVars.Count)` marking which nullableVars were relevant (bit i set iff nullableVars[i] contributed), and the caller's gate is `bits.All(0, nullableVars.Count)` (every nullable var must contribute). Five self-contained cases ported: (1) `call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca v)` -> `ldloc v` (the D93 `MatchGetValueOrDefault(inst, ILVariablePtr&)` report-variable overload; the relevance bitset marks every nullableVars[i] == v); (2) Conv -> a lifted Conv (the D98 6-arg lifted constructor `Conv(arg, conv.InputType, conv.InputSign, conv.TargetType, conv.CheckForOverflow, isLifted=true)`; gated on the MayThrow/CheckForOverflow guard -- a checked conv may throw, so it lifts only when every nullableVar is its argument, else the lift would drop the throw when a var is null); (3) BitNot -> a lifted BitNot (a NEW `BitNot` ILAst node -- UnaryInstruction, `IsLifted`/`UnderlyingResultType` fields, `ResultType()` = the argument's ResultType [O when lifted since the arg is a lifted Nullable<T>], `.lifted` dump suffix -- faithful to the generated Instructions.cs + the hand-written UnaryInstruction.cs BitNot; `BitNot(arg, isLifted, stackType)` lifted constructor); (4) BinaryNumericInstruction -> a lifted BNI via `DoLiftBinary` (the BNI gained `IsLifted` + `UnderlyingResultType()` + a `ResultType()` override returning O when lifted + a lifted constructor `BNI(left, right, op, underlyingResultType, checkForOverflow, signed, isLifted)` + a `.lifted` dump suffix -- faithful to the C# BinaryNumericInstruction ILiftableInstruction impl; the MayThrow guard is computed inline as `CheckForOverflow || Operator==Div || Operator==Rem` matching the C# `HasDirectFlag(MayThrow)` -- this port's BNI DirectFlags stays None, the MayThrow is computed inline in DoLift to avoid the ripple of adding a DirectFlags override); (5) the bool? operator! Comp -- `comp(eq, call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca v:Nullable<bool>), ldc.i4 0)` -> a ThreeValuedLogic-lifted Comp (the D91 model; the right `ldc.i4 0` is cloned via a `ClonePureExpression` helper that covers LdcI4/LdcI8/LdcF4/LdcF8/LdNull/LdStr/LdLoc -- the ClonePureLoad fast-path instruction set -- and returns null for an uncloneable pure expression, which makes DoLift bail conservative-correct). The 6th case (a Call to a user-defined operator) is deferred (needs `Call.Method.IsOperator` + `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator` + `NullableType.IsNullable`) and returns failure. `DoLiftBinary(lhs, rhs, leftExpectedType, rightExpectedType, nullableVars)` lifts both sides; when one lifts and the other is a pure non-nullable expression, the pure side is embedded (`NewNullable`) so the lifted binary has two nullable operands -- returns a `(Left, Right, Bits)` triple where Bits is `leftBits ?? rightBits` unioned with `rightBits` (the embedded side's bits are null, so the union degenerates to the lifted side's bits); the C# `bits.UnionWith` is replicated via `BitSet::UnionWith`. `NewNullable(inst, underlyingType)` is a faithful subset: a null `underlyingType` (the `SpecialType.UnknownType` sentinel -- this port has no SpecialType) returns `inst` unchanged, matching the C#; the real-type `new Nullable<T>(inst)` wrap (a newobj Call with a Nullable<T> declaring type) is deferred -- it needs constructing a Nullable<T> IType from the non-owning `underlyingType` (this port's IType has no virtual Clone), and the only caller in the wired DoLift path (DoLiftBinary for the BNI case) passes UnknownType for both operands, so the real-type path does not fire yet (the deferred `LiftCSharpComparison` path will need a faithful NewNullable). The wired LiftNormal else-branch: when `AnalyzeCondition` succeeded (the D97 gate) and neither the early-out nor the conv.nop.lifted case fired, call `DoLift(exprToLift, nullableVarS)`; when it succeeds and `bits.All(0, nullableVars.Count)`, wrap the lifted value per the `isNullCoalescingWithNonNullableFallback` / `MatchNull(falseInst, utype)` gates (matching LiftNormal: NullableWithValueFallback for the non-NullableCtor case, Nullable for the NullableCtor case when falseInst is not `default(Nullable<T>)`, or no-wrap when falseInst is `default(Nullable<T>)`), with `UnderlyingResultType = exprToLift->ResultType()` (the C# `exprToLift.ResultType`); `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue` applies the D96 block-model adaptation (ReplaceWith for a sub-expression value-if, or the node becomes a non-terminal + a Branch final for a block-final if). The `exprToLift->ResultType()` is read BEFORE the if is destroyed (DoLift builds the lifted value independently of exprToLift, so exprToLift is unchanged after DoLift); the NullCoalescing node is a value, so the if-as-final block-model adaptation applies (matching the D88/D92/D94/D96/D97/D98 precedent). KEY block-model point: the DoLift path's true arm is a value expression (a Conv/BNI/BitNot/Comp wrapping a GVO), so the if-as-final after ConditionDetection (arms are Branches) never matches the pattern -- the fold fires only for a sub-expression value-if (the common case), where ReplaceWith is a clean in-place swap; the block-final case applies the Add+SetFinal(Branch) adaptation (tested by the helper's hand-built node-invariant tests, not a wired block-final test, since the corpus never produces the shape). KEY FINDING: the DoLift path is a Roslyn-era `Nullable<T>` expression-lifting codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the CLI `--csharp` `??` count stays 0, matching the D89/D90/D92/D94/D96/D97/D98 precedent) -- ported for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the 5 DoLift cases + the relevance-gate failure + the non-Boolean reject + the 3 wired folds (a Conv true arm, a BinaryNumeric true arm with a pure-constant embedded operand, and the 2-nullable relevance-gate reject) and the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants hold (the NullCoalescing count is non-decreasing -- already asserted by the D89 sweep invariant; the DoLift path produces one when it fires, which is 0 on this corpus) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (452047 lines, 517 `lock`, 313 `using`, 0 `??` -- the DoLift is ILAst-level infrastructure for the future real back end, like the lifted Comp / ThreeValuedBool / conv.nop.lifted). Deferred vs the C#: the Call user-defined-operator case (needs `Call.Method.IsOperator` + `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator` + `NullableType.IsNullable`), the `MatchIfInstructionPositiveCondition` pre-processing (not observable -- the nested if folds first, per D98), the `LiftCSharpUserComparison` path (needs the Call-operator case + DoLift), the `MatchCompOrDecimal`/`LiftCSharp*` comparison-lift path (the Comp-branch `MakeLifted` + `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison`/`LiftCSharpComparison`, needs `CompOrDecimal.LeftExpectedType`/`RightExpectedType` + a faithful `NewNullable`), the NullPropagation path (a separate 583-line transform), `Run(BinaryNumericInstruction)` (the BitAnd-as-short-circuit analysis), and the `RunStatements(Block, int)` block transform are the subsequent in-order targets. 14 new gtest cases (11 in NullableLiftingTransform_Test -- DoLiftLiftsGetValueOrDefaultToLdLoc, DoLiftLiftsConvGetValueOrDefault, DoLiftLiftsBitNotGetValueOrDefault, DoLiftLiftsBinaryNumericWithPureOperand, DoLiftLiftsBoolNotCompToThreeValuedLogic, DoLiftReturnsFailureForUnliftableShape, DoLiftRelevanceGateFailsWhenAVarDoesNotContribute, DoLiftCompThreeValuedLogicRejectsNonBooleanUnderlying, NewNullableReturnsInstForUnknownType, BitNotLiftedNodeInvariant, BinaryNumericLiftedNodeInvariant; 3 in ExpressionTransforms_Test -- RunIfNullableLiftFoldsHasValueConvGetValueOrDefault, RunIfNullableLiftFoldsHasValueBinaryNumericGetValueOrDefault, RunIfNullableLiftDoLiftRejectsWhenAVarDoesNotContribute). 606/606 gtest cases pass (14 new; was 592); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D101 | NullableLifting MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* comparison-lift path (the seventh NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `MatchCompOrDecimal`/`LiftCSharp*` comparison-lift path of `NullableLiftingTransform.Run(IfInstruction)`'s `Lift` (the C# section after the D97/D98/D100 LiftNormal pieces and before the D94 bool? equality folds -- the C# `Lift` order: logic.not unwrap [D94], NullPropagation [deferred], AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal [D97 early-out + D98 conv.nop.lifted + D100 DoLift], MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* [NEW], bool? equality [D94], `&`/`|` on bool? [D96]) -- the next in-order NullableLifting piece after the D100 DoLift path. Three pieces: (1) `CompOrDecimal::MakeLifted` (the Comp branch, a new method on the `CompOrDecimal` struct in NullableLiftingTransform.hpp): builds a C#-lifted Comp (`Comp(newComparisonKind, ComparisonLiftingKind::CSharp, comp->InputType, comp->Unsigned, left, right)`, the D91 model) carrying the original comp's InputType/Unsigned so the lifted comparison's operands (whose ResultType is O, a boxed Nullable<T>) compare the underlying value. The Decimal/Call branch (a Call to a lifted user-defined operator, needs `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator`) is deferred -- MatchCompOrDecimal only matches the Comp branch, so a Call Instruction never reaches MakeLifted and it returns null for a non-Comp. `CompOrDecimal::LeftExpectedType`/`RightExpectedType` (the Call-branch parameter types) are not carried: for the Comp branch both are `SpecialType.UnknownType` (nullptr), which the caller passes directly to DoLiftBinary. (2) `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison(valueComp, newComparisonKind, hasValueTest)` (a new static method, Comp branch only): `hasValueTest` is the trueInst after the equality swap (the C# `Swap(ref trueInst, ref falseInst)` for Inequality is done by the caller). Peels logic.not from the hasValueTest (the C# `while (hasValueTest.MatchLogicNot)`). The hasValueComp case (comparing two nullables): `hasValueTest` is a Comp, `valueComp.IsLifted || hasValueComp.IsLifted` rejects, the effective HasValue kind (negated if the peel negated) must equal `newComparisonKind`, `MatchHasValueCall` on both operands reports `leftVar`/`rightVar`, DoLift the left with `[leftVar]` then replace `[0]` with `rightVar` and DoLift the right (the C# `nullableVars = {leftVar}; DoLift(Left); nullableVars[0] = rightVar; DoLift(Right)`), gate on `leftBits[0] && rightBits[0]` (each side's GVO is on its var -- a direct BitSet index, not `All`) + `IsPure(left.Flags) && IsPure(right.Flags)`, then `MakeLifted(newComparisonKind, left, right)`. The fall-back case (comparing nullable with non-nullable): a single `MatchHasValueCall(hasValueTest, v)` (Equality not-negated, or Inequality negated) -> `LiftCSharpComparison(valueComp, newComparisonKind, [v])`. The `LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison` fall-back (`?? LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison`, needs `Call.Method.IsOperator` + `CSharpOperators`) is deferred -- when the ported lift fails, `RunIfNullableLift` returns false (the if stays), matching the C# `return null`. (3) `LiftCSharpComparison(comp, newComparisonKind, nullableVars)` (a new static method): the `!comp.IsLifted` case calls `DoLiftBinary(comp.Left, comp.Right, nullptr, nullptr, nullableVars)` (both expected types UnknownType for the Comp branch), gates on `IsPure(left.Flags) && IsPure(right.Flags)` + `bits.All(0, nullableVars.Count)`, then `MakeLifted(newComparisonKind, left, right)`. The `comp.IsLifted` special case (legacy csc `num.GetValueOrDefault() == const && num.HasValue`, where Run(Comp) [D92] already lifted the comp): only `nullableVars.Count == 1`, one operand must be `ldloc nullableVars[0]` (the C# `comp.Left.MatchLdLoc(nullableVars[0]) || comp.Right.MatchLdLoc(nullableVars[0])`), clone both operands via `ClonePureExpression` (the ClonePureLoad set -- LdLoc/LdcI4/etc.; an uncloneable operand makes it bail conservative-correct, matching the C# `Clone()` which this port's ILInstruction has no virtual Clone for), then `MakeLifted(newComparisonKind, leftClone, rightClone)`. The Call-branch `LeftExpectedType`/`RightExpectedType` (the Decimal operator's parameter types) are deferred -- a Call never reaches LiftCSharpComparison (MatchCompOrDecimal only matches Comp). Wired into `ExpressionTransforms.VisitIfInstruction.RunIfNullableLift` (the section after the AnalyzeCondition/LiftNormal scope and before the bool? equality folds): when `MatchCompOrDecimal(condition, comp)` matches (the condition is a Comp), the equality branch (`IsEqualityOrInequality(comp.Kind)`) swaps the arms for Inequality (local `eqTrueInst`/`eqFalseInst`, matching the C# `Swap`), then `IsLdcI4(eqFalseInst, 0)` -> `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison(comp, Equality, eqTrueInst)` (return false on lift failure, matching the C# `??` exit), `IsLdcI4(eqFalseInst, 1)` -> `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison(comp, Inequality, eqTrueInst)`; the relational branch (`else if (!comp.IsLifted)`) tries the 4 shapes (`IsLdcI4(falseInst, 0) && AnalyzeCondition(trueInst)` -> `LiftCSharpComparison(comp, comp.Kind)`, `IsLdcI4(trueInst, 0) && AnalyzeCondition(falseInst)` -> `LiftCSharpComparison(comp, NegateComparison(comp.Kind))`, `IsLdcI4(falseInst, 1) && AnalyzeNegatedCondition(trueInst)` -> `MakeLogicNot(LiftCSharpComparison(comp, comp.Kind))`, `IsLdcI4(trueInst, 1) && AnalyzeNegatedCondition(falseInst)` -> `MakeLogicNot(LiftCSharpComparison(comp, NegateComparison(comp.Kind)))`); each shape's guard (`IsLdcI4 && Analyze*Condition`) must fully succeed before the lift is attempted -- if the guard's `IsLdcI4` holds but `Analyze*` fails, fall through to the next shape; if the lift returns null, return false (the C# `if (result == null) return result` exits Lift). Two new file-local helpers in ExpressionTransforms.cpp's anonymous namespace: `AnalyzeNegatedCondition` (the C# `condition.MatchLogicNot(out arg) && AnalyzeCondition(arg)`, using the file-local `MatchLogicNot`) and `MakeLogicNot` (a faithful port of `Comp.LogicNot(arg)` -- `new Comp(Equality, Sign.None, arg, new LdcI4(0))`, a NON-folding wrap; the existing `NegateCondition` helper FOLDS [unwraps `comp(eq, X, 0)` or negates the kind], which is NOT what `Comp.LogicNot` does for a lifted comp, so a separate `MakeLogicNot` is needed for the relational negated-condition shapes that wrap a lifted relational comp). The equality swap is local to the equality branch -- the relational branch uses the original (unswapped) arms. Gated on `LiftNullables` (already checked at the top of RunIfNullableLift). KEY control-flow point: when `MatchCompOrDecimal` matches (the condition is a Comp), the equality/relational branches `return` (either the lifted result via `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue`, or false on lift failure) -- they do NOT fall through to the bool? equality folds when a lift is attempted (the C# exits Lift when MatchCompOrDecimal matches and a lift is attempted; a Comp condition is not a GetValueOrDefault call, so the bool? folds wouldn't fire anyway, but `return false` on lift failure is the faithful behavior matching the C# `return null`). When the equality branch's `IsLdcI4` guard fails (falseInst is neither 0 nor 1), it falls through (the C# `else if IsGenericNewPattern` is deferred), exiting the MatchCompOrDecimal block and reaching the bool? folds. KEY block-model point (matching the D88/D92/D94/D96/D97/D98/D100 precedent): the lifted Comp + the logic.not wrapper are values (ResultType I4 for a C#-lifted comp), so `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue` applies the block-model adaptation (ReplaceWith for a sub-expression value-if, or the node becomes a non-terminal + a Branch final for a block-final if); the MatchCompOrDecimal condition is a Comp (a value, not a GetValueOrDefault call), so the if-as-final after ConditionDetection (arms are Branches) never matches the pattern -- the fold fires only for a sub-expression value-if (the common case), where ReplaceWith is a clean in-place swap. The condition/arms are NOT detached -- the lifted value is built from fresh nodes (DoLift/DoLiftBinary/MakeLifted build new instructions; the ClonePureExpression clones for the IsLifted case), so the if's condition/arms are destroyed with the if (no dangling pointers). KEY FINDING: the MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* comparison-lift path is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the CLI `--csharp` `??`/lifted-comp counts are unchanged -- 0 `??`, matching the D89/D90/D92/D94/D96/D97/D98/D100 precedent; the equality hasValueComp two-nullable case, the single-nullable fall-back, and the relational/IsLifted cases are all Roslyn-era `Nullable<T>` comparison lowerings absent from the legacy-csc corpus) -- ported for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the equality hasValueComp + fall-back folds, the relational + IsLifted folds, and the negatives (mismatched kind, non-HasValue operands, irrelevant-var relevance gate, multiple-var IsLifted reject), and the 3 wired folds (the equality two-nullable fold, the relational fold, the relational-negated logic.not wrap) verify the block-model adaptation + the `Comp.LogicNot` wrap; the 8000-method mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants hold (the C#-lifted Comp count is non-decreasing -- already asserted by the D92 RunCompNullableLift sweep invariant; the 1-arg get_HasValue/GetValueOrDefault counts are non-increasing -- already asserted by the D94 bool? fold sweep invariant, since the equality hasValueComp case removes both HasValue calls and both GVO calls, and the relational AnalyzeCondition case removes the HasValue call) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (452008 lines, 517 `lock`, 313 `using`, 0 `??` -- within the D30 non-determinism range; the MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* is ILAst-level infrastructure for the future real back end, like the lifted Comp / ThreeValuedBool / DoLift). Deferred vs the C#: the user-defined-operator fall-backs (`LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison`/`LiftCSharpUserComparison`, need `Call.Method.IsOperator` + `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator` + `NullableType.IsNullable`), the Decimal branch of MatchCompOrDecimal (needs `Call.Method.IsOperator` + `KnownTypeCode::Decimal`), `IsGenericNewPattern` (needs `MatchDefaultValue` + `Call.Method.FullName` + `TypeKind`), the NullPropagation path (a separate 583-line transform), `Run(BinaryNumericInstruction)` (the BitAnd-as-short-circuit analysis), and the `RunStatements(Block, int)` block transform are the subsequent in-order targets. 13 new gtest cases (10 in NullableLiftingTransform_Test -- MakeLiftedCompBranchBuildsCLiftedComp, MakeLiftedRejectsNonCompInstruction, LiftCSharpEqualityComparisonFoldsTwoNullables, LiftCSharpEqualityComparisonFoldsSingleNullableFallback, LiftCSharpEqualityComparisonRejectsMismatchedKind, LiftCSharpEqualityComparisonRejectsNonHasValueOperands, LiftCSharpComparisonFoldsRelational, LiftCSharpComparisonRejectsIrrelevantVar, LiftCSharpComparisonFoldsAlreadyLiftedComp, LiftCSharpComparisonAlreadyLiftedRejectsMultipleVars; 3 in ExpressionTransforms_Test -- RunIfNullableLiftFoldsCLiftedEqualityComparison, RunIfNullableLiftFoldsCLiftedRelationalComparison, RunIfNullableLiftFoldsCLiftedRelationalNegated). 619/619 gtest cases pass (13 new; was 606); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D102 | NullableLifting Run(BinaryNumericInstruction) + LiftNullableCore refactor (the eighth NullableLiftingStatementTransform piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports NullableLiftingTransform.Run(BinaryNumericInstruction) -- the VS2017.8 / Roslyn 2.9 `&&`-as-`&` optimization on bool operands, analysed as-if short-circuit -- the next in-order NullableLifting entry point after the D101 MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharp* path. The C# Run(BinaryNumericInstruction) is a thin wrapper: Lift(bni, bni.Left, bni.Right, new LdcI4(0)) (condition = bni.Left, true arm = bni.Right, false arm = a fresh LdcI4(0)) then bni.ReplaceWith(lifted). To share the Lift machinery with Run(IfInstruction) (which calls Lift via iff->Condition/TrueInst/FalseInst + ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue), the Lift body is extracted into a private member LiftNullableCore(condition, trueInst, falseInst, trueSink, falseSink) -> unique_ptr<ILInstruction> (the lifted value, or nullptr): RunIfNullableLift becomes a thin caller (empty sinks -- both arms in-tree -- then ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue on success), and a new RunBinaryNumericNullableLift is a thin caller (falseSink = a fresh LdcI4(0); a BNI is always a value, so bni->ReplaceWith on success, no block-model adaptation). KEY ownership divergence: the BNI falseInst is a fresh LdcI4(0) that is NOT a child of bni (it has no Parent), so the existing DetachFromParent(falseInst) (which TakeChilds at the view ChildIndex) would return nullptr and crash when a fold needs to consume it. A new ConsumeArm(view, sink) helper handles both cases: an in-tree arm (has a Parent -- an if arm or a BNI operand) -> DetachFromParent (TakeChild); a fresh-node arm (no Parent) -> std::move(sink). For the Run(IfInstruction) caller the sinks are empty, so ConsumeArm is behaviourally identical to DetachFromParent (the refactor is a no-op for the if case -- all 619 prior tests pass unchanged); for the Run(BinaryNumericInstruction) caller the fresh LdcI4(0) lives in falseSink and is moved out only when a fold consumes it. The logic.not unwrap loop swaps BOTH the views and the sinks (std::swap(trueSink, falseSink)) so the view<->sink correspondence is preserved across the swap. The bool? equality FinishIfNullableLift helper (which built the lifted Comp AND called ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue) is inlined into LiftNullableCore (returns the make_unique<Comp> directly) so the shared core returns the lifted value without applying the if-specific replacement; FinishIfNullableLift is removed. VisitBinaryNumericInstruction gains the C# `case BitAnd` arm: when both operands are Boolean-typed (the C# InferType == Boolean; this port has no InferType, so a conservative IsBooleanValue recognizer is used -- Comp, a Nullable<T> get_HasValue call, a Nullable<bool> GetValueOrDefault call, a BitAnd/BitOr of bool operands [recursive], a BitNot of a bool operand, a LdLoc of a Boolean-typed variable; returns false for anything else so an `int & int` BitAnd does not enter the lift), RunBinaryNumericNullableLift is invoked. The BNI lift fires the SAME LiftNullableCore paths as the if case (the full Lift machinery is shared): the MatchCompOrDecimal/LiftCSharpEqualityComparison hasValueComp case (BitAnd(comp(eq,GVO(a),GVO(b)), comp(eq,HV(a),HV(b))) ==> comp.lifted[C#](eq, ldloc a, ldloc b)), the bool? equality folds (BitAnd(GVO(v), HV(v)) on Nullable<bool> ==> comp.lifted[C#](eq, ldloc v, ldc.i4 1), i.e. `v == true`), the LiftNormal conv.nop.lifted / DoLift wraps (which consume the fresh LdcI4(0) falseInst via ConsumeArm), and the `&`/`|` on bool? path. KEY FINDING: the Run(BinaryNumericInstruction) BNI lift is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern (the `&&`-as-`&` optimization) that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the CLI `--csharp` `??`/lifted-comp counts are unchanged -- 0 `??`, matching the D89/D90/D92/D94/D96/D97/D98/D100/D101 precedent) -- ported for faithfulness: the hand-built tests verify the MatchCompOrDecimal equality BNI fold, the bool? equality BNI fold, the non-Boolean-operand reject (the gate), the LiftNullables-off no-op, and the plain-bool BitAnd stays; the 8000-method mscorlib sweep already guards the BNI lift via the existing per-method monotone invariants (the C#-lifted Comp count is non-decreasing, the 1-arg get_HasValue/GetValueOrDefault counts are non-increasing -- the BNI lift only ADDS lifted Comps and REMOVES HasValue/GVO calls, so it respects them) and the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. | D103 | NullableRewrap / NullableUnwrap ILAst nodes (foundation for the next in-order NullPropagationTransform, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the NullableRewrap / NullableUnwrap ILAst nodes (`cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/NullableInstructions.hpp`, a combined header mirroring the C# `NullableInstructions.cs` grouping and the port's `ArrayInstructions.hpp` / `MemoryInstructions.hpp` / `ThreeValuedBoolInstructions.hpp` multi-node convention) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order transform (NullPropagationTransform, the C# `v != null ? v.AccessChain : null` -> `v?.AccessChain` lowering), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model / D95 ThreeValuedBool precedent (port the nodes the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). `x?.Member` lowers to `nullable.rewrap(Member(nullable.unwrap(x)))`: NullableUnwrap is the `?.` dereference (evaluates its Argument -- a reference type, a Nullable<T>, or a generic -- and either returns the unwrapped value for a non-null input or transfers control to the nearest surrounding NullableRewrap for a null input; it carries MayUnwrapNull so the rewrap can find it), and NullableRewrap is the join point (evaluates to null when a nested unwrap took the null branch, else its Argument). NullableRewrap: UnaryInstruction base (Argument slot, inlineable), DirectFlags = ControlFlow, ResultType O for a non-void Argument (a boxed Nullable<T> on the eval stack; Void for a void Argument -- a `?.` statement whose value is discarded), and a Flags() override faithfully porting the C# ComputeFlags (strip the Argument's MayUnwrapNull + EndPointUnreachable -- the unwrap's null branch is the rewrap's own control flow, not a real branch past it -- then add ControlFlow). NullableUnwrap: UnaryInstruction base, a ResultType field (the unwrapped type, set by the constructor -- the underlying type for a Nullable<T>, the reference type itself, or Ref for the RefOutput generic case where the input reference is returned), a RefInput bool (the C# compiler sometimes passes a managed reference to the nullable to avoid copying the whole struct before the null-check), DirectFlags = MayUnwrapNull (base None | MayUnwrapNull), a Flags() override (base union | MayUnwrapNull, so the flag propagates up to the surrounding rewrap), a RefOutput() helper (ResultType == Ref), and a `nullable.unwrap.[refinput.]<ResultType>(arg)` dump (a file-local StackTypeName helper mirrors the C# stack-type names since StackType has no ToString in this port). Neither node is an IStoreInstruction (both are UnaryInstruction with no Variable), so like NullCoalescingInstruction (D88) / ThreeValuedBool (D95) they need no ComputeVariableUsage store-counting case; the default branch plus the Argument child recursion handles them. The OpCode::NullableRewrap / OpCode::NullableUnwrap enum values were already present in the port's OpCode.hpp (declared ahead of the nodes), so porting needed only the UnaryInstruction-subclass header. The ILAstToCSharp seed gained Expr cases: NullableRewrap renders as its argument (the rewrap is implicit in the `?.` surface syntax, like how box is implicit in C# -- the real back end's VisitNullableRewrap emits a UnaryOperatorExpression(NullConditionalRewrap) the output visitor elides), and NullableUnwrap renders with a trailing `?` (a placeholder until the real back end lands -- the `?.` postfix sits on the receiver of the surrounding member access, which the seed has no member-access context for). This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform constructs these nodes yet, so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed's cases only fire when a NullableRewrap/NullableUnwrap is in the tree, which the current pipeline never produces); the value is ILAst-level infrastructure for the next in-order NullPropagationTransform (a separate 583-line transform consulted first inside NullableLiftingTransform.Lift and as a per-statement child of StatementTransform, after NullableLiftingStatementTransform in the GetILTransforms() order). The foundation is exercised on the real corpus: the 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructs a `nullable.rewrap(call M(nullable.unwrap(ldloc v)))` `?.` chain for every method whose body loads at least one variable, asserting the node invariant holds, the MayUnwrapNull flag propagates from the unwrap to the rewrap's Flags (where it is stripped), ResultType is O, and the dump renders -- the volume the foundation will see once NullPropagationTransform is wired. A corpus probe (this iteration) confirmed the field-cached delegate shapes (the D77-deferred `CachedDelegateInitializationWithField` / `RoslynInStaticWithLocal` / `RoslynWithLocal` / VB shapes) do NOT appear in the .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus in the C# expected form: there are 0 if-finals whose condition compares a compiler-generated `ldsfld` against `ldnull`, and 0 `stobj(ldsflda CG field, delegateConstruction)` stores. The corpus's 67 CG `ldsflda` nodes are Roslyn display-class cached-delegate fields (`<>c::<>9__N_M`), but the actual post-pre-pipeline shape is the brtrue/else form (`stloc t(ldsfld cache); if (ldloc t) br usage else Block { stobj(ldsflda cache, newobj); ... }`) with an extra result temp, NOT the C# `WithField` brfalse form (`if (comp(ldsfld == ldnull)) { stsfld(Delegate) }`) or the C# `RoslynInStaticWithLocal` form (`stloc s(ldsfld cache); if (comp(ldloc s == ldnull)) { stloc s(stobj(ldsflda cache, newobj)) }`) -- a Roslyn variant with structural differences (the bare-`ldloc t` condition, the result captured in a separate variable, the extra delegate-construction temp) that the C# shapes do not directly match, so the field-cached delegate port is a larger, riskier slice deferred until the exact Roslyn variant is understood. The D77 probe's claim of "2 WithField occurrences" (System.Security.SecurityContext::cleanupCode, System.Threading.CompressedStack::cleanupCode) was not reproducible: those `cleanupCode` methods do not exist in this mscorlib (SecurityContext/CompressedStack have no method of that name), and the broader scan finds 0 occurrences of the C# `WithField` shape -- the D77 count was likely a broader/different pattern match. 11 new gtest cases (RewrapNodeInvariantFlagsAndDump; RewrapResultTypeIsVoidForVoidArgument; RewrapFlagsStripMayUnwrapNullAndAddControlFlow; UnwrapNodeInvariantFlagsAndDump; UnwrapRefInputFlavour; UnwrapRefOutput; ChildrenAreInTypedSlots; SeedRendersNullableRewrap; SeedRendersNullableUnwrap; SeedRendersNullArgument; MscorlibConstructFromRealLoadsSweep). 634/634 gtest cases pass (11 new; was 623); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451796 lines, 517 `lock` / 313 `using` unchanged). The next in-order target the foundation unblocks is NullPropagationTransform (the `?.` lowering): its `Run(condition, trueInst, falseInst)` entry needs these nodes (the `?.` result is a NullableRewrap around the access chain, and IntroduceUnwrap rewrites the receiver loads into NullableUnwrap nodes) plus IsValidAccessChain (call/field-access chain analysis) + IntroduceUnwrap + InferType (this port's minimal type system has no InferType, the recurring blocker) + NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType; the IsProtectedIfInst static helper (excludes logic.and/or in a condition slot from null-propagation) is a small self-contained piece that could land first. |

The two modes recognise a Nullable<T>-tested `?.` whose condition is `call get_HasValue(loadInst)` (not the ReferenceType `comp(ldloc v ==/!= null)`): NullableByValue when `loadInst` is `ldloca v` (the nullable is used by value -- the JIT passes the managed pointer to avoid copying the struct), NullableByReference when `loadInst` is `ldloc v` (the nullable is used by reference). The `Run` entry gained the `NullableLiftingTransform::MatchHasValueCall(condition, loadInst)` branch (the D68 1-arg report overload): when it matches, `MatchLdLoca(loadInst, testedVar)` -> TryNullPropagation(testedVar, trueInst, falseInst, NullableByValue); `MatchLdLoc(loadInst, testedVar)` -> TryNullPropagation(testedVar, trueInst, falseInst, NullableByReference). The Comp (ReferenceType) branch stays first and unchanged -- a Comp condition is never a HasValue call (different OpCode), so the two branches are disjoint. `IsValidAccessChain`'s end-of-chain check was generalised from the ReferenceType-only `MatchLdLocRef` to a dispatching `IsValidEndOfChain(inst, testedVar, mode)` helper: ReferenceType -> `ldloc`/`ldloca` of testedVar (MatchLdLocRef); NullableByValue -> `call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca testedVar)` (the D98 match-against-v MatchGetValueOrDefault overload); NullableByReference -> `call GetValueOrDefault(ldloc testedVar)` (the D68 1-arg report MatchGetValueOrDefault + a match-against-v LdLoc check done INLINE, because a file-local match-against-v MatchLdLoc(ILInstruction*, const ILVariable*) overload would be ambiguous with the extract MatchLdLoc(ILInstruction*, ILVariable*&) for an ILVariable* lvalue -- the D66/D94 precedent; the inline check is `arg->Op == LdLoc && arg->Variable.get() == testedVar`). The NullableUnwrap's end-of-chain reject (the unwrap's argument cannot be the end of the chain -- that would produce two `?.` in a row) was likewise generalised from the ReferenceType-only check to the dispatching `IsValidEndOfChain`. `IntroduceUnwrap` gained the NullableByValue/NullableByReference cases: the varLoad (the end of the access chain) is `call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca/ldloc testedVar)`, and the C# `new NullableUnwrap(varLoad.ResultType, new LdLoc(testedVar), refInput)` constructs a FRESH `LdLoc(testedVar)` as the unwrap's Argument (NOT the varLoad itself -- the varLoad is the GVO call, which is discarded). This port's `LdLoc` holds an `ILVariablePtr` (shared_ptr) and `ILVariable` does not inherit `enable_shared_from_this`, so there is no raw-pointer->shared_ptr conversion; the shared_ptr to testedVar is taken from the GVO call's first argument (the ldloca/ldloc), which holds the same variable (the IsValidAccessChain end-of-chain check already confirmed it). It is extracted BEFORE the `TakeChild` that detaches the varLoad, so a bail (extraction failure) leaves the tree intact (no dangling empty parent slot) -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline the D58/D75/D83 lessons reinforce. NullableByReference passes `refInput=true` (the C# `refInput: true`); NullableByValue passes the default `refInput=false`. The varLoad (the GVO call) is then detached via TakeChild and destroyed (it is not reused as the unwrap's Argument). The `ldnull` and `default(Nullable<T>)` output cases are shared with the ReferenceType mode (TryNullPropagation dispatches on nullInst, not on mode) -- the fold produces `NullableRewrap(nonNullInst)` in both output cases, identical to the D104/D105 ReferenceType fold; the only mode-specific work is the condition recognition (Run) + the end-of-chain check (IsValidEndOfChain) + the unwrap construction (IntroduceUnwrap). All helpers the two modes need were already in place from prior iterations (the D68 1-arg report MatchHasValueCall/MatchGetValueOrDefault, the D98 match-against-v MatchGetValueOrDefault) -- no new helper was needed, only the three call sites (Run / IsValidEndOfChain / IntroduceUnwrap) and the inline LdLoc match-against-v check. Added a new `NullPropagationStatementTransform` class (IStatementTransform, in NullPropagationTransform.{hpp,cpp}) mirroring the C# `NullPropagationStatementTransform` (an IStatementTransform child of StatementTransform that calls `NullPropagationTransform.RunStatements`). The C# `RunStatements(block, pos)` checks `block.Instructions[pos] is IfInstruction` -- the if is a NON-TERMINAL in the C# block model. This port's if-as-final block model makes the if the block's FinalInstruction, so the per-statement driver visits it at the last non-terminal position (pos == size-1, matching how ExpressionTransforms visits the if-final) or for an if-final-only block (pos == -1 == size-1). The `Run` method bails at any other position so the fold fires once. The void-call `?.` pattern: the if is the block's FinalInstruction with FalseInst == nullptr (no else, the C# `ifInst.FalseInst.MatchNop()`), the condition is `comp(ldloc v != null)` (Inequality only, the C# `comp.Kind == ComparisonKind.Inequality`) for ReferenceType or `call get_HasValue(ldloca/ldloc v)` for NullableByValue/NullableByReference, and the TrueInst is a Block with exactly one instruction (the void call, the C# `body.Instructions.Count == 1`). `TryNullPropForVoidCall` (a new file-local function in NullPropagationTransform.cpp): strips a NullableRewrap wrapper from the body instruction (the C# `bodyInst.MatchNullableRewrap(out arg); bodyInst = arg`), calls `IsValidAccessChain` (the D104 helper, shared for all three modes via the dispatching `IsValidEndOfChain`), calls `IntroduceUnwrap` (wraps the receiver load at the end of the access chain in a NullableUnwrap -- the D104 ReferenceType / D106 NullableByValue/Reference construction), detaches the bodyInst from its parent, builds a `NullableRewrap(bodyInst)` (Void ResultType for a void call -- the `?.` statement form), and applies the block-model adaptation: the void NullableRewrap becomes a non-terminal statement (block->Add) and a Branch to the next block (NextBlockInContainer) replaces the if-final (block->SetFinal), destroying the if and the now-empty body Block (the same adaptation NullCoalescingTransform / ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue use). KEY ownership fix: when the NullableRewrap is stripped, `bodyInst` points to the inner instruction (the Call) inside the NullableRewrap (body->Instructions[0]->Argument), NOT to body->Instructions[0] (the NullableRewrap itself). Detaching via `DetachFromParent(bodyInst)` (bodyInst->Parent->TakeChild(bodyInst->ChildIndex)) correctly detaches the inner Call from the NullableRewrap, leaving the NullableRewrap with a null Argument (destroyed harmlessly when the if-final is replaced). A naive `body->TakeChild(0)` would detach the NullableRewrap (not the Call), producing a double `NullableRewrap(NullableRewrap(Call(...)))` -- a bug caught by the `RunStatementsUnwrapsNullableRewrapFromBody` test. The fall-through target (NextBlockInContainer) is resolved BEFORE the bodyInst is detached (the precondition-before-mutation discipline: a detached bodyInst with no next block would leave the tree in a corrupt state). Wired as the 4th child of StatementTransform in the CLI pipeline (after ILInlining, ExpressionTransforms, NullCoalescingTransform -- the C# GetILTransforms() order, skipping the deferred DynamicIsEventAssignmentTransform / TransformAssignment / NullableLiftingStatementTransform). The value-position `Run` entry (D104/D105/D106, wired into ExpressionTransforms.LiftNullableCore) does NOT conflict with the void-call `RunStatements`: the value-position `Run` requires the if's arms to be values (not a Block TrueInst + null FalseInst), and `TryNullPropagation` returns nullptr when nullInst is nullptr (the void-call has no false arm) or when nonNullInst is a Block (IsValidAccessChain rejects a Block as not a valid access chain start). So ExpressionTransforms' VisitIfInstruction visits the if-final first but does not fire for the void-call shape; the NullPropagationStatementTransform then fires. KEY FINDING: the void-call `?.` is a Roslyn-era (C# 6.0) codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the legacy csc does not emit the `?.` operator; it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET), matching the D59 DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks / D60 LdLocaDupInitObj / D69 SwitchOnNullable / D90 NullCoalescingTransform / D104/D105/D106 `?.` precedent -- the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the ReferenceType void-call fold, the NullableByValue void-call fold, the NullableByReference void-call fold with refInput=true, the NullableRewrap-strip fold, and 5 negatives: else arm, multi-instruction Block, non-Block TrueInst, Equality condition, non-access-chain body) and the mscorlib sweep (updated to include the NullPropagationStatementTransform in the StatementTransform pipeline) verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (not a fold count). The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (451838 lines, 517 lock, 313 using, 0 ?? -- within the D30 non-determinism range; the seed renders a void NullableRewrap as its argument, but the current pipeline never constructs one on this corpus). Deferred vs the C#: the UnconstrainedType mode (RunStatements only), the `TransformNullPropagationOnUnconstrainedGenericExpression` pattern (a 5-instruction block sequence -- `stloc target(targetInst); stloc defaultTemporary(default); if (logic.not(comp.o(box(ldloc default) != ldnull))) Block { ... }; stloc result(leave(constrained call)); br endBlock` -- needs the if-as-final block-model adaptation for the multi-instruction shape + a corpus probe, the D73/D75 precedent), the `NullCoalescing` output case (needs InferType + NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType + IsByRefLike), the AddressOf/LdObjIfRef/Dynamic* access-chain cases, and the Call IsStatic/IsExtensionMethod/IsAccessor/IsGetter/ConstrainedTo checks (need Call method metadata) are the subsequent in-order targets. 9 new gtest cases (RunStatementsFoldsReferenceTypeVoidCall; RunStatementsFoldsNullableByValueVoidCall; RunStatementsFoldsNullableByReferenceVoidCall; RunStatementsRejectsElseArm; RunStatementsRejectsMultiInstructionBlock; RunStatementsRejectsNonBlockTrueInst; RunStatementsRejectsEqualityCondition; RunStatementsRejectsNonAccessChainBody; RunStatementsUnwrapsNullableRewrapFromBody). 673/673 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 664); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). |
| D108 | Call::IsOperator flag + MatchCompOrDecimal Decimal branch (the next in-order NullableLifting piece, Phase 4) | port | Ports the `Call::IsOperator` flag (a new bool on Call, set by the IL reader from the resolved method name) and the Decimal/Call branch of `NullableLiftingTransform::MatchCompOrDecimal` -- the piece the D91 MatchCompOrDecimal foundation explicitly deferred ("the Decimal branch needs Call.Method.IsOperator"). The C# `IMethod.IsOperator` is a SymbolKind derived from the MethodDef's SpecialName/RTSpecialName flag + the `op_` name prefix recognised by `OperatorDeclaration.GetOperatorType`; this port approximates it by the method name alone: a Call is an operator when the short name (the substring after '::') is one of the recognised `op_*` names (op_Equality, op_Addition, op_Implicit, op_CheckedAddition, op_UnsignedRightShift, ... -- the full OperatorDeclaration.GetOperatorType table). A new `IsOperatorName` helper + `ShortMethodName` helper in the IL reader's anonymous namespace set `call->IsOperator` for every call/callvirt/newobj from the resolved MethodName. The C# additionally requires the MethodDef's SpecialName flag; reading it for in-module MethodDefs (the MethodDef Flags column) and resolving it for cross-assembly MemberRefs/MethodSpecs would need the full type system for no practical gain on C#-compiled corpora (every C#-compiled operator overload carries SpecialName, so the name is the real signal) -- the name-only approximation is faithful for C#-compiled code. The MatchCompOrDecimal Decimal branch: a Call with `IsOperator`, exactly 2 arguments (the C# `call.Arguments.Count == 2`), a short name that is one of the 6 comparison operators (op_Equality/op_Inequality/op_LessThan/op_LessThanOrEqual/op_GreaterThan/op_GreaterThanOrEqual -- the C# switch), and a declaring type that is `KnownTypeCode::Decimal` (the C# final gate `call.Method.DeclaringType.IsKnownType(KnownTypeCode.Decimal)`, checked via the D93 `IsKnownType` helper). It reports `Kind` from the name switch, `Left`/`Right` = the call's two arguments, and `IsLifted = false` (the C# `!call.IsLifted`; this port's Call has no IsLifted field, but the C# `Call.IsLifted` is for lifted user-defined operators produced by CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator, which this port does not produce, so every Call is non-lifted -- faithful). A non-Decimal operator (e.g. a user op_Equality on another type) passes IsOperator + the name switch but FAILS the Decimal-declaring-type check, so MatchCompOrDecimal returns false for it -- the branch is narrow (Decimal comparison operators only). Decimal has no IL Comp instruction, so a Decimal comparison (`a == b` for Decimal) lowers to an `op_Equality` call; the MatchCompOrDecimal Decimal branch recognises these. The Decimal lift (LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison / LiftCSharpUserComparison, which build a lifted user-defined operator via CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator) is deferred -- needs the Phase 5 C# resolver (CSharpOperators + IMethod ReturnType/Parameters + NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType); the recognition here is the foundation those consumers will consult. KEY safety analysis: adding the Decimal branch is safe for the existing wired LiftCSharp* paths. A Decimal Call CompOrDecimal flows into `RunIfNullableLift`'s equality branch (when falseInst is ldc.i4 0/1) -> `LiftCSharpEqualityComparison`, which checks `hasValueTest->Op == Comp` (the trueInst); if the trueInst is a Comp, DoLift on the Call's non-nullable arguments bails -> null; the fall-back `MatchHasValueCall(hasValueTest, v)` -> `LiftCSharpComparison`, whose `!comp.IsLifted` branch runs DoLiftBinary on the Call's arguments (bail -> null). `CompOrDecimal::MakeLifted` returns null for a Call (the `Instruction->Op == OpCode::Comp` check fails). So no fold fires for a Decimal Call CompOrDecimal -- the recognition is a no-op until the resolver-backed lift lands (behavior-preserving: a Decimal Call condition with falseInst == ldc.i4 0/1 now exits the equality branch early via `return nullptr` instead of falling through to the bool? folds, but the bool? folds would not match a Call condition anyway). KEY FINDING: the recognition IS exercised on the real .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus -- System.Decimal defines the 6 comparison operators (op_Equality/op_Inequality/op_LessThan/op_LessThanOrEqual/op_GreaterThan/op_GreaterThanOrEqual) as in-module MethodDefs, so the reader marks them `IsOperator` and `MatchCompOrDecimal` recognises them (the MscorlibOperatorAndDecimalSweep asserts `operatorCalls > 0` and `decimalCompMatches > 0`); the fold is deferred (resolver), so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (no lifted Decimal comparisons appear). This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation matching the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D76 MatchDelegateConstruction + Call::IsNewObj / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model / D95 ThreeValuedBool / D98 MatchGetValueOrDefault-v / D103 NullableRewrap/Unwrap precedent: the recognition lands ahead of the resolver-backed lift, so the lift port is a separate lower-risk iteration once the resolver is in place. The existing `MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsCall` test (which built a Decimal op_Equality Call without IsOperator) was renamed `MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsCallWithoutIsOperator` (it now tests the IsOperator gate, not the deferral). 7 new gtest cases (MatchCompOrDecimalOnDecimalEqualityCall; MatchCompOrDecimalOnDecimalLessThanCall; MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsNonDecimalOperator; MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsNonComparisonOperator; MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsCallWithWrongArgCount; MscorlibOperatorAndDecimalSweep; plus the renamed MatchCompOrDecimalRejectsCallWithoutIsOperator). 679/679 gtest cases pass (6 new + 1 renamed; was 673); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451792 lines, 517 lock / 313 using, within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: the Decimal lift (LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison / LiftCSharpUserComparison, need CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator + the C# resolver), the IsGenericNewPattern fold (needs `c.Method.TypeArguments.Count` -- the MethodSpec instantiation count, a Call metadata addition + `MatchDefaultValue` + `TypeKind.TypeParameter` + IType equality), the NullCoalescing output case (needs InferType + NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType + IsByRefLike), the UnconstrainedType mode + TransformNullPropagationOnUnconstrainedGenericExpression (a 5-instruction block sequence, needs a corpus probe), the AddressOf/LdObjIfRef/Dynamic* access-chain cases, and the Call IsStatic/IsExtensionMethod/IsAccessor/IsGetter/ConstrainedTo checks are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D109 | ResolveTokenToString MethodSpec unwrap + Call::TypeArgumentsCount + IsGenericNewPattern fold (the next in-order NullableLifting piece, Phase 4) | port | Ports the three pieces the D108 deferred list grouped as the IsGenericNewPattern fold ("needs `c.Method.TypeArguments.Count` -- the MethodSpec instantiation count, a Call metadata addition + `MatchDefaultValue` + `TypeKind.TypeParameter` + IType equality"): (1) `MetadataFile::ResolveTokenToString` now unwraps a MethodSpec token (table 0x2B) to its underlying MethodDefOrRef (column 0, the same MethodDefOrRef coded-index unwrap `GetMethodSignature` / `ResolveMethodDeclaringType` already use) and resolves THAT name, so a generic-instantiation call renders its resolved method name (`System.Array.IndexOf(...)`, `System.Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal.SizeOf(...)`) instead of the raw hex token -- a general improvement to the `--csharp` output (before this, every generic call rendered as `0x2B000007(...)`). (2) `Call::TypeArgumentsCount` (a new int field, default 0) populated by the IL reader from a new `MetadataFile::GetMethodSpecTypeArgumentCount(token)`, which reads the MethodSpec row's Instantiation blob (column 1) and decodes the ECMA-335 II.23.2.15 MethodSpecSig (a 0x0A GENERICINST marker, then a compressed generic-argument count, then that many Type blobs) via a new `DecodeMethodSpecTypeArgCount` helper in SignatureDecoder (it validates the count by skipping each Type blob; a non-0x0A marker or a malformed blob returns -1 -> 0). (3) `NullableLiftingTransform::MatchDefaultValue` (the general ILInstruction.MatchDefaultValue extension -- a DefaultValue reports its Type; the general form of the D93 MatchNull which narrows to Nullable<T>) and `NullableLiftingTransform::IsGenericNewPattern` (the `(default(T) == null) ? Activator.CreateInstance<T>() : default(T)` => `Activator.CreateInstance<T>()` fold): the false arm is `default(T)`, the true arm is a Call to `System.Activator::CreateInstance` with `TypeArgumentsCount == 1`, the Type's Kind is `TypeParameter`, the comp's left is another `default(T)` of the SAME type (`IType::Equals`), and the comp's right is ldnull. Wired into `ExpressionTransforms.RunIfNullableLift`'s MatchCompOrDecimal equality branch (after the `IsLdcI4(eqFalseInst, 0/1)` LiftCSharpEqualityComparison checks, matching the C# `else if (!comp.IsLifted && IsGenericNewPattern) return trueInst`): the C# `return trueInst` returns the Activator call; this port detaches it from the if (it is the if's TrueInst arm) via `DetachFromParent` and returns it, so `RunIfNullableLift`'s `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue` applies the block-model adaptation (a Call is a value, so a clean ReplaceWith for a sub-expression value-if; the pattern's arms are values -- a Call + a DefaultValue -- so the if-as-final after ConditionDetection with Branch arms never matches, and the block-final path does not arise). The equality swap (the C# `Swap(ref trueInst, ref falseInst)` for Inequality) is local to the equality branch, so the port uses `eqTrueInst`/`eqFalseInst` (the swapped views); for Equality (no swap) these are the original arms (Activator / default) and the pattern matches; for Inequality (swap) `eqFalseInst` is the Activator (not a default), so the pattern's `MatchDefaultValue(falseInst)` rejects it -- faithful, the pattern only fires for Equality. KEY FINDING (MethodSpec blob format, settled empirically): the MethodSpecSig format is `0x0A <GenArgCount: compressed> <Type0>...` (the 0x0A is the GENERICINST marker for a method spec, distinct from the type-encoding 0x15 GENERICINST). A corpus probe (the new `MscorlibMethodSpecTypeArgumentsSweep` test) confirmed this on the real .NET Framework 4 mscorlib: scanning 8000 methods found 345 generic-instantiation calls, ALL with resolved method names (EXPECT_EQ(genericCalls, resolvedGenericNames)), zero raw-hex `0x2B` fallbacks (EXPECT_EQ(rawHexCalls, 0)), and a max type-arg count of 8; the first sample was `System.Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal::SizeOf` with count 1 (`Marshal.SizeOf<T>()`). If the format had been wrong, `genericCalls` would be 0 (the parser returns 0 for every non-0x0A blob). The IsGenericNewPattern is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern (the `new T()` constraint check `default(T) == null` + `Activator.CreateInstance<T>()`) that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (ported for faithfulness, matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D92/D94/D96/D97/D98/D100/D101/D104/D105/D106/D107 precedent); the hand-built tests verify the fold + 4 negatives (non-type-parameter default, mismatched default types, non-Activator name, non-generic Activator) and the corpus sweep verifies the foundation. 5 new gtest cases (MscorlibMethodSpecTypeArgumentsSweep; RunIfNullableLiftFoldsIsGenericNewPattern; RunIfNullableLiftIsGenericNewPatternRejectsNonTypeParameter; RunIfNullableLiftIsGenericNewPatternRejectsMismatchedDefaults; RunIfNullableLiftIsGenericNewPatternRejectsNonActivatorAndNonGeneric). 684/684 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 679); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression) and the `--csharp` output now renders generic method calls with their resolved names (e.g. `System.Array.IndexOf(...)`, `Marshal.SizeOf(...)`) instead of raw `0x2B...` tokens -- a visible improvement. Deferred vs the C#: the Decimal lift (LiftCSharpUserEqualityComparison / LiftCSharpUserComparison, need CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator + the C# resolver), the NullCoalescing output case (needs InferType + NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType + IsByRefLike), the UnconstrainedType mode + TransformNullPropagationOnUnconstrainedGenericExpression (a 5-instruction block sequence, needs a corpus probe), the AddressOf/LdObjIfRef/Dynamic* access-chain cases, and the Call IsStatic/IsExtensionMethod/IsAccessor/IsGetter/ConstrainedTo checks are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D110 | ExpressionTransforms VisitTryCatchHandler (TransformCatchVariable / TransformCatchWhen, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `VisitTryCatchHandler` subset of `ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ExpressionTransforms.cs` -- the catch-variable copy inlining that the D81 deferral list explicitly named ("VisitTryCatchHandler visit methods") and that is now the most self-contained remaining ExpressionTransforms visit method (the rest -- VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc HandleCompoundAssign, HandleSwitchExpression, VisitDynamic* -- need TransformAssignment / Span / dynamic nodes / the resolver, all deferred; the NullableLifting/NullPropagation pieces the D92-D109 iterations ported are wired into VisitComp/VisitIfInstruction/VisitCall/VisitBinaryNumericInstruction and are now largely blocked by the Phase 5 resolver). The C# `TransformCatchVariable` inlines the catch-variable copy csc emits at the start of every catch block: the runtime pushes the caught exception into the `E_<offset>` exception stack slot (the handler's `Variable`, an `ExceptionStackSlot`), and csc copies it into a local `V_0` (`stloc V_0(ldloc E)`); the body uses `V_0`. The transform promotes `V_0` to the handler's catch variable (`Kind=ExceptionLocal`, name/type/generated-name copied from the E slot) and drops the copy stloc, giving `catch V_0 : T { <body using V_0> }`. KEY enabling finding (no UsesInitialValue model needed): the C# guard `!handler.Variable.IsSingleDefinition || handler.Variable.LoadCount != 1` on the E slot requires the slot to have `StoreCount == 1` (the caught exception is its initial value, modelled via `UsesInitialValue`). This port's `ComputeVariableUsage` ALREADY counts the `TryCatchHandler` itself as a store for its `Variable` (the caught exception -- `case OpCode::TryCatchHandler: ++h->Variable->StoreCount`), so an E slot loaded once by the copy stloc is `IsSingleDefinition` with `LoadCount == 1` NATURALLY, with no `UsesInitialValue` field or DefiniteAssignment analysis (the D62-deferred `ResetUsesInitialValueFlag` dataflow) required -- the transform fires on the existing variable-usage model. (After the promotion, the new catch variable `V_0` -- now `ExceptionLocal` -- is likewise `IsSingleDefinition` after a recompute, since the `TryCatchHandler` counts as its store, faithfully matching the C# catch-variable `StoreCount == 1`.) The per-variable `LoadInstructions`/`StoreInstructions`/`AddressInstructions` lists the C# `allUses` check consults are replaced by a tree walk (`CollectCatchVariableUses` over the whole function body, gathered by walking the `Parent` chain to the `ILFunction` root -- `FunctionOf`, since the IStatementTransform `Run` has no function handle, unlike PatternMatchingTransform's whole-function `Run`); each use of the promoted local must be a descendant of the handler (`IsDescendantOf`, the UsingTransform/PatternMatchingTransform precedent). The `UnboxAny` unwrap for a type-parameter catch (`stloc V_0(unbox.any T(ldloc E))` -> the slot load unwrapped when `unboxAny.Type.Equals(handler.Variable.Type)`) is ported with a null guard -- this port's `handler.Variable.Type` stays null until catch-type resolution lands, so the type-parameter catch case bails (deferred with the catch-type resolution slice); the common case (a concrete catch type, no unbox.any) fires. `TransformCatchWhen` additionally inlines a single-Leave catch-when filter condition: the C# `entryPoint.Instructions.Count == 1 && [0].MatchLeave` becomes this port's `entryPoint->Instructions.empty() && FinalInstruction is a Leave with a Value` (the filter entry's leave is this port's `FinalInstruction`, not a non-terminal as in the C# block model -- the same divergence the D59/D73/D75 iterations documented); the filter `BlockContainer` is replaced by the leave's `Value` (detached via `TakeChild` before the old filter is destroyed, then re-parented to the handler). The "remove inlined UnboxAny" branch (when the copy was already inlined and the E slot's single load sits inside an `UnboxAny` -- `handler.Variable.LoadInstructions.Single().Parent is UnboxAny`) is deferred (needs catch-type resolution + per-variable load lists). `ILVariable` gained a `HasGeneratedName` bool (default false), set `true` for the `ExceptionStackSlot` at IL-reader creation (faithful to the C# ILReader's exception-slot name) and copied to the promoted local -- the port's `AssignVariableNames` does not consult it yet, so it is faithful-but-currently-cosmetic. Wired into `ExpressionTransforms::Visit`'s `OpCode::TryCatchHandler` dispatch (the recursive `Visit` reaches it by descending into a `TryInstruction` -- the `BlockBuilder` places the `TryInstruction` as a non-terminal in a wrapper block, so the per-statement driver visits it and recurses into the handlers); the C# order (visit the Filter + Body children first, then `TransformCatchWhen` on a single-block filter container, then `TransformCatchVariable` on the catch body) is preserved. KEY FINDING (real-corpus, unlike many recent faithfulness-only transforms): the fold fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- 28 promotions across 8000 methods (the sweep's `CountExceptionLocalHandlers` is monotone non-decreasing and `EXPECT_GT(totalCatchVarPromotions, 0)`); the rest of the ~692 catches are either single-use copies the per-statement `ILInlining` (the first StatementTransform child) already inlined (leaving the catch using the E slot directly -- already the desired form, no promotion needed), or have escaped uses / unused variables / non-Local copy targets. The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451978 lines, within the D30 non-determinism range); the promotion is internal (the promoted local inherits the E slot's `E_<offset>` name, so the `--csharp` catch output shows `catch (System.Exception E_33)` in both the promoted and the inlined cases -- the value is ILAst-level cleanliness for the future real back end, like the D84 VisitBox fold). 9 new gtest cases (TransformCatchVariablePromotesCopyLocalToCatchVariable; TransformCatchVariableRejectsNonLocalCopyTarget; TransformCatchVariableRejectsNonLdLocSlotValue; TransformCatchVariableRejectsEscapedUse; TransformCatchVariableRejectsMultiLoadSlot; TransformCatchVariableUnwrapsUnboxAnyForTypeParameterCatch; TransformCatchVariableRejectsMismatchedUnboxAnyType; TransformCatchVariableRejectsNonStLocFirstInstruction; TransformCatchWhenInlinesSingleLeaveFilter). 693/693 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 684); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the "remove inlined UnboxAny" branch (needs catch-type resolution + per-variable load lists), the type-parameter catch unwrap (needs catch-type resolution), and the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc HandleCompoundAssign, HandleSwitchExpression, VisitDynamic*, the VisitConv `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D111 | NullCoalescingTransform throw-expression (reference-types `a ?? throw ...` arm, the next in-order NullCoalescingTransform piece, Phase 4) | port | Ports the reference-types throw-expression case of `NullCoalescingTransform.TransformRefTypes` (the C# 7.0 `a ?? throw ...` form) -- the next self-contained piece of the NullCoalescingTransform after the D90 reference-types simple/temp folds, unblocked now that the Throw node carries a mutable resultType. The C# `TransformRefTypes` has three true-arm shapes: (1) `stloc s(fallback)` (the simple fold, D90); (2) the 2-instruction temp Block `{ stloc temp(fallback); stloc s(ldloc temp) }` (the temp fold, D90); (3) `context.Settings.ThrowExpressions && trueInst is Throw throwInst` -> `stloc s(if.notnull(valueInst, throw(...)))` (the throw-expression fold, this iteration). The C# mutates `throwInst.resultType = StackType.O` so the `NullCoalescingInstruction`'s `ResultType` (the FallbackInst's, the Throw) matches the reference-type value (O); this port's Throw node previously had a fixed `ResultType() = StackType::Void` override, so a new mutable `StackType resultType = StackType::Void` field (faithful to the C# `internal StackType resultType = StackType.Void` in TryInstruction.cs) was added and the `ResultType()` override now returns it -- a no-op for every ordinary `throw ...;` statement (the field defaults Void) and only the throw-expression fold sets it to O. The fold detaches the Throw's argument via `TakeChild(0)` before the if is destroyed (no GC; a raw pointer would dangle), builds a fresh `Throw` carrying the argument with `resultType = O` as the `NullCoalescingInstruction(Ref)`'s FallbackInst, and replaces the if-final with a `Branch` to the next block (the same block-model adaptation the D90 simple/temp folds use -- the if is the block's `FinalInstruction` in this port's block model, not a non-terminal at `Instructions[pos+1]` as in the C#; `BlockUnwrap` peels a single-instruction expression Block wrapping the Throw so both the bare-Throw and the Block-wrapped-Throw true arms fire). The `ThrowExpressions` setting (DecompilerSettings.ThrowExpressions, a C# 7.0 setting, default true -- false only for the C# 6 / .NET Framework 1.x compatibility profile) gates the fold; added to `ILTransformSettings`. KEY FINDING: the `a ?? throw ...` throw-expression is a Roslyn-era (C# 7.0) codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the legacy csc emits `if (s == null) throw ...` as a statement, not the `??`-with-throw lowering; the reference-type `??` itself fires 0 on the corpus per D90), so the fold is faithfulness-only on this corpus -- matching the D90 NullCoalescingTransform / D59 DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks / D60 LdLocaDupInitObj / D69 SwitchOnNullable precedent: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the bare-Throw fold, the Block-wrapped-Throw fold, the ThrowExpressions-off no-op, the non-Throw-true-arm reject) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (not a fold count). The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (the throw-expression fold fires 0 times on the corpus; the seed renders a `NullCoalescingInstruction` whose FallbackInst is a Throw as `(value ?? throw ...)` -- the seed's Throw case is a statement form, so the expression-position rendering is a placeholder the future real back end will replace, but the current pipeline never constructs one on this corpus). Deferred vs the C#: the `TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` case (needs `ILFunction.Method` metadata [IsConstructor/IsStatic] + `ILInlining.IsInConstructorInitializer` + `ILInlining.FindLoadInNext` with a movable expression -- the FindLoadInNext helper is file-local in ILInlining.cpp and not yet exposed) and the `TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes` case (needs `MatchLogicNot` + `MatchHasValueCall` wiring + `FindLoadInNext` with a movable expression -- the value-types `?.`-with-throw fold that redirects the first following use of the nullable var) are the subsequent in-order NullCoalescingTransform targets; the remaining `Run(IfInstruction)` paths (LiftCSharpUserComparison rest of LiftNormal [needs the C# resolver's CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator; the Call::IsOperator gate it consults is in place], the Decimal lift [same resolver need], NullPropagation's remaining modes [UnconstrainedType + the NullCoalescing output case + the unconstrained-generic pattern]) are the subsequent in-order targets across the broader nullable/NullPropagation family. 4 new gtest cases (ThrowExpressionFoldProducesNullCoalescing; ThrowExpressionFoldWithBlockWrappedThrow; ThrowExpressionRejectsNonThrowTrueArm; ThrowExpressionsOffKeepsThrow). 697/697 gtest cases pass (4 new; was 693); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451651 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 0 ??, within the D30 non-determinism range). |
| D112 | ILInlining.FindLoadInNext exposed as a public helper (foundation unblocking the remaining NullCoalescingTransform cases, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Exposes `ILInlining.FindLoadInNext` (and the `FindResultType` / `FindResult` types) as a public free function in `ILInlining.hpp` -- the common prerequisite the D111 deferral explicitly flagged for BOTH remaining NullCoalescingTransform cases (`TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes` needs `MatchLogicNot` + `MatchHasValueCall` wiring + `FindLoadInNext` with a movable expression; `TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` needs `ILFunction.Method` metadata + `IsInConstructorInitializer` + `FindLoadInNext` with a movable expression -- "the FindLoadInNext helper is file-local in ILInlining.cpp and not yet exposed"). The helper was previously a file-local function in `ILInlining.cpp`'s anonymous namespace, used only by `InlineOneIfPossible`; it is now at namespace scope (declared in the header) so other per-statement transforms can call it, matching the D90 `InlineOneIfPossible` exposure precedent. `FindResult::loadInst` changed from `LdLoc*` to `ILInstruction*` so it can hold either an `LdLoc` or an `LdLoca` (the C# `FindResult.LoadInst` is an `ILInstruction` the C# asserts is `LdLoc`/`LdLoca`). KEY faithful change: the C# `FindLoadInNext` returns `Found` for BOTH an `LdLoc(v)` and an `LdLoca(v)` match (modulo `SatisfiesSlotRestrictionForInlining`, which this port has no SlotInfo for); the prior C++ port returned `Stop` for an `LdLoca(v)` (a simpler way to achieve "don't inline ldloca", but NOT faithful to the C# API, and it BREAKS the value-types throw fold, which needs `Found` for the `ldloca v` that is the `GetValueOrDefault` call argument -- the C# then checks `MatchGetValueOrDefault(result.LoadInst.Parent, v)`). The `LdLoca(v)` case now returns `Found(lda)` (faithful); the CONSUMER gates whether an ldloca can actually be inlined. `InlineOneIfPossible` gained the gate: it only inlines when `r.loadInst->Op == OpCode::LdLoc`, skipping an `LdLoca` found and falling through to the dead-store check -- preserving the prior "don't inline ldloca" behavior (the deferred ldloca-into-addressof path needs an `AddressOf` node + `IsGeneratedTemporaryForAddressOf`, not yet ported). This is faithful to the C# `DoInline`, which gates an `LdLoca` found on `IsGeneratedTemporaryForAddressOf` (always false in this port). KEY safety analysis: `VariableCanBeUsedForInlining` requires `LoadCount + AddressCount == 1`, so a variable has exactly one use -- either a single `ldloc` OR a single `ldloca` (disjoint). For an ldloca-only variable, the prior `LdLoca->Stop` and the new `LdLoca->Found` + gate both reach the same net result: `InlineOneIfPossible` skips the inline (gate) and the dead-store check (`AddressCount == 1`, not 0) does not fire, so the stloc and ldloca survive -- behavior-preserving. The recursion `Continue`/`Stop` propagation is also unaffected: `FindLoadInNext` returns the FIRST found load, and with a single use there is only one, so the `Found` vs `Stop` difference at the ldloca is fully absorbed by the gate. The existing `InliningOnMscorlibReducesVariableCount` sweep (8000 methods) confirms no corpus regression -- inlining still reduces the total variable count, so the `LdLoca->Found` + gate change is behavior-preserving on real code. This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation matching the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D76 MatchDelegateConstruction / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model / D95 ThreeValuedBool / D98 MatchGetValueOrDefault-v / D103 NullableRewrap/Unwrap / D108 Call::IsOperator + MatchCompOrDecimal Decimal branch precedent: the exposed helper lands ahead of the two NullCoalescingTransform folds that consume it, so each fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration. No pipeline transform calls the exposed `FindLoadInNext` yet (the two consuming folds are the next in-order targets), so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged. 4 new gtest cases (FindLoadInNextFindsLdLocOfVariable; FindLoadInNextFindsLdLocaOfVariable -- the faithful `Found`-for-`LdLoca` change; FindLoadInNextReturnsStopForNullExpression; DoesNotInlineLdLocaOnlyVariable -- the regression guard pinning that the `LdLoca->Found` change + the `InlineOneIfPossible` `LdLoc`-gate preserve the prior "don't inline ldloca" behavior). 701/701 gtest cases pass (4 new; was 697); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451804 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 0 ??, within the D30 non-determinism range). |
| D113 | NullCoalescingTransform value-types throw-expression (`a ?? throw ...` for Nullable<T>, the next in-order NullCoalescingTransform piece D112 unblocked) | port | Ports the value-types `a ?? throw ...` throw-expression fold of `NullCoalescingTransform.TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes` (the C# 7.0 `Nullable<T>` form `stloc v(value); if (!v.HasValue) throw; use(call GetValueOrDefault(ldloca v))` => `use(if.notnull(value, throw))`), wired into `NullCoalescingTransform::Run` after `TransformRefTypes`, gated on the `ThrowExpressions` setting. The D112-exposed `ILInlining.FindLoadInNext` locates the `GetValueOrDefault(ldloca v)` inside the use, and `NullableLiftingTransform::MatchHasValueCall` / `MatchGetValueOrDefault` (the D93/D98 helpers) recognise the nullable calls. KEY BLOCK-MODEL DIVERGENCE (confirmed by a pre-pipeline probe, the D73/D75/D79 precedent of probing rather than reasoning from the C# structure): the C# `TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes` sees the post-ConditionDetection shape `stloc v(value); if (logic.not(call get_HasValue(ldloca v))) throw; use` (a sibling at pos+2), where the condition is `comp(eq, HV, 0)` (logic.not), the throw is the if's TRUE arm, and the use is a non-terminal sibling at pos+2 in the SAME block. This port's ConditionDetection INVERTS the early-exit pattern instead: the reader emits `if (comp(eq, HV(ldloca v), 0)) br THROW` (brfalse) with fall-through to the use block; ConditionDetection's `TryInlineIfFallThrough` (the true arm is a bare Branch, so the gate is skipped) inlines the use block into the if's FalseInst, then `TryInvertIfExit` negates the condition (`NegateCondition` unwraps `comp(eq, X, 0)` -> the bare `X`, so the condition becomes the BARE `call get_HasValue(ldloca v)`, not a Comp) and moves the use Block into the TrueInst, dropping the goto (FalseInst = null, fall-through to THROW). So the post-ConditionDetection shape this transform sees is: `block.Instructions = [stloc v(value)]`, `block.FinalInstruction = if (call get_HasValue(ldloca v)) { Block { use; leave } }` (FalseInst null, fall-through to the throw block), and the throw block's FinalInstruction = `throw(arg)`.
