The fold therefore matches the BARE HasValue call (not the C# logic.not -- ExpressionTransforms's VisitCompHeadRewrites `comp(x != 0) => x` would already have unwrapped a `comp(ne, HV, 0)` form to the bare HV, so only the bare call arises), the TrueInst is a Block (not a sibling), and the throw is in the NEXT block (the fall-through, not the if's true arm). The fold: verifies v has StoreCount==1/LoadCount==0/AddressCount==2 (the two ldloca v: the HasValue call's arg + the GetValueOrDefault call's arg), the stloc is the LAST non-terminal (so the if-final immediately follows, the C# pos+1 requirement), the if has no else, the TrueInst is a Block, and the next block's Final is a Throw; detaches the stloc's value and MOVES the throw out of the throw block (the throw block is left with a Leave placeholder final -- a dead but valid block, per the D58 don't-delete-unreachable-blocks convention; a null-argument Throw also passes CheckInvariant via ChildCount==0, but a Leave is cleaner); builds a fresh Throw carrying the argument with `resultType = resultType` (the underlying Nullable<T> stack type from `GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable(call->DeclaringType)` + `StackTypeOf`, NOT O -- the value-types throw-expression's result type is the underlying type so the `NullCoalescingInstruction(NullableWithValueFallback)`'s ResultType matches the value-type result, faithful to the C# `throwInst.resultType = resultType` where resultType is the Nullable's underlying stack type, distinct from the D111 reference-types case which sets O); builds `NullCoalescingInstruction(NullableWithValueFallback, value, throw)` with `UnderlyingResultType = resultType`; calls `FindLoadInNext(iff->TrueInst.get(), v, nc.get())` (which recurses into the Block's children and finds the ldloca v inside the GetValueOrDefault call); on Found + `MatchGetValueOrDefault(r.loadInst->Parent, v)`, replaces the GetValueOrDefault call with the nc (the use becomes `call Outer(nc)`), then INLINES the TrueInst Block back into the host block (detach the Block from the if via TakeChild(1), remove the stloc, move the Block's Instructions into the host block, and SetFinal the Block's Final -- the leave replaces the if; the if with its redundant HasValue condition is destroyed). This inline-the-TrueInst-Block step is the block-model compensation for the use living INSIDE the if (ConditionDetection's inversion) rather than as a sibling -- the C# just `RemoveRange(pos, 2)` (the use is a sibling, so removing the stloc+if leaves the use); this port must extract the use from the if's TrueInst Block. On failure (FindLoadInNext not Found or the load's parent not a GetValueOrDefault on v), the fold restores the stloc's value, the throw's argument, and the throw block's throw (replacing the Leave placeholder) -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline (no GC; the detached children are moved back). KEY FINDING: the value-types `a ?? throw ...` throw-expression is a Roslyn-era (C# 7.0) codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the legacy csc emits `if (!v.HasValue) throw` as a statement, not the `??`-with-throw lowering; the `??` count stays 0), so the fold is faithfulness-only on this corpus -- matching the D59 DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks / D60 LdLocaDupInitObj / D69 SwitchOnNullable / D90 NullCoalescingTransform / D111 reference-types throw-expression precedent: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (not a fold count). The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (451899 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 0 `??`, within the D30 non-determinism range; the seed renders the NullCoalescingInstruction but the current pipeline never constructs one on this corpus). 8 new gtest cases (ThrowExpressionValueTypesFoldsToNullCoalescing; ThrowExpressionValueTypesFoldsThroughPrePipeline -- exercises the full pre-pipeline + ConditionDetection inversion; ThrowExpressionValueTypesRejectsNonHasValueCondition; ThrowExpressionValueTypesRejectsNonBlockTrueInst; ThrowExpressionValueTypesRejectsElseArm; ThrowExpressionValueTypesRejectsWrongGvoVariable; ThrowExpressionValueTypesRejectsNoUse; ThrowExpressionValueTypesThrowExpressionsOff). 709/709 gtest cases pass (8 new; was 701); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the `TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` case (needs `ILFunction.Method` metadata [IsConstructor/IsStatic] + `ILInlining.IsInConstructorInitializer` -- the last remaining NullCoalescingTransform target) is the subsequent in-order target; the broader nullable family's deferred pieces (LiftCSharpUserComparison / the Decimal lift [need the C# resolver's CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator], NullPropagation's remaining modes [UnconstrainedType + the NullCoalescing output case + the unconstrained-generic pattern]) are the subsequent in-order targets across the broader nullable/NullPropagation family. |
| D114 | ILFunction.Method metadata foundation (IsConstructor/IsStatic + MethodDefKindInfo, the next in-order NullCoalescingTransform prerequisite, Phase 4) | port | Ports the `ILFunction.Method` metadata half of the D113-deferred `TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` gate as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D76 MatchDelegateConstruction + Call::IsNewObj / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model / D95 ThreeValuedBool / D98 MatchGetValueOrDefault-v / D103 NullableRewrap/Unwrap / D108 Call::IsOperator / D112 FindLoadInNext precedent: port the prerequisite the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). The C# `TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` gate `function?.Method is not { IsConstructor: true, IsStatic: false }` requires the function's method handle; this port's `ILFunction` carried no Method handle (the transforms carry no MetadataFile/IMethod), so the gate was unrepresentable. Three pieces landed: (1) a new `MethodDefKindInfo` struct (`IsConstructor`/`IsStatic`) and `MetadataFile::GetMethodDefKindInfo(token)` reading the MethodDef row's `Flags()` (winmd's `MethodAttributes`) + Name: `IsConstructor = (Flags().SpecialName() || Flags().RTSpecialName()) && name in {".ctor", ".cctor"}` (faithful to the C# `MetadataMethod`'s `SymbolKind == Constructor` gate, which checks the `SpecialName|RTSpecialName` flags plus the name), `IsStatic = Flags().Static()` (faithful to `MethodAttributes.Static`); returns a default false/false for an out-of-range or wrong-table token, never throws. (2) `ILFunction` gains `bool IsConstructor = false; bool IsStatic = false;` (default false models a null Method, matching the C# `function?.Method is not {...}` bail when Method is null), documented as the pre-resolved subset of the C# `ILFunction.Method` handle the transforms consult. (3) both IL reader entry points (`ReadStraightLineIL` + `ReadIL`) populate the flags from `GetMethodDefKindInfo(methodToken)` right after constructing the ILFunction, matching the `Call::IsNewObj` / `Call::IsOperator` / `LdFlda::IsCompilerGeneratedField` precedent of pre-resolving metadata at reader time (the port's transforms have no MetadataFile handle). The gate the fold consults is `IsConstructor && !IsStatic` (an instance constructor); a static `.cctor` has `IsConstructor == true` but `IsStatic == true` (excluded by the `!IsStatic` part), faithful to the C#. This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform consults the flags yet, so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged. 3 new gtest cases in a new `ILFunctionMethod` suite in `ILReader_Test.cpp`: `GetMethodDefKindInfoReportsConstructorAndStatic` (System.Object..ctor IsConstructor && !IsStatic; the unambiguous static `ReferenceEquals(object, object)` -- chosen over `Equals`, which has both an instance and a static overload -- IsStatic && !IsConstructor; instance `ToString()` neither; an out-of-range token and a wrong-table TypeDef token both default false/false), `ReadILPopulatesConstructorStaticFlags` (both `ReadStraightLineIL` and the branch-aware `ReadIL` flag System.Object..ctor as IsConstructor && !IsStatic; the static case is left to the sweep since many static methods have branches and bail the straight-line reader), and `MscorlibConstructorStaticFlagSweep` (cross-checks the reader-populated flags against the helper across 4000+ decoded straight-line methods -- the reader must call the helper -- and asserts all four combinations appear: instance ctor / static ctor / instance method / static method). KEY FINDING: the recognition IS exercised on the real .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus -- the sweep decodes 4000+ straight-line methods and finds instance ctors, static ctors (`.cctor`), instance methods, and static methods, all cross-checking against the helper, so the foundation sees real corpus data and the reader wiring is verified (not just the hand-built unit tests). 712/712 gtest cases pass (3 new; was 709); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining `ILInlining.IsInConstructorInitializer` half (needs `ILFunction.ChainedConstructorCallILOffset` -- the IL offset of the first chained `.ctor` `Call` (not `NewObj`) on a reference-type declaring type whose parent is a Block, which requires per-instruction `StartILOffset`/`EndILOffset` this port does not carry, plus the `inst.Ancestors.LastOrDefault(instr => instr.Parent is Block)` walk) is the subsequent in-order target; once it lands, the full `TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` fold (which also needs the D112-exposed `FindLoadInNext` + `function.RegisterVariable`) can be ported. |
| D115 | Per-instruction ILRange + ILFunction.ChainedConstructorCallILOffset/RegisterVariable + ILInlining.IsInConstructorInitializer (the foundation the D114-deferred TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard needs, Phase 4) | port | Ports the per-instruction ILRange and the constructor-initializer gate the D114 decision-log entry flagged as the next in-order target, as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D76 MatchDelegateConstruction + Call::IsNewObj / D88 NullCoalescingInstruction / D91 Comp-lifting-model / D95 ThreeValuedBool / D98 MatchGetValueOrDefault-v / D103 NullableRewrap/Unwrap / D108 Call::IsOperator / D112 FindLoadInNext / D114 ILFunction.Method precedent: port the prerequisite the next in-order transform needs ahead of the transform, so the transform port is a separate lower-risk iteration). Three pieces landed: (1) the ILAst now carries each instruction's IL byte-offset range -- a `StartILOffset`/`EndILOffset` pair (`std::int32_t`, default {0,0} = the empty range, faithful to the C# `ILInstruction.ILRange` `Interval`) plus `IsILRangeEmpty` (`Start >= End`, the C# `Interval.IsEmpty`), `SetILRange(start,end)`/`SetILRange(src)` (direct copy, the reader's per-instruction assignment), and `AddILRange(start,end)`/`AddILRange(src)` (the `CombineILRange` merge: an empty side adopts the other; disjoint ranges keep the earlier; overlapping/adjacent join into [min Start, max End)). The IL reader populates the range centrally: both decode loops (`ReadStraightLineIL` and `ReadIL`) snapshot the expression-stack / block-Instructions / block-FinalInstruction sizes before each `DecodeOne`, then a shared `TagCreatedRange` helper tags the one instruction the opcode created with its `[start, pos)` span (a final-setting opcode -> the new FinalInstruction; a statement-adding opcode -> the new back of Instructions; otherwise a value-producing opcode's Push, recorded in a new `ReaderState::lastCreated` so consume-then-push opcodes like `add`/`conv` whose net stack change is negative still tag their new stack-top result). The central placement covers every opcode uniformly rather than threading AddILRange through ~80 per-creation sites; synthetic multi-instruction opcodes (dup's two ldlocs, the stack-slot flush's stlocs) keep the empty default, which no current consumer consults. (2) `ILFunction::ChainedConstructorCallILOffset()` -- the lazy, cached offset of the first `: base(...)`/`: this(...)` call, or -1 when this is not an instance constructor or no chained call was found (faithful to the C# `ILFunction.ChainedConstructorCallILOffset`, using an explicit computed-flag cache instead of the C# `int.MinValue` sentinel). A pre-order DFS over the body descendants finds the first `Call` (not newobj, the C# `!(call is NewObj)`) whose resolved method short name is ".ctor" (the C# `call.Method.IsConstructor`, approximated by name since this port's Call carries no method handle -- the D76/D108 name-only precedent), whose `DeclaringType` is a reference type (`IsReferenceType == true`, the shared D84 helper -- System.Object resolves to `KnownType(Object)` Kind Class), and whose parent is a Block (the C# `call.Parent is Block`; a chained call is a void call the reader added as a block statement). `ILFunction::RegisterVariable(kind, type, name)` -- the C# `ILFunction.RegisterVariable` -- creates an `ILVariable`, generates an `I_<n>` name (the per-function `helperVariableCount_`) with `HasGeneratedName` for a blank name, and adds it to `Variables`; the hoisted-constructor-argument null-guard fold uses it to allocate the redirect temp. (3) `ILInlining::IsInConstructorInitializer(function, inst)` -- faithful to the C# static: returns false for a null function or an instruction whose `EndILOffset` is after the chained call starts, then walks the ancestor chain (including the node itself, the C# `Ancestors` first element) for the last ancestor whose parent is a Block (the top-level statement, via the exposed `TopLevelStatement` helper) and returns whether that statement's `EndILOffset` is at or before the chained call. KEY FINDINGS: the recognition IS exercised on the real .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus -- the `MscorlibChainedConstructorCallSweep` decodes 4000+ straight-line methods and finds instance constructors that chain a base `.ctor` call (offset >= 0), while every non-constructor reports -1; the `ReaderPopulatesILRangesOnObjectCtor` test pins System.Object..ctor's `ret`-only Leave at range [0,1); the `ReaderPopulatesMonotoneRangesAcrossMethod` test confirms the reader's per-instruction ranges are well-formed and strictly increasing across a multi-instruction straight-line body. This is a foundation only: no pipeline transform consults `IsInConstructorInitializer`/`ChainedConstructorCallILOffset`/`RegisterVariable` yet, so the CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged. 17 new gtest cases in `ILInlining_Test.cpp` (8 `ChainedConstructorCallILOffset*` covering the reference-type-found / newobj / value-type-declaring-type / non-.ctor-name / value-position / non-instance-ctor / empty-body / cached cases; 2 `RegisterVariable*`; 2 `TopLevelStatement*`; 5 `IsInConstructorInitializer*` covering guard-before / guard-after / null-function / non-ctor / empty-range) plus 3 in `ILReader_Test.cpp` (`ReaderPopulatesILRangesOnObjectCtor`, `ReaderPopulatesMonotoneRangesAcrossMethod`, `MscorlibChainedConstructorCallSweep`). 732/732 gtest cases pass (20 new; was 712); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451908 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 0 ??, within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: with all three prerequisites now in place (the D112 `FindLoadInNext`, the D114 `ILFunction.Method` IsConstructor/IsStatic, and now the `IsInConstructorInitializer` gate + `RegisterVariable`), the full `NullCoalescingTransform.TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` fold is the subsequent in-order target -- it needs only the block-model adaptation (the C# reads the guard `if (comp(ldloc param == ldnull)) throw` as a non-terminal at `block.Instructions[pos]` with the use as a sibling; this port's if-as-final + ConditionDetection shape needs a corpus probe, the D73/D75/D79 precedent) and the `IsArgumentNullGuard` match, both self-contained. The ILRange is per-instruction from the reader only -- transforms that clone/merge/rearrange instructions (CloneBody, ReplaceWith, the cache-field/binary reshuffles) do NOT yet propagate ranges via AddILRange (the foundation sets ranges at decode time and the consumers consult instructions the pre-pipeline leaves at their reader positions, like the chained call and the guard); propagating through rearranging transforms is deferred to when a consumer needs a range on a synthesized/cloned instruction (the future debug-info / sequence-point work). |
| D116 | NullCoalescingTransform.TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard (the `arg ?? throw ...` constructor-argument null-guard fold, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the full `NullCoalescingTransform.TransformHoistedConstructorArgumentNullGuard` fold (the C# 7.0 `arg ?? throw ...` form for a constructor argument that is evaluated more than once) -- the last NullCoalescingTransform target, now that all three prerequisites the D112/D114/D115 iterations landed are in place (`FindLoadInNext`, `ILFunction.Method` IsConstructor/IsStatic, `IsInConstructorInitializer` + `RegisterVariable`). The compiler hoists the null-guard `if (comp(ldloc param == ldnull)) throw(...)` in front of the chained `: base(...)`/`: this(...)` call when an argument is `arg ?? throw ...` and `arg` is evaluated more than once. The C# fold replaces the guard with `stloc temp(if.notnull(ldloc param, throw(...)))`, redirects the parameter's first following use (inside the chained call's arguments) to `temp`, and calls `InlineOneIfPossible` to move the coalescing into the call argument. KEY BLOCK-MODEL DIVERGENCE (confirmed by a pre-pipeline probe, the D73/D75/D79/D113 precedent): the C# reads the guard `if (comp(ldloc param == ldnull)) throw` as a NON-TERMINAL at `block.Instructions[pos]` with the use (the call) as a sibling at pos+1. This port's ConditionDetection INVERTS the early-exit pattern: the reader emits `if (comp(eq, ldloc param, ldnull)) br THROW` (brfalse) with fall-through to the call block; `TryInlineIfFallThrough` inlines the call block into the if's FalseInst, then `TryInvertIfExit` negates the condition (`comp(eq, ..)` -> `comp(ne, ..)` Inequality) and moves the call Block into the TrueInst (FalseInst = null, fall-through to THROW). So the post-ConditionDetection shape this transform sees is: the guard block has no non-terminal Instructions, the `FinalInstruction` is `if (comp(ne, ldloc param, ldnull)) { Block { call(..., ldloc param, ...); leave } }` (FalseInst null), and the throw is in the fall-through block (the next block's `FinalInstruction` is a `Throw`). The condition is INEQUALITY (the inverted form), the use (the call) is INSIDE the if's TrueInst Block (not a sibling), and the throw is in the fall-through block (not the if's true arm). The fold: matches the condition as `comp(Equality/Inequality, ldloc param, ldnull)` where param is a `Parameter` (the inverted Inequality form is what arises; the non-inverted Equality form is accepted for robustness but does not fire after ConditionDetection); the if has no else (FalseInst == null); only the Inequality form proceeds (the use is inside the TrueInst Block, the throw is in the fall-through); the TrueInst is a Block; `IsInConstructorInitializer(function, guard)` is true (the guard ends before the chained call starts); the throw is in the next block's `FinalInstruction`. The fold detaches the `ldloc param` from the condition `Comp`, moves the throw out of the throw block (replacing it with a `Leave` placeholder -- a dead but valid block, per the D58 don't-delete-unreachable-blocks convention), builds a fresh `Throw` carrying the argument with `resultType = O` (the throw-expression form), and builds `NullCoalescingInstruction(Ref, ldloc param, throw)`. `FindLoadInNext(TrueInst Block, paramVar, nc)` finds the first `ldloc param` inside the call (recursing into the Block's children, past the `ldloc this` first argument via `MayReorder`); the load must be an `LdLoc` (not `LdLoca`, the C# `result.LoadInst is not LdLoc` gate). On Found, the fold allocates a temp via `function->RegisterVariable(StackSlot, param.Type)`, redirects the first use (`firstUse->Variable = temp`), builds `stloc temp(nc)` with the guard's ILRange, adds it as the first non-terminal, then INLINES the TrueInst Block back into the host block (detach the Block via `TakeChild(1)`, move its Instructions after the stloc, `SetFinal` the Block's `FinalInstruction` -- the leave replaces the if-final, destroying the if with its redundant condition). `ComputeVariableUsage(*function)` is called before `InlineOneIfPossible` so the fresh temp's counts are fresh (StoreCount=1, LoadCount=1); without this, `InlineOneIfPossible`'s dead-store path would fire (stale LoadCount==0) and replace the stloc with the nc as a standalone instruction, leaving the call's `ldloc temp` dangling -- the C# avoids this via incremental variable-usage tracking (`AddStoreInstruction` / the load's `Variable` reassignment), which this port's `ComputeVariableUsage` tree walk is the equivalent of. `InlineOneIfPossible` then inlines the stloc into the call (replacing `ldloc temp` with the nc, removing the stloc), giving `call Base..ctor(..., if.notnull(ldloc param, throw), ...)`. On failure (FindLoadInNext not Found, or the load is an LdLoca), the fold restores the `ldloc param` into the condition and the throw in the throw block (replacing the Leave placeholder) -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline (no GC; the detached children are moved back). Wired into `NullCoalescingTransform::Run` after `TransformRefTypes` and before `TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes` (the C# order). KEY FINDING: the `arg ?? throw ...` constructor-argument null-guard is a Roslyn-era (C# 7.0) codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the legacy csc does not emit the `??`-with-throw lowering for constructor arguments), matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D111/D113 precedent -- the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the through-pre-pipeline fold, the direct post-ConditionDetection fold, and 9 negatives: non-constructor, static ctor, non-Parameter, ThrowExpressions-off, not-in-initializer, Equality condition, non-Block TrueInst, else arm, no throw in next block) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. The CLI `--csharp` output is unchanged (517 lock / 313 using / 0 `??`, no crash, no regression). 11 new gtest cases (HoistedCtorArgNullGuardFoldsThroughPrePipeline; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardFoldsDirectPostShape; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsNonConstructor; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsStaticCtor; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsNonParameter; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsThrowExpressionsOff; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsNotInInitializer; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsEqualityCondition; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsNonBlockTrueInst; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsElseArm; HoistedCtorArgNullGuardRejectsNoThrowInNextBlock). 743/743 gtest cases pass (11 new; was 732); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the broader nullable family's deferred pieces (LiftCSharpUserComparison / the Decimal lift [need the C# resolver's CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator], NullPropagation's remaining modes [UnconstrainedType + the NullCoalescing output case + the unconstrained-generic pattern]) are the subsequent in-order targets across the broader nullable/NullPropagation family; the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc HandleCompoundAssign, HandleSwitchExpression, VisitDynamic*, the VisitConv `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D117 | NullableLiftingStatementTransform (RunStatements, the block-tail nullable expression lift, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports `NullableLiftingStatementTransform.RunStatements` -- the next per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform after NullCoalescingTransform (D77/D90/D111/D113/D116) and before NullPropagationStatementTransform (D55), filling the gap the D55 iteration skipped (it wired NullPropagationStatementTransform position 7 before NullableLiftingStatementTransform position 6). The C# `RunStatements` lifts the block-tail nullable expression `if (!condition) { leave(default(Nullable<T>)) }; leave(newobj Nullable<T>(expr))` into a single leave carrying the lifted value, by calling the shared `Lift` (the C# `Lift(ifInst, ifInst.Condition, thenLeave.Value, elseLeave.Value)`) on the two leaves' values. KEY refactor: the shared `Lift` core (`ExpressionTransforms::LiftNullableCore`, extracted in D50 as a private non-static member of ExpressionTransforms using the `settings_` instance member) is refactored to a PUBLIC STATIC method taking `const ILTransformSettings* settings` as the first parameter, so `NullableLiftingStatementTransform` (in NullableLiftingTransform.cpp, a different translation unit) can call the shared Lift without duplicating it. The two existing ExpressionTransforms callers (`RunIfNullableLift` / `RunBinaryNumericNullableLift`) now pass `settings_`; the file-local helpers (`ConsumeArm` / `MatchLogicNot` / `AnalyzeCondition` / `ReplaceIfWithLiftedValue` / etc.) stay in ExpressionTransforms.cpp's anonymous namespace and are callable from the now-static `LiftNullableCore` (a static member can call file-local free functions in the same TU). KEY BLOCK-MODEL DIVERGENCE (confirmed by a pre-pipeline probe, the D73/D75/D79/D113 precedent of probing rather than reasoning from the C# structure): the C# `RunStatements` reads the if as a NON-TERMINAL at `block.Instructions[Count-2]` with the else-leave as the block's last instruction (`Instructions[Count-1]`), the if's TrueInst is `Block { leave(default) }`, and the fall-through is `leave(newobj)`. This port's ConditionDetection INVERTS the early-exit pattern: the reader emits `if (comp(eq, HV(ldloca v), 0)) br L_then` (brfalse) with fall-through to the newobj-leave; `TryInlineIfFallThrough` inlines the newobj-leave block into the if's FalseInst, then `TryInvertIfExit` negates the condition (`comp(eq, ..)` -> the BARE `call get_HasValue(ldloca v)`, via `NegateCondition` unwrapping `comp(eq, X, 0)`) and moves the newobj-leave Block into the TrueInst (FalseInst = null, fall-through to the default-leave). So the post-ConditionDetection shape this transform sees is: block0's `FinalInstruction` is `if (HV(ldloca v)) Block { leave(newobj) }` (FalseInst null), and the default-leave is the NEXT block's `FinalInstruction`. The condition is the BARE HasValue call (not logic.not -- `ExpressionTransforms`' `VisitCompHeadRewrites` `comp(x != 0) => x` would already have unwrapped a `comp(ne, HV, 0)` form), the newobj-leave is INSIDE the if's TrueInst Block, and the default-leave is in the fall-through (next block). The fold: matches the if as block0's FinalInstruction (pos == Instructions.size()-1, matching the D55 NullPropagationStatementTransform pattern), the if has no else (FalseInst == null), the TrueInst carries the then-leave (via a `GetLeaveFromArm` helper handling a bare Leave and a Block-wrapped Leave as FinalInstruction -- the probe showed the TrueInst is a Block whose FinalInstruction is the leave, Instructions empty, so `BlockUnwrap` does not apply), the next block (`NextBlockInContainer`) has a Leave FinalInstruction (the else-leave), and both leaves target the same container. `LiftNullableCore(settings, iff, iff->Condition, thenLeave->Value, elseLeave->Value, emptySinks, emptySinks)` is called; on success (the LiftNormal NullableCtor + GVO + MatchNull case: `v.HasValue ? newobj Nullable<T>(v.GetValueOrDefault()) : default(Nullable<T>)` lifts to `v` -- a fresh LdLoc(v), no wrap, the MatchNull case returns `std::move(lifted)` WITHOUT ConsumeArm so the arms stay in the leaves), the fold sets `thenLeave->SetChild(0, std::move(lifted))` (SetChild, not a direct `Value =` assignment -- the D110 direct-assignment-bypasses-reparenting lesson: a direct `Value = make_unique<LdLoc>(...)` leaves the new child's Parent null, failing CheckInvariant), detaches the then-leave from its parent (a bare Leave -> `iff->TakeChild(1)`; a Block-wrapped Leave -> `std::move(innerBlock->FinalInstruction)`, leaving the innerBlock's FinalInstruction null -- the innerBlock is the if's TrueInst, destroyed when the if-final is replaced), and replaces block0's FinalInstruction (the if) with the then-leave via `block.SetFinal(std::move(thenLeaveOwned))` (destroying the if and the now-empty TrueInst Block). The next block (else-leave) becomes unreachable (the if-final was replaced with the then-leave, which leaves the container -- the next block is no longer the fall-through); per the D58 convention it is left in the tree (unreachable, harmless) rather than erased during the StatementTransform Walk's live tree traversal. KEY FINDING: the block-tail nullable expression lift is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the legacy csc does not emit the `v.HasValue ? new Nullable<T>(v.GetValueOrDefault()) : default(Nullable<T>)` block-tail lift), matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D111/D113/D116 precedent -- the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the direct post-ConditionDetection fold, the through-pre-pipeline fold, and 3 negatives: else arm, wrong GVO variable, different-container leaves) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (the existing NullCoalescingTransform / NullPropagationTransform sweeps, now with NullableLiftingStatementTransform in the pipeline). 5 new gtest cases (RunStatementsFoldsBlockTail; RunStatementsRejectsElseArm; RunStatementsRejectsWrongGvoVariable; RunStatementsRejectsDifferentContainer; RunStatementsFoldsThroughPrePipeline). 748/748 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 743); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451681 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 0 `??`). The `ExpressionTransforms::LiftNullableCore` refactor to public static is behavior-preserving (the two existing callers pass `settings_`; the `LiftNullableCore` body replaces `settings_` with `settings`; all 743 prior tests pass unchanged). Deferred vs the C#: the broader nullable family's deferred pieces (LiftCSharpUserComparison / the Decimal lift [need the C# resolver's CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator], NullPropagation's remaining modes [UnconstrainedType + the NullCoalescing output case + the unconstrained-generic pattern]) and the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc HandleCompoundAssign, HandleSwitchExpression, VisitDynamic*, the VisitConv `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D118 | NullPropagationTransform NullCoalescing output case (`v?.AccessChain ?? fallback`, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `NullCoalescing` output case of `NullPropagationTransform.TryNullPropagation` (the last remaining `Run` output case after the D104 ReferenceType / D105 default(Nullable<T>) / D106 NullableByValue/NullableByReference modes) -- `testedVar != null ? testedVar.AccessChain : nullInst` where the access chain returns a non-nullable value type (and is not a by-ref-like type that cannot be wrapped in Nullable<T>) folds into `testedVar?.AccessChain ?? nullInst` (a `NullCoalescingInstruction(NullableWithValueFallback)` wrapping a `NullableRewrap(accessChain)` with `UnderlyingResultType = nullInst->ResultType()`). The C# gate is `!removedRewrapOrNullableCtor && NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType(returnType) && !returnType.IsByRefLike` where `returnType = nonNullInst.InferType(context.TypeSystem)`. This port's transforms have no MetadataFile handle (D78) and no InferType, so the three pieces the gate needs landed: (1) `Call::ReturnIType` (a new `TypeSystem::ITypePtr` field on Call, populated by the IL reader from `callSig->ReturnType` -- the same signature the existing `Call::ReturnType = StackTypeOf(callSig->ReturnType)` derives from; for `newobj` the declared void return is stored, but newobj is excluded by `!IsNewObj` in `IsValidAccessChain`; carried so transforms that need the full return IType can consult it without a MetadataFile handle, matching the `Call::DeclaringType` / `LdFlda::FieldToken` precedent). (2) `NullableLiftingTransform::IsNonNullableValueType(const IType*)` -- a faithful port of `NullableType.IsNonNullableValueType` (`type.IsReferenceType == false && !IsNullable(type)`): uses the shared D84 `IsReferenceType` tri-state helper (== false means the optional has a value and it is false -- a Struct/Enum/Void/NInt/NUInt/FunctionPointer, NOT a reference type and NOT the indeterminate TypeParameter/ByReference/Pointer/Unknown) and `GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable` for the not-Nullable<T> check (returns non-null for a Nullable<T>). (3) `NullableLiftingTransform::IsByRefLike(const IType*)` -- a permissive port of `IType.IsByRefLike`: a `ByReferenceType` (Kind == ByReference) -> true; a `ParameterizedType` (e.g. `Span<T>`) unwraps to its generic definition and checks `KnownTypeCode::SpanOfT` / `ReadOnlySpanOfT`; a bare `KnownType` checks the same codes; everything else -> false (permissive -- a user-defined ref struct is not recognised without the `[IsByRefLike]` attribute this port does not resolve, matching the D52/D72/D73 permissive-type-recognition precedent). (4) a file-local `InferAccessChainType` helper -- a minimal `ILInstruction.InferType(typeSystem)` for the access chain root: a `Call` -> `Call::ReturnIType.get()` (the method's return type IType); a `LdObj` (LdFld = `LdObj(LdFlda(..), type)`) -> the `LdObj`'s `Type`; otherwise nullptr (conservative -- the NullCoalescing output case does not fire). The C# InferType uses the resolver and covers more node kinds; this minimal port covers the access-chain roots the `?.` lowering produces (a Call or a field load). The fold: captures `nullInst->ResultType()` into a local BEFORE detaching (the C# `UnderlyingResultType = nullInst.ResultType`, the precondition-before-mutation discipline), calls `IntroduceUnwrap` (wraps the receiver load in a `NullableUnwrap`), detaches `nonNullInst` and `nullInst` via `DetachFromParent`, and builds `NullCoalescingInstruction(NullableWithValueFallback, NullableRewrap(nonNullInst), nullInst)` with the captured `UnderlyingResultType`. The `!removedRewrapOrNullableCtor` guard prevents the case when a `NullableRewrap` / `NullableCtor` was stripped from the non-null arm (the C# would double-wrap). KEY FINDING (real-corpus, unlike most recent NullableLifting/NullPropagation pieces): the `NullCoalescing` output case fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- 78 `??` folds across the full 25315-method corpus (e.g. `obj?.GetHashCode() ?? type.GetHashCode()`, `zone?.get_SecurityZone() ?? 0`, `d1?.Equals(d2) ?? (d2 == null)`, `culture?.get_LCID() ?? 1033`). The legacy csc emits `v != null ? v.M() : fallback` (where `v.M()` returns a value type) as a plain if/else / ternary, which the `NullPropagation::Run` (called from `ExpressionTransforms::LiftNullableCore` at the start of the Lift, before the LiftNullables-gated paths) detects and folds to `v?.M() ?? fallback`. This corrects the D104 deferral note which implied the `?.` family fires 0 times on the corpus -- that was measuring the ldnull output case (the `v?.M()` form, which IS 0 on the legacy csc); the NullCoalescing output case (the `v?.M() ?? fallback` form, where the fallback is a non-null value and the chain returns a value type) is a DIFFERENT fold that fires on plain-ternary patterns the legacy csc DOES emit. The CLI `--csharp` output now shows 78 `??` lines (was 0), a real readability improvement (the `?.`/`??` form is more compact than the if/else); the line count drops ~190 lines (the folds replace multi-line if/else with single-line `??`), within the D30 non-determinism range. 8 new gtest cases (RunFoldsInequalityNullCheckToNullCoalescing; RunFoldsFieldAccessChainToNullCoalescing; RunRejectsReferenceTypeReturnForNullCoalescing; RunRejectsByRefLikeReturnForNullCoalescing; RunRejectsStrippedRewrapForNullCoalescing; NullableLiftingTransform.IsNonNullableValueTypeChecksValueStructNotNullable; NullableLiftingTransform.IsByRefLikeChecksRefStructsAndByRef; ILReader.MscorlibCallReturnITypeSweep -- cross-checks the reader populates `Call::ReturnIType` for real call sites across 4000+ straight-line methods). 756/756 gtest cases pass (8 new; was 748); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- ~451188 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??`, the `??` count deterministic across runs, the line count within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining NullPropagation pieces are the UnconstrainedType mode + the `TransformNullPropagationOnUnconstrainedGenericExpression` 5-instruction block sequence (needs a block-model corpus probe, the D73/D75/D79 precedent) and the AddressOf / LdObjIfRef / Dynamic* access-chain cases; the broader nullable family's deferred pieces (LiftCSharpUserComparison / the Decimal lift [need the C# resolver's CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator]) and the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (VisitCall/VisitNewObj/VisitLdObj/VisitStObj/VisitStLoc HandleCompoundAssign, HandleSwitchExpression, VisitDynamic*, the VisitConv `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D119 | LdcDecimal ILAst node + ExpressionTransforms.VisitLdObj TransformDecimalFieldToConstant (the `ldsfld Decimal.One/Zero/MinusOne` -> `1m`/`0m`/`-1m` fold, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `LdcDecimal` ILAst node (the System.Decimal constant) and the `TransformDecimalFieldToConstant` subset of `ExpressionTransforms.VisitLdObj` (the next in-order ExpressionTransforms visit method after the D81 VisitComp/D84 VisitBox/D85 VisitConv/D86 conv.r.un/D87 shift-size/D89 VisitCall-fold/D91-D101 NullableLifting/D104-D118 NullPropagation pieces). The C# `LdcDecimal` carries a `decimal Value`; this port has no native decimal type, so a faithful `DecimalValue` struct models System.Decimal's internal layout -- a 96-bit unsigned mantissa (lo/mid/hi), a sign (`isNegative`), and a scale (0..28, the count of digits right of the point) -- exactly the components the 5-arg `new decimal(lo, mid, hi, isNegative, scale)` constructor exposes, so the value round-trips. Factory helpers `FromInt32`/`FromUInt32`/`FromInt64`/`FromUInt64`/`FromBits` build the value from the single-arg constructors (handling the INT32_MIN/INT64_MIN magnitude edges via int64 negation) and `One`/`Zero`/`MinusOne` produce the named-constant fields. `ToString` formats the 96-bit mantissa via repeated divmod-by-1e9 (the largest power of ten whose remainder fits in 32 bits), inserts the decimal point `scale` digits from the right (padding with leading zeros when the mantissa has fewer digits than the scale), preserves trailing zeros (System.Decimal.ToString semantics), and applies the sign (zero is always unsigned); the largest 96-bit decimal (2^96 - 1 = 79228162514264337593543950335) round-trips. The node is a `SimpleInstruction` leaf (no children, DirectFlags None, ResultType O, faithful to the C# `override StackType ResultType => StackType.O`), with an `ldc.decimal(<value>)` ILAst dump. The ILAstToCSharp seed renders the C# decimal literal form with the trailing `m` suffix (`1m`/`0m`/`-1m`/`1.5m`), faithful to the real back end's `VisitLdcDecimal` -> `ConvertConstantValue` -> a `PrimitiveExpression` with the `m` suffix. `TransformDecimalFieldToConstant` (wired into `ExpressionTransforms::VisitLdObj`, dispatched from the `Visit` `OpCode::LdObj` case): a static field load `ldobj(ldsflda System.Decimal::One/Zero/MinusOne)` folds into the corresponding `LdcDecimal` constant. The C# checks `inst.MatchLdsFld(out var field) && field.DeclaringType.IsKnownType(Decimal)` then switches on `field.Name`; this port's reader models `ldsfld F` as `ldobj(ldsflda F)` where `LdsFlda` carries the resolved `FieldName` ("Namespace.Type::Field", per `ResolveTokenToString`), so the exact name match `System.Decimal::One`/`Zero`/`MinusOne` carries both the declaring type (System.Decimal) and the field name -- the faithful equivalent of the two-part C# gate, and robust (a user type with a field named `One` has a different namespace prefix and does not match). The remaining `VisitLdObj` pieces (`AddressOfLdLocToLdLoca` -- needs an `AddressOf` node this port does not model; `LdObjToLdLoc` already ran in `EarlyExpressionTransforms` D81) are deferred. KEY FINDING (real-corpus, unlike most recent nullable-family faithfulness-only pieces): the fold fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- the `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` asserts `totalDecimalFieldFolds > 0` and the CLI `--csharp` output now shows 12 `m`-suffixed decimal literals (`0m`/`1m`/`-1m`, e.g. `System.Decimal.op_Equality(d1, 0m)`, `System.Decimal.op_LessThan(value, 0m)`, `op_GreaterThan(value, -1m)`) with 0 residual `Decimal::One/Zero/MinusOne` field references (was 0 literals before -- the field loads rendered as `*(ldsflda ...)` placeholders). The per-method monotone invariants (LdcDecimal count non-decreasing, Decimal-constant-field-load count non-increasing) are the deterministic correctness gate. 15 new gtest cases (10 in a new `LdcDecimal_Test.cpp` covering the node invariant/flags/ResultType/dump, the `DecimalValue` factory helpers + INT32_MIN/INT64_MIN/UInt64 edges, the `ToString` integer/scaled/sign/zero formatting, the full 96-bit mantissa, equality, the seed `m`-suffix rendering, and a mscorlib sweep constructing LdcDecimals over real Decimal static-field loads; 5 in `ExpressionTransforms_Test` covering the One/Zero/MinusOne folds + the non-Decimal-field / non-constant-Decimal-field / instance-field-load rejects + the seed `d = 1m` rendering, plus the updated `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` with the LdcDecimal/Decimal-field-load per-method monotone invariants and the `totalDecimalFieldFolds > 0` assertion). 771/771 gtest cases pass (15 new; was 756); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451333 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` / 12 `m`-decimal-literals, within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: the sibling `EarlyExpressionTransforms.TransformDecimalCtorToConstant` (`newobj Decimal(int/long/ulong/5-arg)` -> `LdcDecimal`, which the `LdcDecimal` node + the `FromInt32`/`FromInt64`/`FromBits` factories now unblock) and the Decimal lift (`LiftCSharpUserComparison`'s Decimal branch [needs the C# resolver's `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator`]) are the subsequent in-order targets; the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (`VisitNewObj`/`VisitLdObjIfRef`/`VisitStObj`/`VisitStLoc` `HandleCompoundAssign`, `HandleSwitchExpression`, `VisitDynamic*`, the `VisitConv` `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) and the remaining `NullPropagation` pieces (`UnconstrainedType` mode + the unconstrained-generic 5-instruction block sequence, the `AddressOf`/`LdObjIfRef`/`Dynamic*` access-chain cases) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D120 | EarlyExpressionTransforms.TransformDecimalCtorToConstant + Call::ParameterIType (the `newobj Decimal(...)` -> `LdcDecimal` fold, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the sibling `EarlyExpressionTransforms.TransformDecimalCtorToConstant` (the next in-order Decimal piece the D119 `LdcDecimal` node + the `FromInt32`/`FromUInt32`/`FromInt64`/`FromUInt64`/`FromBits` factories unblocked), wired into `EarlyExpressionTransforms::Run` (the whole-function transform; the C# lives in `EarlyExpressionTransforms.VisitNewObj`, and this port's `EarlyExpressionTransforms::Run` is the whole-function equivalent -- the NewObj fold is a fourth collect-then-transform pass alongside the StObj/LdObj/Comp passes). `newobj` is modelled as a `Call` with `IsNewObj` (D76); a `newobj Decimal(...)` Call whose `DeclaringType` resolves (via a file-local `KnownTypeCodeOf` dynamic_cast) to `KnownType(Decimal)` folds into the corresponding `LdcDecimal` constant via `ReplaceWith` (a clean value-position swap -- a newobj Call is a value, never a block final, so no if-as-final block-model adaptation is needed, unlike HandleConditionalOperator/FoldMatchTrueFalse). KEY prerequisite: the 1-arg case dispatches on the first parameter's `KnownTypeCode` (Int32/UInt32/Int64/UInt64) -- the int/uint/long/ulong overloads share the resolved name `System.Decimal::.ctor`, so the resolved `Call::MethodName` cannot distinguish them. A new `Call::ParameterIType` (`std::vector<TypeSystem::ITypePtr>`) field, populated by the IL reader from `callSig->ParameterTypes` (the same signature the existing `Call::ReturnType`/`ReturnIType` derive from), carries the resolved parameter types -- the faithful equivalent of the C# `Method.Parameters[i].Type` (the C# also carries the parameter name/kind; this port's transforms consult only the type), mirroring the `Call::ReturnIType` (D118) / `Call::DeclaringType` / `LdFlda::FieldToken` precedent of pre-resolving metadata at reader time (the transforms carry no MetadataFile handle, per D78). The signature decoder decodes the primitive element types to bare `KnownType(Int32)`/`(UInt32)`/`(Int64)`/`(UInt64)`, so the plain `dynamic_cast<const KnownType*>` + `Code()` suffices (no ParameterizedType unwrap, unlike the NullableLifting `KnownTypeCodeOf` which unwraps generic instantiations -- a Decimal ctor parameter is never a generic instantiation). The 1-arg fold: `MatchLdcI` (LdcI4 sign-extended to int64, or LdcI8) gets the constant's `long` value, then `DecimalValue::FromInt32`/`FromUInt32`/`FromInt64`/`FromUInt64` (with the `unchecked((int)/(uint)/(ulong)val)` truncation faithful to the C#) builds the value -- the signed/unsigned distinction matters (e.g. `newobj Decimal((uint)0xFFFFFFFF)` => 4294967295m, NOT -1m), so the parameter type is mandatory for faithfulness. The 5-arg fold: five `MatchLdcI4` args (lo/mid/hi/isNegative/scale) + the C# `unchecked((byte)scale) <= 28` guard (the byte cast truncates to the low 8 bits, faithful to the C# unchecked cast) + `DecimalValue::FromBits`. KEY FINDING (real-corpus, like the D119 field fold): the ctor fold fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- a corpus probe (8000 methods) found 24 `newobj Decimal` sites (15 one-arg, 6 five-arg, 3 other arg counts); of those, 3 one-arg-Int32-with-constant + 6 five-arg-all-LdcI4 = 9 folds across the full 25315-method corpus (the 12 one-arg-with-non-constant-arg and 3 other-arg-count sites correctly do not fold -- `MatchLdcI` fails on a variable load). The legacy csc emits NO UInt32/Int64/UInt64-overload Decimal ctors (all 3 one-arg-constant sites are the Int32 overload), so the UInt32/Int64/UInt64 dispatch paths are faithfulness-only on this corpus (matching the D59/D60/D69 precedent) -- the hand-built tests verify each overload's fold (the signed/unsigned interpretation) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone invariants (LdcDecimal count non-decreasing, newobj-Decimal count non-increasing) + `totalDecimalCtorFolds > 0`. The CLI `--csharp` output now shows 21 `m`-suffixed decimal literals (was 12 from the D119 field fold), including the 5-arg-ctor-only large constants `System.Decimal.MaxValue = 79228162514264337593543950335m`, `MinValue = -79228162514264337593543950335m`, and `NearNegativeZero = -0.000000000000000000000000001m` (scale 27) -- a real-corpus readability improvement; lock/using/`??` counts unchanged (517/313/78). 14 new gtest cases (FoldsNewObjDecimalInt32Ctor; FoldsNewObjDecimalInt32Negative; FoldsNewObjDecimalUInt32Ctor; FoldsNewObjDecimalInt64Ctor; FoldsNewObjDecimalUInt64Ctor; FoldsNewObjDecimalFiveArgCtor; FoldsNewObjDecimalFiveArgNegative; RejectsNonDecimalNewObj; RejectsNonConstantArg; RejectsTwoArgCtor; RejectsFiveArgWithBadScale; RejectsFiveArgWithNonConstantArg; SeedRendersFoldedDecimalLiteral; MscorlibDecimalCtorSweepPreservesInvariant). 785/785 gtest cases pass (14 new; was 771); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451365 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` / 21 `m`-decimal-literals, within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: the Decimal lift (`LiftCSharpUserComparison`'s Decimal branch [needs the C# resolver's `CSharpOperators.LiftUserDefinedOperator`]) is the subsequent in-order Decimal target; the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (`VisitNewObj`/`VisitLdObjIfRef`/`VisitStObj`/`VisitStLoc` `HandleCompoundAssign`, `HandleSwitchExpression`, `VisitDynamic*`, the `VisitConv` `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) and the remaining `NullPropagation` pieces (`UnconstrainedType` mode + the unconstrained-generic 5-instruction block sequence, the `AddressOf`/`LdObjIfRef`/`Dynamic*` access-chain cases) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D121 | LdVirtDelegate ILAst node + ExpressionTransforms.TransformDelegateCtorLdVirtFtnToLdVirtDelegate (the `newobj Delegate(target, ldvirtftn M(target))` -> `ldvirtdelegate Delegate M(target)` fold, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the `LdVirtDelegate` ILAst node (the C# `new DelegateType(target, ldvirtftn Method(target))` normalization) and `ExpressionTransforms.TransformDelegateCtorLdVirtFtnToLdVirtDelegate` (the VisitNewObj piece of C# ExpressionTransforms -- this port models a newobj as a Call with `IsNewObj` [D76], so the C# `VisitNewObj` dispatches from `VisitCall`): a `newobj DelegateType(target, ldvirtftn Method(target))` folds into `ldvirtdelegate DelegateType Method(target)`, unifying the delegate target and the virtual method so the later `DelegateConstruction` transform handles both the NewObj and LdVirtDelegate shapes uniformly (the C# `MatchDelegateConstruction` switches on `NewObj`/`LdVirtDelegate`). The `LdVirtDelegate` node (a `UnaryInstruction` with the target as its inlineable `Argument` child, an `ITypePtr Type` [the delegate type, mutable], and a `std::string MethodName` [the resolved method name -- the C# carries an `IMethod`; this port carries the resolved name string, matching the `LdFtn`/`LdVirtFtn` precedent], ResultType O, DirectFlags `None | MayThrow`, dump `ldvirtdelegate <type> <method>(<arg>)`) is faithful to the C# generated `LdVirtDelegate`; `OpCode::LdVirtDelegate` was pre-declared. The C# checks `Method.DeclaringType.Kind != TypeKind.Delegate`, `Arguments.Count != 2`, the 2nd arg is an `LdVirtFtn`, `IsPure(Arguments[0].Flags)`, and `Arguments[0].Match(ldVirtFtn.Argument).Success` (the newobj target and the ldvirtftn target are the same instruction). This port's `LdVirtFtn` carries only the method name (the IL reader discards the ldvirtftn target -- it is not a tree child, per the reader's `// We emit the LdVirtFtn but lose the object reference -- approximate` comment), so the structural-equality check is skipped: the newobj's first argument is the only target in the tree, so the `LdVirtDelegate` carries it directly. C# never emits mismatched targets (`new DelegateType(ta, ldvirtftn M(tb))` with ta != tb is semantically incoherent and not produced by any C# compiler), so the approximation is safe. A `LdVirtDelegate` is a value (ResultType O), so the fold is a clean value-position `ReplaceWith` (the C# `inst.ReplaceWith(ldVirtDelegate)`); no if-as-final block-model adaptation is needed (a newobj/LdVirtDelegate is never a block final). The `DeclaringType` (ITypePtr copy) and `MethodName` are captured before `TakeChild(0)` detaches the target (the call is destroyed by `ReplaceWith` -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline); the ldvirtftn (the 2nd arg) carries only the method name (already captured) and no tree children, so it is discarded with the call. The ILAstToCSharp seed renders an `LdVirtDelegate` as `new DelegateType(target.Method)` -- the real back end's `VisitLdVirtDelegate` (`CallBuilder.Build` -> `HandleDelegateConstruction`) folds the target and the virtual method into a `target.Method` method group inside `new DelegateType(...)`; the delegate type renders as its reflection name (matching the existing newobj rendering `new System.Action(...)`), and the method is the short name (after `::`) so `target.Bar` is the C# method-group form. KEY FINDING (real-corpus, unlike most recent nullable-family faithfulness-only pieces): the fold fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- 6 folds across the 8000-method `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant`, on virtual delegate constructions such as `new System.Reflection.TypeFilter(__Filters.FilterTypeName)`, `new BeginChildrenCallback(this.BeginChildren)`, `new CreateAttributeCallback(this.CreateAttribute)`, `new AsyncRenewal(sponsor.Renewal)` -- the `new DelegateType(target.Method)` form (the CLI `--csharp` output has 0 `ldvirtftn` two-arg forms; every virtual delegate construction now folds and renders as the combined `new DelegateType(target.Method)` form, the cleaner C#). 7 new gtest cases (TransformDelegateCtorLdVirtFtnToLdVirtDelegateFolds; TransformDelegateCtorRejectsNonDelegateDeclaringType; TransformDelegateCtorRejectsLdFtnSecondArg; TransformDelegateCtorRejectsImpureTarget; TransformDelegateCtorRejectsWrongArgCount; TransformDelegateCtorRejectsNonNewObj; SeedRendersLdVirtDelegateAsNewDelegateTargetMethod) plus the updated `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` with the LdVirtDelegate/newobj-virtual-delegate per-method monotone invariants (non-decreasing / non-increasing). 792/792 gtest cases pass (7 new; was 785); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451450 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` / 21 `m`-decimal-literals, within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining `VisitNewObj` pieces (`TransformSpanTCtorContainingStackAlloc` [needs `LocAllocSpan`/Span + stackalloc], `TransformArrayInitializers.TransformSpanTArrayInitialization` [needs `TransformArrayInitializers`]) and the rest of `ExpressionTransforms` (`VisitLdObjIfRef`/`VisitStObj`/`VisitStLoc` `HandleCompoundAssign` [needs `TransformAssignment`], `HandleSwitchExpression`, `VisitDynamic*` [needs Dynamic nodes], the `VisitConv` `conv.i4(ldlen)` first rewrite -- still blocked by the LdLen model divergence) and the remaining `NullPropagation` pieces (`UnconstrainedType` mode + the unconstrained-generic 5-instruction block sequence, the `AddressOf`/`LdObjIfRef`/`Dynamic*` access-chain cases) are the subsequent in-order targets; the `MatchDelegateConstruction` helper's `LdVirtDelegate` branch (the C# switches on `NewObj`/`LdVirtDelegate`) is a future follow-up now that the node exists (it lands with the full `DelegateConstruction` transform, which runs after `StatementTransform` in `GetILTransforms()`). |

The `DelegateConstructionMatch` out-struct is unchanged; both branches populate `target`/`targetMethod`/`delegateType`. Two pieces: (1) the `LdLen` ILAst node (`Decompiler/IL/Instructions/LdLen.hpp`) now carries a `StackType resultType` field faithful to the C# `LdLen(StackType type, ILInstruction array)` (the C# `Debug.Assert(type == I || I4 || I8)`): the raw `ldlen` opcode pushes a native int (`StackType::I`), and the synthetic `ldlen.i4`/`ldlen.i8` forms the VisitConv fold produces carry `I4`/`I8`. The constructor requires the StackType (the C# has no default); `ResultType()` returns the field; the dump renders `ldlen.I4(array)` / `ldlen.I8(array)` / `ldlen.I(array)` faithful to the C# WriteToCore (`OpCode` `.` `resultType`) via a static `StackTypeName` helper (StackType has no ToString in this port). The IL reader (`ILReader.cpp`) now emits `LdLen(StackType::I, array)` for the `ldlen` opcode (was a fixed-I4 `LdLen(array)`). (2) the `ExpressionTransforms::VisitConv` `conv.iN(ldlen)` fold (inserted after the argument visit, before the conv.r.un combining, matching the C# VisitConv order): `conv.i4(ldlen array)` / `conv.i8(ldlen array)` / `conv.i(ldlen array)` -- a `Conv` whose Argument is a raw native-int `LdLen` (`ResultType() == I`) and whose `TargetType` is an integer (`IsIntegerType(TargetType)`) -- folds into a single `LdLen(GetStackType(TargetType), array)` so the cast does not appear in the output (the array length is already the target type); the array is detached from the old LdLen via `TakeChild(0)` before the conv is destroyed by `ReplaceWith` (no GC). A checked `conv.ovf.iN(ldlen)` (`CheckForOverflow`) folds only when the new `AssumeArrayLengthFitsIntoInt32` setting (default true, faithful to `DecompilerSettings.AssumeArrayLengthFitsIntoInt32`) is on -- the array length fits in int32, so the overflow check cannot throw. The fold is a clean value-position `ReplaceWith` (a Conv is always a value, never a block final), so no if-as-final block-model adaptation is needed. KEY FINDING (real-corpus, high-frequency): the fold fires thousands of times across the 8000-method mscorlib sweep -- the .NET Framework 4 legacy csc emits `ldlen; conv.i4` for every `array.Length` use (the raw ldlen pushes a native int; the conv.i4 widens it to int32), so the CLI `--csharp` output now has no `(int)array.Length` casts (the conv folds into a single `ldlen.I4` before rendering) and `array.Length` renders cleanly. | D124 | ExpressionTransforms.VisitComp ldlen + C++/CLI null-comparison special cases (the `comp(ldlen == conv.i(0))` -> `comp(ldlen.i4 == ldc.i4 0)` and `comp(conv.i(ldloc obj) == conv.i(ldc.i4 0))` -> `comp(ldloc obj == ldnull)` folds, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the two `comp(... == 0)` equality/inequality special cases of `ExpressionTransforms.VisitComp`'s tail that D123 explicitly flagged as deferred (the `else if (rightWithoutConv.MatchLdcI4(0) && inst.Kind.IsEqualityOrInequality())` block, after the D81 unsigned `> 0`/`<= 0` tail rewrite). The Comp node already carries a settable public `InputType` field (D91), so the only new machinery is the fold logic. (1) The ldlen special case: `comp(ldlen[I] == conv.i(ldc.i4 0))` (the C# compiler compares a raw native-int ldlen against a sign/zero-extended 0 instead of widening the ldlen -- the special case where it does not generate conv.i4 after ldlen) folds into `comp(ldlen.i4[I4] == ldc.i4 0)`: the comp's `InputType` becomes `I4`, the Left (the raw `LdLen(I, array)`) is replaced with a fresh `LdLen(I4, array)` (the array detached from the old LdLen via `TakeChild(0)` before `SetChild(0, ...)` destroys it -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline), and the conv around the 0 is dropped (the bare `ldc.i4 0` is detached from the innermost conv via a new `DetachUnwrappedZero` helper that walks the same SignExtend-then-ZeroExtend path `IsLdcI4ZeroMaybeConv` walks, returning the owning `unique_ptr` to the detached `ldc.i4 0`; a bare `ldc.i4 0` Right with no conv stays -- the helper returns null and the Right is left unchanged). (2) The C++/CLI null comparison: `comp(conv.i(ldloc obj) == conv.i(ldc.i4 0))` (C++/CLI compares an object pointer conv'd to native int against a sign-extended 0) folds into `comp(ldloc obj == ldnull)`: the comp's `InputType` becomes `O`, the Left conv's argument (the `ldloc obj`) is detached via `TakeChild(0)` and becomes the new Left (the conv is destroyed by `SetChild(0, ...)`), and the Right is replaced with a fresh `LdNull`. The C# checks `conv.TargetType == PrimitiveType.I && conv.Argument.ResultType == StackType.O` (not the Conv Kind -- a `conv.i(ldloc obj)` has Kind StopGCTracking, not SignExtend); the port matches on `TargetType` and the argument's `ResultType` identically. Neither special case re-visits (the C# falls through with no `VisitComp(inst); return;` -- neither changes the Kind, so the head rewrites would not re-fire), unlike the D81 unsigned `> 0`/`<= 0` tail rewrite which re-visits. So both are ported for faithfulness (the hand-built tests verify the rewrites; the mscorlib sweep verifies the per-method monotone-non-increasing comp-with-raw-ldlen-left count invariant holds across the corpus), matching the D59/D60/D69/D90 precedent of a 0-firing-on-corpus transform.
