| D125 | NumericCompoundAssign ILAst node + CompoundAssignmentInstruction base (Phase 4 compound-assignment foundation, next-in-order) | port | Ports the NumericCompoundAssign ILAst node (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/CompoundAssignmentInstruction.hpp, from the generated Instructions.cs + the hand-written CompoundAssignmentInstruction.cs) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the next in-order per-statement child of StatementTransform -- TransformAssignment (its HandleCompoundAssign folds `stloc V(binary.op(ldloc V, rhs))` into a NumericCompoundAssign `V op= rhs`) -- following the MatchInstruction (D70) / UsingInstruction (D74) / NullCoalescingInstruction (D88) / ThreeValuedBoolAnd/Or (D95) precedent. The CompoundAssignmentInstruction abstract base carries the two children Target (slot 0, inlineable -- the store target, an address or a property-getter call) and Value (slot 1, inlineable -- the RHS), plus EvalMode (CompoundEvalMode::EvaluatesToOldValue / EvaluatesToNewValue -- whether the expression evaluates to the old (post-increment) or the new (compound/pre) value) and TargetKind (CompoundTargetKind::Address / Property / Dynamic -- how the Target is interpreted); DirectFlags = None, the base ComputeFlags = Target.Flags | Value.Flags. NumericCompoundAssign (CompoundAssignmentInstruction + the C# ILiftableInstruction) carries Operator (BinaryNumericOperator), CheckForOverflow, Sign (the D85 TypeSystem::Sign enum -- None/Signed/Unsigned), LeftInputType / RightInputType (StackType), UnderlyingResultType (StackType, the ILiftableInstruction impl), IsLifted, and the Type operand (an ITypePtr, the store type); ResultType = IsLifted ? O : UnderlyingResultType (a lifted compound assign operates on boxed Nullable<T> and produces O); DirectFlags = SideEffect (+ MayThrow when CheckForOverflow or the operator is Div/Rem, faithful to the C# ComputeFlags/DirectFlags); the ILAst dump is `compound.assign.<operator>[.ovf][.unsigned|.signed].<type>[.lifted].<suffix>(target, value)` where the suffix is `.address`/`.property` (TargetKind) + `.new`/`.old` (EvalMode), faithful to the C# WriteToCore. The C# NumericCompoundAssign constructor copies Operator/Sign/LeftInputType/RightInputType/UnderlyingResultType/IsLifted from a BinaryNumericInstruction; this port's BinaryNumericInstruction carries Operator/Signed(bool)/CheckForOverflow/ResultStackType/IsLifted but NOT the Sign enum or the per-operand input types (it carries a Signed bool, not the C# Sign, and the result type, not the per-operand input types), so the node takes its fields explicitly (the D48 lifted-BNI precedent) -- the foundation tests construct it directly, and the future HandleCompoundAssign transform will build it from a BinaryNumericInstruction (approximating Sign from the binary's Signed bool + operator, and the input types from the operands' ResultType). A BNI Sign/input-type reconciliation is a separate, contained model change deferred to that transform. The C# CheckValidTarget asserts the Target's ResultType/Op for each TargetKind (Address -> Ref/I; Property -> a Call; Dynamic -> a Dynamic* instruction); this port's CheckInvariant is non-virtual and checks tree consistency only (the other ported nodes skip their C# per-node asserts the same way). CompoundAssignmentInstruction extends ILInstruction (NOT IStoreInstruction -- the C# `: ILInstruction`), so it needs no ComputeVariableUsage store-counting case; the default children recursion handles it. The OpCode::NumericCompoundAssign / UserDefinedCompoundAssign / DynamicCompoundAssign values were pre-declared in OpCode.hpp (the enum was seeded with every instruction kind early), so porting the node needed only the subclass header. Added the MakeAssignmentExpressions (C# 2.0, default true) + IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement (default true) settings to ILTransformSettings -- both gate TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign (the C# `if (!Settings.MakeAssignmentExpressions || !Settings.IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement) return false;`); the nodes themselves are unconditional. The ILAstToCSharp seed renders a NumericCompoundAssign as `target op= value` (the operator mapped: Add `+=` ... ShiftRight `>>=`), with the Address Target (an LdLoca) rendered as the bare variable name (the back end loads the value at the address), and the post-increment/decrement (Add/Sub, EvaluatesToOldValue, ldc.i4 1 RHS) rendered as `target++`/`target--`; an EmitStatement case renders the statement form `target op= value;`. No pipeline transform constructs these nodes yet, so `--csharp` output is unchanged (the seed already renders `V op= expr` from the StLoc pattern at the text level; this node is the ILAst-level representation the future HandleCompoundAssign transform produces for the real back end). 13 new gtest cases (node invariant/flags/ResultType/dump; the dump variants -- ovf+signed/unsigned, signed+lifted, EvaluatesToOldValue suffix; Div/Rem MayThrow; children in typed slots + re-parenting; flags propagation; seed rendering of `num += 1;`/`num++;`/`num--;`/`num &= 1;`; the two setting defaults; and an 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructing a NumericCompoundAssign over each real BinaryNumericInstruction with a LdLoc/LdLoca Left operand, asserting the invariant/dump/ResultType hold across the corpus). 825/825 gtest cases pass (was 812; +13). The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). The full TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign (the transform that builds these nodes) is the next in-order target it unblocks: it needs IsCompoundStore / IsMatchingCompoundLoad / UnwrapSmallIntegerConv / ValidateCompoundAssign (the type-system-aware validation) + the BNI Sign/input-type reconciliation + RecombineVariables (the finalizeMatch for the StLoc case) + the per-variable store-list tree walk -- a larger slice that is a subsequent iteration. The UserDefinedCompoundAssign (needs IMethod) and DynamicCompoundAssign (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses stay deferred. |
| D126 | TransformAssignment foundation: type-system helpers (GetSize/IsSmallIntegerType/GetSign on PrimitiveType + GetSize/IsSmallIntegerType/IsCSharpSmallIntegerType/SwapSign on IType) + the UnwrapSmallIntegerConv transform helper (Phase 4 compound-assignment foundation, next-in-order after D125) | port | Ports the self-contained type-system helpers and the UnwrapSmallIntegerConv helper the next in-order per-statement child of StatementTransform, TransformAssignment (its HandleCompoundAssign / TransformPostIncDecOperator / TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore folds `stloc V(binary.op(ldloc V, rhs))` into a NumericCompoundAssign `V op= rhs` -- the node D125 ported), need as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, following the MatchInstruction (D70) / UsingInstruction (D74) / NumericCompoundAssign (D125) precedent. (1) PrimitiveType.hpp (IL namespace, mirroring ILTypeExtensions.cs): `GetSize(PrimitiveType)` -- 1 for I1/U1, 2 for I2/U2, 4 for I4/U4/R4, 8 for I8/R8/U8/R, kNativeIntSize (6) for I/U/Ref, 0 for None/Unknown; `IsSmallIntegerType(PrimitiveType)` -- `GetSize < 4` (so I1/U1/I2/U2 are small; faithful to the C# which only ever consults this for a real Conv.TargetType the reader populated from an opcode, never None/Unknown); `GetSign(PrimitiveType)` -- Signed for I1/I2/I4/I8/R4/R8/R/I, Unsigned for U1/U2/U4/U8/U, None for Ref/None/Unknown (added `#include Decompiler/TypeSystem/Sign.hpp` since Sign is in the TypeSystem namespace; no cycle -- Sign.hpp includes only `<cstdint>`). (2) TypeUtils.hpp (TypeSystem namespace, mirroring TypeUtils.cs): `kNativeIntSize` (constexpr 6, the C# TypeUtils.NativeIntSize); `GetSize(const IType*)` -- pointer-sized kinds (Pointer/ByReference/Class/NInt/NUInt) report kNativeIntSize, an Enum KnownType falls through to the KnownTypeCode switch (this minimal port has no per-enum underlying type, so a bare Enum KnownType reports 0 -- the compound-assignment validation only consults IsCSharpSmallIntegerType, the KnownTypeCode switch), a KnownType reports its primitive size by KnownTypeCode (1 for Boolean/SByte/Byte, 2 for Char/Int16/UInt16, 4 for Int32/UInt32/Single, kNativeIntSize for IntPtr/UIntPtr, 8 for Int64/UInt64/Double), 0 otherwise (O/Void/unknown); `IsSmallIntegerType(const IType*)` -- `GetSize > 0 && GetSize < 4` (the > 0 guard excludes Void/unknown, unlike the PrimitiveType version); `IsCSharpSmallIntegerType(const IType*)` -- only Byte/SByte/Int16/UInt16 (C# does NOT consider bool/char/enum small, unlike the ILAst IsSmallIntegerType -- the compound-assignment validation consults this to decide whether a small-integer LHS requires the binary to be signed, since C# numeric-promotes a small integer to int); `SwapSign(const IType*)` -- the opposite-sign KnownType for a primitive integer (I1<->U1, I2<->U2, I4<->U4, I8<->U8, I<->U), returning a fresh KnownType (the C# throws ArgumentException for a type with no opposite sign; this port returns nullptr because the callers only consult SwapSign after a sign-mismatch guard, and a nullptr propagates as a no-fold). KEY: Char maps to U2 so SwapSign(Char) returns Int16 (faithful, NOT nullptr -- a test initially asserting nullptr caught this); Boolean/Single/Double/Object/unknown map to None/not-in-set and return nullptr. (3) TransformAssignment.{hpp,cpp} (Transforms/, the C# ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/TransformAssignment.cs foundation): `UnwrapSmallIntegerConv(ILInstruction* inst, Conv*& conv)` -- the helper the compound-assignment folds consult to peel the compiler's `conv` truncation to a small integer that a compound assign to a small-integer local/field carries (`stloc V(conv.i1(binary.add(ldloc V, ldc.i4 1)))`): a `Conv` with `Kind == ConversionKind::Truncate` and `IsSmallIntegerType(TargetType)` unwraps to its `Argument` (reported via `conv` for the caller's ValidateCompoundAssign); any other instruction (a non-Truncate conv, a Truncate to a non-small-integer target, or a non-Conv) is returned unchanged (the C# `conv = inst as Conv` assignment may leave a stale non-null conv on the no-unwrap path, but the caller only consults `conv` when the returned instruction is a BinaryNumericInstruction, which a Conv never is, so it is harmless). The helper is a non-owning view (the caller holds ownership; the returned pointer is either `inst` or its `Argument` child, still owned by `inst`). Wired TransformAssignment.cpp into the ilspy CMake build (Decompiler/IL/CMakeLists.txt); the helper is NOT wired into the pipeline (the full transform is a subsequent iteration), so `--csharp` output is unchanged. 13 new gtest cases (8 in a new tests/Decompiler/TypeSystem/TypeUtils_Test.cpp: GetSize/IsSmallIntegerType/GetSign on PrimitiveType; GetSize/IsSmallIntegerType/IsCSharpSmallIntegerType on IType over all primitive KnownTypes; SwapSign opposite-sign pairs + null-for-non-integer; 5 in a new tests/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/TransformAssignment_Test.cpp: UnwrapSmallIntegerConv unwraps a Truncate-to-small-integer conv [incl. over a BinaryNumericInstruction, the shape the fold consumes], does NOT unwrap a Truncate-to-non-small-integer / a non-Truncate conv / a non-Conv instruction). 838/838 gtest cases pass (13 new; was 825). The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). The full TransformAssignment (IsCompoundStore [needs InferType for the StObj case + IsSameMember for the Call case + Variable.Kind for the StLoc case] / IsMatchingCompoundLoad [LdObj/StObj match, getter/setter match needs IMethod/AccessorOwner, LdLoc/StLoc match needs RecombineVariables] / ValidateCompoundAssign [needs NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType, which needs the BNI Sign + IsCSharpSmallIntegerType + GetSign + IsImplicitTruncation] + the BNI Sign/input-type reconciliation [a contained model change D125 flagged as deferred to the transform] + RecombineVariables [the finalizeMatch for the StLoc case] + the per-variable store-list tree walk [the repeatedly-deferred infrastructure piece]) is the next in-order target this foundation unblocks -- a larger slice that is a subsequent iteration. The UserDefinedCompoundAssign (needs IMethod) and DynamicCompoundAssign (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses stay deferred. |
| D127 | BinaryNumericInstruction Sign / LeftInputType / RightInputType reconciliation + ComputeResultType (the contained model change D125/D126 flagged as deferred to the TransformAssignment transform, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the faithful Sign / input-type model on BinaryNumericInstruction (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/BinaryNumericInstruction.hpp) -- the contained model change D125 explicitly flagged as deferred to the TransformAssignment transform and D126 reiterated as the prerequisite the full compound-assignment transform's ValidateCompoundAssign / NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType consults. The C# BinaryNumericInstruction carries Sign (the TypeSystem::Sign enum: None/Signed/Unsigned), LeftInputType / RightInputType (StackType), and computes the result stack type via ComputeResultType(op, LeftInputType, RightInputType) (Ecma-335 Table 2/5/6/7); this port's BNI previously carried only a Signed bool + ResultStackType (no Sign enum, no per-operand input types), so the NumericCompoundAssign node (D125) took its fields explicitly and the future HandleCompoundAssign would have had to approximate Sign from the Signed bool. The reconciliation: (1) added a Sign field (TypeSystem::Sign, defaulting None) + LeftInputType / RightInputType (StackType, defaulting I4) to BinaryNumericInstruction; (2) added a static ComputeResultType(op, left, right) helper faithful to the C# -- same-type operands (or any shift) yield the left input type; mixed Ref operands yield Ref (or I for sub(&,&), but that inner branch is dead code -- the C#'s first `left == right` guard short-circuits and returns `left` = Ref for sub(Ref,Ref), so the `sub(&,&) = I` comment is misleading; the faithful behavior is Ref); otherwise Unknown; (3) added a faithful 5-arg constructor (left, right, op, checkForOverflow, sign) that derives LeftInputType/RightInputType from the operands' ResultType in the ctor body (the BinaryInstruction base init moves them into Left/Right, valid post-init per the D91 learning) and computes ResultStackType via ComputeResultType -- the form the IL reader uses; (4) replaced the 7-arg lifted constructor with an 8-arg lifted constructor (left, right, op, leftInputType, rightInputType, checkForOverflow, sign, isLifted) faithful to the C# 8-arg ctor (the lifted operands have ResultType O, but the input types are the underlying Nullable<T> types, taken explicitly) -- computes ResultStackType via ComputeResultType; (5) kept the 4-arg legacy constructor (left, right, op, resultStackType=I4) for backward compat (the pre-reconciliation tests construct BNI with an explicit result stack type), which now also derives the input types and defaults Sign.None / CheckForOverflow false; (6) kept the legacy Signed bool (derived: `Sign != Unsigned`, true for None/Signed, false for Unsigned) for the dump's .un suffix + the NullableLifting DoLiftBinary call site, which was updated to pass bni->LeftInputType / bni->RightInputType / bni->Sign to the new 8-arg lifted ctor (was bni->ResultStackType / bni->CheckForOverflow / bni->Signed). The IL reader's IL_BIN macro (ILReader.cpp) now passes the per-opcode Sign + CheckForOverflow faithfully, matching the C# ILReader BinaryNumeric(op, checkForOverflow, sign) helper: plain add/sub/mul/and/or/xor/shl -> Sign.None; div/rem/shr -> Sign.Signed; the _un forms (div_un/rem_un/shr_un) -> Sign.Unsigned; the _ovf forms (add_ovf/sub_ovf/mul_ovf) -> Sign.Signed with CheckForOverflow; _ovf_un -> Sign.Unsigned with CheckForOverflow. The dump suffix order was reordered to .ovf before .un (matching the C#'s .ovf before .unsigned), so add_ovf_un renders `binary.add.ovf.un(` (was `binary.add.un.ovf(`). The NumericCompoundAssign node (D125) can now copy Sign/LeftInputType/RightInputType from a BinaryNumericInstruction directly (the C# constructor does), and the future ValidateCompoundAssign / IsBinaryCompatibleWithType consult binary.Sign. KEY FINDING: the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus emits all three Sign flavours -- the MscorlibBinaryNumericSignSweep (4000 straight-line methods) counts Sign.None (plain add/sub/mul/and/or/xor/shl) > 0, Sign.Signed (div/rem/shr) > 0, and Sign.Unsigned (_un forms) > 0, and the LeftInputType == Left.ResultType / RightInputType == Right.ResultType invariant (the C# CheckInvariant's non-lifted assertion) holds with 0 violations across the corpus; the reader-emitted binaries are never lifted (the lift machinery runs in NullableLifting, later in the pipeline). The CLI --csharp output is unchanged (517 lock / 313 using / 78 ??, exit 0, ~451641 lines, no regression -- the seed renders V op= expr from the StLoc pattern at the text level, not from the BNI Sign). 6 new gtest cases (5 in a new tests/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/BinaryNumericInstruction_Test.cpp: ComputeResultType [same-type/shift/Ref/Unknown, incl. the sub(Ref,Ref)=Ref dead-code clarification], FaithfulConstructorDerivesSignAndInputTypes [None/Signed/Unsigned/ovf/I8], LegacyConstructorDefaultsSignAndDerivesInputTypes, LiftedConstructorTakesInputTypesExplicitly [ResultType O, UnderlyingResultType I4, .lifted dump], DumpSuffixes [.un for Unsigned, .ovf for CheckForOverflow, .ovf.un for _ovf_un]; 1 in ILReader_Test.cpp: MscorlibBinaryNumericSignSweep asserting all three Sign flavours appear + the input-type invariant holds + Signed bool consistent with Sign). 844/844 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 838); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the full TransformAssignment (IsCompoundStore / IsMatchingCompoundLoad / ValidateCompoundAssign [needs NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType, which now has the BNI Sign but still needs IsImplicitTruncation -- a larger slice with CheckImplicitTruncation / GetEnumUnderlyingType] / RecombineVariables / the per-statement Run wiring + the per-variable store-list tree walk) is the next in-order target this reconciliation unblocks; the NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType validator itself (the IsLifted/Enum/Pointer/SmallInteger/IsImplicitTruncation gate) is the natural next piece. |
| D128 | TransformAssignment.CheckImplicitTruncation / IsImplicitTruncation / CommonImplicitTruncation + ImplicitTruncationResult enum + PrimitiveType.HasOppositeSign (the `IsImplicitTruncation` half of the NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType gate D127 flagged as the natural next piece, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports the implicit-truncation analysis the next in-order `NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType` validator (and the broader TransformAssignment.ValidateCompoundAssign / IsCompoundStore) consults -- the piece D127 explicitly framed as the sizable sub-piece ("still needs IsImplicitTruncation -- a larger slice with CheckImplicitTruncation / GetEnumUnderlyingType") -- as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, following the MatchInstruction (D70) / UsingInstruction (D74) / NumericCompoundAssign (D125) / TransformAssignment-UnwrapSmallIntegerConv (D126) precedent. (1) `HasOppositeSign(PrimitiveType)` in PrimitiveType.hpp (IL namespace, mirroring ILTypeExtensions.HasOppositeSign): true for the integer primitives I1/I2/I4/I8/U1/U2/U4/U8/I/U (which have a distinct opposite-sign counterpart, so a sign-mismatch truncation can be fixed by flipping the target sign); false for R4/R8/R/Ref/None/Unknown. Consulted by the Conv case's same-size-opposite-sign branch. (2) `ImplicitTruncationResult` enum (ValuePreserved / ValueChanged / ValueChangedDueToSignMismatch) + `CheckImplicitTruncation(value, type, allowNullableValue)` + `IsImplicitTruncation(value, type, allowNullableValue)` + a file-local `CommonImplicitTruncation` in TransformAssignment.{hpp,cpp} (Transforms/, mirroring TransformAssignment.cs). Faithful to the C# CheckImplicitTruncation: implicit truncation in ILAst only happens for small-integer types (the ILReader inserts `conv` for other truncations), so a non-small-integer target returns ValuePreserved before consulting the value shape; the analysis then recurses into (a) `LdcI4` -- a range check via the small-integer target's KnownTypeCode (Boolean -> 0/1, Byte -> 0..255, SByte -> -128..127, Int16 -> -32768..32767, UInt16/Char -> 0..65535), ValuePreserved when the constant fits, ValueChanged otherwise; (b) `Conv` -- `conv.TargetType == type.ToPrimitiveType()` -> ValuePreserved, same-size + opposite sign + `HasOppositeSign(primitiveType)` -> ValueChangedDueToSignMismatch, else ValueChanged; (c) `Comp` -- always ValuePreserved (comp returns 0/1); (d) `BinaryNumericInstruction` BitAnd/BitOr/BitXor -- recurse Left then Right with `CommonImplicitTruncation`, short-circuiting the Right recursion when the Left is a plain ValueChanged; (e) `IfInstruction` -- recurse TrueInst then FalseInst with `CommonImplicitTruncation`, short-circuiting the False recursion when the True is a plain ValueChanged. KEY approximation: the C# else-branch consults `value.InferType(compilation)` to compare the inferred type's size/sign against the target (and `NullableType.GetUnderlyingType` when `allowNullableValue`); this minimal type system has no InferType, so the else-branch is approximated conservatively as `ValueChanged` (matching the C# Unknown fallthrough -- a value whose inferred type is unknown might be truncated), so a compound assignment to a small integer with an unmodeled RHS (e.g. a bare `LdLoc`) does not fold. `allowNullableValue` (the C# consults it only in the InferType else-branch) is kept for API fidelity but is not consulted by the conservative approximation. The `type.GetEnumUnderlyingType().GetDefinition()?.KnownTypeCode` the C# ldc.i4 case consults reduces to the KnownType's code directly here: this port's `IsSmallIntegerType(IType*)` (D126) is only true for the small-integer KnownTypes (Boolean/SByte/Byte/Char/Int16/UInt16 -- an Enum KnownType reports GetSize 0, not small, so GetEnumUnderlyingType is moot for the small-integer guard), via a file-local `SmallIntegerKnownTypeCode(const IType*)` that dynamic_casts to KnownType and returns its Code (None otherwise). `value` is a non-owning view. The `ICompilation` parameter is dropped (this port has no ICompilation; the only consumer was the InferType else-branch). The helper is NOT wired into a pipeline transform (no consumer yet -- the IsBinaryCompatibleWithType validator is the subsequent iteration), so `--csharp` output is unchanged. 23 new gtest cases (22 in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp's new `CheckImplicitTruncationTest` suite: non-small-integer-target ValuePreserved; LdcI4 fits/overflows Byte/SByte/Boolean/Char/UInt16/Int16 boundaries; Conv same-primitive-type preserved / same-size-opposite-sign sign-mismatch / different-size changed; Comp always preserved; BitAnd of fitting LdcI4 preserved / non-fitting side changed / left-ValueChanged short-circuit / sign-mismatch+preserved -> changed / non-bitwise falls through; IfInstruction both-arms-fit preserved / one-arm-overflow changed; unmodeled LdLoc -> ValueChanged; IsImplicitTruncation == (Check != ValuePreserved); 1 in Conv_Test.cpp: `Conv.PrimitiveTypeHasOppositeSign` covering I1..U8/I/U true + R4/R8/R/Ref/None/Unknown false). 867/867 gtest cases pass (23 new; was 844); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??`, within the D30 non-determinism range; the foundation is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D127). Deferred vs the C#: the `NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType` validator itself (the IsLifted/Enum/Pointer/SmallInteger/IsImplicitTruncation gate, now unblocked on the IsImplicitTruncation + BNI Sign [D127] sides; still needs an `IsNullable`/`GetUnderlyingType` gate via the D93 `NullableLiftingTransform::GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable`, the Pointer case's `PointerArithmeticOffset.Detect`, and the `NativeIntegers`/`UnsignedRightShift` settings) is the subsequent in-order target this foundation unblocks; the rest of the full TransformAssignment (IsCompoundStore / IsMatchingCompoundLoad / ValidateCompoundAssign / RecombineVariables / the per-statement Run wiring + the per-variable store-list tree walk) follows. |
| D129 | NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType validator (the IsLifted/Unknown/Enum/IntPtr-UIntPtr/Sign/IsImplicitTruncation gate, Phase 4 next-in-order after D128) | port | Ports the `NumericCompoundAssign.IsBinaryCompatibleWithType` static validator (the C# static gate on `NumericCompoundAssign` that `TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign` / `ValidateCompoundAssign` consults before building a `NumericCompoundAssign` from a `stloc V(binary.op(ldloc V, rhs))` pattern) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, completing the compound-assignment-validation prerequisites D127/D128 each flagged. The C# `IsBinaryCompatibleWithType(BinaryNumericInstruction binary, IType type, DecompilerSettings? settings)` is a sequence of gates: (1) the IsLifted gate -- a lifted binary requires the store type to be `Nullable<T>`, then the rest of the checks operate on the underlying type (`NullableType.IsNullable` + `GetUnderlyingType`); (2) the Unknown gate -- `type.Kind == Unknown` rejects (avoid a potentially-incorrect compound assignment); (3) the Enum gate -- Add/Sub/BitAnd/BitOr/BitXor are OK, other operators rejected; (4) the Pointer gate -- Add/Sub consult `PointerArithmeticOffset.Detect`, other operators rejected; (5) the IntPtr/UIntPtr gate -- a `KnownType(IntPtr)`/`(UIntPtr)` with `Kind is not (NInt or NUInt)` rejects shifts and rejects the whole compound assign when `NativeIntegers` is off (the `n(u)int`-cast trick does not work for shifts); (6) the Sign gate -- `binary.Sign != None` consults `type.IsCSharpSmallIntegerType()` (the binary must be signed for a C# small integer, since C# numeric-promotes it to int) and `type.GetSign()` (must match the binary's sign), with a `signMismatchAllowed` carve-out for an unsigned right shift when `UnsignedRightShift` is on (the C# 11 `>>>` operator); (7) the `IsImplicitTruncation` gate -- an RHS that would be truncated for the LHS type is rejected. The port (`CompoundAssignmentInstruction.cpp`, out-of-line since the node is header-only) ports gates (1)/(2)/(3)/(5)/(6)/(7) faithfully: the IsLifted gate unwraps `Nullable<T>` via the D93 `NullableLiftingTransform::GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable` (the faithful equivalent of `NullableType.IsNullable` + `GetUnderlyingType` -- returns the type argument T for a `Nullable<T>`, nullptr otherwise); the Enum gate's operator switch is verbatim; the IntPtr/UIntPtr gate uses the D93 `NullableLiftingTransform::IsKnownType` (the faithful equivalent of `type.IsKnownType(IntPtr)` -- `GetDefinition()?.KnownTypeCode == IntPtr`, and a `KnownType(IntPtr)` IS its own definition with Kind==Struct per the port's `KnownTypeCode.cpp` table, NOT NInt, so the `Kind is not (NInt or NUInt)` guard fires); the Sign gate uses the D126 `TypeSystem::IsCSharpSmallIntegerType` + `TypeSystem::GetSign(IType*)` and the new `NativeIntegers`/`UnsignedRightShift` settings; the IsImplicitTruncation gate uses the D128 `IsImplicitTruncation(binary->Right.get(), effectiveType, binary->IsLifted)` (the `null` ICompilation is dropped; `binary.IsLifted` is the `allowNullableValue`; the type is the effective -- possibly-unwrapped -- store type). The `Pointer` case (gate 4) is DEFERRED-CONSERVATIVE: the C# consults `PointerArithmeticOffset.Detect` (needs the `SizeOf` ILAst node + `ComputeSizeOf` [which needs `GetEnumUnderlyingType`] + `UnwrapConv(ConversionKind.SignExtend)` + `NormalizeTypeVisitor.TypeErasure.EquivalentTypes` -- a substantial deferred slice), so the port returns `false` for pointer types (no pointer compound assignment is confirmed). This is behavior-preserving for the .NET Framework 4 mscorlib corpus (pointer arithmetic requires `unsafe`, absent from C#-compiled code); the Add/Sub vs other-operator distinction is kept structural in the switch so the `PointerArithmeticOffset` port can fill in the Add/Sub arm later. The `NativeIntegers` (C# 9.0, default true) + `UnsignedRightShift` (C# 11.0, default true) settings are added to `ILTransformSettings` (both matching `DecompilerSettings`); a null `settings` pointer is treated as the defaults (NativeIntegers/UnsignedRightShift permissive), matching the C# constructor `Debug.Assert(IsBinaryCompatibleWithType(binary, type, null))` (the C# `settings != null && !settings.NativeIntegers` skips when null; `settings == null || settings.UnsignedRightShift` is true when null). The validator is a static method on `NumericCompoundAssign` (matching the C#), with a forward-declared `ILTransformSettings` parameter (the node header avoids including `IILTransform.hpp` to keep the include graph lean); the out-of-line `.cpp` includes `NullableLiftingTransform.hpp` (for `GetUnderlyingTypeOfNullable`/`IsKnownType`), `TypeUtils.hpp` (for `IsCSharpSmallIntegerType`/`GetSign`), and `TransformAssignment.hpp` (for `IsImplicitTruncation`). The validator is NOT wired into a pipeline transform (no consumer yet -- the full `TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign` is the subsequent iteration), so `--csharp` output is unchanged. 21 new gtest cases in `CompoundAssignmentInstruction_Test.cpp`'s new `IsBinaryCompatibleWithTypeTest` suite (LiftedBinaryOverNullableTypePasses; LiftedBinaryOverNonNullableTypeFails; UnknownTypeFails; EnumAllowsAddSubBitOps; EnumRejectsOtherOperators; PointerRejectedConservatively; IntPtrWithNativeIntegersPasses; IntPtrWithoutNativeIntegersFails; IntPtrShiftFails; UIntPtrWithNativeIntegersPasses; NIntBypassesIntPtrGate; SignedBinaryOverSignedTypePasses; UnsignedBinaryOverSignedTypeFails; UnsignedRightShiftOverSignedTypePasses; UnsignedRightShiftFailsWhenSettingOff; UnsignedBinaryOverCSharpSmallIntegerFails; SignedBinaryOverCSharpSmallIntegerPasses; TruncatingRhsFails; FittingRhsPasses; NullSettingsTreatedAsDefaults; plus the `MscorlibValidatorSweep` exercising every gate on real binary operations + their resolved variable types across 8000 methods) plus the updated `AssignmentSettingsDefaultTrue` asserting the new `NativeIntegers`/`UnsignedRightShift` defaults. 888/888 gtest cases pass (21 new; was 867); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- within the D30 non-determinism range; the validator is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D128). Deferred vs the C#: the `Pointer` case's `PointerArithmeticOffset.Detect` (the `SizeOf` node + `ComputeSizeOf` + `UnwrapConv` + `NormalizeTypeVisitor.TypeErasure.EquivalentTypes`) is the remaining validator gap; the full `TransformAssignment` (IsCompoundStore [needs `InferType` for the StObj case + `IsSameMember` for the Call case + `Variable.Kind` for the StLoc case] / IsMatchingCompoundLoad [needs `RecombineVariables` + getter/setter `IMethod`/`AccessorOwner`] / ValidateCompoundAssign [now unblocked -- the validator is in place, only the `Pointer` case is deferred-conservative] / RecombineVariables [the finalizeMatch for the StLoc case] / the per-statement Run wiring + the per-variable store-list tree walk [the repeatedly-deferred infrastructure piece]) is the next in-order target; the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (needs `IMethod`) and `DynamicCompoundAssign` (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses stay deferred. |
| D130 | ILFunction.RecombineVariables (the per-variable store-list tree walk [the repeatedly-deferred infrastructure piece], Phase 4 next-in-order after D129) | port | Ports `ILFunction::RecombineVariables(ILVariablePtr variable1, ILVariablePtr variable2)` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/ILFunction.{hpp,cpp}, mirroring the C# ILFunction.RecombineVariables) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, resolving the "per-variable store-list tree walk [the repeatedly-deferred infrastructure piece]" D125/D126/D127/D128/D129 each listed as a remaining TransformAssignment prerequisite. The C# RecombineVariables iterates the per-variable LoadInstructions / StoreInstructions / AddressInstructions lists (the infrastructure this port never built) and sets each instruction's Variable -- the setter maintains the lists and the counts; it then removes variable2 from the function's Variables. This port has no per-variable instruction lists, so a tree walk (ReassignUses, descending into every child like CountUsage in VariableUsage.cpp) replaces the C# list iteration and a manual count increment replaces the C# property-setter's list maintenance: every load/store/address of variable2 in the body is reassigned to variable1, variable1's usage counts are incremented by exactly one per reassigned use (so v1's counts end at original + reassigned), variable2's counts are zeroed (the C# setter would have drained its lists), and variable2 is dropped from the function's Variables list. A no-op when variable1 and variable2 are the same variable (or either is null). The store-bearing opcodes reassigned are StLoc, MatchInstruction, UsingInstruction, TryCatchHandler (all IStoreInstruction in the C#, all counted as stores by CountUsage); the load/address opcodes are LdLoc / LdLoca. The walk descends into nested ILFunctions (harmless -- variable2 belongs to this function, so it cannot appear in a nested function's tree), matching the existing CountUsage convention. RecombineVariables is the finalizeMatch the next in-order TransformAssignment.IsMatchingCompoundLoad LdLoc/StLoc branch calls (`finalizeMatch = context => context.Function.RecombineVariables(ldloc.Variable, stloc.Variable)`) so a `stloc V(binary.op(ldloc V, rhs))` whose load and store are split fragments of one original variable (equal per the C# ILVariableEqualityComparer -- same Function, Kind, Index) collapses to a single variable for the `V op= rhs` compound assign. It is NOT wired into a pipeline transform (no consumer yet -- the full TransformAssignment.HandleCompoundAssign is the subsequent iteration), so `--csharp` output is unchanged. 5 new gtest cases in ILInlining_Test.cpp (alongside the other ILFunction API methods RegisterVariable / ChainedConstructorCallILOffset): RecombineVariablesIsNoOpForSameVariable; RecombineVariablesIsNoOpForNullVariable; RecombineVariablesReassignsLoadStoreAndAddress (a store + an ldloca arg + a leave ldloc all reassigned to v1, counts migrated, v2 dropped); RecombineVariablesSumsCountsWhenTargetHasExistingUses (v1 already has a load; v2's store+load are added on top, so v1 LoadCount == 2 / StoreCount == 1); RecombineVariablesPreservesOtherVariablesCounts (a third variable v3 is untouched -- the manual increment only touches v1 and v2, unlike a full ComputeVariableUsage recompute). 893/893 gtest cases pass (5 new; was 888); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451558 lines, 517 lock / 313 using, within the D30 non-determinism range). Deferred vs the C#: the full TransformAssignment (IsCompoundStore [needs InferType for the StObj case + IsSameMember for the Call case + Variable.Kind for the StLoc case] / IsMatchingCompoundLoad [now has RecombineVariables for the LdLoc/StLoc finalizeMatch; still needs the LdObj/StObj case's IsDuplicatedAddressComputation + previousInstruction, and the getter/setter MatchingGetterAndSetterCalls case's IMethod/AccessorOwner] / ValidateCompoundAssign [now unblocked -- the D129 validator is in place, only the Pointer case's PointerArithmeticOffset.Detect is deferred-conservative] / the per-statement Run wiring [TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall / TransformInlineAssignmentLocal / HandleCompoundAssign / TransformPostIncDecOperator / TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore / TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore]) is the next in-order target this foundation unblocks; the UserDefinedCompoundAssign (needs IMethod) and DynamicCompoundAssign (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses stay deferred. |
| D131 | TransformAssignment IsCompoundStore (StLoc case) + IsMatchingCompoundLoad (LdLoc/StLoc case) + ValidateCompoundAssign wrapper (Phase 4 compound-assignment foundation, next-in-order after D130) | port | Ports the shared helpers the next in-order TransformAssignment compound-assignment folds (HandleCompoundAssign / TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore / TransformPostIncDecOperator / TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore) consult, as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (the MatchInstruction / UsingInstruction / NumericCompoundAssign / UnwrapSmallIntegerConv / CheckImplicitTruncation / IsBinaryCompatibleWithType / RecombineVariables precedent), completing the self-contained helper subset D130 flagged as 'the next iteration can port IsCompoundStore's StLoc case + IsMatchingCompoundLoad's LdLoc/StLoc case'. (1) `IsCompoundStore(ILInstruction* inst, ITypePtr& storeType, ILInstruction*& value)` ports the StLoc case of the C# IsCompoundStore: a `stloc V(...)` whose Variable.Kind is Local or Parameter sets `storeType` to V.Type and `value` to the StLoc's Value child (a non-owning view) and returns true. The StObj case (needs InferType for the target's real type, unwrapping a ByReferenceType/PointerType element type via IsCompatibleTypeForMemoryAccess) and the Call case (needs IsSameMember + IMethod for the property-setter gate) are deferred (return false with null outputs). (2) `IsMatchingCompoundLoad(load, store, target&, targetKind&, finalizeMatch&, forbiddenVariable)` ports the LdLoc/StLoc case: when the load is an LdLoc and the store is an StLoc of the same variable (per a file-local `VariablesEqual` faithful port of ILVariableEqualityComparer -- same object, or split fragments with the same Kind + Index; StackSlot/PatternLocal always distinct; this port's ILVariable has no Function field [variables from different functions are distinct shared_ptrs, already handled by the same-object check] and no StateMachineField, so the equality reduces to the Index check, with Index == -1 the synthetic-slot sentinel for the C# Index == null), it sets `target` to a fresh LdLoca of the load's variable, `targetKind` to Address, and `finalizeMatch` to a `std::function<void(ILFunction&)>` callback that calls `ILFunction::RecombineVariables(ldloc.Variable, stloc.Variable)` (the D130 finalizeMatch -- a no-op when they are the same object); `forbiddenVariable` rejects the match when the load/store uses it (some transforms effectively move a store, valid only if the variable is not in the load/store). The LdObj/StObj case (needs IsDuplicatedAddressComputation + the previousInstruction) and the MatchingGetterAndSetterCalls case (needs IMethod/AccessorOwner) are deferred (return false). The `CompoundFinalizeMatch` callback type takes the ILFunction directly (the per-statement Run obtains it via a Parent-chain walk, since the StatementTransformContext carries no function handle); the callback captures the load/store shared_ptrs (outliving the instructions) so it can call RecombineVariables after the match. (3) `ValidateCompoundAssign(const BinaryNumericInstruction* binary, const Conv* conv, const IType* targetType, const ILTransformSettings* settings)` is the full wrapper (the C# TransformAssignment.ValidateCompoundAssign): it calls the D129 `NumericCompoundAssign::IsBinaryCompatibleWithType`, then when a small-integer conv was unwrapped (`conv` != null) it checks `conv->TargetType == ToPrimitiveType(targetType)` and `conv->CheckForOverflow == binary->CheckForOverflow` (the conv must faithfully represent the truncation the compound assign performs); a null `settings` is treated as the defaults (matching the C# constructor Debug.Assert). The helpers are NOT wired into a pipeline transform (no consumer yet -- the full TransformAssignment per-statement Run wiring is the subsequent iteration), so `--csharp` output is unchanged. 17 new gtest cases in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp (4 IsCompoundStore: StLoc Local/Parameter match, StackSlot reject, non-StLoc reject; 7 IsMatchingCompoundLoad: same-variable match [target is a fresh LdLoca, TargetKind Address, finalizeMatch set], split-fragments match [the finalizeMatch fires RecombineVariables, dropping the store variable from the function], forbiddenVariable reject, different-Index reject, different-StackSlots reject [two distinct StackSlot shared_ptrs -- the ILVariableEqualityComparer StackSlot-distinctness; a same-object StackSlot pair matches by reference equality, but that case never arises since IsCompoundStore rejects a StackSlot store first], non-LdLoc-load reject, non-StLoc-store reject; 6 ValidateCompoundAssign: compatible-binary-and-type pass, lifted-binary-over-non-Nullable fail, null-conv pass, matching-conv pass, conv-TargetType-mismatch fail, conv-CheckForOverflow-mismatch fail). 910/910 gtest cases pass (17 new; was 893); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 451512 lines, 517 lock / 313 using, within the D30 non-determinism range; the foundation is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D130). Deferred vs the C#: the StObj/Call cases of IsCompoundStore (need InferType / IsSameMember + IMethod), the LdObj/StObj and getter/setter cases of IsMatchingCompoundLoad (need IsDuplicatedAddressComputation + previousInstruction / IMethod + AccessorOwner), and the per-statement Run wiring (the TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore local/StLoc-case fold -- the simplest wired fold these helpers unblock, which fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc corpus for local post-increment; it operates on a single non-terminal `block.Instructions[pos]` so the if-as-final block-model adaptation is trivial, but it needs a corpus probe of the post-pre-pipeline nested-stloc-in-binary shape per the D73/D75/D79 precedent) are the subsequent in-order targets; the full HandleCompoundAssign StObj/Call cases (the property/field compound assigns) follow once InferType/IsSameMember land. The `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (needs IMethod) and `DynamicCompoundAssign` (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses stay deferred. |

| D132 | TransformAssignment per-statement Run wiring + TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore (binary case) (Phase 4 next-in-order after D131) | port | Ports `TransformAssignment` as an `IStatementTransform` (a child of the GetILTransforms() `StatementTransform`, after `ExpressionTransforms` and before `NullCoalescingTransform` -- the C# order, skipping the deferred `DynamicIsEventAssignmentTransform`) and implements `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` (the binary case), the simplest wired fold the D131 `IsCompoundStore` / `IsMatchingCompoundLoad` / `ValidateCompoundAssign` helpers unblock. The fold recognizes the local/StLoc post-increment/decrement `stloc target(binary.op(stloc tmp(ldloc target), ldc.i4 1))` (a single non-terminal at `block.Instructions[pos]` whose `binary.Left` is the "inline store" `stloc tmp(ldloc target)` -- the compiler's temp that captures the old value and yields it as the binary's left operand) and rewrites it to `stloc tmp(NumericCompoundAssign.op.old(ldloca target, ldc.i4 1))` = `tmp = target++` (the C# `EvaluatesToOldValue` compound assign). The implementation consults the D131 `IsCompoundStore` (the store is a `stloc V` Local/Parameter, `targetType` = V.Type, `value` = the stored value), `UnwrapSmallIntegerConv` (peels a small-integer `conv` truncation, reporting the conv), the IsLdcOne gate (`binary.Right` is `ldc.i4/ldc.i8/ldc.f4/ldc.f8` 1), the Add/Sub-only operator gate, the conv sign-swap (when `GetSize(storeType) == GetSize(conv.TargetType) && GetSign(storeType) != GetSign(conv.TargetType)`, `SwapSign(storeType)` corrects the store type so `ValidateCompoundAssign`'s conv-match gate sees the corrected type), `ValidateCompoundAssign`, the inner-StLoc `Local || StackSlot` gate, `IsMatchingCompoundLoad` (the load `stloc.Value` = `ldloc target` and the store `stloc target` access the same variable -> a fresh `LdLoca(target)` target, `TargetKind` Address, the D130 `RecombineVariables` `finalizeMatch` [a no-op for the common same-variable case], `forbiddenVariable` = tmp), and `IsImplicitTruncation` (the old value must not be implicitly truncated for tmp's type). The `NumericCompoundAssign` is built from the binary's `Operator/CheckForOverflow/Sign/LeftInputType/RightInputType/ResultStackType/IsLifted` fields (the D127 reconciliation) + the fresh `LdLoca` target + the detached `binary.Right` (the constant) + the (possibly sign-swapped) store type, in `EvaluatesToOldValue` mode; the new `StLoc(tmp, nca)` replaces the store at `pos` via `block.SetChild` (the old store, with its now-null-Right binary and the inline-store StLoc, is destroyed -- the `binary.Right` is detached via `std::move` before the destroy, the precondition-before-mutation discipline). The `Run` is gated on `IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement` (default true) and calls `RequestRerunCurrentPosition` after a fold (the C# `context.RequestRerun()` so a later ILInlining pass folds the new top-level stloc). The operator-call (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`) case (needs the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` node + `Call.IsLifted`) and the `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` / `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` / `TransformPostIncDecOperator` (non-inline-store) / `TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` StObj/Call cases (need `InferType` / `IsSameMember` / `IMethod`) are deferred (the operator-call case bails conservatively; the others are not yet implemented). A corpus probe (the `--ilast` dump + a `binary.add(stloc(` grep over the full mscorlib module) found the WithInlineStore shape (`binary.Left` a StLoc) does NOT arise on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (0 `binary.(add|sub)(stloc(` occurrences across the full `--ilast`, 0 `compound.assign...old(` nodes); the legacy csc emits the statement form `stloc V(binary.add(ldloc V, ldc.i4 1))` whose `binary.Left` is an LdLoc -- the deferred `TransformPostIncDecOperator` / `HandleCompoundAssign` shape), so the fold fires 0 times on the corpus -- a faithfulness-only transform matching the D59/D60/D69/D90 precedent (the expression-form `x = V++` post-increment is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern absent from the legacy-csc corpus; the D131 prediction that the fold "fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc corpus for local post-increment" was WRONG -- it conflated the WithInlineStore expression-form shape with the statement-form shape). The `IsImplicitTruncation` conservative approximation (no `InferType`, per D128) means the sign-swap case with an `ldloc` inner value over a small-integer tmp type is conservatively rejected (the C# `InferType(ldloc target)` resolves to the variable's type which fits tmp.Type; the port's else-branch returns `ValueChanged`), a faithfulness gap -- the hand-built sign-swap test uses `tmp.Type = Int32` (not a small integer) so `IsImplicitTruncation(ldloc, Int32)` returns `ValuePreserved` immediately (the sign-swap is on the STORE type `target.Type = Byte`, independent of `tmp.Type`). 9 new gtest cases in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp (8 hand-built: `PostIncDecWithInlineStoreFoldsAdd` [the positive Add fold verifying the result StLoc carries tmp, the NumericCompoundAssign has Operator Add / EvalMode EvaluatesToOldValue / TargetKind Address, the Target is a fresh LdLoca(target), the Value is ldc.i4 1], `FoldsSub`, `RejectsMul`, `RejectsNonOneRight`, `RejectsParameterTmp`, `RejectsNonStLocLeft` [the statement-form `stloc V(binary.add(ldloc V, ..))` whose binary.Left is an LdLoc does not fold -- that is the deferred TransformPostIncDecOperator / HandleCompoundAssign shape], `SettingOffNoOp`, `SmallIntConvSignSwap` [Byte store + conv.i1 -> SByte]; 1 `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` running the full pre-pipeline + `StatementTransform{ILInlining, ExpressionTransforms, TransformAssignment, NullCoalescingTransform, NullableLiftingStatementTransform, NullPropagationStatementTransform}` over 8000 mscorlib methods, asserting the ILAst invariant holds and the per-method NumericCompoundAssign-EvaluatesToOldValue count is monotone non-decreasing). 919/919 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 910); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged, within the D30 non-determinism range; the WithInlineStore fold fires 0 on the corpus so the `++`/`+=` line counts are the seed's StLoc-binary-pattern rendering, not NumericCompoundAssign nodes). Deferred vs the C#: the operator-call (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`) case (needs `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` + `Call.IsLifted`), the `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` / `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` / `TransformPostIncDecOperator` (non-inline-store, the statement-form `stloc V(binary.add(ldloc V, ..))` that DOES arise on the legacy-csc corpus) / `TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` StObj/Call cases (need `InferType` / `IsSameMember` / `IMethod`), the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (needs `IMethod`) and `DynamicCompoundAssign` (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses, and the `IsImplicitTruncation` `InferType` faithfulness gap (the sign-swap + small-integer-tmp case) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D133 | TransformAssignment.TransformPostIncDecOperator (non-inline-store, two-instruction local post-increment/decrement fold, Phase 4 next-in-order after D132) | port | Ports `TransformPostIncDecOperator` (the non-inline-store binary case) into `TransformAssignment` as the second inc/dec fold in the `Run` dispatch (after `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore`, matching the C# `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore || TransformPostIncDecOperator || TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` order, gated on `IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement`). The C# comment: `stloc tmp(ldloc target)` + `stloc target(binary.op(ldloc tmp, ldc.i4 1))` -> `stloc tmp(compound.op.old(ldloca target, ldc.i4 1))` = `tmp = target++`, and the store at the next position is removed; when tmp is dead (single-def, load-count 0) the StLoc is replaced with the compound assign directly (a statement-level `target++`). This pattern occurs with legacy csc for static fields and with Roslyn for most post-increments (the WithInlineStore expression form is Roslyn-era and fires 0 times on the legacy-csc corpus per D132). The implementation: `inst = block.Instructions[pos] as StLoc` (the tmp store), `store = block.Instructions[pos+1]` (the compound store), `IsCompoundStore(store, ...)` (the StLoc case -- Variable.Kind Local/Parameter, targetType = V.Type, value = the stored value), `CheckImplicitTruncation(inst->Value, targetType, ...)` (the ldloc target must not be implicitly truncated for the store type; the ValueChangedDueToSignMismatch case is only fixable for a StObj store which this port does not handle, so it bails), `IsMatchingCompoundLoad(inst->Value, store, ...)` (the load ldloc target and the store stloc target access the same variable -> a fresh LdLoca(target), TargetKind Address, the D130 RecombineVariables finalizeMatch; forbiddenVariable = tmp), `UnwrapSmallIntegerConv(value, conv)` -> the BinaryNumericInstruction, the Add/Sub-only operator gate, `binary.Left.MatchLdLoc(tmpVar)` (the temp capturing the old value), `IsLdcOne(binary->Right)` (the PointerType target case + PointerArithmeticOffset.Detect is deferred; the D129 validator rejects pointer types conservatively anyway), the conv sign-swap (same as WithInlineStore), `ValidateCompoundAssign`. The fold: detach `binary->Right` (the constant 1) before the store is destroyed, build the `NumericCompoundAssign` (EvaluatesToOldValue) from the binary fields + the fresh LdLoca target + the detached constant + the (possibly sign-swapped) store type, `inst->SetChild(0, nca)` (replace the ldloc target with the compound assign), `block.RemoveInstructionAt(nextIdx)` (remove the store, renumbering ChildIndex), `ComputeVariableUsage(*fn)` (recompute after the removal -- the C# InstructionCollection ref-counting cascades `Disconnected()` through the removed store to the `ldloc tmp` inside it, decrementing `tmp.LoadCount`; this port has no ref-counting so the stored counts are stale, and a fresh `ComputeVariableUsage` [the D64 recompute fixpoint precedent] gives the correct `LoadCount` for the dead-tmp check), then if `tmpVar->IsSingleDefinition() && tmpVar->LoadCount == 0` (tmp is dead), `inst->TakeChild(0)` + `inst->ReplaceWith(std::move(val))` (replace the StLoc with the bare NCA = a statement-level `target++`). The operator-call (op_Increment/op_Decrement) case (needs UserDefinedCompoundAssign + Call.IsLifted) and the StObj/Call compound-store cases (need InferType / IsSameMember / IMethod) are deferred (the operator-call case bails conservatively; the StObj/Call cases are not yet implemented). A corpus probe (a temporary gtest running the pre-pipeline through CachedReadOnlySpanInitialization WITHOUT the StatementTransform, counting the two-instruction shape) found 16 occurrences of the `stloc tmp(ldloc target)` + `stloc target(binary.add(ldloc tmp, 1))` shape across 8000 mscorlib methods (and 40 of the single-instruction `stloc target(binary.add(ldloc target, 1))` shape that HandleCompoundAssign leaves as-is for locals -- the C# returns false for StLoc+builtin), so the fold is a real-corpus transform, not faithfulness-only. The CLI `--csharp` output now has `++`/`--` operators (2446 lines with `++`/`--`) instead of the `V = V + 1` form -- a readability improvement (the seed renders a NumericCompoundAssign with EvaluatesToOldValue + Add/Sub + ldc 1 as `target++`/`target--`). 9 new gtest cases in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp (9 hand-built: `PostIncDecFoldsAddDeadTmp` [the positive Add fold with a dead tmp -- the StLoc is replaced with the bare NCA, the store at [1] is gone, the NCA has Operator Add / EvalMode EvaluatesToOldValue / TargetKind Address / Target LdLoca(target) / Value ldc.i4 1], `PostIncDecFoldsSubDeadTmp` [the Sub fold with a dead tmp], `PostIncDecFoldsAddLiveTmp` [tmp is live -- the leave final returns tmp, so the StLoc survives as `stloc tmp(target++)`], `PostIncDecRejectsMul` [non-Add/Sub operator], `PostIncDecRejectsNonOneRight` [right operand is not 1], `PostIncDecRejectsNonStLocStore` [the store at [1] is not a StLoc], `PostIncDecRejectsNonStLocInst` [the inst at [0] is not a StLoc], `PostIncDecRejectsNonMatchingLeft` [binary.Left is not ldloc tmp], `PostIncDecSettingOffNoOp` [IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement off]; plus the updated `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` now asserting `totalFolds > 0` -- the transform fires on the corpus, not just faithfulness-only). 928/928 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 919); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged; the `++`/`--` operators now appear in the output, 2446 lines, up from the seed StLoc-binary rendering). Deferred vs the C#: the operator-call (op_Increment/op_Decrement) case (needs UserDefinedCompoundAssign + Call.IsLifted), the `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` / `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` / `TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` StObj/Call cases (need InferType / IsSameMember / IMethod), the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (needs IMethod) and `DynamicCompoundAssign` (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses, and the `IsImplicitTruncation` InferType faithfulness gap (the sign-swap + small-integer-tmp case) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D134 | TransformAssignment.TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore (local/StLoc pre-increment/decrement inline-store expression fold, Phase 4 next-in-order after D133) | port | Ports `TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` (the binary case) into `TransformAssignment` as the third inc/dec fold in the `Run` dispatch (after `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` and `TransformPostIncDecOperator`, matching the C# `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore || TransformPostIncDecOperator || TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` order, gated on `IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement`). The C# comment: `stloc outer(stloc inner(binary.op(ldloc target, ldc.i4 1)))` -> `stloc outer(compound.op.new(ldloca target, ldc.i4 1))` = `outer = ++target` (the C# `EvaluatesToNewValue` compound assign). This is the pre-increment analog of the D132 `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` (which was post-increment, `EvaluatesToOldValue`); the key difference is a DOUBLE `IsCompoundStore` -- the outer store's Value is the inner `stloc target` (the inline-store expression form, where a StLoc as a value position yields its stored value), and `binary.Left` is a bare `ldloc target` (not a StLoc like the D132 post-increment case). The implementation: `store = block.Instructions[pos]` (the outer `stloc outer`), `IsCompoundStore(store, targetType1, value1)` (the StLoc case -- Variable.Kind Local/Parameter, targetType1 = outer.Type, value1 = the stored value = the inner `stloc target`), `IsCompoundStore(value1, targetType2, value2)` (value1 is also a StLoc -- Variable.Kind Local/Parameter, targetType2 = target.Type, value2 = the binary), `targetType1 == targetType2` (the C# reference equality is ported as structural equality via `IType::Equals`, the faithful equivalent since the C# type system deduplicates IType instances -- the port does not; a null type on either side fails the match), `stloc_outer = store as StLoc`, `stloc_inner = value1 as StLoc`, `UnwrapSmallIntegerConv(value2, conv)` -> the BinaryNumericInstruction, the Add/Sub-only + `IsLdcOne(binary->Right)` gate (the float 1.0 cases MatchLdcF4/F8 are covered by the same `IsLdcOne` helper), the conv sign-swap (same as the D132/D133 PostIncDec cases -- `SwapSign` when the store type and the conv's target have the same size but opposite sign), `ValidateCompoundAssign`, `ldloc = binary.Left as LdLoc`, the `stloc_outer.Variable.Kind == Local || StackSlot` guard, `IsMatchingCompoundLoad(ldloc, stloc_inner, ...)` (the load ldloc target and the inner store stloc target access the same variable -> a fresh LdLoca(target), TargetKind Address, the D130 RecombineVariables finalizeMatch; no forbiddenVariable, matching the C#), `IsImplicitTruncation(stloc_outer.Value, stloc_outer.Variable.Type, ...)` (the outer store's Value = the inner StLoc must not be implicitly truncated for the outer variable's type; for a non-small-integer outer type like Int32 the outer guard returns ValuePreserved immediately, so the check passes; for a small-integer outer the conservative else-branch [no InferType] returns ValueChanged, rejecting the fold -- a faithfulness gap, not a bug). The fold: `finalizeMatch?.invoke(context)` (via FunctionOf), detach `binary->Right` (the constant 1) before the outer store is destroyed by `SetChild` (no GC -- the binary is inside stloc_inner inside stloc_outer inside block.Instructions[pos]; a raw pointer into the binary would dangle), build the `NumericCompoundAssign` (EvaluatesToNewValue) from the binary fields + the fresh LdLoca target + the detached constant + the (possibly sign-swapped) store type, `block.SetChild(pos, StLoc(outerVar, nca))` (replace the store; the old store with its inner stloc and the now-null-Right binary is destroyed). The operator-call (op_Increment/op_Decrement) case (needs UserDefinedCompoundAssign + Call.IsLifted) is deferred (bails conservatively). A corpus probe is not needed: the pre-increment expression form (`x = ++target`) is a Roslyn-era codegen pattern that fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the legacy csc emits the statement form `stloc target(binary.add(ldloc target, 1))` whose `binary.Left` is an LdLoc and which HandleCompoundAssign leaves as-is for locals -- the C# returns false for StLoc+builtin; the D133 corpus probe confirmed 0 `binary.(add|sub)(stloc(` WithInlineStore shapes and 40 single-instruction `stloc target(binary.add(ldloc target, 1))` shapes that stay as `target = target + 1`), so the fold is faithfulness-only on this corpus -- matching the D59/D60/D69/D132 precedent (the hand-built tests verify the rewrite, the sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds and the per-method NewValue-NCA count is 0 on the corpus). 8 new gtest cases in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp (8 hand-built: `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreFoldsAdd` [the positive Add fold -- the result StLoc carries the outer variable, the NCA has Operator Add / EvalMode EvaluatesToNewValue / TargetKind Address / Target LdLoca(target) / Value ldc.i4 1, the inner stloc is eliminated], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreFoldsSub` [the Sub fold = `outer = --target`], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreRejectsMul` [non-Add/Sub operator], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreRejectsNonOneRight` [right operand is not 1], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreRejectsMismatchedTargetType` [outer.Type != target.Type -- Int32 vs Int64], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreRejectsStackSlotOuter` [the outer variable is a StackSlot, not Local/Parameter -- the stloc_outer.Variable.Kind guard rejects it], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreRejectsNonMatchingLdLoc` [binary.Left loads a different variable than the inner stloc's -- IsMatchingCompoundLoad rejects], `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreSettingOffNoOp` [IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement off]; plus the updated `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` now counting `EvaluatesToNewValue` NCAs and asserting `totalNewFolds == 0` -- the pre-increment expression form does not arise on the legacy-csc corpus, so no NewValue NCAs are produced). 936/936 gtest cases pass (8 new; was 928); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged, the `++`/`--` operators from D133 unchanged at 2446 lines since the PreIncDec form fires 0 times on the corpus). Deferred vs the C#: the operator-call (op_Increment/op_Decrement) case (needs UserDefinedCompoundAssign + Call.IsLifted), the `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` / `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` StObj/Call cases (need InferType / IsSameMember / IMethod), the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (needs IMethod) and `DynamicCompoundAssign` (needs dynamic nodes) subclasses, and the `IsImplicitTruncation` InferType faithfulness gap (the sign-swap + small-integer-outer case) are the subsequent in-order targets. |

| D135 | UserDefinedCompoundAssign ILAst node + Call::IsLifted + IsIncrementOrDecrement/IsStringConcat helpers + CheckedOperators setting (Phase 4 compound-assignment foundation, next-in-order after D134) | port | Ports the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` ILAst node (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/CompoundAssignmentInstruction.hpp, from the generated Instructions.cs + the hand-written CompoundAssignmentInstruction.cs) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, ahead of the operator-call (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`) case of the TransformAssignment increment/decrement folds that D134 listed as its first deferred target, following the NumericCompoundAssign (D125) / MatchInstruction (D70) / UsingInstruction (D74) precedent. The node is the C# user-defined-operator compound assignment: a compound assign built from a user-defined operator call (`target op= value` for a 2-arg operator, or `target++`/`++target`/`target--`/`--target` for `op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`). This port has no `IMethod`, so the C# `readonly IMethod Method` is modelled as the resolved method name (`std::string`, "Namespace.Type::Method") + the declaring type (`ITypePtr`) + the return StackType (`Method.ReturnType.GetStackType()` drives `ResultType`), mirroring how `Call` carries method metadata. `IsLifted` is hardcoded false (faithful to the C# `public bool IsLifted => false; // TODO: implement lifted user-defined compound assignments`); it is a settable field so a future resolver-backed path can mark a lifted user-defined operator. `DirectFlags`/`Flags` add `SideEffect | MayThrow` (faithful to the C# generated overrides -- a user-defined operator call can throw), unlike `NumericCompoundAssign` which only adds `SideEffect` (+ `MayThrow` for div/rem/ovf). The dump is `compound.assign.userdefined.<suffix>(<method>, target, value)` (the family-consistent `compound.assign` root the NumericCompoundAssign node uses, with a `.userdefined` marker; the C# root is `user.compound`). The `IsIncrementOrDecrement(const Call*, const ILTransformSettings*)` and `IsStringConcat(const Call*)` static helpers are the gates the folds consult on the operator `Call` before building the node (the C# takes the `IMethod`; this port takes the `Call`, which carries the method metadata -- `IsOperator`, `IsInstanceCall`, `MethodName`, `DeclaringType`). `IsIncrementOrDecrement` ports the C# faithfully: a static operator overload (`call->IsOperator && !call->IsInstanceCall`) named `op_Increment`/`op_Decrement` is always recognised; `op_CheckedIncrement`/`op_CheckedDecrement` are recognised only when the new `CheckedOperators` setting is on (the C# `settings?.CheckedOperators ?? true`; a null settings is permissive). `IsStringConcat` ports the C# `method.Name == "Concat" && method.IsStatic && method.DeclaringType.IsKnownType(String)` via the short method name (after `::`) + `!IsInstanceCall` + the D93 `NullableLiftingTransform::IsKnownType(DeclaringType, String)`. `Call` gained an `IsLifted` flag (default false): the C# `CallInstruction.IsLifted` is `Method is CSharp.Resolver.ILiftedOperator`, a resolver-level concept (the C# resolver wraps a lifted operator's method in an `ILiftedOperator` marker); this port has no resolver/`ILiftedOperator`, so the field defaults false and the IL reader does not populate it -- the inc/dec folds bail on a lifted operator call with `if (operatorCall.IsLifted) return false; // TODO`, so a default-false call never trips that bail (faithful for the common case; lifted user-defined operators are rare). The `CheckedOperators` setting (C# 11.0, default true, matching `DecompilerSettings.CheckedOperators`) is added to `ILTransformSettings`. The ILAstToCSharp seed renders a `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (a new `OpCode::UserDefinedCompoundAssign` Expr case + an EmitStatement case mirroring NumericCompoundAssign) as the unary `target++`/`++target`/`target--`/`--target` (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`/`op_CheckedIncrement`/`op_CheckedDecrement`; postfix for `EvaluatesToOldValue`, prefix for `EvaluatesToNewValue`) or the binary `target op= value` (`op_Addition` -> `+=`, `op_Subtraction` -> `-=`, `op_Multiply` -> `*=`, `op_Division` -> `/=`, `op_Modulus` -> `%=`, `op_BitwiseAnd` -> `&=`, `op_BitwiseOr` -> `|=`, `op_ExclusiveOr` -> `^=`, `op_LeftShift` -> `<<=`, `op_RightShift` -> `>>=`, `op_UnsignedRightShift` -> `>>>=`; `string.Concat` -> `+=`), matching the real back end's `VisitUserDefinedCompoundAssign` (`GetUnaryOperatorTypeFromMetadataName` / `GetAssignmentOperatorTypeFromMetadataName`). The node + helpers are NOT wired into a pipeline transform (no consumer yet -- the operator-call case of the inc/dec folds is the subsequent iteration), so `--csharp` output is unchanged. The `OpCode::UserDefinedCompoundAssign` value was pre-declared in OpCode.hpp, so porting the node needed only the subclass header (the ThreeValuedBoolAnd/Or D95 / NumericCompoundAssign D125 precedent). 27 new gtest cases in CompoundAssignmentInstruction_Test.cpp's new `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` suite (node invariant/flags/ResultType/dump; the `.property.new` dump suffix; `IsLifted` is false; children in typed slots + re-parenting; flags propagation; 8 `IsIncrementOrDecrement` cases -- op_Increment/op_Decrement/checked-on/checked-off/null-settings-permissive/non-operator/instance/op_Addition rejects + null call; 5 `IsStringConcat` cases -- String-Concat/non-String/instance/wrong-name rejects + null call; 7 seed-rendering cases -- post/pre increment/decrement, checked increment, op_Addition `+=`, string.Concat `+=`; `CheckedOperators` default; and an 8000-method mscorlib sweep constructing the nodes from real operator calls -- System.Decimal's op_Equality/op_Addition/etc. -- asserting the invariant/dump/ResultType hold), plus the updated `AssignmentSettingsDefaultTrue` asserting `CheckedOperators` defaults true. 963/963 gtest cases pass (was 936; +27); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- no `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` nodes are constructed yet so the output is byte-identical to D134). Deferred vs the C#: the operator-call (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`) case of the inc/dec folds (`TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` / `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` / `TransformPostIncDecOperator`) -- the wired fold that consumes this foundation, which builds a `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` from a 1-arg operator `Call` (gated on `IsIncrementOrDecrement` + `!call->IsLifted`); the `HandleCompoundAssign` 2-arg-operator case and the string.Concat case (build a `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` from a 2-arg operator `Call` / a `Concat` call); the `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` / `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` StObj/Call cases (need `InferType` / `IsSameMember` / `IMethod`); the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` lifted-operator case (the C# `IsLifted => false` TODO); and the `DynamicCompoundAssign` subclass (needs dynamic nodes) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D136 | TransformAssignment operator-call (op_Increment/op_Decrement) case of all three inc/dec folds (Phase 4 next-in-order after D135) | port | Wires the D135 `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` foundation into the per-statement `TransformAssignment` `Run` by adding the operator-call (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`) case to all three inc/dec folds -- `TransformPostIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore`, `TransformPostIncDecOperator`, and `TransformPreIncDecOperatorWithInlineStore` -- each of which previously had only the binary case (D132/D133/D134) ported and bailed conservatively on a non-binary value. Each fold now recognises a 1-arg operator `Call` (the compiler's `call op_Increment`/`op_Decrement` codegen for a user-defined-operator-type `++`/`--`, e.g. `System.Decimal`'s `op_Increment`) where the binary case matched, and builds a `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` (D135) from the operator Call's resolved method metadata instead of a `NumericCompoundAssign` from a `BinaryNumericInstruction`. A file-local `ShortMethodName(std::string_view)` helper (mirroring the `NullableLiftingTransform`/`CompoundAssignmentInstruction.cpp` helpers) extracts the short method name (after `::`) for the bare-name checks. The `TransformPostIncDecOperator` fold shares the common tail (the `RemoveInstructionAt` of the store at `pos+1`, the `ComputeVariableUsage` recompute, the dead-tmp `ReplaceWith` for a statement-level `target++`); the `Debug.Assert(truncation == ValuePreserved)` the C# has for the operator-call case holds trivially since the `ValueChanged`/`ValueChangedDueToSignMismatch` cases already returned false above. The operator-call (`op_Increment`/`op_Decrement`) inc/dec fold fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (a corpus probe over 8000 methods found 0 `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` nodes after the full per-statement pipeline -- the legacy csc does not emit `call op_Increment` for `++` on user-defined-operator types in this corpus; the `NumericCompoundAssign`-OldValue count is 151, confirming the D133 binary fold fires on the corpus), so the fold is faithfulness-only on this corpus -- matching the D59/D60/D69/D90/D132 precedent (the hand-built tests verify the rewrite, the sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds). The `IsImplicitTruncation` conservative approximation (no `InferType`, per D128) means a small-integer target with an unmodeled RHS is conservatively rejected, a faithfulness gap shared with the binary cases. 10 new gtest cases in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp (`PostIncDecWithInlineStoreOperatorCallFoldsIncrement` / `FoldsDecrement` [the WithInlineStore operator-call fold -- the result StLoc carries tmp, the UCA has EvalMode EvaluatesToOldValue / TargetKind Address / Target LdLoca(target) / Value ldc.i4 1 / MethodName the operator], `RejectsCheckedVariant` [op_CheckedIncrement is NOT accepted by the inline-store bare-name check], `RejectsLifted` [Call::IsLifted set true bails]; `PostIncDecOperatorCallFoldsIncrementDeadTmp` [the non-inline-store operator-call with a dead tmp -> a statement-level `target++` UCA], `FoldsDecrementLiveTmp` [tmp live -> `stloc tmp(target--)`], `FoldsCheckedIncrement` [op_CheckedIncrement IS accepted by the non-inline-store IsIncrementOrDecrement, gated on CheckedOperators], `RejectsNonMatchingArg` [the operator's argument is not ldloc tmp]; `PreIncDecWithInlineStoreOperatorCallFoldsIncrement` / `FoldsDecrement` [the PreIncDec operator-call fold -- the result StLoc carries outer, the UCA has EvalMode EvaluatesToNewValue]), plus the updated `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` counting `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` nodes and asserting `totalUserDefinedFolds == 0` (the operator-call fold fires 0 times on the legacy-csc corpus). 973/973 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 963); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- the operator-call fold fires 0 times on the corpus so the output is byte-identical to D135).
