| D137 | TransformAssignment.TransformInlineAssignmentLocal (the inline-assignment-to-local fold, Phase 4 next-in-order after D136) | port | Ports `TransformAssignment.TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` (the C# `stloc s(value)` + `stloc l(ldloc s)` -> `stloc s(stloc l(value))` inline-assignment-to-local fold, the next in-order `TransformAssignment` piece the D136 deferral listed) as an `IStatementTransform` `Run` dispatch wired into the CLI pipeline, adapted to the port's block model. The C# `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` (the simplest of the two `TransformInlineAssignment*` folds -- the self-contained sibling of `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall`, which needs `InferType` / `IsSameMember` / `IMethod` for the property/field inline-assign and stays deferred) folds the compiler's stack-temp-to-local copy: `stloc s(value)` at `Instructions[i]` (s a `StackSlot` evaluation-stack temp) + `stloc l(ldloc s)` at `Instructions[i+1]` (l a `Local` or `Parameter`) into the inline-assignment expression `stloc s(stloc l(value))` so a later transform can treat the whole thing as an assignment expression. The implementation consults only self-contained helpers already in place: `MatchLdLoc` (the `nextInst.Value` must be an `LdLoc` of `inst->Variable`, via `dynamic_cast<LdLoc*>` + `ldloc->Variable.get() == inst->Variable.get()` -- the same match-against-variable pattern the inc/dec folds use), the D128 `IsImplicitTruncation` (the value must not be implicitly truncated for s's type or l's type), `VariableKind` (s must be `StackSlot`, l must be `Local`/`Parameter`), and `StackTypeOf` (the C# `nextInst.Variable.StackType == StackType.Ref` reject, since `ILVariable` has no `StackType` member -- it is `Type.GetStackType()`, so the port computes it via `StackTypeOf(Variable->Type.get())`). The block-model adaptation: detach `inst->Value` via `std::move(inst->Value)` before `RemoveInstructionAt(pos)` destroys `inst` (no GC -- a raw pointer into `inst` would dangle; `inst->Value` is the StLoc's owning `unique_ptr` member, not a child via `SetChild`, so `std::move` takes ownership cleanly and leaves `inst` with `ChildCount` 0), capture `stackVar`/`var` (shared_ptr copies outliving the StLocs), `block.RemoveInstructionAt(pos)` (destroys the empty `inst`; `nextInst` shifts down to `pos` and its `ChildIndex` is renumbered by `RenumberChildren`, so the subsequent `ReplaceWith` -- which uses `Parent` + `ChildIndex` -- targets the correct slot), then `nextInst->ReplaceWith(make_unique<StLoc>(stackVar, make_unique<StLoc>(var, std::move(value))))` replaces the shifted `nextInst` with the inline-assignment expression. The `Run` gate is updated to the faithful C# top-level gate (`!MakeAssignmentExpressions || !IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement` returns early -- the C# gates the whole Run on BOTH settings, not just `IntroduceIncrementAndDecrement` the D132-only port used; both default true so no existing test breaks), and `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` is dispatched BEFORE the inc/dec folds (the C# order: `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall || TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` first, then the inc/dec folds; the deferred `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` is omitted so only `TransformInlineAssignmentLocal` runs). The inline-assignment fold does NOT call `RequestRerunCurrentPosition` (the C# `return` after the inline-assignment folds; only the inc/dec folds request a rerun). The fold fires 104 times across the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` `CountInlineAssignmentLocal` -- a `stloc s(StackSlot)` whose `Value` is a `stloc l(Local|Parameter)` -- asserts `EXPECT_GT(totalInlineAssignFolds, 0)`, a real-corpus transform unlike most recent nullable-family faithfulness-only pieces), and the inc/dec fold's `NumericCompoundAssign`-OldValue count is UNCHANGED at 151 (the inline-assignment fold's pattern `stloc l(ldloc s)` is disjoint from the inc/dec fold's pattern `stloc target(binary.op(ldloc tmp, 1))` -- the second store's value is a load in the inline-assignment case but a binary in the inc/dec case, so the two never compete). The CLI `--csharp` `++`/`--` line count drops from ~2446 (D134/D135/D136) to ~2200 -- a faithful consequence: the inline-assignment fold merges 104 stack-temp-to-local copies into expression form, changing the ILAst shape the later ILInlining/rendering sees (the loop `i++` rendering is non-deterministic ~7/run and the fold's shape change shifts it); the deterministic `NumericCompoundAssign`-OldValue count (151) is unchanged, confirming the inc/dec fold is not consumed. 9 new gtest cases in TransformAssignment_Test.cpp (`InlineAssignmentLocalFoldsLocal` [the positive fold -- the two instructions merge into `stloc s(stloc l(ldc.i4 5))`; the outer StLoc carries the StackSlot s, the inner StLoc carries the Local l and the value], `InlineAssignmentLocalFoldsParameter` [l is a Parameter, the C# accepts Local||Parameter], `InlineAssignmentLocalRejectsNonStackSlotS` [s is a Local, not a StackSlot], `InlineAssignmentLocalRejectsNonLocalL` [l is a StackSlot, not a Local/Parameter], `InlineAssignmentLocalRejectsNonLdLocValue` [nextInst.Value is a LdcI4, not a load of s], `InlineAssignmentLocalRejectsNonMatchingLdLoc` [nextInst.Value loads a different variable than s], `InlineAssignmentLocalRejectsRefLocal` [l is a `ByReferenceType` ref local -- `StackType::Ref` rejects], `InlineAssignmentLocalMakeAssignmentExpressionsOffNoOp` [the gate], `InlineAssignmentLocalIntroduceIncDecOffNoOp` [the C# top-level gate requires BOTH settings]), plus the `MakeInlineAssignLocal` test helper and the `CountInlineAssignmentLocal` sweep helper, the updated `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` asserting `EXPECT_GT(totalInlineAssignFolds, 0)`, and the `ByReferenceType` using-declaration. 982/982 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 973); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged; the `++`/`--` line count drops to ~2200 as a faithful consequence of the 104 inline-assignment folds changing the ILAst shape). Deferred vs the C#: the `TransformInlineAssignmentStObjOrCall` StObj/Call inline-assign (needs `InferType` for the StObj target's real type via `IsCompatibleTypeForMemoryAccess` + `IsSameMember`/`IMethod` for the property-setter gate + the `BlockKind::CallInlineAssign` inline-block shape + `HandleCompoundAssign` on the resulting call), the `HandleCompoundAssign` entry (the StObj/Call compound-assign, needs the StObj/Call `IsCompoundStore` cases [InferType/IsSameMember/IMethod] + the LdObj/StObj and getter/setter `IsMatchingCompoundLoad` cases [IsDuplicatedAddressComputation/IMethod/AccessorOwner] -- the 2-arg-operator and string.Concat cases D136 flagged are blocked by these same StObj/Call prerequisites), the `UserDefinedCompoundAssign` lifted-operator case (the C# `IsLifted => false` TODO), and the `DynamicCompoundAssign` subclass (needs dynamic nodes) are the subsequent in-order targets. |

| D139 | UserDefinedLogicTransform (LegacyPattern + MatchCondition/MatchBitwiseCall, Phase 4 next-in-order after D138) | port | Ports `UserDefinedLogicTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/UserDefinedLogicTransform.{hpp,cpp}, from ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/UserDefinedLogicTransform.cs) as an `IStatementTransform` wired into the CLI `StatementTransform` pipeline as the 7th child (after `ILInlining` / `ExpressionTransforms` / `TransformAssignment` / `NullCoalescingTransform` / `NullableLiftingStatementTransform` / `NullPropagationStatementTransform`, the GetILTransforms() order skipping the deferred `TransformArrayInitializers` / `TransformCollectionAndObjectInitializers` / `TransformExpressionTrees` / `IndexRangeTransform` / `DeconstructionTransform` / `NamedArgumentTransform` / `RemoveUnconstrainedGenericReferenceTypeCheck` and before `InterpolatedStringTransform`), consuming the D138 `UserDefinedLogicOperator` node foundation. The C# `UserDefinedLogicTransform` constructs `UserDefinedLogicOperator` nodes (the C# user-defined short-circuiting `&&` / `||` operator, the `op_BitwiseAnd` / `op_BitwiseOr` overloads paired with `op_True` / `op_False`) from two block-tail shapes: the `LegacyPattern` (the legacy-csc shape) and the `RoslynOptimized` pattern (the "in combination with return statement" shape). This iteration ports the `LegacyPattern` and the shared `MatchCondition` / `MatchBitwiseCall` static helpers; the `RoslynOptimized` pattern and the C# `Transform` static method are deferred (see below). `MatchCondition(condition, out v, out conditionMethodName)` recognises `call op_True/op_False(ldloc v)` -- a `Call` with `IsOperator` (D108), `Arguments.size() == 1`, `!IsLifted` (D135), the short method name (after `::`) `op_True`/`op_False`, and `Arguments[0]` an `LdLoc` of `v`; faithful to the C# `MatchCondition` (which checks `call.Method.IsOperator`, `Arguments.Count == 1`, `!call.IsLifted`, the name, and `call.Arguments[0].MatchLdLoc(out v)` -- the C# does NOT check `IsStatic`, so the port does not check `IsInstanceCall`). `MatchBitwiseCall(call, v, conditionMethodName)` recognises `call op_BitwiseAnd/op_BitwiseOr(ldloc v, rhs)` -- a `Call` with `IsOperator`, `Arguments.size() == 2`, `!IsLifted`, `Arguments[0]` an `LdLoc` of `v`, and the name matches the condition (`op_False` -> `op_BitwiseAnd`, `op_True` -> `op_BitwiseOr`); faithful to the C# `MatchBitwiseCall`. `LegacyPattern(block, pos, context)`: the legacy-csc shape `stloc s(lhsInst)` + `if (logic.not(call op_False(ldloc s))) Block { stloc s(call op_BitwiseAnd(ldloc s, rhsInst)) }` -> `stloc s(user.logic op_BitwiseAnd(lhsInst, rhsInst))`, adapted to this port's if-as-final block model (the C# reads `block.Instructions[pos]` as the stloc and `block.Instructions[pos+1]` as the if [a non-terminal]; this port makes the `IfInstruction` the block's `FinalInstruction`, so the if is `block->FinalInstruction` and "removing" it = replacing the if-final with a `Branch` to the next block [the positional fall-through the if's null `FalseInst` represented -- the legacy pattern has no else; the else is the fall-through to the use], the D90 `NullCoalescingTransform` / D113 `TransformThrowExpressionValueTypes` if-as-final precedent). The condition is `logic.not(call op_False(ldloc s))` = this port's `comp(Equality, call op_False(ldloc s), ldc.i4 0)` shape (the reader's brfalse); a file-local `MatchLogicNot` unwraps the `logic.not`. The true arm is a `Block` (or a bare `StLoc` ConditionDetection unwrapped) carrying `stloc s(call op_BitwiseAnd(ldloc s, rhsInst))`; a file-local `UnwrapLegacyTrueInst` handles both the bare-`StLoc` case and the `Block`-with-one-instruction-plus-`Branch`-final case (the C# `Block.Unwrap` returns the single-instruction expression Block; this port's Block carries an explicit `Branch` final [the fall-through to the join], so the `NullCoalescingTransform` `BlockUnwrap` [which requires no final] does not apply). The `s.IsUsedWithin(call.Arguments[1])` reject (the rhs must not reference `s`, or short-circuiting would change semantics -- the user logic operator only evaluates the rhs when the lhs is "false", so an rhs that reads `s` would observe a different value) is ported as a tree walk (`IsUsedWithin`) over the variable-bearing opcodes (`LdLoc`/`LdLoca`/`StLoc`), matching the D110 `TransformCatchVariable` `CollectCatchVariableUses` / D130 `RecombineVariables` `ReassignUses` precedent (this port never built the C# per-variable `LoadInstructions`/`StoreInstructions`/`AddressInstructions` lists). The fold detaches `lhsInst` (the stloc's `Value`, via `TakeChild(0)`) and `rhsInst` (the call's `Arguments[1]`, via `TakeChild(1)`) before the old nodes are destroyed by `SetFinal` (no GC; raw pointers into the call would dangle -- the precondition-before-mutation discipline), builds a `UserDefinedLogicOperator(call->MethodName, call->DeclaringType, lhsInst, rhsInst)`, sets `stloc->Value` to it, and replaces the if-final with a `Branch` to `NextBlockInContainer`. The C# `context.RequestRerun()` (so a later `ILInlining` pass folds the now-single-use `stloc s` into its load) ports as `context.RequestRerunCurrentPosition()`. Deferred vs the C#: the `RoslynOptimized` pattern (the "in combination with return statement" shape whose if has `leave` early-return arms + a trailing `leave` as the fall-through) needs a pre-pipeline corpus probe of the real post-`ConditionDetection` form (the `MatchIfInstructionPositiveCondition` + `MatchReturn` shape in this port's block model, where the if-with-leave-arms + trailing-leave interaction is not predictable from the C# structure -- the D73/D75/D79/D113 precedent), and the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus carries no `op_True`/`op_False` operator definitions so neither pattern fires on it anyway; the C# `Transform` static method (the shared core that builds a `UserDefinedLogicOperator` from a condition + a true arm [ldloc] + a false arm [the bitwise call], used by `RoslynOptimized`) is deferred because it would need a general `Clone` (the C# `new UserDefinedLogicOperator(call.Method, call.Arguments[0], call.Arguments[1])` passes the call's argument instructions, kept alive by GC; this port's `ILInstruction` has no virtual `Clone` [D48], so the `LegacyPattern` inlines the construction with a `TakeChild` detach instead -- the `Transform` helper lands with the `RoslynOptimized` pattern it is the helper for); the `TransformDynamic` path (needs `DynamicUnaryOperatorInstruction` / `DynamicBinaryOperatorInstruction` / `DynamicLogicOperatorInstruction` / `Box` + the resolver); and the `UserDefinedLogicOperator` lifted case stay deferred. The `LegacyPattern` fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (a user-defined short-circuiting `&&` / `||` is a rare C# feature; mscorlib has no operator-overloading types that define `op_True`/`op_False`), so it is a faithfulness-only transform on this corpus, matching the `DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks` (D59) / `LdLocaDupInitObj` (D60) / `SwitchOnNullable` (D69) precedent -- the hand-built tests verify the rewrite and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (not a fold count). 25 new gtest cases in a new `UserDefinedLogicTransform_Test.cpp` (7 `MatchCondition` -- op_False/op_True positives + non-operator/lifted/wrong-arg-count/wrong-name/non-LdLoc-arg/non-Call rejects; 6 `MatchBitwiseCall` -- op_BitwiseAnd-with-op_False / op_BitwiseOr-with-op_True positives + op_BitwiseAnd-with-op_True mismatch / non-LdLoc-first-arg / wrong-arg-count / lifted rejects; 10 `LegacyPattern` -- the `&&` fold [the stloc's Value is the `UserDefinedLogicOperator`, the if-final is a `Branch` to the next block, the Left is the lhs load, the Right is the rhs load] + the `||` fold + 8 negatives [non-StackSlot s, non-if final, non-logic.not condition, condition on a different variable, an if with an else arm, an rhs that references s, a true arm storing to a different variable, a non-bitwise-call true arm]; and `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` running the full pre-pipeline + the full per-statement `StatementTransform` [now with `UserDefinedLogicTransform`] over 8000 mscorlib methods, asserting the ILAst invariant holds and the `UserDefinedLogicOperator` count is 0). 1015/1015 gtest cases pass (25 new; was 990); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged; no `UserDefinedLogicOperator` nodes are constructed on this corpus so the output is byte-identical to D138). The remaining in-order per-statement children (`TransformArrayInitializers` / `TransformCollectionAndObjectInitializers` / `TransformExpressionTrees` / `IndexRangeTransform` / `DeconstructionTransform` / `NamedArgumentTransform` / `RemoveUnconstrainedGenericReferenceTypeCheck` / `InterpolatedStringTransform`), the `RoslynOptimized` pattern, and the remaining `TransformAssignment` StObj/Call pieces (blocked by `InferType` / `IsSameMember` / `IMethod`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D140 | HighLevelLoopTransform.Run (MatchWhileLoop) + LoopDetection exit-path materialization + While-container rendering | port | Ports the MatchWhileLoop subset of HighLevelLoopTransform.Run: a Loop container whose entry point's if is the while condition becomes a While container, rendering as `while (cond) { body }` instead of `while (true) { ...; if (cond) break; }`. Two shapes handled: (1) the C# shape `if (cond) leave loop` (TrueInst a Leave, no else) -- the break condition, negated to get the while condition; (2) this port's LoopDetection shape `if (cond) br body else leave(loop)` (TrueInst a Branch to the body, FalseInst a Leave) -- the continue condition, no negation. Also fixes LoopDetection.ConstructLoop: the new loop entry point's while-condition if (`if (cond) br body`) had its fall-through going to the body (the next block in the container) instead of the exit, losing the loop exit; now when the if's true arm branches to a block that will be inside the loop, a `leave(loop)` is added as the false arm (the break), materializing the exit path. Adds ContainerKind::While and the seed's While-container rendering (extracts the condition from the entry's if, renders the body blocks after the entry, drops trailing back-edge branches and labels on the body entry). The CLI runs HighLevelLoopTransform::Run after the StatementTransform, before AssignVariableNames. MatchForLoop and MatchDoWhileLoop are deferred. System.Array.CreateInstance now shows `while (num < lengths.Length) { ... }` instead of `while (true) { if (num < lengths.Length) { goto body; } else { break; } ... }`. |
| D141 | ILAstToCSharp seed polish batch: byref/this deref, loop continue/break, C# keyword types, bool literals, operators, accessors, this.-less fields, ref, parens | port | A batch of ILAstToCSharp rendering improvements that make the `--csharp` output read like idiomatic C#: (1) `ldobj/stobj` on a byref variable or the implicit `this` renders as the bare name (`*(this)` -> `this`, `*(array)` -> `array`); (2) a Loop-kind container renders as `while (true) { ... }` with mid-loop back-edges as `continue`, loop-exit leaves as `break`, and the loop header unlabeled; (3) `CSharpTypeName` (now public) maps KnownTypeCode to C# keywords and recurses into array/byref/pointer, used for local declarations, the method signature, newarr, casts, and `as` (`int num`, `byte[] array`, `new byte[1]`, `(string)x`, `byte* ptr`); (4) `comp(eq, ldloc boolVar, 0)` renders as `!boolVar` for Boolean-typed variables; (5) `stloc(boolVar, ldc.i4 0/1)` and `return ldc.i4 0/1` in a bool function render as `false`/`true`; (6) static binary operator calls (op_Equality etc.) render symbolically (`a == b`), unary operators (`-x`, `!x`, `~x`, `++x`), and conversion operators (`(decimal)x`); (7) `get_`/`set_` accessor calls render as property access (`receiver.Major` / `receiver.Major = value`); (8) instance field accesses drop the `this.` prefix (`_Major` not `this._Major`); (9) `ldloca` renders as `ref V` (C# byref syntax); (10) redundant outer parens stripped from `if`/`while` conditions and `as` assignment values (`if (value >= 0)` not `if ((value >= 0))`); (11) `0 - x` renders as `-x`; (12) `conv.i4(ldlen)` renders as `arr.Length` (no redundant `(int)` cast). Together these make simple methods decompile to clean idiomatic C# (e.g. `int Sign(int value) { if (value < 0) { return -1; } if (value <= 0) { return 0; } return 1; }`). |
| D142 | CFS throw-block folding + CopyPropagation (subset) | port | (1) CFS now folds `br throwBlock` (throwBlock = a single Throw whose argument is a cloneable newobj) into a direct throw, eliminating the goto to the throw block. CloneThrowArgument / CloneCallWithPureArgs clone a newobj whose args are cheap pure loads (ldloc/ldc/ldstr/ldnull), recursing one level so `newobj(ctor, call Method(ldstr))` -- the common `throw new ArgumentException(GetResourceString("msg"))` shape -- also clones. System.Decimal.SetBits now renders as a clean sequence of early-exit throws with no gotos. Goto counts drop across mscorlib (Decimal 51->30, Array 200->151, Math 12->8). (2) Ports CopyPropagation (subset): dead-store removal (a single-def stack slot with no loads: drop if pure, keep side effects if not) + argument copy propagation (a single-def stack slot assigned from a never-assigned parameter/another single-def local has all its loads replaced with the source, store dropped -- the argument-to-local copy csc emits). The C# ldloca/ldsFlda sources and virtual-Clone cases are deferred; this port handles the common ldloc source. Wired into the CLI after the StatementTransform + HighLevelLoopTransform. 1022/1022 gtest cases pass (4 new CopyPropagation). |
| D143 | HighLevelLoopTransform MatchDoWhileLoop + CFS Call-value folding + seed goto-to-next dropping | port | (1) Adds MatchDoWhileLoop to HighLevelLoopTransform::Run: a Loop container whose last block is a do-while condition (an if with a true-arm Branch to the loop header [continue] and a fall-through that exits [break]), and whose entry is NOT a while-condition if, becomes a DoWhile container rendering as `do { ... } while (cond);`. Adds ContainerKind::DoWhile. Does not fire on the legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (while-shaped loops), faithfulness-only. (2) CFS value-return folding extended to Call values (single-pred, pure-load args) via CloneCallWithPureArgs -- `goto returnBlock` -> `return Method(args)` when the return block is single-pred. Goto counts: Math 6->5, String 260->229. (3) ConditionDetection 4th transform: drop trailing goto-to-next-block from if arms (the redundant `goto IL_XXXX` where XXXX is the next block) + swap empty true arm to `if (!cond)`. Goto counts: Decimal 30->18, String 308->260, Array 152->125. (4) Seed rendering: drop redundant goto-to-next-block from plain block finals (IsFallThroughGoto, rendering-only, block-final Branches only -- not if-arm/switch-section). 84% of sampled mscorlib methods are now goto-free (was ~60% at session start). 1023/1023 gtest cases pass. |
| D144 | InterpolatedStringTransform (the last per-statement StatementTransform child, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/InterpolatedStringTransform.cs -- the last per-statement child of the GetILTransforms() StatementTransform (after UserDefinedLogicTransform D139, skipping the deferred TransformArrayInitializers / TransformCollectionAndObjectInitializers / TransformExpressionTrees / IndexRangeTransform / DeconstructionTransform / NamedArgumentTransform / RemoveUnconstrainedGenericReferenceTypeCheck that sit between NullPropagationStatementTransform and UserDefinedLogicTransform in the C# order and are blocked by array/expression-tree/Index-Range/tuple/IMethod/InferType infrastructure). It folds the C# 10/.NET 6 `$"..."` lowering via DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler: `stloc v(newobj DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler..ctor(ldc.i4, ldc.i4)); call AppendLiteral(ldloca v, ldstr); call AppendFormatted(ldloca v, expr); ...; call ToStringAndClear(ldloca v)` into a single Block(Kind=InterpolatedString) holding the stloc + Append calls with the ToStringAndClear call as its FinalInstruction, so the back end can render it as `$"literal{expr}..."`. The handler variable v is promoted to VariableKind::InitializerTarget. Gated on the new StringInterpolation setting (default true, matching DecompilerSettings.StringInterpolation, a C# 6.0 setting). The type checks (`v.Type` and `newObj.DeclaringType` are `KnownTypeCode::DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler`) use the D93 `NullableLiftingTransform::IsKnownType`; the `call.Method.IsStatic` gate (reject static Append calls) is `call->IsInstanceCall` (the D76/D108 instance-call flag); `FindToStringAndClear` uses the D112-exposed `ILInlining::FindLoadInNext` free function to locate the `ldloca v` inside the ToStringAndClear call (its Parent is the call = insertionPoint). Three prerequisites landed: (1) a new `BlockKind` enum (`ControlFlow` default / `InterpolatedString`, in `Decompiler/IL/BlockKind.hpp`, mirroring the VariableKind.hpp / ContainerKind precedent) + a `BlockKind Kind` field on `Block` (default ControlFlow -- all existing blocks unaffected); (2) the `StringInterpolation` setting on `ILTransformSettings`; (3) the ILAstToCSharp seed renders a Block(InterpolatedString) as `$"..."` by walking Instructions[1..] (skip the stloc handler-init): AppendLiteral renders its LdStr arg as literal text (`{`/`}` escaped), AppendFormatted renders its value as `{expr}` with optional `,alignment` and/or `:format`, faithful to the real back end's TranslateInterpolatedString. The ToStringAndClear final is the implicit conversion to string and is not emitted. Adapted to this port's block model identically to the C#: the stloc + Append calls are non-terminal `block.Instructions`, the ToStringAndClear call is the first instruction after them (block.Instructions[interpolationEnd]); FindLoadInNext locates the `ldloca v` inside it (its Parent is the call), the call is detached via `TakeChild` and becomes the replacement block's FinalInstruction, the replacement block takes the call's slot via `SetChild`, and the stloc + Append slots are erased + RenumberChildren. No if-as-final block-model divergence -- the pattern is a flat instruction sequence, not an if/Block tail. KEY FINDING: DefaultInterpolatedStringHandler is a .NET 6+ type, so the fold fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (the handler-construction codegen is absent from it); it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET. Ported for faithfulness, matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks (D59) / LdLocaDupInitObj (D60) / SwitchOnNullable (D69) / NullCoalescingTransform (D90) precedent: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the positive fold produces a Block(InterpolatedString) with the stloc + Appends as Instructions and the ToStringAndClear as FinalInstruction, v promoted to InitializerTarget; the seed renders `$"Hello{x}"`; 11 negatives pin each gate: non-Local v, non-handler v type, non-newobj value, non-handler ctor DeclaringType, non-LdcI4 ctor arg, static Append call, unknown Append name, missing ToStringAndClear, wrong usage counts, StringInterpolation-off) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (not a fold count). Wired into the CLI pipeline as the last child of StatementTransform (after UserDefinedLogicTransform); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged). 1036/1036 gtest cases pass (13 new; was 1023). This completes the StatementTransform per-statement child set modulo the six infrastructure-blocked transforms (TransformArrayInitializers / TransformCollectionAndObjectInitializers / TransformExpressionTrees / IndexRangeTransform / DeconstructionTransform / NamedArgumentTransform / RemoveUnconstrainedGenericReferenceTypeCheck), which need array/expression-tree/Index-Range/tuple/IMethod/InferType infrastructure that lands with Phase 5; the UserDefinedLogicTransform RoslynOptimized pattern (D139 deferral) also remains. |
| D145 | FixRemainingIncrements (the op_Increment/op_Decrement calls TransformAssignment missed, Phase 4 next-in-order) | port | Ports ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/FixRemainingIncrements.cs -- the next in-order transform in GetILTransforms() after the StatementTransform set (after the deferred ProxyCallReplacer, before CopyPropagation [D142]). It handles the user-defined op_Increment/op_Decrement calls that TransformAssignment's inc/dec folds (D132/D133/D134/D136) did NOT fold -- the cases where the variable-being-incremented was optimized out by Roslyn (e.g. Console.WriteLine(++num) compiles to Console.WriteLine(UserType.op_Increment(a + b))), where the call's argument is not a load of the store variable so TransformAssignment's MatchLdLoc gate rejects it. The transform turns stloc V(call op_Increment(expr)) (the call is a StLoc ValueSlot + the StLoc is a Block InstructionSlot) into stloc V(expr); compound.assign op_Increment(V) (a UserDefinedCompoundAssign [D135] EvaluatesToNewValue, Address target, LdcI4(1) value, inserted after the store) so a later pass renders V = expr; ++V. This iteration ports the primary branch (the call is a StLoc Value and the StLoc is a non-terminal instruction in a Block -- the C# call.SlotInfo == StLoc.ValueSlot && call.Parent.SlotInfo == Block.InstructionSlot shape, ported structurally since this port does not model per-slot SlotInfo: call->Parent is a StLoc at ChildIndex 0 and call->Parent->Parent is a Block whose Instructions[store->ChildIndex] is the store). The else branch (the call is in any other position) needs ILInstruction.Extract (ILExtraction.cs -- the fresh-temporary-for-expression machinery, a larger slice) and is deferred. Decimal op_Increment/op_Decrement is skipped (the C# handles it in ReplaceMethodCallsWithOperators, a resolver path this port does not model; the call stays as a call). Gated indirectly via UserDefinedCompoundAssign::IsIncrementOrDecrement (the D135 helper, which consults CheckedOperators for the op_CheckedIncrement/Decrement variants). Prerequisite: Block gained an InsertAt(index, instruction) method (mirroring the C# block.Instructions.Insert(i, inst)) since the port previously only had Add/RemoveInstructionAt. The method metadata (MethodName/DeclaringType/ReturnType) is captured before the call is destroyed by ReplaceWith (no GC; the non-owning views would dangle). KEY FINDING: the transform fires 0 times on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus (mscorlib has no non-Decimal op_Increment/op_Decrement operator definitions, and the Roslyn optimized-out-variable codegen is absent from legacy-csc output); it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET. Ported for faithfulness, matching the DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks (D59) / LdLocaDupInitObj (D60) / SwitchOnNullable (D69) precedent: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the op_Increment / op_Decrement / op_CheckedIncrement primary-branch folds + 6 negatives: Decimal-skip, non-operator, wrong-arg-count, CheckedIncrement-off, non-StLoc-ValueSlot [the deferred Extract branch]) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus. Wired into the CLI pipeline after HighLevelLoopTransform and before CopyPropagation (per GetILTransforms: ProxyCallReplacer [deferred], FixRemainingIncrements, CopyPropagation); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 313 using / 78 ?? unchanged). 1045/1045 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 1036). The remaining post-StatementTransform GetILTransforms() targets are ProxyCallReplacer (needs the full IMethod/type-system/IL-reader context -- it reads and re-decompiles a proxy method body), DelegateConstruction (the full transform, D122 ported only the MatchDelegateConstruction helper -- needs IMethod resolution + ITypeResolveContext + SimpleTypeResolveContext + the lambda/closure machinery), LocalFunctionDecompiler, TransformDisplayClassUsage, ReduceNestingTransform, IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals (needs the dynamic node family + Await), and IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals (needs per-variable LoadInstructions/StoreInstructions lists + InferType) -- each needs Phase 5 resolver/type-system infrastructure. |
| D146 | ReduceNestingTransform (EliminateRedundantTryFinally subset, Phase 4 next-in-order after D145) | port | Ports the `EliminateRedundantTryFinally` piece of `ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.cs` (the C# `ReduceNestingTransform.Run` runs `Visit` for nesting reduction then a `function.Descendants.OfType<TryFinally>()` loop calling `EliminateRedundantTryFinally`; this iteration ports only the latter -- the `Visit`/`ReduceNesting`/`ReduceSwitchNesting`/`ImproveILOrdering`/`ExtractElseBlock` nesting-reduction pieces need a general `ILInstruction.Clone` for the keyword-exit (return/break/continue) duplication and stay deferred). The transform removes the redundant try-finally the C# compiler wraps a `fixed` block in: once `DetectPinnedRegions` (D57) has formed the `PinnedRegion`, a `.try BlockContainer { Block { PinnedRegion ... } } .finally BlockContainer { Block { leave (nop) } }` collapses to just the `PinnedRegion` (the empty finally is the compiler artifact `leave IL_xxxx (nop)`). The fold: the finally must be a single value-less `Leave` of the finally container (the C# `finallyContainer.SingleInstruction().MatchLeave(finallyContainer)`); the try must be a single-block container whose sole non-terminal instruction is a `PinnedRegion` (the C# `tryBlock.Instructions.Count == 1 && [0] is PinnedRegion`), with an optional trailing value-less `Leave` of the try container (the C# `Count == 2 && [1].MatchLeave(tryContainer)` case -- the `DetectPinnedRegions` `Leave(source)` final when the pin block is the last block in the try). The `PinnedRegion` is detached via `TakeChild(0)` (orphaning it) before `tryFinally->ReplaceWith` destroys the TryFinally subtree (no GC; the precondition-before-mutation discipline the D58/D75 lessons reinforce). KEY block-model adaptation (the recurring C#-`Block.Instructions`-includes-the-final vs this port's split `Instructions`+`FinalInstruction` divergence): the C# `BlockContainer.SingleInstruction` (`Blocks.Count != 1` -> container; `Blocks[0].Instructions.Count != 1` -> block; else `Blocks[0].Instructions[0]`) is ported as: `Blocks.size() != 1` -> container; `Instructions.empty() && FinalInstruction` -> the FinalInstruction (the single-instruction block -- the finally's `leave (nop)` is this port's `FinalInstruction`, per D72); else -> the block. Likewise the C# `tryBlock.Instructions.Count == 1` (the PinnedRegion is the only instruction, the final/last in C#) maps to this port's `Instructions.size() == 1` (the PinnedRegion is the sole non-terminal) with the trailing instruction living in `FinalInstruction` (null for the C# `Count == 1` case, a value-less `Leave(tryContainer)` for the C# `Count == 2` case); any other `FinalInstruction` (a `Branch`, a `Leave` of a different container) means the try block has real trailing control flow and is not collapsed. KEY safety (no GC, the D58 use-after-free lesson): the C# iterates `function.Descendants.OfType<TryFinally>()` and folds each -- folding an outer TryFinally destroys any TryFinallys nested in its try block, and this port's `unique_ptr` frees them immediately, so a pre-collected pointer vector would dangle. The port re-walks the tree for each fold instead: the walk stops at the first `CanEliminate` candidate, folds it (`Eliminate`), then the outer `while (changed)` loop re-walks the mutated tree. The redundant shape is rare (and corpus-dependent), so the bounded re-walks are cheap. The `MatchNop`/`MatchLeave(container)` helpers are ported faithfully: `IsNop` accepts a null slot (the reader emits `Leave(container)` with no `Value` for a void leave rather than a `Nop`) or an actual `Nop` node; `MatchLeave(inst, container)` checks a `Leave` whose `TargetContainer == container` and whose `Value` is a Nop/null. Wired into the CLI pipeline after `CopyPropagation`/`AssignVariableNames` and before `RemoveRedundantReturn` (the C# `GetILTransforms()` order is `HighLevelLoopTransform`, `ReduceNestingTransform`, `RemoveRedundantReturn`; the C++ pipeline runs `FixRemainingIncrements`/`CopyPropagation` after `HighLevelLoopTransform` per D145/D142, and `AssignVariableNames` before `RemoveRedundantReturn` per D46/D48, so `ReduceNestingTransform` sits right before `RemoveRedundantReturn`, preserving its C# relative order). KEY FINDING: the redundant try-finally-around-PinnedRegion shape does NOT arise on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- a corpus probe (the full pre-pipeline through `HighLevelLoopTransform` then `ReduceNestingTransform` over 8000 methods) counted `totalFolds == 0` (mscorlib's 204 methods with pinned locals / 303 PinnedRegions per D57 are NOT wrapped in a try-finally-with-empty-finally where the pin block is the sole try block; the legacy csc does not emit the redundant `fixed`-wrapping try-finally). The fold is ported for faithfulness -- it fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET that emits the artifact -- matching the `DetectCatchWhenConditionBlocks` (D59) / `LdLocaDupInitObj` (D60) / `SwitchOnNullable` (D69) / `NullCoalescingTransform` (D90) precedent: the hand-built tests verify the rewrite (the trailing-leave fold, the no-trailing-leave fold, multiple folds, the outer-folds-destroying-inner no-dangle case, and 8 negatives: valued finally leave, non-empty finally, multi-block try, non-PinnedRegion try, trailing Branch final, trailing leave of another container, finally leave of another container, try with an extra non-terminal) and the mscorlib sweep verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (not a fold count). The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 421795 lines, 517 lock / 313 using / 78 `??` unchanged; the fold fires 0 times on the corpus so the output is byte-identical to D145). 13 new gtest cases (was 1045, now 1058). Deferred vs the C#: the nesting-reduction pieces (`Visit` / `ReduceNesting` / `ReduceSwitchNesting` / `ImproveILOrdering` / `ExtractElseBlock` / `RemoveRedundantExit` / `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` / `CanDuplicateExit` / `UpdateStats`/`ComputeStats`/`ShouldReduceNesting` heuristics) need a general `ILInstruction.Clone` for the keyword-exit duplication plus `ConditionDetection.InvertIf` / `ConditionDetection.GetStartILOffset` (the latter is unblocked by the D115 per-instruction ILRange; the former needs exposing the port's `ConditionDetection` inversion) and the `ControlFlowGraph` dominator analysis (D39) for `ReduceSwitchNesting`; the `EliminateRedundantTryFinally` 2-block try case (`tryBlock.Instructions.Count == 2` with a non-leave second instruction) is structurally handled but does not arise; the rest of the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full transform / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) need Phase 5 resolver/type-system infrastructure per D145.
| D147 | ILInstruction.Clone foundation (Phase 4, the explicitly-cited blocker for ReduceNestingTransform nesting-reduction) | port | Ports `ILInstruction.Clone` (the deep-clone port of the C# generated `ILInstruction.Clone()`, one `sealed override` per instruction class in `Instructions.cs`) as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D138 UserDefinedLogicOperator precedent. D146 (and D139) cite the absence of a general virtual `Clone` as the blocker for the `ReduceNestingTransform` nesting-reduction pieces (`Visit`/`ReduceNesting`/`ReduceSwitchNesting`/`ImproveILOrdering`/`ExtractElseBlock` -- the keyword-exit return/break/continue duplication) and the `UserDefinedLogicTransform.RoslynOptimized`/`Transform` helper; this iteration lands the clone so those subsets' construction side is unblocked (the nesting-reduction still also needs `ConditionDetection.InvertIf` + the `ControlFlowGraph` dominator analysis (D39) for `ReduceSwitchNesting`; the `RoslynOptimized` still needs a corpus probe of the Roslyn-compiled post-`ConditionDetection` if-with-leave-arms shape, absent from the legacy-csc mscorlib corpus). The C# `Clone` is a virtual `sealed override` per class; this port implements the dispatch as a single `switch` over `Op` in one new translation unit (`cpp/Decompiler/IL/Instructions/ILInstructionClone.cpp`) rather than a per-class virtual override, so the clone logic is centralized/auditable and the ~60 concrete instruction classes need no header edits (the port has ~60 concrete classes vs the C#'s ~200; the not-yet-ported families -- dynamic, expression-tree, deconstruct, await, ... -- have no class here and fall through to a default assert). Each case constructs a fresh node copying the scalar fields, deep-clones the owned children (via their own `Clone`), copies the owned-collection elements (`Call.Arguments`, `Block.Instructions`/`FinalInstruction`, `BlockContainer.Blocks`, `SwitchInstruction.Sections`, `MatchInstruction.SubPatterns`, `NewArr`/`LdElema.Indices`, `TryCatch.Handlers`) by cloning each, and propagates the IL byte-range. References that are NOT owned children -- `ILVariable` (shared_ptr), `Branch.TargetBlock` (non-owning `Block*`), and `Leave.TargetContainer` (non-owning `BlockContainer*`) -- are copied by reference, matching the C# where those are not owned subtrees; a clone inserted elsewhere keeps pointing at the original tree's variables/blocks/containers and the caller fixes them up. The clone is disconnected (`Parent == nullptr`, `ChildIndex == -1`) but internally a valid tree (its children's `Parent` back-pointers point at the clone, set by the constructors / `Add`/`AddArg`/`AddSection`/`AddHandler`/`AddBlock`/`SetBody`/`SetFinal` helpers; only `ILFunction.Body` is a raw public field set without a Parent-setting helper, so the clone sets `Body->Parent`/`ChildIndex` manually). KEY field-fidelity notes: `Block` shadows the base `StartILOffset` with its own `uint32` field (the one the seed uses for labels) -- copied explicitly, and a `Block` clone's `IncomingEdgeCount` is left at 0 (a disconnected clone has no incoming edges until `RecomputeIncomingEdgeCounts` runs in its new tree); the `Comp`/`Conv`/`BinaryNumericInstruction` constructors re-derive `InputType`/`Kind`/`ResultStackType`/`Signed` from operands, so those clones overwrite the derived fields with the original's values for exact fidelity (the dump is otherwise equal but the non-dumped fields would drift). The `OpCode::ILInstruction::Clone` declaration is added to `ILInstruction.hpp` as a non-pure virtual (the default case asserts `"unhandled OpCode"` and returns a `Nop` as a release-degradation fallback; the debug sweep catches any gap). Wired into the `ilspy` CMake build. NOT wired into any pipeline transform or the `ILAstToCSharp` seed (a grep confirms the only `Clone()` references outside the impl/tests are two stale comments and the unrelated `Util::BitSet::Clone`), so `--csharp` output is byte-identical to D146 -- the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash; 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 0 `for` -- the lock/using/?? within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range, no for-loops since `MatchForLoop` is not ported). 15 new gtest cases in `ILInstructionClone_Test.cpp`: 14 hand-built (`LdLoc` shared-variable, `LdcI4` scalar, `StLoc` deep-copies Value + mutating the clone leaves the original untouched, `Comp` preserves Kind/LiftingKind/InputType/Unsigned, `BinaryNumeric` preserves overridden ResultStackType/IsLifted/Signed, `Call` clones Arguments + preserves MethodName/IsInstanceCall/IsNewObj/TypeArgumentsCount, `IfInstruction` 3 slots + null FalseInst preserved, `Block` clones Instructions/Final/Kind/StartILOffset + IncomingEdgeCount 0, `BlockContainer` clones Blocks + reparents to container, `Branch` copies TargetBlock reference + TargetOffset/HasOffset, `Leave` copies TargetContainer + clones Value, `TryFinally` clones both blocks, `NumericCompoundAssign` preserves Operator/IsLifted/EvalMode/TargetKind, `ILFunction` clones Body + reparents to the cloned function + shares Variables + copies IsConstructor/IsStatic) each asserting the clone invariants (dump-equal, disconnected, children are fresh deep copies reparented to the clone), and `MscorlibSweepClonesEveryInstruction` running the full pre-pipeline through `HighLevelLoopTransform` over 8000 mscorlib methods (so the synthesized node kinds -- PinnedRegion, SwitchInstruction, MatchInstruction, LockInstruction, UsingInstruction, While/DoWhile containers, ... -- appear, not just the raw-reader kinds) and cloning EVERY instruction, asserting the dump-equality + disconnected invariants hold -- the completeness gate (any instruction kind whose `Clone` is missing or wrong surfaces as a dump mismatch or the default assert; the sweep cloned a meaningful number of instructions and passed, so `Clone` is complete for the corpus). 1073/1073 gtest cases pass (15 new; was 1058); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Two now-stale comments (`NullableLiftingTransform.cpp` `ClonePureExpression` and `UserDefinedLogicTransform.hpp` `Transform`-deferral) that stated "this port has no virtual Clone" are updated to remain accurate and standalone. KEY LEARNING for future iterations: the `cpp/build/windows-ninja` build dir is configured with MSVC 2017 (14.16), whose older front-end mis-parses a C++17 raw-string literal in `ILAstToCSharp_Test.cpp` (`error C2017: illegal escape sequence` inside an `R"(...)"`); the previous iterations built and tested with the `cpp/build/windows-vs2026` dir (Visual Studio 18 2026 / MSVC 14.5x, Insiders at `C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\18\Insiders`), which handles it -- build and run the tests with `cmake --build cpp/build/windows-vs2026 --target ilspy_tests --config Debug` (and `--target ilspy_cli --config Debug`) after sourcing its `vcvarsall.bat x64`, not the `windows-ninja`/MSVC-2017 dir. The next in-order targets the foundation unblocks are the `ReduceNestingTransform` nesting-reduction pieces (need `ConditionDetection.InvertIf` + the D39 dominator analysis in addition) and the `UserDefinedLogicTransform.RoslynOptimized` pattern (need the corpus probe); the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) still need Phase 5 resolver/type-system infrastructure per D145/D146. |
| D148 | CopyPropagation ldloca/ldsflda source case (the D142-deferred virtual-Clone case, unblocked by D147) | port | Extends the D142 CopyPropagation subset with the ldloca/ldsflda address-load source case the D142 port deferred (`The C# ldloca/ldsFlda sources and virtual-Clone cases are deferred; this port handles the common ldloc source`), now that the D147 `ILInstruction::Clone` foundation landed. The C# `CanPerformCopyPropagation` returns true unconditionally for `LdLoca`/`LdsFlda` (an address load never throws and always yields the same address for a given operand); the C# `DoPropagate` then clones the source per load (`var clone = copiedExpr.Clone(); ...; expr.ReplaceWith(clone)`), un-inlines the source's child-instruction arguments into fresh stack slots (a no-op for ldloca/ldsflda -- their only operand is the variable/field, not a child-instruction slot, so `copiedExpr.Children.Count == 0`), clears the clone's IL range (`clone.SetILRange(new Interval())` -- the expression is copied from afar and reusing the source's range would mis-locate sequence points), and removes the store. The D142 port's ldloc case swaps the loaded variable (`ReplaceAllLoads` re-points `ldloc v` at `src`) -- no clone needed, since a ldloc is just a variable load; the ldloca/ldsflda case requires a clone because the load `ldloc v` is replaced by `ldloca src` / `ldsflda field` (a different opcode, not a re-pointed ldloc), so the load instruction is `ReplaceWith`-ed with a fresh clone of the source. The clone is taken before the store is removed (the source stays alive across the clones; `ReplaceWith` detaches each load mid-loop so the loads are collected first into a vector, then replaced). Adapted to this port's block model identically to the D142 ldloc case: the case fires only on `ControlFlow`-kind blocks' non-terminal `StLoc`s (the `block->Instructions` walk), gated on `v->Kind == VariableKind::StackSlot && v->IsSingleDefinition()` (the D142 dead-store case handles `LoadCount == 0` first, so the ldloca/ldsflda case fires with `LoadCount > 0`). The C# `target.Type.IsSmallIntegerType()` reject is irrelevant for a ldloca/ldsflda source (the target holds a managed pointer, never a small integer); the C# `splitVariables` reject (non-local code move might change semantics when the target is a split variable) is omitted, matching the D142 ldloc case's simplification. The C# propagates ldloca/ldsflda to ANY single-def target (the `switch` case returns true regardless of `target.Kind`); this port gates on `StackSlot` to match the D142 ldloc case's simplification -- the byref-local case (`target.Kind == Local`, `target.StackType == Ref`, the C#-faithful extension) is the case this port's StackSlot gating skips. Deferred vs the C#: the `LdElema`/`LdFlda` source case (the C# returns `!settings.UseRefLocalsForAccurateOrderOfEvaluation` -- those may throw and the setting controls whether moving the throw site is acceptable; the setting is not modelled here) and the generic leaf-instruction virtual-Clone `default` case (`value.Flags == InstructionFlags.None && value.Children.Count == 0 && target.Kind == StackSlot` -- a catch-all for pure leaf instructions like ldnull/ldc/ldstr stored to a stack slot, redundant with ILInlining for single-use constants) stay deferred; the `DoPropagate` argument-un-inline (needs fresh `ILVariable`s + `block.Instructions.Insert` -- the `Block::InsertAt` D145 helper covers the insert) stays folded into the ldloca/ldsflda no-op. KEY FINDING: the ldloca/ldsflda case fires 6 times over 3000 mscorlib methods (a corpus probe running the full `CopyPropagation` over the first 3000 method bodies counted the `PropagateAddressSource` invocations) -- NOT faithfulness-only; it eliminates 6 redundant byref-materialisation stack slots (`stloc v(ldloca/ldsflda src)` whose `ldloc v` loads become `ldloca src` / `ldsflda field` clones, the store dropped), matching the C# `CopyPropagation` which handles ldloca/ldsflda unconditionally. So the D142 `dominant ldloc case` (the argument-to-local copy) is joined on this corpus by a small but real ldloca/ldsflda case. 4 new gtest cases in `CopyPropagation_Test.cpp` (`PropagatesLdLocaSourceToEveryLoad` [stloc S(ldloca src); ldloc S; ldloc S -- both ldloc S become ldloca src clones, store dropped, 2 ldloca src inserted], `PropagatesLdsFldaSourceToEveryLoad` [stloc S(ldsflda field); ldloc S -- the load becomes a ldsflda field clone, store dropped], `DoesNotPropagateLdLocaWhenAddressTaken` [ldloca S makes S not single-def -- AddressCount > 0 -- so the case is skipped, the ldloca S and the store remain], `DoesNotPropagateLdLocaWhenTargetIsNotStackSlot` [stloc localV(ldloca src); ldloc localV -- localV is a Local, not a StackSlot, so the StackSlot gate skips it; the C# would propagate, this port's gating does not]) + the existing `MscorlibSweepPreservesInvariant` (now exercising the ldloca/ldsflda case across 3000 methods, asserting the ILAst invariant holds). 1077/1077 gtest cases pass (4 new; was 1073); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ?? / 0 for; the 6 ldloca/ldsflda folds do not change the coarse lock/using/??/for counts, and the line count is within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range). The remaining post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`), the `ReduceNestingTransform` nesting-reduction pieces (need `ConditionDetection.InvertIf` + the D39 dominator analysis), the `UserDefinedLogicTransform.RoslynOptimized` pattern (need the corpus probe), the `HighLevelLoopTransform.MatchForLoop` (the remaining while/for subset), and the `CopyPropagation` `LdElema`/`LdFlda` + generic-leaf virtual-Clone default cases are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D149 | ReduceNestingTransform nesting-reduction heuristics foundation (ComputeStats / UpdateStats / ShouldReduceNesting + pattern helpers, Phase 4 next-in-order after D148) | port | Ports the self-contained heuristics and pattern helpers the next in-order ReduceNestingTransform nesting-reduction folds (Visit / ReduceNesting / ReduceSwitchNesting / ImproveILOrdering / ExtractElseBlock) consult, as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation, following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D128 CheckImplicitTruncation / D129 IsBinaryCompatibleWithType / D130 RecombineVariables precedent (port the self-contained analysis/gate the next in-order transform needs ahead of the wired fold, so the fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration). D146 ported only the EliminateRedundantTryFinally piece and deferred the nesting-reduction pieces pending a general ILInstruction.Clone; D147 landed Clone, unblocking the construction side. This iteration lands the analysis side: (1) the ComputeStats / UpdateStats / ShouldReduceNesting heuristics (the C# ReduceNestingTransform.ComputeStats recursively tallies the number of statements and the maximum nested depth of an instruction; UpdateStats takes the max over one path; ShouldReduceNesting decides whether duplicating exits into the sibling blocks to reduce the nesting of a block by 1 is worthwhile -- max depth >= 2 always, depth 1 if the block is the largest, else twice as large); (2) the self-contained pattern helpers the heuristics and the future folds consult -- BlockUnwrap (a single-instruction expression Block unwraps to its instruction), MatchBranch (a Branch to / reporting a target Block), MatchLeave (a value-less Leave of a container), MatchConditionBlock (a For/While container entry point is a condition block -- an if whose false arm leaves the container and whose true arm branches to the body), UnwrapElseIf (a Block wrapping a single IfInstruction -- an else-if, handling both the C# model [the if is the sole non-terminal + Nop final] and this port's model [Instructions empty + the if as the FinalInstruction]). The heuristics are public static methods on ReduceNestingTransform (so they are testable); the helpers are file-local in the .cpp. KEY block-model adaptation (the recurring C#-Block.Instructions-includes-the-final vs this port's split Instructions+FinalInstruction divergence, D33/D72/D146): the C# Block.Instructions does NOT include the FinalInstruction (a void block's final is a Nop, and the control flow Leave/Branch are non-terminals in Instructions), so the C# block.Instructions.Last() (the last non-terminal, which IS the control flow) is this port's block->FinalInstruction. ComputeStats is adapted: a Block counts its non-terminal Instructions AND its FinalInstruction (the control flow) as statements the way the C# counts Instructions (which includes the control flow); the C# Nop-final-as-expression is a no-op and skipped. The BlockContainer case's lastInst (the C# containerBody.Instructions.Last()) is this port's containerBody->FinalInstruction; the For/While MatchConditionBlock is adapted (the if is either the sole non-terminal [C#] or the FinalInstruction [this port's While model, D140]). The IfInstruction else-if chain (Block.Unwrap(elseInst) is IfInstruction) is adapted via UnwrapElseIf (handles both models). KEY overload-resolution gotcha caught + fixed: the file-local MatchLeave(ILInstruction*, const BlockContainer*) [match against a container] and a MatchLeave(ILInstruction*, BlockContainer*&) [report the container] are ambiguous for a BlockContainer* lvalue argument (it binds to both -- the const-pointer via conversion, the reference exactly -- and the reference wins), so CanEliminate's MatchLeave(tryBlock->FinalInstruction.get(), tryContainer) call resolved to the report overload (returns true for any value-less Leave, ignoring the container) and mis-eliminated the RejectsTrailingLeaveOfOtherContainer / RejectsFinallyLeaveOfOtherContainer negatives; the report overload is not consulted by any heuristic and is removed, leaving the single match-against-container MatchLeave(ILInstruction*, const BlockContainer*). The For ContainerKind is not in this port (Normal/Loop/While/DoWhile/Switch only; no For since MatchForLoop is not ported), so the ComputeStats For cases (MatchConditionBlock for For/While, MatchBranch(Blocks.Last()) for For/DoWhile) drop the For arms -- behavior-preserving since no For containers arise. KEY FINDING: the heuristics are NOT wired into any pipeline transform (no transform consults ComputeStats/UpdateStats/ShouldReduceNesting yet -- the wired Visit/ReduceNesting/ReduceSwitchNesting folds are the subsequent iteration), so the CLI --csharp output is byte-identical to D148 (517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??; the deterministic lock/using/?? counts match the D148 baseline across runs; the line count is within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range). The foundation is exercised on the real corpus: the MscorlibComputeStatsSweep runs ComputeStats over 3000 decoded method bodies asserting non-negative finite stats (the heuristics must be robust on real trees) and maxDepth > 0 (some method has nested depth). 6 new gtest cases (ComputeStatsCountsBlockStatements [2 StLocs + Leave final = 3 statements], ComputeStatsNormalContainerDropsImplicitLeave [container + 2 StLocs, the implicit Leave not counted = 3, depth 1], ComputeStatsNestedIfAddsDepth [an if with a then-block adds depth >= 2], ShouldReduceNestingDepthHeuristic [a shallow block that is not the largest / not twice as large does not reduce], ShouldReduceNestingSizeHeuristic [a block 4x a 1-statement sibling reduces], MscorlibComputeStatsSweep). 1083/1083 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 1077); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the wired Visit / ReduceNesting (if/else no-else + else-if-tree cases) / ReduceSwitchNesting (needs the ControlFlowGraph dominator analysis [D39] for the default-case dominator-tree check) / ImproveILOrdering (needs ConditionDetection.InvertIf + GetStartILOffset exposed -- the latter is unblocked by the D115 per-instruction ILRange, the former needs exposing the port's ConditionDetection inversion) / ExtractElseBlock / RemoveRedundantExit / EnsureEndPointUnreachable (needs D147 Clone, now landed) / CanDuplicateExit (the recursive leave-walk, needs the block-model adaptation for the instruction-following-the-target-container) folds, plus the block-model corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection if-with-exit shape (the recurring D73/D75 divergence -- this port's if-as-FinalInstruction model makes the C# block.Instructions[i]/[i+1] if+exit two-instructions-in-one-block shape a if-final + next-block-exit shape) are the subsequent in-order targets; the post-StatementTransform GetILTransforms() targets (ProxyCallReplacer / DelegateConstruction full / LocalFunctionDecompiler / TransformDisplayClassUsage / IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals / IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals) still need Phase 5 resolver/type-system infrastructure per D145/D146.
| D150 | ConditionDetection.GetStartILOffset exposed as a public static (the foundation the wired ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering fold consults, Phase 4 next-in-order after D149) | port | Ports `ConditionDetection.GetStartILOffset` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/ConditionDetection.{hpp,cpp}) as a public static helper -- the first of the two statics the D149 deferral named as the foundation the next in-order wired ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering fold needs ("needs ConditionDetection.InvertIf + GetStartILOffset exposed -- the latter is unblocked by the D115 per-instruction ILRange, the former needs exposing the port's ConditionDetection inversion"), now that the D115 per-instruction ILRange (StartILOffset/EndILOffset + IsILRangeEmpty on every ILInstruction) is in place. GetStartILOffset is the simpler of the two: a pure function over an instruction's ILRange (no tree mutation, no block-model divergence), so it is the cleanest, lowest-risk next slice, while InvertIf (which mutates the tree and needs the block-model corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection if-as-FinalInstruction + next-block-exit shape, the recurring D73/D75 divergence) is a separate, larger iteration. Faithful to the C# `public static int GetStartILOffset(ILInstruction inst, out bool isEmpty)`: the IL byte-offset of the first IL instruction an ILAst instruction represents, for IL-order comparisons (ImproveILOrdering decides whether inverting an if to match IL order helps by comparing the then-branch's start offset against the fall-through's). The C# special case -- a valued Leave (a `return expr` whose Value is not a Nop) reports its Value's StartILOffset, because some compilers merge the leave instructions for different arguments using stack variables and the Leave's Value's ILRange is a better indicator of the actual location than the Leave's own -- is ported: a Leave whose Value is neither null nor a Nop reports the Value's offset + the Value's IsILRangeEmpty; otherwise the instruction's own StartILOffset + IsILRangeEmpty. This port's reader emits `Leave(container)` with no Value for a void leave (per D72/D146) rather than a Nop Value, so the "valued Leave" gate is `leave->Value && leave->Value->Op != OpCode::Nop` (a non-null, non-Nop Value -- the faithful equivalent of the C# `!leave.Value.MatchNop()`, where `MatchNop()` is `inst is Nop` and the C# leave.Value is never null); a null Value or a Nop Value is "not a valued leave" and falls through to the instruction's own offset. A defensive null-inst guard (returns 0 + isEmpty=true) covers a degenerate caller, where the C# would NPE (the C# is only ever called with non-null instructions from ImproveILOrdering/OrderIfBlocks). The `out bool isEmpty` parameter is a `bool&` out-param (the C#-idiomatic form this port uses elsewhere, matching the ILTransformContext/CompoundFinalizeMatch conventions). This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation matching the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables precedent: the helper lands ahead of the wired ImproveILOrdering fold that consumes it, so the fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration once InvertIf lands. No pipeline transform calls GetStartILOffset yet (ImproveILOrdering is the subsequent iteration, and OrderIfBlocks is part of ConditionDetection's own Run which this port's subset does not port), so the CLI --csharp output is byte-identical to D149. The foundation is exercised on the real corpus: the MscorlibGetStartILOffsetSweep decodes mscorlib methods and calls GetStartILOffset on every instruction (the reader-populated ILRange, D115), asserting a non-negative offset and that the isEmpty flag is consistent with the effective range (IsILRangeEmpty for the non-Leave case, the Value's IsILRangeEmpty for a valued Leave), and that the corpus carries valued `return expr` leaves (the helper's special case fires on real data, not just the hand-built tests) and instructions with the default empty range. 6 new gtest cases (GetStartILOffsetReturnsInstructionOffset [a plain LdcI4 with a set ILRange reports its StartILOffset, isEmpty false], GetStartILOffsetReturnsEmptyForDefaultRange [the default {0,0} range is empty, isEmpty true], GetStartILOffsetReturnsLeaveValueOffsetForValuedLeave [a Leave(container, LdLoc) reports the LdLoc's offset + the LdLoc's IsILRangeEmpty, NOT the Leave's -- the special case], GetStartILOffsetReturnsLeaveOffsetForValuelessLeave [a Leave with no Value reports its own offset], GetStartILOffsetReturnsLeaveOffsetForNopValuedLeave [a Leave with a Nop Value reports its own offset -- the Nop-Value case, the reader's null-Value form is the common one but a Nop Value can arise from transforms], MscorlibGetStartILOffsetSweep [~200k instructions checked, valued-leaves > 0, empty-ranges > 0]). 1089/1089 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 1083); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the `ConditionDetection.InvertIf` static (the second foundation the wired ImproveILOrdering/ReduceNesting folds need -- the "invert if to match IL order" operation that swaps the if's then-body with the block's fall-through and negates the condition; it needs the block-model corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection if-as-FinalInstruction + next-block-exit shape, since the C# reads the if as a non-terminal at block.Instructions[i] with the exit as a sibling at [i+1], while this port's if-as-final model + ConditionDetection's inversion makes the shape an if-final + next-block-exit, the recurring D73/D75 divergence), the wired ReduceNestingTransform folds (Visit / ReduceNesting / ReduceSwitchNesting [needs the D39 ControlFlowGraph dominator analysis, already in place] / ImproveILOrdering [now needs only InvertIf -- GetStartILOffset is in place] / ExtractElseBlock / RemoveRedundantExit / EnsureEndPointUnreachable [needs D147 Clone, landed] / CanDuplicateExit), and the post-StatementTransform GetILTransforms() targets (ProxyCallReplacer / DelegateConstruction full / LocalFunctionDecompiler / TransformDisplayClassUsage / IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals / IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D151 | ConditionDetection.InvertIf exposed as a public static (the second foundation the wired ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering/ReduceNesting folds need, Phase 4 next-in-order after D150) | port | Ports `ConditionDetection.InvertIf` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/ControlFlow/ConditionDetection.{hpp,cpp}) -- the C# `internal static void InvertIf(Block block, IfInstruction ifInst, ILTransformContext context)` -- as a public static helper, the second of the two statics the D149/D150 deferral named as the foundation the next in-order wired ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering/ReduceNesting folds need (D150 landed GetStartILOffset, the simpler pure-function half; this lands InvertIf, the tree-mutating half), following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables / D150 GetStartILOffset precedent (the foundation lands ahead of the wired fold, so the fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration). The C# InvertIf: `if (cond) { then (exits) }` (no else) + `falseCode...; exit` (sibling instructions after the if in the same block) -> `if (!cond) { falseCode...; exit }` + `then...` (the old then moved to after the if). The C# reads `ifInst` as a non-terminal at `block.Instructions[i]` with the `falseCode...; exit` as siblings `[i+1 .. Count)` (the C# `Block.Instructions` includes the control flow as the last non-terminal, `FinalInstruction` is a Nop); the C# `GetExit(block)` = `block.Instructions.Last()` (the exit), and the two cases are `if (ifInst != SecondToLastOrDefault)` (2+ instructions after the if: `ExtractBlock` the range into a `new Block()` as `ifInst.TrueInst`) vs `else` (if is second-to-last, exactly 1 instruction after = the exit: `ifInst.TrueInst = exitInst; block.Instructions.RemoveLast()`); then `if (thenInst is Block) block.Instructions.AddRange(thenBlock.Instructions) else block.Instructions.Add(thenInst)` (the old then appended after the if); then `ifInst.Condition = Comp.LogicNot(...)` + `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement`. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence the D150 deferral flagged): this port makes the `IfInstruction` the block's `FinalInstruction`, so the `falseCode...; exit` is NOT a sibling in the same block -- it is the NEXT block in the container (the fall-through). The adaptation: (1) the if must be the block's `FinalInstruction` (the C# `ifInst.Parent == block`), `FalseInst` null (no else, the C# `IsEmpty(FalseInst)` -- a null FalseInst is this port's "no else" since the reader emits a void if with a null FalseInst rather than a Nop), `TrueInst` unreachable (the C# `TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)`); (2) the next block (`NextBlockInContainer`) must be single-predecessor (`IncomingEdgeCount == 1`, only this block's fall-through) so moving its content into the if's TrueInst and the old then into the next block is semantics-preserving -- the C# has the falseCode in the same block as the if, so it is single-pred by construction; this port checks `IncomingEdgeCount == 1` explicitly (RecomputeIncomingEdgeCounts counts the positional fall-through edge, so a 1-pred next block is one with no Branch edges targeting it); (3) the if's new TrueInst is built from the next block's content -- Case 1 (next block has non-terminals = falseCode): wrap the next block's `Instructions` + `FinalInstruction` in a `Block` (the C# `ExtractBlock` into a `new Block()`); Case 2 (next block has no non-terminals = just the exit): the if's then is the bare exit (the C# `ifInst.TrueInst = exitInst`); (4) the old then moves into the next block (the "after the if" position): if the then is a Block, its `Instructions` AND its `FinalInstruction` (the control flow, which this port carries in `FinalInstruction` not `Instructions` as in the C#) spread into the next block; if the then is a bare exit, it becomes the next block's `FinalInstruction`; (5) the condition is negated via the existing file-local `NegateCondition` (which folds -- the C# `Comp.LogicNot` is a non-folding wrap + `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit; `NegateCondition` gives the same net result without the re-visit, which is not ported). The `context.Step` logging and the `RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit are dropped (the foundation is not wired; the wired ImproveILOrdering/ReduceNesting folds handle the re-visit if needed). The `DetachFromParent`-before-mutation discipline (D58/D75/D83) is followed: the old `TrueInst` is `std::move`-d out before the slot is reassigned, and the next block's content is moved before its `FinalInstruction` is reset. KEY FINDING (correcting the D150 deferral's framing via a corpus probe): the InvertIf candidate shape (if-final + TrueInst unreachable + FalseInst null) is COMMON on the post-ConditionDetection corpus, NOT rare/faithfulness-only. A temporary instrumented probe (the `MscorlibInvertIfShapeProbe` test) running the pre-pipeline (CFS + ILInlining + LoopDetection + ConditionDetection) over 8000 mscorlib methods counted: `ifFinals=7598`, `candidates=5158` (if-final + TrueInst unreachable + FalseInst null), `withNextBlock=4965`, `wouldFire(1-pred)=4142` (next block single-pred -- InvertIf WOULD fire), `multiPredBail=823` (next block multi-pred -- InvertIf bails), `noNextBlock=193`, `fires=2825` (InvertIf actually fired, one per method, invariant held on every fire). The initial analysis (that ConditionDetection's `TryInlineIfFallThrough` would consume the 1-pred fall-through, leaving the null-FalseInst shape only as the multi-pred bail) was WRONG: ConditionDetection's `TryInlineIfFallThrough` inlines the fall-through into `FalseInst`, then `TryInvertIfExit` INVERTS (swaps TrueInst/FalseInst, negates the condition, drops the goto), so the post-ConditionDetection shape IS `if (!cond, {fall-through; exit}, null)` -- TrueInst = the inlined fall-through (unreachable), FalseInst = null -- which IS the InvertIf candidate shape, with a 1-pred continuation next-block. So InvertIf, firing on the 1-pred candidate, INVERTS the already-inverted if back (re-negating the condition, swapping the then and the continuation) -- the operation ImproveILOrdering wants when the post-ConditionDetection inversion put the code in the "wrong" IL order. The probe fires InvertIf on every 1-pred candidate (no IL-order gate), so it over-fires relative to the wired ImproveILOrdering fold (which would add the `GetStartILOffset` order gate); the probe verifies InvertIf is SAFE (the invariant held on all 2825 fires) but the wired fold's IL-order gate is the subsequent iteration. The 823 multi-pred bails and 193 no-next-block bails are the minority (823+193 = 1016 of 5158 candidates = ~20%); the 4142 1-pred candidates are the ~80% majority. This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (no pipeline transform calls InvertIf yet -- the wired ImproveILOrdering/ReduceNesting folds are the subsequent iteration), so the CLI `--csharp` output is byte-identical to D150. 9 new gtest cases (InvertIfFoldsBareExitNoFalseCode [Case 2: no falseCode, the if's then is the bare exit, the old then (a Leave) moves into the next block as its final, the condition is negated], InvertIfFoldsFalseCodeIntoBlock [Case 1: falseCode + exit wrapped in a Block as the if's TrueInst, the old then (a Branch) moves into the next block], InvertIfSpreadsThenBlockIntoNextBlock [the then is a Block -- its non-terminals AND its final move into the next block, since this port's then-Block carries the control flow in FinalInstruction], InvertIfRejectsNonIfFinal [the if is not the block's FinalInstruction -- InvertIf is a no-op], InvertIfRejectsWithElse [FalseInst non-null -- no-op], InvertIfRejectsThenNotUnreachable [TrueInst is a LdLoc, not EndPointUnreachable -- no-op], InvertIfRejectsNoNextBlock [the if is the last block -- no-op], InvertIfRejectsMultiPredNextBlock [the next block has 2 predecessors -- no-op, the move would misbranch], MscorlibInvertIfShapeProbe [the corpus probe + invariant sweep, classifying the if-final shapes and firing InvertIf on the 1-pred candidates]). 1098/1098 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 1089); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the wired ReduceNestingTransform folds (Visit / ReduceNesting / ReduceSwitchNesting [needs the D39 ControlFlowGraph dominator analysis, already in place] / ImproveILOrdering [now unblocked -- both InvertIf [D151] and GetStartILOffset [D150] are in place; needs the IL-order gate `GetStartILOffset(TrueInst) vs GetStartILOffset(nextBlock)` + the trailing-leave keyword-exit handling + the block-model corpus probe of the real post-ConditionDetection trailing-leave shape] / ExtractElseBlock / RemoveRedundantExit / EnsureEndPointUnreachable [needs D147 Clone, landed] / CanDuplicateExit), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), and the post-StatementTransform GetILTransforms() targets (ProxyCallReplacer / DelegateConstruction full / LocalFunctionDecompiler / TransformDisplayClassUsage / IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals / IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals) are the subsequent in-order targets. |

| D152 | ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering wired + ConditionDetection.GetStartILOffset Block-label adaptation + TryInlineIfFallThrough inlineBlock label (the first wired ReduceNestingTransform nesting-reduction fold, Phase 4 next-in-order after D151) | port | Ports the `ImproveILOrdering` fold of `ICSharpCode.Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.cs` -- the first of the wired ReduceNestingTransform nesting-reduction folds the D149/D150/D151 foundation unblocked (the D151 InvertIf + D150 GetStartILOffset statics are in place) -- into the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform::Run`, which now calls a `VisitContainer`/`VisitContainers` walk before the `EliminateRedundantTryFinally` loop (matching the C# `Run` order: `Visit((BlockContainer)function.Body, null)` then the TryFinally loop). The fold: for an if-as-`FinalInstruction` with an unreachable `TrueInst` and a null `FalseInst` (no else -- the InvertIf candidate shape), the IL-order gate decides whether to re-invert ConditionDetection's inversion to restore IL order. ConditionDetection's `TryInlineIfFallThrough` + `TryInvertIfExit` inverted the reader's `if (cond) br trueBlock` (fall-through to falseCode) into `if (!cond) { falseCode; exit }` (TrueInst = the inlined falseCode+exit, FalseInst = null, fall-through to the old trueBlock / next block); ImproveILOrdering re-inverts ONLY when the IL order is wrong (the old then / next block comes BEFORE the falseCode / TrueInst in IL): `GetStartILOffset(TrueInst)` (the falseCode+exit's start) vs `GetStartILOffset(nextBlock)` (the old then's start); if `falseStart < trueStart`, `ConditionDetection::InvertIf` swaps the TrueInst and the next block's content and negates the condition. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence the D151 deferral flagged): the C# `ImproveILOrdering` reads the if as a NON-TERMINAL at `block.Instructions[i]` with the `falseCode...; exit` as sibling instructions after it (the C# `Block.Instructions` includes the control flow); this port makes the if the block's `FinalInstruction`, so the `falseCode...; exit` is the NEXT block in the container (the fall-through), and the C# `Visit`'s `block.Instructions` iteration becomes a check of the block's `FinalInstruction`. The `Visit` recurses into nested containers (the if's arms, the block's non-terminal containers) so nested ifs are visited; `continueTarget` (the C# Loop/While/DoWhile tracking for `CanDuplicateExit`) is not tracked (deferred with the trailing-leave handling). Two ILRange infrastructure fixes landed to make the gate fire on the corpus: (1) `ConditionDetection::GetStartILOffset` now handles the `Block` case -- this port's `Block` shadows the base `ILInstruction::StartILOffset` with its own `uint32` field (the block's label, set by the reader/BlockBuilder to the first instruction's offset), and the base ILRange (StartILOffset/EndILOffset) is NOT propagated through transforms that synthesize or merge blocks (ConditionDetection's inversion, CFS, ...), so a `Block`'s base ILRange is usually empty. The C# `GetStartILOffset` for a Block returns the Block's ILRange start (= the first instruction's offset = the label); this port returns the Block's label field for a Block (the faithful equivalent), with `isEmpty=false` (the label is always a valid offset). The D150 `MscorlibGetStartILOffsetSweep`'s `isEmpty` cross-check is updated to expect `false` for a Block (the label is valid). (2) `ConditionDetection::TryInlineIfFallThrough` now sets the synthesized `inlineBlock`'s `StartILOffset` label = the fall-through block's label, so the Block TrueInst (the inlined falseCode+exit) has a valid offset. KEY FINDING (a corpus probe): the gate fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- a temporary instrumented probe (the `MscorlibImproveILOrderingShapeProbe` test) running the full pre-pipeline through `HighLevelLoopTransform` over 8000 methods counted `gateWouldFire=6` / `fires=6` (InvertIf fired on the would-fire candidates, invariant held on every fire) -- a real-corpus transform, NOT faithfulness-only, matching the D118 NullCoalescing output case / D123 VisitConv precedent (the gate fires because ConditionDetection's inversion put the code in the wrong IL order for some methods). The `nonKeywordLeaveBail=0` -- the would-fire candidates carry a Branch/Throw exit (not a non-keyword Leave), so the trailing-leave handling is a no-op for them. The bare-Leave TrueInst path (the no-falseCode case whose Leave lost its base ILRange during the pre-pipeline -- the reader sets void Leave ranges, 3314 of 3343 have a range right after ReadIL, but the range is lost during the pre-pipeline) still bails at the gate (`trueRangeEmpty`); the ILRange propagation through that path is a separate piece. The trailing-leave handling (the C# `block.Instructions.Last() is Leave && !IsLeavingFunction && TargetContainer.Kind == Normal` check + `CanDuplicateExit` replacement that replaces a non-keyword Leave exit with a keyword return/continue) is deferred: the `CanDuplicateExit` try/finally walk is a larger piece; the bail-for-non-keyword-Leave-exit guard is ported so the fold does not introduce a `goto` (a non-keyword Leave at the fall-through position after InvertIf would render as `goto`).
