The `ReduceNesting` / `ReduceSwitchNesting` / `ExtractElseBlock` folds (the rest of the C# `Visit`) are deferred (need the full `CanDuplicateExit` / `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` / `ExtractElseBlock` helpers + the D39 dominator analysis for `ReduceSwitchNesting`). Wired into the CLI pipeline (the `ReduceNestingTransform().Run` call now runs the `VisitContainer` walk before the EliminateRedundantTryFinally loop); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 421401 lines, the deterministic lock/using/?? counts match the D148 baseline, the line count within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range; the 6 ImproveILOrdering fires re-invert 6 ifs to restore IL order, a small readability improvement that does not change the coarse counts). 3 new gtest cases (`MscorlibImproveILOrderingShapeProbe` [the corpus probe + invariant sweep, asserting `gateWouldFire > 0` and `fires > 0`], `ImproveILOrderingInvertsToMatchILOrder` [the hand-built fold through the pre-pipeline shape -- the if is inverted, the condition is negated via `NegateCondition`'s `comp(eq,v,0) => ldloc v` fold], `ImproveILOrderingNoOpWhenILOrderCorrect` [the gate bails when the next block comes AFTER the TrueInst in IL]). 1101/1101 gtest cases pass (3 new; was 1098); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the trailing-leave handling (`CanDuplicateExit` try/finally walk + the continueTarget tracking), the `ReduceNesting` / `ReduceSwitchNesting` / `ExtractElseBlock` / `RemoveRedundantExit` / `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` folds (the rest of the C# `Visit` -- need the full helpers + the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation, and the post-StatementTransform `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D153 | ReduceNestingTransform.CanDuplicateExit exposed as a public static (the linchpin helper the wired ImproveILOrdering trailing-leave handling and the deferred ReduceNesting/ReduceSwitchNesting folds consult, Phase 4 next-in-order after D152) | port | Ports `ReduceNestingTransform.CanDuplicateExit` -- the C# `private bool CanDuplicateExit(ILInstruction exit, Block continueTarget, out ILInstruction keywordExit)` -- as a public static on the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.{hpp,cpp}), the helper that decides whether an exit instruction is a duplicable keyword exit (return; break; continue;). It is the linchpin the wired ImproveILOrdering trailing-leave handling (the deferred piece from D152) and the deferred ReduceNesting / ReduceSwitchNesting folds all consult; `keywordExit` reports the keyword exit to duplicate (the exit itself for a direct return/break/continue, or the keyword exit found by walking out of a try/pinned/lock container to the instruction following it). Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables / D149 heuristics / D150 GetStartILOffset / D151 InvertIf precedent (the foundation lands ahead of the wired folds that consume it, so each wired fold is a separate lower-risk iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet. The C# logic: a Branch to `continueTarget` is a `continue`; a value-less Leave of the function body is a `return`; a value-less Leave of a non-Normal container (Loop/While/DoWhile/Switch) is a `break`; a valued Leave is not duplicable (duplicating it would re-evaluate the value); a Leave of a Normal container (a try/pinned/lock/etc. body) walks up from `leave.TargetContainer` until it finds an instruction in a Block that is NOT the last instruction, then recurses on the FOLLOWING instruction, bailing if it walks through a finally/fault block (cannot duplicate leaves from finally/fault containers) or a try-finally whose end point is unreachable (the finally always throws/returns). KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence): the C# `Block.Instructions` includes the control flow as the last non-terminal (FinalInstruction is a Nop), so "the last instruction in the block" is the control flow and the "following instruction" of Instructions[i] is Instructions[i+1]; this port splits the block's non-terminal Instructions from its FinalInstruction (the control flow), so "the last instruction" is the FinalInstruction and the "following instruction" of a non-terminal Instructions[i] is Instructions[i+1] if it exists, else the FinalInstruction (ported as the file-local FollowingInstructionInBlock helper). The C# `leavingInst.SlotInfo == TryFinally.FinallyBlockSlot` / `TryFault.FaultBlockSlot` (the FinallyBlock/FaultBlock slot) maps to this port's `leavingInst->ChildIndex == 1` on a TryFinally/TryFault (the FinallyBlock/FaultBlock is at ChildIndex 1). The Branch-to-continueTarget check is inlined (not the file-local MatchBranch) to avoid the D149 MatchBranch report-overload ambiguity (a `Block*` lvalue binds to the `Block*&` report overload, not the `const Block*` match-against overload). KEY DIVERGENCE discovered and handled: this port's `BlockContainer` does NOT replicate the C# `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags`, which sets `EndPointUnreachable` iff `LeaveCount == 0` (no leave targeting the container) and CLEARS it otherwise (the C# `if (LeaveCount == 0) flags |= EndPointUnreachable; else flags &= ~EndPointUnreachable;`); this port's BlockContainer uses the base `Flags() = DirectFlags() | OR of children's Flags()`, which propagates the leave's `EndPointUnreachable` up, so a NORMAL finally (exiting via `leave finC`) would incorrectly set the container's `EndPointUnreachable`. Naively reading `tf->Flags()` for the C# `tryFinally.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)` check would therefore bail for every normal try-finally (a faithfulness bug -- the existing port code never checks EndPointUnreachable on a container, which is why the divergence had not bitten before). The fix is the file-local `EndPointUnreachableCSharp` helper: for a BlockContainer it counts the leaves targeting the container within its subtree (the C# `LeaveCount`, via the recursive `CountLeavesTargeting`) and reports `LeaveCount == 0`; for every other instruction (Block, Leave, Throw, ...) this port's base `Flags()` is faithful to the C# `ComputeFlags` (Block.ComputeFlags = OR of Instructions + FinalInstruction, identical to the base; Leave/Throw set it in DirectFlags), so the flag is read directly. The TryFinally check is then `EndPointUnreachableCSharp(tf->TryBlock) || EndPointUnreachableCSharp(tf->FinallyBlock)` (faithful to the C# `TryFinally.ComputeFlags = TryBlock.Flags | FinallyBlock.Flags | ControlFlow`). A defensive guard bails if the walk reaches the ILFunction (the C# `Debug.Assert(!(leavingInst is ILFunction))`), since this port's `Parent` walk would dereference null past the root. KEY FINDING (a corpus probe via the MscorlibCanDuplicateExitSweep test): the helper is robust on real trees -- running the full pre-pipeline through HighLevelLoopTransform over 3000 mscorlib methods and calling CanDuplicateExit on every Leave and Branch (with continueTarget=null -- the continue detection needs the Visit walk's continueTarget tracking, deferred) checked 8267 exits, of which 1191 are duplicable (returns/breaks and the recursive-walk-out-of-try-finally cases), and the ILAst invariant held on every function (the read-only helper does not corrupt the tree). 10 new gtest cases (CanDuplicateExitContinueBranch [a Branch to the continue target -> true, keywordExit = the Branch], CanDuplicateExitReturnLeave [a Leave of the function body -> true, the return], CanDuplicateExitBreakLeave [a Leave of a Loop container -> true, the break], CanDuplicateExitRejectsValuedReturn [a valued Leave -> false], CanDuplicateExitRejectsNonLeaveNonBranch [a LdLoc -> false], CanDuplicateExitWalksOutOfTryFinally [a Leave of a try container inside a try-finally followed by a Leave(body) -> true, keywordExit = the return after the try-finally, the recursive walk], CanDuplicateExitRejectsFinallyLeave [a Leave of the finally container -> false, cannot duplicate leaves from finally], CanDuplicateExitRejectsFaultLeave [a Leave of the fault container -> false], CanDuplicateExitRejectsUnreachableFinally [a Leave of a try container whose finally always throws (no leave of the finally) -> false, the EndPointUnreachableCSharp finally check fires], MscorlibCanDuplicateExitSweep [the corpus sweep + invariant gate]). 1111/1111 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 1101); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- the helper is not wired into any pipeline transform, so the pipeline is byte-identical to D152; the line count 421379 is within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range, confirmed by a second consecutive run of the same binary yielding 421538 lines, a +/-159 run-to-run variance). Deferred vs the C#: the wired ImproveILOrdering trailing-leave handling (replace the bail-for-non-keyword-Leave-exit guard with the CanDuplicateExit replacement -- needs the Visit walk's continueTarget tracking for the `continue` detection), the `ReduceNesting` / `ReduceSwitchNesting` / `ExtractElseBlock` / `RemoveRedundantExit` / `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` folds (the rest of the C# `Visit` -- need EnsureEndPointUnreachable [needs D147 Clone, landed] + GetElseIfParent + ExtractElseBlock + RemoveRedundantExit + the D39 dominator analysis for ReduceSwitchNesting), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation, the BlockContainer.ComputeFlags LeaveCount-based EndPointUnreachable divergence (replicated inline in CanDuplicateExit for now; a future override of BlockContainer::Flags to match the C# would let the helper read `tf->Flags()` directly and fix the divergence for all consumers), and the post-StatementTransform `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D154 | ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering trailing-leave handling wired + Visit continueTarget tracking (the completion of the wired ImproveILOrdering fold, Phase 4 next-in-order after D153) | port | Ports the trailing-leave handling of the C# `ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering` -- the deferred piece from D152 that the D153 `CanDuplicateExit` foundation unblocked -- into the C++ `ImproveILOrdering`, completing the wired fold. The C# `ImproveILOrdering` checks `block.Instructions.Last()` (the if-block's last instruction) and, when it is a non-keyword Leave (a `Leave` of a `Normal` container that is not the function), replaces it with a keyword exit (return/continue/break) via `CanDuplicateExit` BEFORE `InvertIf`, so the if's then gets the keyword exit instead of a `goto` (InvertIf would otherwise move the non-keyword Leave into the then where it renders as `goto`); if the leave can't be duplicated, the fold bails. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence): the C# `block.Instructions.Last()` (Xn = the old-then exit, the if-block's last non-terminal) does NOT map to this port's `block->FinalInstruction` (which is `ifInst`, the if-as-final model) -- the C# if-block `[ifInst, oldThen..., Xn]` is split in this port: the if is Block A's `FinalInstruction` and the `oldThen...; Xn` is the NEXT block (Block B, the fall-through). So Xn = `nextBlock->FinalInstruction` (Block B's control flow), NOT `block->FinalInstruction` (Block A's `ifInst`). This corrects the D152 guard, which checked `TrueInstExit(ifInst->TrueInst.get())` (the falseCode's exit, inside the TrueInst) -- the WRONG exit (it checked the exit that moves from the then to the fall-through, not the one that moves from the fall-through to the then). The D152 guard was conservative-but-unfaithful: it bailed when the falseCode's exit was a non-keyword Leave (a no-op on this corpus, `nonKeywordLeaveBail=0`), but it did NOT catch the real C# concern (Xn being a non-keyword Leave). The wired handling checks the correct exit (`nextBlock->FinalInstruction`) and replaces it with the keyword clone via `CanDuplicateExit`. The clone is taken BEFORE the `SetFinal` that destroys the old Xn (`CanDuplicateExit` may report `keywordExit = Xn` itself for a direct return/break/continue), so the `keywordExit->Clone()` happens before `nextBlock->SetFinal(std::move(clone))`. The `continueTarget` (the C# parameter, the loop entry-point block a `continue` branches to) is now tracked by the `VisitContainer`/`VisitContainers` walk, matching the C# `Visit(BlockContainer)` switch: Loop/While -> the entry point (`Blocks[0]`), DoWhile -> the last block (`Blocks.back()`), Normal/Switch -> inherit the parent's `continueTarget` (null at the top level). `Run` passes `nullptr` to the top-level `VisitContainer` (the function body's Kind is Normal, so `continueTarget` stays null, matching the C# `Visit((BlockContainer)function.Body, null)`). `continueTarget` flows to `ImproveILOrdering` and through `VisitContainers` to nested containers. KEY FINDING (a corpus probe): the trailing-leave handling is FAITHFULNESS-ONLY on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus. A temporary instrumented probe (the `MscorlibImproveILOrderingExitProbe`, now reverted) running the full pre-pipeline through `HighLevelLoopTransform` over 8000 methods classified the gate-would-fire candidates' exits: Xn (`nextBlock->FinalInstruction`) is never a non-keyword Leave (`csharpTrailingLeaveFire=0` -- the would-fire candidates carry a Branch or `other` exit), and the falseCode's exit (`TrueInstExit`) is also never a non-keyword Leave. So the C# trailing-leave handling would fire 0 times on this corpus (matching the D59/D60/D69 precedent); the wired ImproveILOrdering fold still fires (the IL-order gate fires on the candidates whose ConditionDetection inversion put the code in the wrong IL order -- `gateWouldFire>0`/`fires>0` in the `MscorlibImproveILOrderingShapeProbe`). The CLI end-to-end on mscorlib confirms no regression: exit 0, 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` (the deterministic counts match the D153 baseline exactly), 421746 lines (within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range). 2 new gtest cases (`ImproveILOrderingTrailingLeaveReplacedWithKeyword` [the fire case: the if is inside a try-finally's try body (a Normal container); the next block's exit is a `leave(tryC)` (a non-keyword Leave); `CanDuplicateExit` walks out of the try-finally to the `leave(body)` (a return) after it; ImproveILOrdering replaces Xn with the return clone before InvertIf, so the if's TrueInst is the return (a Leave targeting the function body), not a Leave targeting tryC (a goto)], `ImproveILOrderingTrailingLeaveBailsWhenNotDuplicable` [the bail case: same shape but the finally always throws (a Throw, no leave of finC -- `EndPointUnreachableCSharp(finC)` is true); `CanDuplicateExit` bails, so ImproveILOrdering does not invert -- the condition stays Equality]). 1113/1113 gtest cases pass (2 new; was 1111). The CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the `ReduceNesting` / `ReduceSwitchNesting` / `ExtractElseBlock` / `RemoveRedundantExit` / `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` folds (the rest of the C# `Visit` -- need `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` [needs D147 Clone, landed] + `GetElseIfParent` + `ExtractElseBlock` + `RemoveRedundantExit` + the D39 dominator analysis for `ReduceSwitchNesting`), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (blocks the gate for ~2536 bare-Leave candidates), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now; a future override of `BlockContainer::Flags` to match the C# would let the helper read `tf->Flags()` directly and fix the divergence for all consumers), and the post-StatementTransform `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D155 | ReduceNestingTransform.GetElseIfParent exposed as a public static (the pure-analysis helper the deferred ReduceNesting else-if fold needs, Phase 4 next-in-order after D154) | port | Ports `ReduceNestingTransform.GetElseIfParent` -- the C# `private IfInstruction GetElseIfParent(IfInstruction ifInst)` -- as a public static on the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.{hpp,cpp}), the pure-analysis helper that determines whether an IfInstruction is an else-if (a Block wrapping only that if, nested as the FalseInst of a parent IfInstruction) and, if so, reports the preceding parent IfInstruction; otherwise null. It is the helper the deferred `ReduceNesting` else-if fold consults in two places: (1) the early root-bail `if (GetElseIfParent(ifInst) != null) return false;` (an else-if tree is reduced as a single group from the root IfInstruction, so a non-root else-if is not itself a reduction candidate), and (2) the per-iteration `var elseIfInst = GetElseIfParent(ifInst);` walk up the else-if tree in the `do { ... } while (ifInst != null);` loop that extracts the else block and inserts exit points all the way up. Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables / D149 heuristics / D150 GetStartILOffset / D151 InvertIf / D153 CanDuplicateExit precedent (the foundation lands ahead of the wired ReduceNesting fold that consumes it, so the fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet. The C# logic: `Block.Unwrap(ifInst.Parent) == ifInst` (the else block has only the if), `ifInst.Parent.Parent is IfInstruction elseIfInst` (the parent of the else block is an IfInstruction), and `elseIfInst.FalseInst == ifInst.Parent` (the else block is the false arm, not the true arm). KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence): the C# `Block.Unwrap` returns the block's sole instruction, where the C# `Block.Instructions` includes the control flow as the last non-terminal (FinalInstruction is a Nop) -- so the else block's sole instruction is `Instructions[0]` with a Nop final. This port splits the block's non-terminal Instructions from its FinalInstruction (the control flow), so the else block can carry the if either as a non-terminal (the C# model: `Instructions.size() == 1` with a null/Nop FinalInstruction) or as the FinalInstruction (this port's if-as-final model: `Instructions` empty with the if as the final). Both shapes unwrap to the if; a block with any other shape (more than one instruction, or a single non-if instruction) does not. The check is therefore: `elseBlock->Instructions.size() == 1 && IsNop(elseBlock->FinalInstruction.get()) && elseBlock->Instructions[0].get() == ifInst` (the C# model) OR `elseBlock->Instructions.empty() && elseBlock->FinalInstruction.get() == ifInst` (the if-as-final model). The `elseIfInst->FalseInst.get() != elseBlock` FalseInst check uses `.get()` (raw-pointer equality, the C# `==` reference equality). KEY FINDING (a corpus probe via the `MscorlibGetElseIfParentSweep` test): the else-if shape is COMMON on the post-ConditionDetection corpus -- running the full pre-pipeline through HighLevelLoopTransform over 3000 mscorlib methods and calling GetElseIfParent on every IfInstruction counted `totalIfs=4403`, `elseIfs=393` (GetElseIfParent reported non-null), `csharpModel=0`, `ifFinalModel=393`. ALL 393 else-ifs use this port's if-as-final block model (the if is the else block's FinalInstruction with empty non-terminal Instructions); NONE use the C# model (the if as a non-terminal + a Nop final). This is the recurring D73/D75 divergence in microcosm: ConditionDetection produces the if-as-final shape (the if IS the block's control flow), and the C# model (the if as a non-terminal with the control flow as a sibling) does not arise in this port, so the C#-model branch of the unwrap check is faithfulness-only on this corpus (it fires on 0 real else-ifs; it is ported for faithfulness and is exercised only by the hand-built `GetElseIfParentReturnsParentForCSharpModel` test). The helper is pure analysis (no tree mutation), so the ILAst invariant held on every function after the sweep (the `fn->CheckInvariant` inside the loop is the safety gate). This is a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation (no pipeline transform calls GetElseIfParent yet -- the wired ReduceNesting else-if fold is the subsequent iteration), so the CLI `--csharp` output is byte-identical to D154 (the deterministic 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ?? counts match the D154 baseline exactly; 421609 lines, within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range). 8 new gtest cases (`GetElseIfParentReturnsParentForCSharpModel` [the C#-model else block unwraps to the inner if, the parent is reported], `GetElseIfParentReturnsParentForIfAsFinalModel` [the if-as-final else block unwraps to the inner if], `GetElseIfParentRejectsNull` [null input -> null], `GetElseIfParentRejectsNonBlockParent` [the if is directly the FalseInst of a parent IfInstruction, not wrapped in a Block -> null], `GetElseIfParentRejectsMultiInstructionElseBlock` [the else block has [innerIf, stloc] -> does not unwrap -> null], `GetElseIfParentRejectsNonIfParent` [the else block is a direct block of a container, not the FalseInst of an IfInstruction -> null], `GetElseIfParentRejectsTrueArmElseBlock` [the else block is the TrueInst, not the FalseInst -> null], `MscorlibGetElseIfParentSweep` [the corpus probe + invariant sweep, classifying the else-if shapes]). 1121/1121 gtest cases pass (8 new; was 1113); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression). Deferred vs the C#: the wired `ReduceNesting` fold (the no-else case `if (cond) { ... } exit;` -> `if (!cond) exit; ...; exit;` and the else-if tree case -- need `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` [needs D147 Clone, landed, but the block-model adaptation for the C# `block.Instructions.Add`/`block.Instructions.Last()` on a single block `[ifInst, exitInst]` that this port splits into Block A (if-final) + Block B (exit-final) is the crux], `ExtractElseBlock` [the C# `block.Instructions.Insert` after the if, mapped to this port's two-block model], `RemoveRedundantExit` [the C# `block.Instructions.RemoveLast`, mapped to this port's FinalInstruction]), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 ControlFlowGraph dominator analysis + `ShouldReduceNesting` [done D149]), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (blocks the ImproveILOrdering gate for ~2536 bare-Leave candidates), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in CanDuplicateExit for now), and the post-StatementTransform `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D156 | ILRange propagation through the pre-pipeline clone sites (unblocks the ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering bare-Leave TrueInst path, the D152/D154/D155-deferred piece, Phase 4 next-in-order after D155) | port | Ports the IL byte-range propagation through the pre-pipeline clone sites the D152/D154/D155 deferrals flagged as the piece blocking the wired `ReduceNestingTransform.ImproveILOrdering` gate's bare-Leave TrueInst path (the no-falseCode `InvertIf` case whose Leave lost its base ILRange during the pre-pipeline, so `GetStartILOffset` reported empty and the IL-order gate bailed). Root cause: the C# clones return/break/value-return nodes via `block.Clone()` / `leave.Clone()` (the generated deep-clone, which copies each node's ILRange) and the loop break via `new Leave(loopContainer).WithILRange(branch)` (which `AddILRange`s the branch's range); the port's hand-rolled clone helpers instead built fresh `Leave`/`LdLoc`/`LdcI4`/`LdStr`/`Call` nodes with the default empty range, so a cloned return / break / value-return Leave lost the offset of the original opcode. Six clone sites are fixed to copy the original's ILRange (and, where the C# does, to add the branch's range), faithful to the C#: (1) `ControlFlowSimplification` "Replace branch to leave with leave" (the plain-leave fold) -- `dup->SetILRange(*targetLeave)` (the C# `leave.Clone()`) + `if (!branch->IsILRangeEmpty()) dup->AddILRange(*branch)` (the C# `leave2.AddILRange(branch)`); (2) `ControlFlowSimplification` "Replace branch to value-return with leave" -- the same `SetILRange(*targetLeave)` + `AddILRange(*branch)` on the value-return Leave; (3) `ControlFlowSimplification::ClonePureLoad` (the LdLoc/LdcI4/LdcI8/LdcF4/LdcF8/LdNull/LdStr clone the value-return and throw-block folds use) -- `clone->SetILRange(*v)` on the cloned leaf, so a valued `return expr` whose `GetStartILOffset` reports the Value's offset keeps it; (4) `ControlFlowSimplification::CloneCallWithPureArgs` (the newobj/call-with-pure-args clone) -- `cloned->SetILRange(*call)` on the cloned Call; (5) `InlineReturnTransform::CloneReturnBlock` (the multi-pred return-block clone) -- `newLeave->SetILRange(*leave)` + `newLdloc->SetILRange(*ldloc)` (matching the C# `block.Clone()`, which the C# `InlineReturnTransform` uses for the multi-pred case); (6) `LoopDetection` break-leave (the branch-to-loop-exit -> `leave(loopContainer)` rewrite) -- `leave->AddILRange(*br)` (the C# `.WithILRange(branch)`). The `AddILRange` semantics (an empty side adopts the other; disjoint ranges keep the earlier; overlapping/adjacent join) match the C# `AddILRange`/`WithILRange`, so a fresh Leave with an empty range adopts the branch's range, and a Leave with the original's range joined with the branch's range matches the C# clone+add. KEY FINDING (a temporary instrumented probe, `TEMP_BareLeaveILRangeProbe`, now reverted): the bare-Leave TrueInst path was ENTIRELY empty before the fix (all ~2214 bare-Leave candidates reported empty, `bareLeaveNonEmpty=0`); the fix unblocked it in stages. The CFS plain-leave fix (sites 1) took `bareLeaveNonEmpty` 0 -> 701 (the `goto retBlock` plain-return folds); the `ClonePureLoad` fix (site 3) took it 701 -> 2178 (the `goto retBlock` value-return folds -- ALL 1482 empty returns were VALUED returns `return expr;` whose Value's range GetStartILOffset consults, not plain returns; 0 plain-return empties remained after site 1). Only ~28 bare-Leave candidates remain empty after the fix (2 valued returns + 26 breaks), all from the port-specific `LoopDetection` while-condition false-arm (`leave(loop)` at line 104) and exit-block-null (`leave(parent)` at line 116) sites that are NOT in the C# `ConstructLoop` (the C# uses a fall-through Nop final where this port materializes an explicit leave -- the D72 block-model divergence) -- closing those is a separate, port-specific piece (they are FalseInsts / pre-header finals, not the gate's TrueInst, so they barely affect the gate). The wired `ImproveILOrdering` gate count rose accordingly: a clean full-suite run of the existing `MscorlibImproveILOrderingShapeProbe` shows `gateWouldFire=1783` / `fires=1355` (was `gateWouldFire=4` / `fires=4` at D154 -- a ~445x increase), with the ILAst invariant holding on every fire (no `FAILED`). The `nonKeywordLeaveBail=241` (the trailing-leave Xn bail, faithfulness-only per D154) is unchanged in nature. The 1 new gtest case pins the range preservation: `InlineReturnTransform.ClonePreservesLeaveILRange` (a multi-pred return block whose Leave+LdLoc carry a set ILRange; after `InlineReturnTransform` clones the block, each clone's Leave keeps the original's non-empty range and StartILOffset). TDD: the test was written first and confirmed red (the cloned Leave's range was empty, StartILOffset 0 vs 0x1A), then the `CloneReturnBlock` fix turned it green. The wired fold's extra fires CHANGE the CLI `--csharp` output (1355 more ifs re-inverted to restore IL order, the C#-faithful `if (!cond) ...; return;` early-return shape -- the corpus now shows 4751 `if (!...)` patterns), but the change is MORE faithful to the C# (the C# does the same IL-order-gated inversion, and the C# clone sites preserve the range, so the C# gate fires on these candidates too). CLI end-to-end on mscorlib: exit 0, no crash, no real error/assert markers (the 14134 `error`/`Exception` grep hits are false positives -- 796 are `Exception` type declarations); the deterministic `lock`/`using`/`??` counts match the D154 baseline exactly (517 / 314 / 79 -- ImproveILOrdering is an IL-order re-inversion that does not change the coarse statement counts), 421594 lines (within the D30 run-to-run non-determinism range). 1122/1122 gtest cases pass (1 new; was 1121). Deferred vs the C#: the remaining ~28 empty bare-Leave candidates (the port-specific `LoopDetection` while-condition false-arm / exit-block-null leave sites, which are not in the C# and are a separate port-specific piece), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), the wired `ReduceNesting` / `ReduceSwitchNesting` / `ExtractElseBlock` / `RemoveRedundantExit` / `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` folds (the rest of the C# `Visit` -- need the block-model adaptation for the C# single-block `[ifInst, exitInst]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final] + the D39 dominator analysis for `ReduceSwitchNesting`), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D157 | ReduceNestingTransform.EnsureEndPointUnreachable exposed as a public static (the third mutating helper the wired ReduceNesting fold needs, Phase 4 next-in-order after D156) | port | Ports `ReduceNestingTransform.EnsureEndPointUnreachable` -- the C# `private void EnsureEndPointUnreachable(ILInstruction inst, ILInstruction fallthroughExit)` -- as a public static on the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.{hpp,cpp}), the helper that ensures a block's end point is unreachable by duplicating and appending the [exit] instruction following the end point. It is the third mutating helper the wired `ReduceNesting` fold (the no-else `if (cond) { then } exit;` -> `if (!cond) exit; then; exit;` and the else-if-tree cases) consults to make a then/else block exit before `InvertIf` swaps it with the fall-through; D146 ported EliminateRedundantTryFinally, D149 ported the heuristics, D150 GetStartILOffset, D151 InvertIf, D153 CanDuplicateExit, D155 GetElseIfParent, and the remaining mutating helpers (`ExtractElseBlock` / `RemoveRedundantExit`) plus the wired fold itself are the subsequent iterations. Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables / D149 heuristics / D150 GetStartILOffset / D151 InvertIf / D153 CanDuplicateExit / D155 GetElseIfParent precedent (the foundation lands ahead of the wired fold that consumes it, so the wired fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet. The C# `EnsureEndPointUnreachable`: if `inst` is not a Block, assert it is already `EndPointUnreachable` and return; if it is a Block whose end point is reachable (`!HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)`), append a clone of `fallthroughExit` to `block.Instructions` so the block exits via the exit instead of falling through. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence, now biting `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` directly): the C# `Block.Instructions` does NOT include the `FinalInstruction` (a void block's final is a Nop, and the control flow Leave/Branch are non-terminals in Instructions), so a fall-through block's end point is reachable (no Branch/Leave in Instructions, Nop final). This port splits the block's non-terminal `Instructions` from its `FinalInstruction` (the control flow); a fall-through block's `FinalInstruction` is a `Branch` to the next block, which sets `EndPointUnreachable` (unlike the C# Nop final), so the C# `!HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)` check CANNOT distinguish a fall-through (Branch final) from a real exit (Leave/Throw final) -- both set `EndPointUnreachable`. The faithful check is the `FinalInstruction`'s opcode: the block falls through iff its `FinalInstruction` is a `Branch` (a fall-through, in this port's model where the fall-through is an explicit Branch) or a `Nop`/null (a void fall-through), NOT a `Leave`/`Throw` (a real exit) or an `IfInstruction` (the if-as-final block, Block A). When it falls through, the exit clone REPLACES the fall-through `FinalInstruction` (the C# appends to `Instructions`, keeping the fall-through as dead code; this port's `FinalInstruction` IS the control flow, so the fall-through Branch is dropped and the exit becomes the final -- the block's content in `Instructions` is preserved, and the block now exits via the exit instead of falling through to the same exit). A non-Block (a bare instruction) is a no-op (the C# asserts `EndPointUnreachable`). A `Branch` to a non-next block (a goto) is also treated as a fall-through by this opcode check (the C# `!EndPointUnreachable` is a no-op for a goto since the Branch sets it); this is a documented faithfulness gap that does NOT arise in the `ReduceNesting` no-else case (the then block either exits via a `Leave`/`Throw` final or falls through to the join block via a `Branch`-to-next final -- a goto does not arise after ConditionDetection), so it is safe for the wired fold. KEY FINDING (a mscorlib safety sweep): the helper is robust on real trees -- running `ReadIL` over 3000 mscorlib methods and calling `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` on every block with a `Leave(body)` exit (the body is the function's top container, an ancestor of every block, so the leave is valid) counted 12182 calls, of which 2307 were fall-throughs replaced (a `Branch`/`Nop`/null final -> a `Leave(body)` clone), and the ILAst invariant held after every call (the helper either no-ops or replaces the fall-through with a valid leave; no crash / tree corruption). This tests the helper's safety on real trees; the faithfulness is tested by the hand-built tests (the fall-through Branch replacement, the Leave/Throw/no-Block/null-exit/if-final/Nop/null-final cases). 10 new gtest cases (`EnsureEndPointUnreachableReplacesFallThroughBranchWithExitClone` [a content + Branch-to-next final -> the Branch is dropped, a Leave(body) clone is the new final, the content is preserved], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableIsNoOpForRealExit` [a Leave final -> no-op], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableIsNoOpForThrowExit` [a Throw final -> no-op], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableIsNoOpForNonBlock` [a bare LdLoc -> no-op], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableIsNoOpForNullExit` [a null exit -> no-op], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableIsNoOpForIfFinal` [an if-as-final block (Block A) -> no-op, the if's end point is its own control flow not a fall-through], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableClonesExitNotReuses` [the new final is a fresh clone, not the exit; the clone's TargetContainer is copied by reference], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableReplacesNopFinal` [a Nop final (void fall-through) -> replaced with the exit clone], `EnsureEndPointUnreachableReplacesNullFinal` [a null final (degenerate fall-through) -> replaced], `MscorlibEnsureEndPointUnreachableSweep` [the safety sweep]). 1132/1132 gtest cases pass (10 new; was 1122); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 21 `m`-decimal-literals, the deterministic counts match the D156 baseline exactly; the helper is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D156). Deferred vs the C#: the wired `ReduceNesting` fold (the no-else case + the else-if-tree case -- the no-else case is now unblocked on the `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` + `InvertIf` [D151] + the heuristic [D149] sides; it needs the block-model adaptation for the C# single-block `[ifInst, exitInst]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final] -- the C# `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(block, exitInst)` first call is a no-op in the C# [the block ends with exitInst] and is skipped in this port [Block A's final is the if, not the exit]; the C# `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(ifInst.TrueInst, exitInst)` second call makes the then block exit; `InvertIf` swaps the then with Block B's content; the `RemoveRedundantExit` helper and the `if (!ifInst.TrueInst.Match(exitInst)) ReplaceWith` are the remaining pieces], `ExtractElseBlock` (the C# `block.Instructions.Insert` after the if, mapped to this port's two-block model), `RemoveRedundantExit` (the C# `block.Instructions.RemoveLast` -- in this port the block's last instruction is the `FinalInstruction`; "removing" it makes the block fall through, which needs a Branch-to-next or Nop final adaptation), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D158 | ReduceNestingTransform.RemoveRedundantExit exposed as a public static (the last mutating helper the wired ReduceNesting fold needs, Phase 4 next-in-order after D157) | port | Ports `ReduceNestingTransform.RemoveRedundantExit` -- the C# `private void RemoveRedundantExit(Block block, ILInstruction implicitExit)` -- as a public static on the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.{hpp,cpp}), the helper that removes a redundant block exit instruction. It is the last mutating helper the wired `ReduceNesting` fold (the no-else `if (cond) { then } exit;` -> `if (!cond) exit; then; exit;` and the else-if-tree cases) consults; D146 ported EliminateRedundantTryFinally, D149 the heuristics, D150 GetStartILOffset, D151 InvertIf, D153 CanDuplicateExit, D155 GetElseIfParent, D157 EnsureEndPointUnreachable, and the remaining pieces are `ExtractElseBlock` (the C# `block.Instructions.Insert` after the if) + the wired fold itself (the no-else case is now unblocked on the `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` + `InvertIf` + the heuristic + `RemoveRedundantExit` sides; it needs the block-model adaptation for the C# single-block `[ifInst, exitInst]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final] -- the C# `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(block, exitInst)` first call is a no-op and is skipped [Block A's final is the if, not the exit]; the C# `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(ifInst.TrueInst, exitInst)` second call makes the then block exit; `InvertIf` swaps the then with Block B's content; the `if (!ifInst.TrueInst.Match(exitInst)) ReplaceWith` is the final piece]). Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables / D149 heuristics / D150 GetStartILOffset / D151 InvertIf / D153 CanDuplicateExit / D155 GetElseIfParent / D157 EnsureEndPointUnreachable precedent (the foundation lands ahead of the wired fold that consumes it, so the wired fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet. The C# `RemoveRedundantExit`: if `block.Instructions.Last().Match(implicitExit).Success`, `block.Instructions.RemoveLast()` -- the block's trailing exit (the keyword exit the fold duplicated into the then/else block) is redundant when it equals `implicitExit` (the instruction following the block's end point, i.e. the fall-through), so it is dropped and the block falls through. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence, the crux D157's deferral flagged): the C# `block.Instructions.Last()` (the last non-terminal, which IS the control flow in the C# where FinalInstruction is a Nop) is this port's `block->FinalInstruction`, and `RemoveLast()` (leaving a Nop final = a fall-through) is a replacement of the final with a fall-through: a `Branch` to the next block in the container (the positional fall-through) when there is a next block, or a `Nop` final when there is no next block (the implicit void fall-through / container leave, matching the C# Nop final). The Match is a structural equality (a new file-local `ExitsStructurallyEqual` helper) over the keyword-exit kinds the fold duplicates: a value-less `Leave` (return/break -- same `TargetContainer`, both `Value` null/Nop, or structurally-equal `Value`s for faithfulness) and a `Branch` (continue -- same `TargetBlock`); two nodes of different kinds, or a kind this does not handle (e.g. a `Throw`, or any non-exit), compare unequal (conservative: the exit is not removed rather than mis-removed -- the helper never fires for a `Throw` final, which is correct since a throw is not a duplicable keyword exit). The `NextBlockInContainer` file-local helper (already present from the ImproveILOrdering port) computes the fall-through target. The `Branch(Block*)` constructor carries the target's `StartILOffset` so the seed labels the fall-through goto correctly. A null `implicitExit` is a no-op (the C# `Match(null)` never succeeds); a null `final` is a no-op (no trailing exit to remove); a non-matching final is a no-op. KEY FINDING (a mscorlib safety sweep): the helper is robust on real trees -- running `ReadIL` over 3000 mscorlib methods and calling `RemoveRedundantExit` on every block whose final is a value-less `Leave(body)` (the body is the function's top container, an ancestor of every block, so the leave is valid), passing a fresh `Leave(body)` as the `implicitExit` so the helper fires (the final matches), counted 12182 blocks, of which 797 fired (the final replaced with a `Branch` to the next block or a `Nop`), and the ILAst invariant held after every call (the helper either no-ops or replaces the final with a valid fall-through; no crash / tree corruption). This tests the helper's safety on real trees; the faithfulness is tested by the hand-built tests (the matching Leave -> Branch-to-next, the matching Branch -> Branch-to-next, the no-match / null-implicitExit / null-final no-ops, the no-next-block -> Nop, and the content-preserved case). 8 new gtest cases (`RemoveRedundantExitReplacesMatchingLeaveWithFallThroughBranch` [a block with a Leave(body) final and a Leave(body) implicitExit -> the final is replaced with a Branch to the next block, the stloc content preserved], `RemoveRedundantExitReplacesMatchingBranchWithFallThroughBranch` [a Branch(continue) final matching a Branch(continue) implicitExit -> replaced with a Branch to the next block], `RemoveRedundantExitIsNoOpWhenFinalDoesNotMatch` [a Leave(body) final vs a Leave(otherContainer) implicitExit -> no-op], `RemoveRedundantExitIsNoOpForNullImplicitExit` [a null implicitExit -> no-op], `RemoveRedundantExitIsNoOpForNullFinal` [a null final -> no-op], `RemoveRedundantExitReplacesMatchingLeaveWithNopWhenNoNextBlock` [a single-block container, no next block -> the matching Leave is replaced with a Nop], `RemoveRedundantExitPreservesBlockContent` [two stlocs + a matching Leave final -> both stlocs preserved, final -> Branch to next], `MscorlibRemoveRedundantExitSweep` [the safety sweep]). 1140/1140 gtest cases pass (8 new; was 1132); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 21 `m`-decimal-literals, the deterministic counts match the D157 baseline exactly; the helper is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D157 modulo the D30 run-to-run non-determinism, 421544 lines). Deferred vs the C#: the wired `ReduceNesting` fold (the no-else case + the else-if-tree case -- the no-else case is now unblocked on the `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` [D157] + `InvertIf` [D151] + the heuristic [D149] + `RemoveRedundantExit` [this iteration] sides; it needs the block-model adaptation for the C# single-block `[ifInst, exitInst]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final] + the `if (!ifInst.TrueInst.Match(exitInst)) ReplaceWith` final piece], `ExtractElseBlock` (the C# `block.Instructions.Insert` after the if, mapped to this port's two-block model -- the else block's content moves into the container as new blocks after Block B), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D159 | ReduceNestingTransform.ExtractElseBlock exposed as a public static (the last piece the wired ReduceNesting else-if-tree fold needs, Phase 4 next-in-order after D158) | port | Ports `ReduceNestingTransform.ExtractElseBlock` -- the C# `private void ExtractElseBlock(IfInstruction ifInst)` -- as a public static on the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.{hpp,cpp}), the helper that extracts an if's else block: moves the else block's content out of the if's `FalseInst` into the parent block after the if, then clears the if's else (`ifInst.FalseInst = new Nop()`). It is the last piece the wired `ReduceNesting` else-if-tree fold (the `if (cond) { ... } else { ... } exit;` -> `if (cond) { ...; exit; } ...; exit;` case) consults; the else-if-tree loop calls it per iteration after `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` makes the then exit, to move the else content out so it becomes the fall-through after the if. D146 ported EliminateRedundantTryFinally, D149 the heuristics, D150 GetStartILOffset, D151 InvertIf, D153 CanDuplicateExit, D155 GetElseIfParent, D157 EnsureEndPointUnreachable, D158 RemoveRedundantExit, and the only remaining piece is the wired fold itself (the no-else `ReduceNesting` case is now unblocked on all its helper sides -- `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` + `InvertIf` + the heuristic + `RemoveRedundantExit` -- and needs only the block-model two-block adaptation for the C# single-block `[ifInst, exitInst]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final] + the `if (!ifInst.TrueInst.Match(exitInst)) ReplaceWith` final piece; the else-if-tree case is now unblocked on the `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` + `ExtractElseBlock` [this iteration] + `GetElseIfParent` [D155] sides). Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D63 LongSet / D66 LoopContext / D68 NullableLifting-helpers / D70 MatchInstruction / D74 UsingInstruction / D125 NumericCompoundAssign / D126 TransformAssignment-helpers / D130 RecombineVariables / D149 heuristics / D150 GetStartILOffset / D151 InvertIf / D153 CanDuplicateExit / D155 GetElseIfParent / D157 EnsureEndPointUnreachable / D158 RemoveRedundantExit precedent (the foundation lands ahead of the wired fold that consumes it, so the wired fold port is a separate lower-risk iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet. The C# `ExtractElseBlock`: `Debug.Assert(ifInst.TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable))`; `block = (Block)ifInst.Parent`; `falseBlock = (Block)ifInst.FalseInst`; `insertAt = block.Instructions.IndexOf(ifInst) + 1`; for each `falseBlock.Instructions[i]`, `block.Instructions.Insert(insertAt++, ...)`; `ifInst.FalseInst = new Nop()`. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence, the crux D158's deferral flagged): the C# `Block.Instructions` includes the control flow as the last non-terminal (FinalInstruction is a Nop), so the if is a NON-TERMINAL in the parent `block` and the else content is inserted after it as non-terminals IN THE SAME block (flattening the else Block into the parent block). This port splits the block's non-terminal `Instructions` from its `FinalInstruction` (the control flow); the if is Block A's `FinalInstruction`, so the else content CANNOT be added as non-terminals in Block A (they would precede the if-as-final, not follow it). The faithful adaptation is to move the else `Block` WHOLE into the container (Block A's parent) as a NEW SIBLING block after Block A -- the else block's `Instructions` (content) and `FinalInstruction` (control flow) become the sibling block's content and control flow, and the sibling block falls through to the next block (the exit / Block B) the way the C#'s inlined else content (now non-terminals in the parent block) falls through to `exitInst`. The if's `FalseInst` is set to a `Nop` (the C# `ifInst.FalseInst = new Nop()`; this port's `IsNop` treats both null and Nop as "no else", so a Nop FalseInst is consistent with the C# and with the port's `MatchNop`-style checks -- the wired else-if-tree case does not call `InvertIf` on the extracted if, so the Nop-vs-null distinction does not affect the wired fold). The sibling is inserted at Block A's container index + 1 (between Block A and Block B when Block B exists), and all the container's blocks are re-parented / re-numbered after the insert (the `DetectPinnedRegions` insert-block pattern). A non-Block parent (the if's `Parent` is not a Block, e.g. the if is another if's TrueInst), a non-Block `FalseInst` (e.g. a bare Leave, or null -- no else), or a Block A not found in its container are defensive no-ops (the C# casts both `Parent` and `FalseInst` to Block). KEY FINDING (a mscorlib safety sweep): the else-block shape (an IfInstruction whose `FalseInst` is a Block) does NOT arise after `ReadIL` alone -- a corpus probe running only `ReadIL` over 3000 methods counted `fired=0` (every if's `FalseInst` is a Branch/null after the raw IL read, not a Block; the else-block shape is a `ConditionDetection` artifact). Running the full pre-pipeline through `HighLevelLoopTransform` (the same sequence the D155 `MscorlibGetElseIfParentSweep` uses) over 3000 methods and collecting every IfInstruction whose `FalseInst` is a Block, then calling `ExtractElseBlock` on each (collected BEFORE the calls because `ExtractElseBlock` inserts a block into the container, which would invalidate a range-for walk of the container's `Blocks`; the if pointers stay valid across the moves since `ExtractElseBlock` does not destroy ifs), counted `calls=4403` / `fired=1011` (1011 real else blocks across 3000 methods -- the else-block shape IS common on the post-ConditionDetection corpus, like the D155 else-if shape [398 else-ifs]), and the ILAst invariant held after every method (the helper either no-ops or moves a Block into the container + clears the FalseInst to a Nop; no crash / tree corruption). The sweep calls `ExtractElseBlock` WITHOUT the C# `TrueInst`-EndPointUnreachable precondition (the C# only calls it after `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` makes the then exit), so the sweep tests SAFETY (no crash / tree corruption) on real trees, not faithfulness; the faithfulness is tested by the hand-built tests (the else Block moves after Block A; with Block B the else goes between Block A and Block B; the content + Leave final are preserved; the moved Block is reparented to the container; the if's FalseInst is a Nop; the non-Block-parent / non-Block-FalseInst / null-FalseInst no-ops). 9 new gtest cases (`ExtractElseBlockMovesElseBlockIntoContainerAfterBlockA` [no Block B: the else Block moves to index 1, the if's FalseInst is a Nop], `ExtractElseBlockInsertsElseBeforeBlockB` [with Block B: the else Block is at index 1, Block B stays at index 2], `ExtractElseBlockPreservesElseContent` [the stloc content + Leave(body) final are preserved on the moved Block, the Leave still targets the body], `ExtractElseBlockReparentsMovedElseBlock` [the moved Block's Parent is the container, ChildIndex 1; the if's Nop FalseInst is Parented to the if, ChildIndex 2], `ExtractElseBlockRejectsNull` [null ifInst -> no-op], `ExtractElseBlockRejectsNonBlockParent` [the if is another if's TrueInst (Parent is an IfInstruction) -> no-op, no block moved], `ExtractElseBlockRejectsNonBlockFalseInst` [a bare Leave FalseInst -> no-op], `ExtractElseBlockRejectsNullFalseInst` [a null FalseInst -> no-op], `MscorlibExtractElseBlockSweep` [the full-pipeline safety sweep]). 1149/1149 gtest cases pass (9 new; was 1140); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 21 `m`-decimal-literals, the deterministic counts match the D158 baseline exactly; the helper is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D158 modulo the D30 run-to-run non-determinism, 421285 lines this run vs 421544 at D158, a within-range variance). Deferred vs the C#: the wired `ReduceNesting` fold (the no-else case + the else-if-tree case -- BOTH cases are now unblocked on all their helper sides [EnsureEndPointUnreachable D157 + InvertIf D151 + the heuristic D149 + RemoveRedundantExit D158 + ExtractElseBlock this iteration + GetElseIfParent D155]; the remaining piece is the block-model two-block adaptation for the C# single-block `[ifInst, exitInst]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final] + the `if (!ifInst.TrueInst.Match(exitInst)) ReplaceWith` final piece + the else-if-tree `RemoveRange`/loop wiring), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D160 | ReduceNestingTransform.ReduceNesting (no-else case) exposed as a public static (the wired fold's core, tested-but-not-yet-wired, Phase 4 next-in-order after D159) | port | Ports `ReduceNestingTransform.ReduceNesting` -- the C# `private bool ReduceNesting(Block block, IfInstruction ifInst, ILInstruction exitInst)` -- as a public static on the C++ `ReduceNestingTransform` (cpp/Decompiler/IL/Transforms/ReduceNestingTransform.{hpp,cpp}), the no-else case of the wired fold (the else-if-tree case is deferred -- returns false). It reduces `if (cond) { then } exit;` to `if (!cond) exit; then...; exit;` by duplicating the keyword `exit` (return/break/continue) into the if's then and inverting the if, so the then (which exits) moves after the if and the exit becomes the if's (negated) then. `exitInst` is the keyword exit the caller found via `CanDuplicateExit` (the C# `Visit` passes `keywordExit`). Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D63/D66/D68/D149/D150/D151/D153/D155/D157/D158/D159 precedent (the fold lands ahead of the wired Visit walk that consumes it, so the wiring is a separate iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet (Run does not call it). KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence, the crux D159's deferral flagged -- the C# single-block `[ifInst, exit]` split into Block A [if-final] + Block B [exit-final]): the C# operates on a single block where the if is a non-terminal and the exit is a sibling (case a) or the fall-through `nextInstruction` (case b). This port splits that into Block A (the if is the FinalInstruction) + Block B (the exit is the FinalInstruction, the fall-through from Block A); the C# case a (the exit is a sibling in the block) does NOT arise (the if is Block A's final, so nothing follows it in Block A -- the exit is always in Block B). The C# steps map as: (step 3) `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(block, exitInst)` -- a no-op in this port; the C# appends `exitInst` to the block when it falls through to `nextInstruction` (case b) so the block ends in the exit and InvertIf's `GetExit(block)` finds it, but this port's Block A always falls through to Block B (which holds the exit) and InvertIf reads the exit from Block B (not the block's last instruction), so there is nothing to append. (step 4) `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(ifInst.TrueInst, block.Instructions.Last())` -- ensure the then exits via the keyword `exitInst` (the C# case-b net result, since the C# step 3 appended the keyword clone; this port's step 3 is a no-op, so the equivalent is to use `exitInst` directly). (step 5) `ConditionDetection::InvertIf` -- reads Block B's FinalInstruction (the exit) into the if's TrueInst and moves the old then into Block B. (step 6) `if (!ifInst.TrueInst.Match(exitInst)) ReplaceWith` -- ensure the if's TrueInst is the keyword; InvertIf made it Block B's original FinalInstruction (the exit), which may be a leave-from-try (not the keyword `exitInst` the caller found by walking out of the try), so it is replaced with the keyword clone. The fold bails (returns false, no mutation) if: the if has an else (the else-if-tree case, deferred), the then is shallow (`maxDepth < 2`), Block B is missing or has falseCode (the no-else case requires the exit directly after the if), Block B is not single-predecessor (InvertIf's requirement -- the C# has the falseCode in the same block, single-pred by construction), or the then cannot be made to exit (an if-as-final Block or a bare non-exit -- InvertIf would bail, leaving the fold incomplete; a new `ThenCanBeMadeToExit` file-local helper checks this without mutating, so the fold is atomic -- it bails before any mutation if any precondition fails). KEY FINDING (a corpus sweep, `MscorlibReduceNestingNoElseSweep`): the no-else fold is REAL-CORPUS but VERY RARE on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- running the full pre-pipeline + ReduceNestingTransform (so the wired ImproveILOrdering fold has re-inverted the early-return ifs, matching the shapes the wired ReduceNesting fold would see), then firing ReduceNesting on every no-else if-final whose next-block exit is a duplicable keyword exit, counted `candidates=2787` / `fired=1` across 3000 methods, with the ILAst invariant holding on the fire. The bail distribution (for the ~114 candidates with a duplicable exit): `bailMaxDepth=109` (a shallow then -- the ImproveILOrdering-inverted early-returns `if (!cond) return; rest...` have a shallow then = the return), `deepCandidates=4` (passed maxDepth >= 2 + Block B exists/empty/single-pred), of which `fired=1` and 3 bailed on `ThenCanBeMadeToExit` (the then is an if-as-final Block or a bare non-exit); the remaining ~2673 candidates lack a duplicable next-block exit (`CanDuplicateExit` false -- the next block is regular code, a Branch, or a non-keyword Leave). So the fold fires once (a real, safe fire), not faithfulness-only like D146/D154, but far rarer than the wired ImproveILOrdering fold (1355 fires). Step 4 (`EnsureEndPointUnreachable`) is effectively a no-op on the firing candidates: the no-else case's then must EXIT (a Leave/Throw final) for the fold to fire, because if the then falls through, its fall-through target is Block B (the exit), making Block B multi-pred and the fold bail on the single-pred check -- so the then always exits in the firing case and step 4 is a no-op (a documented consequence of the InvertIf single-pred adaptation [D151]; the C# fires on a falling-through then because InvertIf has no single-pred check there -- a faithfulness gap that does not arise on this corpus since the firing thens all exit). 7 new gtest cases (`ReduceNestingNoElseFoldsDeeplyNestedThen` [the fire case: a deep then (depth 2, two nested ifs) that exits, Block B = the return; the if is inverted, its TrueInst becomes the return, the deep then moves into Block B], `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsOnShallowThen` [maxDepth < 2], `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsOnElse` [the else-if-tree case, deferred], `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsWhenNoBlockB` [no Block B], `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsWhenBlockBHasFalseCode` [Block B has falseCode], `ReduceNestingNoElseReplacesLeaveFromTryWithKeyword` [Block B's exit is a leave-from-try inside a try-finally; step 6 replaces the if's TrueInst with the keyword return after the try-finally], `MscorlibReduceNestingNoElseSweep` [the corpus sweep + invariant sweep, with bail-reason diagnostics counting bailMaxDepth/bailNoBlockB/bailFalseCode/bailMultiPred/deepCandidates]). 1156/1156 gtest cases pass (7 new; was 1149); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 421412 lines, the deterministic counts match the D159 baseline exactly; the fold is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D159 modulo the D30 run-to-run non-determinism). Deferred vs the C#: the wired Visit walk (calling ReduceNesting from the VisitContainer walk after ImproveILOrdering + the RemoveRedundantExit call -- a separate iteration), the else-if-tree case (the `GetElseIfParent` root-bail + the else-if loop + `ExtractElseBlock` + the `RemoveRange` -- both helper sides are landed [D155 GetElseIfParent, D159 ExtractElseBlock], the loop wiring is the remaining piece), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), the falling-through-then case (the InvertIf single-pred adaptation bails where the C# fires -- a documented faithfulness gap that does not arise on this corpus), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D161 | ReduceNestingTransform.ReduceNesting (else-if-tree case) exposed as a public static (completing the wired fold's core, tested-but-not-yet-wired, Phase 4 next-in-order after D160) | port | Ports the else-if-tree case of `ReduceNestingTransform.ReduceNesting` -- the C# `private bool ReduceNesting(Block block, IfInstruction ifInst, ILInstruction exitInst)` else branch (the no-else case landed at D160; this iteration completes the function by handling the `if (cond) { then } else { ... } exit;` and the `if (c1) { t1 } else if (c2) { t2 } else { elseBlock } exit;` shapes). It reduces the nesting by duplicating the keyword `exit` into each then block and moving the else content out so the (exiting) thens stay nested but the else content (the bulk) flattens to the fall-through after the if chain: `if (c1) { t1; exit; } if (c2) { t2; exit; } elseBlock...; exit;`. `exitInst` is the keyword exit (return/break/continue) the caller found via `CanDuplicateExit` (the C# `Visit` passes `keywordExit`). Ported as a tested-but-not-yet-wired foundation following the D146/D149/D150/D151/D153/D155/D157/D158/D159/D160 precedent (the fold lands ahead of the wired Visit walk that consumes it, so the wiring is a separate iteration); no pipeline transform consults it yet (Run does not call it -- only ImproveILOrdering + EliminateRedundantTryFinally are wired). The C# logic: the no-else case (D160) handles `ifInst.FalseInst.MatchNop()`; otherwise the else-if-tree case bails if `GetElseIfParent(ifInst) != null` (an else-if tree is reduced as a single group from the root IfInstruction), walks DOWN the else-if chain (`while (Block.Unwrap(ifInst.FalseInst) is IfInstruction elseIfInst) { UpdateStats(elseIfInst.TrueInst, ...); ifInst = elseIfInst; }`) tallying each else-if's then, bails if the leaf's `FalseInst` is not a Block (no trailing else -- a bare Nop, guarded per #3891) or `!ShouldReduceNesting(falseBlock, maxStatements, maxDepth)`, then a `do { ... } while (ifInst != null)` loop walks LEAF-TO-ROOT calling `EnsureEndPointUnreachable(ifInst.TrueInst, exitInst)` (make the then exit), checking `ifInst.FalseInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)` and `block.Instructions.RemoveRange(...)` (drop the now-dead trailing exitInst when the else exits), and `ExtractElseBlock(ifInst)` (inline the else content into the parent block). KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence, the crux D160's deferral flagged): the C# `ExtractElseBlock` INLINES the else content into the PARENT BLOCK (which can be a nested else block), so the else-if chain flattens into a single block `[ifRoot, ifInner, ..., elseContent]`. This port's if-as-final model makes each if a block's FinalInstruction, so the else content CANNOT be inlined into a nested else block (it would precede the if-as-final). The faithful adaptation is to PROMOTE each else block to the CONTAINER (Block A's parent) as a NEW SIBLING block after Block A's position: after the fold the container is `[Block A (ifRoot), Block C (ifInner's holder), ..., Block E (else content), Block B (exit)]`, which renders the same as the C#'s single-block structure (each block falls through positionally to the next -- the C# output visitor would emit `if (c1) {...; exit;} if (c2) {...; exit;} elseBlock; exit;` either way). The KEY ORDERING difference from the C#: the C# does `ExtractElseBlock` LEAF-TO-ROOT (inlining into the parent block, which always exists). This port MUST do `ExtractElseBlock` ROOT-TO-LEAF, because `ExtractElseBlock` (D159) moves the else block to the CONTAINER of the if's parent block, and an intermediate else-if's parent block is another if's `FalseInst` (NOT a container) until its own parent if's else block has been promoted. After `ExtractElseBlock(ifRoot)` the next else-if's holder block is in the container, so `ExtractElseBlock` on the next if can promote its else; the if pointers stay valid across the moves (`ExtractElseBlock` does not destroy ifs, it moves else blocks and clears `FalseInst` to a Nop). The `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` calls (the then-exit duplication) are independent of the `FalseInst` moves (different slots), so they all precede the `ExtractElseBlock` calls. The dead-exit removal (the C# `RemoveRange`): the C# checks `ifInst.FalseInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)` per iteration and removes the trailing `exitInst` from `block` when the else exits. This port promotes the else content to the container (its end point is invariant across the moves -- `ExtractElseBlock` moves the whole Block, preserving its `FinalInstruction`), so a single check on the promoted else content (`BlockExitsReal` -- a Leave/Throw final, the same opcode check `EnsureEndPointUnreachable` uses, faithful to the C# `HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)` which this port's Block-container `Flags()` does not replicate per the D157 divergence) drops `Block B` via `RemoveEmptyExitBlock` (only when Block B has no non-terminal Instructions -- a conservative faithfulness gap for the Block-B-with-falseCode case that does not arise in the else-if-tree shape, where the exit is directly after the if). Two new file-local helpers: `BlockExitsReal` (a block's end point is unreachable -- its `FinalInstruction` is a Leave/Throw, not a Branch/Nop fall-through or an IfInstruction if-as-final) and `RemoveEmptyExitBlock` (erase a bare-exit Block B from its container and re-parent/re-number the remaining blocks). The plain if-else case (`ifRoot.FalseInst` is a Block not wrapping an if) is handled by the same code path: the walk-down does not descend (the else block does not `UnwrapElseIf`), `leaf == ifRoot`, and the else content is `ifRoot.FalseInst` directly. KEY FINDING (a corpus sweep, `MscorlibReduceNestingElseIfTreeSweep`): the else-if-tree fold is FAITHFULNESS-ONLY on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- running the full pre-pipeline + `ReduceNestingTransform` (so the wired ImproveILOrdering fold has re-inverted the early-return ifs) then firing `ReduceNesting` on every root if-final (not an else-if -- `GetElseIfParent` null) whose `FalseInst` is a Block (has an else) and whose next-block exit is a duplicable keyword exit, counted `candidates=716` / `fired=0` across 3000 methods (the invariant held; no crash / tree corruption). The 716 candidates are root if-finals with an else; of those, only 1 had a duplicable next-block exit and it bailed on `bailNoElseBlock` (the leaf's `FalseInst` is not a Block -- a no-trailing-else chain), and 0 bailed on `bailShallowElse` (`ShouldReduceNesting` false). So the fold never fires on this corpus (matching the D146/D154 faithfulness-only precedent -- the ReduceNesting heuristics fire on Roslyn-compiled/modern .NET, not the legacy-csc mscorlib); the no-else case (D160) fired 1 real fire, the else-if-tree case fires 0. The hand-built tests exercise the fire path (the deep-else else-if tree, the deep-else plain if-else) and the bail paths (the root-else-if bail, the no-trailing-else bail, the shallow-else bail via the renamed `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsOnShallowElse`). 6 new gtest cases (`ReduceNestingElseIfTreeFoldsDeepElse` [the fire case: a 2-level else-if tree with a deep exiting else; both ifs' else cleared to Nop, the else-if holder block and the else content block promoted to the container as siblings after Block A, Block B dropped because the else exits], `ReduceNestingElseIfTreeKeepsExitWhenElseFallsThrough` [the fire case where the else content falls through (a Branch-to-next final, not a Leave); Block B is kept -- it is not dead], `ReduceNestingElseIfTreeBailsOnRootElseIf` [a non-root else-if is not a reduction candidate -- `GetElseIfParent` non-null bails], `ReduceNestingElseIfTreeBailsOnNoTrailingElse` [a chain whose leaf has no else Block (a null `FalseInst`) bails], `ReduceNestingPlainIfElseFoldsDeepElse` [the plain if-else case -- no else-if chain, the else block is promoted directly], `MscorlibReduceNestingElseIfTreeSweep` [the corpus sweep + invariant sweep, with bail-reason diagnostics]). The existing `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsOnElse` test was renamed to `ReduceNestingNoElseBailsOnShallowElse` and its comment updated: it now bails because the shallow else block does not meet the `ShouldReduceNesting` heuristic (not because the else-if-tree case is deferred -- it is now handled, but the fold still bails on a shallow else). 1162/1162 gtest cases pass (6 new; was 1156 at D160); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 421408 lines, the deterministic counts match the D160 baseline exactly; the fold is not wired so the output is byte-identical to D160 modulo the D30 run-to-run non-determinism). Deferred vs the C#: the wired Visit walk (calling `ReduceNesting` from the `VisitContainer` walk after `ImproveILOrdering` + the `RemoveRedundantExit` call -- `ReduceNesting` now handles both the no-else and else-if-tree cases, so the wiring is the remaining piece; it also needs the content-block recursion `Visit(trueBlock)` / `Visit(falseBlock)` and the separate `ExtractElseBlock` call in the `ifInst.FalseInst is Block falseBlock` + `ifInst.TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)` branch), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), the falling-through-then case (the InvertIf single-pred adaptation bails where the C# fires -- a documented faithfulness gap that does not arise on this corpus), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D162 | ReduceNesting wired into the Visit walk (the no-else and else-if-tree folds now fire from `Run`), Phase 4 next-in-order after D161 | port | Wires `ReduceNestingTransform.ReduceNesting` (the no-else case [D160] + the else-if-tree case [D161]) into the `Run` Visit walk -- the `VisitContainer` file-local function now calls `CanDuplicateExit` + `ReduceNesting` + `RemoveRedundantExit` after `ImproveILOrdering` on each if-as-FinalInstruction, matching the C# `Visit` (`if (CanDuplicateExit(NextInsn(), continueTarget, out var keywordExit2) && ReduceNesting(block, ifInst, keywordExit2)) RemoveRedundantExit(block, nextInstruction)`). The C# `NextInsn()` (`block.Instructions.ElementAtOrDefault(i + 1) ?? nextInstruction`, the instruction after the if) maps to this port's next block's first instruction -- Block B's `FinalInstruction` when Block B has no non-terminal `Instructions` (the exit directly after the if, the no-else/else-if-tree shape), or Block B's first non-terminal when it has content. KEY BLOCK-MODEL ADAPTATION (the recurring D73/D75 divergence): the C# `Visit(BlockContainer)` iterates `container.Blocks` by index with a `container.Blocks[i] == block` assert, and the C# `ExtractElseBlock` inlines the else content into the SAME block (the container's block list is unchanged -- only appends are possible per the C# comment). This port's else-if-tree fold (D161) PROMOTES else blocks to the CONTAINER (inserts after the current block) and DROPS the dead exit block (removes after the current block), so a range-for over `container->Blocks` would be invalidated (std::vector iterator invalidation on insert/erase). The faithful adaptation is the index-based loop (matching the C#): the fold only inserts/removes blocks AFTER the current index (Block A is unchanged at index i), so the `assert(container->Blocks[i].get() == block)` holds and the loop re-reads the (possibly grown/shrunk) block list each iteration. The `RemoveRedundantExit` call: the C# passes `nextInstruction` (the fall-through after the block); for container blocks the C# passes null (a container block's end point is unreachable, so the `Debug.Assert(block.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable) || nextInstruction != null)` holds), and after the fold Block A's `FinalInstruction` is the if (not a keyword exit), so `RemoveRedundantExit(block, nullptr)` is a no-op here (the if is not a Leave/Branch, so `ExitsStructurallyEqual` returns false; and nullptr never matches). The content-block recursion (the C# `Visit(trueBlock, continueTarget, NextInsn())` / `Visit(falseBlock, continueTarget, NextInsn())` for single-Block then/else arms, where `RemoveRedundantExit` would fire with a real `nextInstruction`) and the separate `ExtractElseBlock` branch (the C# `if (ifInst.TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)) { ExtractElseBlock(ifInst); break; }` in the `ifInst.FalseInst is Block falseBlock` case) are deferred -- the current `VisitContainers` recursion into the if's arms processes nested BlockContainers but not single-Block if-as-final arms, so nested ifs in a Block then/else are not reduced (a subset of the full behavior, safe but incomplete; the content-block recursion is the subsequent iteration). KEY FINDING: the wired fold fires on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- the `MscorlibReduceNestingNoElseSweep` (which runs `ReduceNestingTransform().Run(*fn, ctx)` -- now including the wired ReduceNesting -- then walks and fires ReduceNesting again) shows the walk's `fired=0` (was 1 at D160) because the wired `Run` now fires the 1 corpus fire BEFORE the sweep's walk (the walk sees the post-`Run` shapes, where the 1 deep-then candidate was already folded by `Run`); the sweep's `candidates=2794` (was 2787) reflects the post-`Run` candidate set. The `MscorlibReduceNestingElseIfTreeSweep` shows `candidates=726` / `fired=0` (was 716/0 at D161 -- the wired `Run`'s else-if-tree fold fires 0 times, matching the D161 faithfulness-only finding). So the total fires are unchanged (1 no-else fire, 0 else-if-tree fires), now occurring in the wired `Run` rather than the sweep's direct call. 1 new gtest case (`ReduceNestingWiredRunFoldsNoElse` [the same no-else deep-then shape as `ReduceNestingNoElseFoldsDeeplyNestedThen`, but exercised via the wired `Run` walk rather than a direct `ReduceNesting` call; asserts the if is inverted, its TrueInst is the return (Block B's exit), and the deep then moved into Block B -- verifying the wiring, not just the static]). 1163/1163 gtest cases pass (1 new; was 1162 at D161); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` / 421432 lines, the deterministic counts match the D160/D161 baseline exactly; the no-else fold is a nesting-reduction that operates on if/return/break/continue structures, not lock/using/??, so the coarse statement counts are unchanged). The wired fold's 1 corpus fire changes the IL structure of 1 method (the deep-then no-else shape is inverted), which is MORE faithful to the C# (the C# does the same ReduceNesting fold from its `Visit` walk).
