Deferred vs the C#: the content-block recursion (`Visit(trueBlock, continueTarget, NextInsn())` / `Visit(falseBlock, continueTarget, NextInsn())` for single-Block then/else arms -- where `RemoveRedundantExit` fires with a real `nextInstruction` and nested ifs in a Block then/else get reduced; needs the content-block `nextInstruction` plumbing), the separate `ExtractElseBlock` branch (the C# `if (ifInst.TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)) { ExtractElseBlock(ifInst); break; }` in the `ifInst.FalseInst is Block falseBlock` case -- a post-ConditionDetection else-block extraction that fires when the then exits and there is an else), `ReduceSwitchNesting` (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit` for now), the falling-through-then case (the InvertIf single-pred adaptation bails where the C# fires -- a documented faithfulness gap that does not arise on this corpus), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals`) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D163 | ReduceNestingTransform wired ExtractElseBlock branch (the C# `Visit(Block)` if-case `if (TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)) { ExtractElseBlock(ifInst); break; }` branch, Phase 4 next-in-order after D162) | port | Wires the ExtractElseBlock branch of the C# `ReduceNestingTransform.Visit(Block)` if-case into the C++ `VisitContainer` walk (the C# runs it after `Visit(trueBlock)` and the ReduceNesting fold, before `Visit(falseBlock)`). When the if's then exits (`EndPointUnreachable`) and the else is a single Block, extract the else -- the else content becomes the fall-through after the if (the then exits, so the else is the only fall-through path): `if (cond) { ...; return; } else { elseContent }` => `if (cond) { ...; return; } elseContent`. This is the `if (ifInst.TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)) { ExtractElseBlock(ifInst); break; }` branch, a NEW fold distinct from the ReduceNesting else-if-tree case (D161): ReduceNesting else-if-tree requires a duplicable exit AFTER the if + the ShouldReduceNesting heuristic; the ExtractElseBlock branch fires when ReduceNesting did NOT fire (no duplicable exit after the if, or the else is too shallow for ShouldReduceNesting) but the then exits and there's a plain else Block. ExtractElseBlock (D159) promotes the else to the container as a new sibling block after Block A and clears the if's FalseInst to a Nop; the new sibling is visited by the container loop's next iteration (i+1). The C# `break` exits the block's instruction loop; this port's if-as-final is the block's last child (no further non-terminals), so no break is needed. KEY block-model divergence handled (the recurring D157 divergence): the C# uses `ifInst.TrueInst.HasFlag(EndPointUnreachable)`. This port's `Block::Flags()` unions ALL children's flags (the D157 divergence -- the C# Block.HasFlag is just the last instruction's flags), so `HasFlag(block->Flags(), EndPointUnreachable)` over-fires when a nested child exits but the block falls through. A new file-local `ThenExits` helper uses `BlockExitsReal` (the structural final-is-Leave/Throw check, faithful to the C# Block.HasFlag) for a Block then, `HasFlag` for a bare instruction then (a Leave/Throw, which carries EndPointUnreachable in its DirectFlags), and returns false for a BlockContainer then (deferred -- `HasFlag` over-fires badly for a container, and the container end-point check needs the unported `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount semantics). ImproveILOrdering bails safely for this shape (it requires no else -- `if (ifInst->FalseInst) return;`), and ReduceNesting bails when there's no duplicable exit after the if (the then-exits-else shape with no trailing exit), so the ExtractElseBlock branch fires cleanly. KEY FINDING: the `if (cond) { ...; return; } else { ... }` (then-exits-WITH-else) shape is RARE on the .NET Framework 4 legacy-csc mscorlib corpus -- ConditionDetection inverts early-returns to `if (!cond) { rest }` (no else) or `if (cond) return; rest` (no else), so the then-exits-else shape rarely arises. The wired ExtractElseBlock branch is faithfulness-only on this corpus (matching the D161 else-if-tree precedent: candidates=716, fired=0); the hand-built `WiredExtractElseBlockFoldsWhenThenExitsAndElseIsBlock` test verifies the fold fires (the else Block is promoted to the container as a sibling, the if's FalseInst becomes a Nop), the two negatives (then falls through, else is not a Block) verify the gates, and the `MscorlibWiredExtractElseBlockSweep` verifies the ILAst invariant holds across the corpus (asserts `totalFolds == 0`, the faithfulness-only contract) and reports the candidate/fold counts. The branch fires on Roslyn-compiled / modern .NET corpora (the then-exits-else shape is more common there). 4 new gtest cases (WiredExtractElseBlockFoldsWhenThenExitsAndElseIsBlock; WiredExtractElseBlockBailsWhenThenFallsThrough; WiredExtractElseBlockBailsWhenElseIsNotBlock; MscorlibWiredExtractElseBlockSweep). 1167/1167 gtest cases pass (4 new; was 1163); the CLI decompiles the full mscorlib module end-to-end (exit 0, no crash, no regression -- 517 `lock` / 314 `using` / 79 `??` unchanged; the branch fires 0 on this corpus so the output is byte-identical to D162). Deferred vs the C#: the BlockContainer-then case of the ExtractElseBlock branch (the container end-point check, needs the unported `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` semantics -- approximated as `false` for now), the content-block recursion (`Visit(trueBlock)`/`Visit(falseBlock)` for single-Block then/else arms -- a no-op for content-block ifs in this port's model: ReduceNesting/ImproveILOrdering bail for content-block ifs because there is no Block B / next-block-in-container, so nested ifs in Block arms cannot be reduced; the recursion would only visit nested containers VisitContainers already handles), ReduceSwitchNesting (needs the D39 dominator analysis), the `ExpressionTransforms.RunOnSingleStatement` re-visit (the wired fold's condition re-fold), the bare-Leave TrueInst ILRange propagation (the remaining ~28 port-specific LoopDetection sites), the `BlockContainer.ComputeFlags` LeaveCount-based `EndPointUnreachable` divergence (replicated inline in `CanDuplicateExit`/`ThenExits` for now), the falling-through-then case (the InvertIf single-pred adaptation bails where the C# fires -- a documented faithfulness gap that does not arise on this corpus), and the post-`StatementTransform` `GetILTransforms()` targets (`ProxyCallReplacer` / `DelegateConstruction` full / `LocalFunctionDecompiler` / `TransformDisplayClassUsage` / `IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals` / `IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals` -- each needs Phase 5 resolver/type-system infrastructure) are the subsequent in-order targets. |
| D164 | LoopDetection determinism fix (ConstructLoop block-move order + FindExitPoint exit pick) | fix | Ports no new C# transform; fixes a blocking-grade determinism defect in the already-ported `LoopDetection.cs`. Two `std::set<ControlFlowNode*>` iteration points leaked raw pointer-address order into the ILAst, so `ilspycmd --csharp` was a NON-deterministic function of its input (two identical runs on mscorlib differed by ~36543 lines: block reordering, goto-vs-empty-body, if-inversion, if/else presence, label placement; even the `??`/goto counts varied 80/79, 22025/22092, destabilizing the verify signature). Root cause 1: `ConstructLoop` moved loop-member blocks into the new loop container by iterating the `loop` set (address order), so member block order (and downstream label/goto naming) varied run-to-run. Root cause 2: `FindExitPoint` returned the first out-of-loop successor found in address order, so a multi-exit loop picked a run-to-run-varying exit block. Fix 1 moves member blocks by scanning `parent->Blocks` in the enclosing container's existing order (a membership `unordered_set<Block*>` for O(1)); Fix 2 iterates the loop members ordered by block `StartILOffset` and picks among all out-of-loop successor blocks the earliest one (min StartILOffset, the natural layout-next block), keeping the simplified single-exit model (the C# picks via a post-dominance analysis; deferred) but making the choice stable. Verified at the level the bug manifests: the mscorlib `--csharp` double-run went from always-different (red) to byte-identical (green); exit 0; signature unchanged at the known-good 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??; gotos even improved 22k -> 20124. 1171/1171 gtest pass (+1, `LoopDetection.PicksEarliestOutOfLoopExitDeterministically`, a guard asserting a two-exit loop exits to the earliest-offset block, not the later one). `ilast-all` (decode only, no transforms) was already deterministic, confirming the defect lived in the transform pipeline, not the reader or the seed emitter. This determinism is a precondition for trusting the runtime verify gate (the 517/314/79 signature is only meaningful on a stable output). Deferred (next nondeterminism candidates if any resurface): SwitchDetection `unordered_set`/`unordered_map` iteration (externalSet / breakTargets / primaryByTarget / toRemove), ControlFlowSimplification `std::set<Branch*> destroyed`, VariableUsage `unordered_set`s -- none currently observed to diverge after this fix. The remaining Phase-4 in-order tail after ReduceNestingTransform (D163) is effectively exhausted for productive porting: RemoveRedundantReturn's recursive descent is emitter-bound Phase-5 work (the seed pins non-body leaves to `break;` / body leaves to `return;`, and retargeting would trade a valid redundant `return;` for an invalid `break;`, breaking pinned emitter tests), and the post-StatementTransform GetILTransforms() targets (ProxyCallReplacer / IntroduceDynamicTypeOnLocals / IntroduceNativeIntTypeOnLocals / full DelegateConstruction / LocalFunctionDecompiler / TransformDisplayClassUsage) all need the Phase-5 resolver/type-system. |
| D165 | LoopDetection.FindExitPoint prefers the loop-exit convergence over min-StartILOffset (follow-up to D164) | fix | Corrects a latent exit mis-selection in the D164 deterministic pick. D164 chose the lowest-StartILOffset out-of-loop successor among all candidates; but a multi-exit loop's match/break path block often has a LOWER offset than the true post-loop convergence (e.g. mscorlib OAVariantLib.GetCVTypeFromClass: break-path block at IL_0015, true convergence at IL_0027), so min-offset exits into the break path rather than the block every exit flows into. FindExitPoint now collects the distinct out-of-loop successor candidates in deterministic offset order and, when there is more than one, prefers the unique candidate that every other candidate REACHES without re-entering the loop (`ReachesOutsideLoop` DFS, visited-set bounded; a fixed graph property, order-independent) -- the deterministic subset of the C# reverse-CFG post-dominator analysis (the full analysis stays deferred). Divergent exits (or a single candidate) fall back to the D164 min-offset pick, so D164's PicksEarliest guard and the byte-identical mscorlib determinism are preserved (the convergence branch fires 0 times on the mscorlib corpus -- single-exit/divergent or coincident -- so --csharp output is unchanged at 423460 lines, 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??, exit 0, deterministic). TDD: new LoopDetection.PrefersConvergenceExitOverEarlyBreakPath builds the GetCVTypeFromClass block graph (loop {cond, equals-test, increment} with a lower-offset match path and a higher-offset convergence) and was RED under min-offset (picked the break path) before the fix, GREEN after; full suite 1172/1172. NOT fixed (deferred, Phase 5 seed rendering): the GetCVTypeFromClass emitted text remains jumbled (a dangling `goto IL_001D` entry to the absorbed condition block, an early-break rendered as a backward `goto IL_0015` after the `while`, the break-to-non-designated-exit lost) -- the ILAst loop structure is now correct, but the seed cannot yet render break/continue to a non-designated exit (its documented approximation: "other leaves (switch/try) are break for now (the real back end disambiguates)"); that is emitter/rendering work, not a transform-correctness gap. |
| D166 | Seed drops loop pre-header entry branches (dangling-goto fix), no blank lines for dropped branches | fix | Fixes a class of compiled-invalid output: `ilspycmd --csharp` emitted 54 `goto IL_XXXX;` references whose `IL_XXXX:` label was never emitted (they won't compile), because a while/do-while/loop condition block becomes the loop head and gets no label -- yet a pre-header entry branch to it was still rendered. The seed's `GotoText`/`IsFallThroughGoto` only dropped the plain goto-to-next-sibling-block case; a pre-header branch into a nested loop's front block isn't a container sibling, so it fell through to a dangling `goto`. Added `IsLoopEntryFallThrough`: a block-final branch whose target is the FIRST block of a Loop/While/DoWhile container, from OUTSIDE the loop, where that container is the leading instruction of the very next block in the enclosing container, is a redundant fall-through into the loop and is dropped (returns "" like IsFallThroughGoto). Also made the `Branch` case of EmitStatement skip an empty GotoText entirely (previously a dropped goto emitted a stray whitespace-only line). Verified: mscorlib --csharp byte-identical across two runs (deterministic), exit 0, signature unchanged at the known-good 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??; gotos dropped 20124 -> 18586 (the 1538 removed were all redundant loop-entry fall-throughs), dangling gotos 54 -> 26 (28 invalid references eliminated), 2570 fewer output lines overall. TDD: new ILAstToCSharp.PreHeaderEntryBranchToWhileConditionIsDropped builds a pre-entry block branching to a While container's condition block and was RED (emitted `goto IL_0020` with no label) before the fix, GREEN after; full suite 1173/1173. The debug dump / instrumentation used to trace the corpus shape (getenv ILSPY_DUMP_ILAST_METHOD / ILSPY_DBG_LOOPENTRY) were temporary and removed before commit. DEFERRED known-issue: the remaining 26 dangling gotos are a different, genuinely-unstructured shape where a break/exit path block sits between the entry branch and the loop (e.g. OAVariantLib.GetCVTypeFromClass's `goto IL_001D`, whose emitted text remains semantically tangled); they need the real break/continue/label-disambiguation back end (Phase 5), not a local drop. |
| D167 | Generic (parameterized) types render as C# names, not ECMA reflection names | fix | `ilspycmd --csharp` rendered generic types from their ECMA reflection names: type declarations came out as a mangled last-segment substring (`String>` for `List<string>`), locals were named from the type-argument segment (`string>`, `exceptionDispatchInfo>`), and `newobj` emitted `new System.Collections.Generic.List`1<System.String>(...)`. Three fixes: (1) `CSharpTypeName` handles `ParameterizedType` -> `SimpleName<T1, ...>` with the base-name ``N arity suffix stripped and each type argument recursively C#-named (so System.String -> string); (2) `AssignVariableNames.InferName` cuts the reflection name at the first `<` (removing the `<...>` type-argument list) before taking the last segment, so the local is named from the base/container type; (3) the `newobj` expression in `CallText` renders the resolved `DeclaringType` via `CSharpTypeName` when it is a `ParameterizedType` (non-generic ctors leave DeclaringType unset/SimpleType and keep their existing full-name rendering). mscorlib reflection-name type refs (the ``N<` pattern) dropped 2597 -> 628 (~76%); e.g. `String> string> = new System.Collections.Generic.List`1<System.String>()` -> `IList<string> iList = new List<string>()`. Deterministic, exit 0, signature unchanged at 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??. TDD: three new tests (`CSharpTypeNameRendersGeneric` incl. nested `Dictionary<int, List<string>>`, `AssignVariableNames.GenericTypeTakesBaseNameNotTypeArg`, `GenericNewObjRendersCSharpTypeArguments`) were RED before, GREEN after; full suite 1176/1176. DEFERRED: the remaining 628 are member-access references on generic types (`TypeName`N<Args>.Member` static/field refs like `EmptyArray`1<System.Byte>`) which flow through the FlattenMetadataName/member-name paths (no IType on those names, would need a string-level generic-name rewriter); and the newobj constructer type when only a non-parameterized name is available. |
| D168 | A Nop else-arm renders no `else { }` (empty-else elimination) | fix | `ilspycmd --csharp` emitted an empty `else { }` block for ~5989 if-statements on mscorlib: those are block-final `IfInstruction`s whose else arm is a `Nop` (the IL idiom for "else fall through"), which the `isEmptyArm` check did not recognize (it only treated a null arm or a truly-empty Block as empty). A Nop else-arm carries no content, so the empty `else { }` is pure noise that real ilspycmd never emits. One-line change: `isEmptyArm` now also returns true for `arm->Op == OpCode::Nop`. Verified: empty-else blocks on mscorlib dropped 5989 -> 0 (else lines 8747 -> 2758), ~18000 noise lines removed; output byte-identical across runs (deterministic), exit 0, signature unchanged at the known-good 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??; the deeper reason some of these are `if (cond) goto L; else nop` scaffolding that should collapse/invert via ConditionDetection is a separate Phase-5-quality matter. TDD: new ILAstToCSharp.NopElseArmIsNotEmitted (an if with a Nop else-arm) was RED (emitted `else { }`) before, GREEN after; full suite 1177/1177. The temporary ILAst-dump debug hook used to find the shape was removed from ILSpyCmd/main.cpp before commit. |
| D169 | Drop redundant conditional gotos to the next block + gate label creation on emitted gotos | fix | A block-final `if (cond) br nextBlock` (no else) whose target is the immediately-next block is a no-op (both paths reach it), but ~81 such on mscorlib emitted a redundant `goto IL_XXXX;` plus an orphaned label, because `IsFallThroughGoto` only recognized a plain block-final branch, not a branch that is the true arm of a block-final no-else if. Two changes in the seed: (1) `IsFallThroughGoto` now also treats a branch that is the TrueInst of an enclosing block-final IfInstruction with no FalseInst as a fall-through, so the whole if is dropped; (2) `CollectLabels` only creates an `IL_XXXX` label for a branch that will actually emit a `goto` (skipping IsFallThroughGoto/IsLoopEntryFallThrough-dropped branches), so dropped branches no longer leave orphaned labels. The two helpers are forward-declared at anonymous-namespace scope so CollectLabels (earlier in the class) can call them. Verified: mscorlib byte-identical across runs (deterministic), exit 0, signature unchanged at 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??; gotos 18586 -> 18505 (the 81 removed), ~3k fewer lines; the per-method dangling-goto count is unchanged (926 both before and after -- computed method-scoped, correcting an earlier global-metric artifact where cross-method IL_ offset collisions made it look like 26/27). The remaining 926 dangling gotos are genuinely-unstructured jump-into-loop / multi-exit shapes needing the Phase-5 break/label back end. TDD: new ILAstToCSharp.RedundantConditionalGotoToNextBlockIsDropped was RED (`if (1 != 0) goto IL_0020;` + `IL_0020:` before) then GREEN after; full suite 1178/1178. |
| D170 | FlattenMetadataName strips generic type-arity before the type-argument list | fix | Doubles down on D167's generic-name work, which only covered type/variable declarations via `CSharpTypeName` and the `newobj` path via `CallText`. Remaining references -- static fields/properties/method calls on generic types, `typeof(...)`, and any member-ref rendered through the string-based `FlattenMetadataName` -- still leaked the ECMA `` `N `` arity suffix (`EqualityComparer`1<System.Byte>`, `List`1<System.String>`). `FlattenMetadataName` now runs `StripGenericArity`: scanning for `<` preceded by `` `Ndigits` (and that preceded by a backtick) and erasing the `` `N `` marker, recursing naturally into nested args by continuing from the erased position. The guard (must be `` `Ndigits` `` immediately before `<`) keeps compiler-generated names that merely *start* with `<` (`<>c`, `<Foo>d__0`) and comparison/pointer operator text unaffected -- only ECMA arity encodings match. Concrete-arg generic member refs on mscorlib drop 628 -> 178 (the remaining 178 are all `<!0>`/`<!!0>` *type-parameter* placeholders -- the CLR's VAR/MVAR index encoding -- whose names require reading the GenericParam metadata table per declaring type, a Phase-2 type-system task, deferred). Deterministic, exit 0, signature unchanged (517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??), gotos still 18505. TDD: new ILAstToCSharp.GenericMemberRefMetadataNameRendersCSharpForm (a `typeof(List`1<System.String>)` emission) was RED (`typeof(System.Collections.Generic.List`1<System.String>)`) before, GREEN after; full suite 1179/1179. |
| D171 | Render a `StLoc` used as an expression value as a chained assignment | fix | The C# seed fell back to `(default)/*op=34*/` whenever a `StLoc` appeared where an expression value is expected. That shape is a real ILAst idiom -- an inline assignment whose value IS another store (`stloc dup_N, stloc V, call(...)`, produced by ILInlining/CopyPropagation folding the C# `(hr = Marshal.GetLastWin32Error())` style test into a temp), so on mscorlib it printed 346 `var dup_N = (default)/*op=34*/;` broken declarations. `Expr` now handles `OpCode::StLoc` by rendering `name = Expr(value)`; chained assignment is right-associative so no parentheses are needed (`dup_34 = num_2 = Marshal.GetLastWin32Error()`) -- matching what the C# back end's inlining produces for the same pattern. All 346 occurrences now render as valid C#. Verified: mscorlib --csharp deterministic, exit 0, signature unchanged at 517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??, 18505 gotos. TDD: new ILAstToCSharp.NestedInlineAssignmentRendersChained was RED (`var dup_0 = (default)/*op=34*/`) before, GREEN after; full suite 1180/1180. LEFT-NOTE: the other `(default)/*op=N*/` fallbacks remaining in the seed are the ExpressionTransforms placeholder paths plus truly unrecognized ops (ldobj with nested IL-tree-class that can't express `--` quite right, NullableRewrap, RefAnyType / typedrefs) -- each is a decode, not a rendering, gap worth its own D-block. |
| D172 | Name MethodDef VAR/MVAR generic params from owner TypeDef/MethodDef GenericParam rows (`GetMethodSignature` passes a GenericParamNames to `DecodeMethodSignatureBlob`); member-ref/local/TypeSpec sites unchanged | 1181/1181; VAR-labeled lines 3471→2028; deterministic |
| D173 | Extend generic-param naming to local sigs, field sigs, and TypeSpec operands | fix | D172 only named VAR/MVAR in method-signature decode (GetMethodSignature). The remaining VAR/MVAR placeholder text came from three more decode surfaces, each with its own scoping rule now resolved: (1) LOCAL_SIG decodes in the owning method body's scope -- GetLocalTypes/GetLocalTypesWithPinned take an optional ownerMethodToken (both ILReader call sites pass it), so `T[] V_1` locals in generic methods come back authored-named; (2) a field sig's VAR scopes to the field's own declaring TypeDef, resolvable from the FieldDef row alone -- GetFieldSignature collects the parent TypeDef's GenericParam rows; (3) TypeSpec blobs decode in the scope of the method body the operand appears in -- ResolveTypeToken (newarr/castclass/isinst/box/...) and ResolveMethodDeclaringType (MemberRef-parent TypeSpec) take an optional ownerMethodToken threaded through a new ReaderState.ownerMethodToken in the IL reader, so `new T[newSize]`, `new List<T>()`, and `ArraySortHelper<T>.Default` call sites resolve their instantiation arg names. Shared build logic factored into one BuildGenericParamNames(db, methodRow) helper (also re-used by GetMethodSignature, replacing the inline D172 lambda). The inner TypeSpec-blob reader in the decoder now inherits the outer reader's name context (a TypeSpec wraps one type of the same signature). VAR/MVAR-bearing lines on mscorlib --csharp: 2028 -> 483 (D172 measured 3471 pre-naming). Remaining: `EqualityComparer`1<!0>.Default`-style static-member reference TEXT built through the string-level FlattenMetadataName path (the member-ref name renderer keeps no IType, so it cannot consult the name context) and MemberRef-signature parameter naming (the MemberRef's own sig decodes in the referenced method's scope, a separate pairing). Deterministic, exit 0, signature unchanged (517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??, 18505 gotos). TDD: new SignatureDecoder.LocalSignatureGenericParamsResolveFromOwner (scans a generic type's methods for a VAR-typed local) was RED ("local VAR in <>f__AnonymousType0`1::ToString should be named") before, GREEN after; new ILAstToCSharp.GenericArrayNewRendersMethodTypeParamName (Array.Resize<T> end-to-end emission) was RED (`T[] V_1 = new !!0[newSize]`) before, GREEN after; full suite 1183/1183. |
| D174 | Name VAR/MVAR in member-reference strings; strip arity on static property-accessor prefixes | fix | The last generic-placeholder class: static calls/fields on generic types whose declaring type is a TypeSpec render through ResolveTokenToString (Call::MethodName, LdFld/LdFtn/LdVirtFtn/SizeOf operands) -- a STRING path, so D173's IType-level naming never reached it. ResolveTokenToString gains the same optional ownerMethodToken and feeds its TypeSpec branches (MemberRef-parent, MethodSpec unwrap recursion); the IL reader passes ReaderState.ownerMethodToken at all 8 string-resolution sites. A second bug surfaced under it: the static property-accessor emitter extracts its short type name via rfind('.') without going through FlattenMetadataName, so `EqualityComparer`1<T1>` kept its ECMA arity marker even after the name resolved -- the accessor path now runs StripGenericArity on the extracted segment, so `EqualityComparer`1<T1>.Default.GetHashCode(...)` becomes `EqualityComparer<T1>.Default.GetHashCode(...)`. VAR/MVAR-bearing lines on mscorlib --csharp: 483 -> 0 (the D172 measurement started at 3471; generics-name the arc complete). Deterministic, exit 0, signature unchanged (517 lock / 314 using / 79 ??, 18505 gotos). TDD: new ILAstToCSharp.StaticCallOnCallerContextGenericRendersNamedArg (ValueTuple`1::GetHashCode end-to-end) was RED (`EqualityComparer`1<!0>.Default.GetHashCode(Item1)`) before, first GREEN attempt exposed the residual arity marker, then fully GREEN after the emitter fix; full suite 1184/1184. |
